  - **xAI REPLACES OpenAI rather than joining it**, because the seat count is fixed at three and a council's value is that its members fail differently. Grok is a fourth lab, and it is not a rubber stamp: single-shot it voted with sonnet-5 against the other four models.
  - **VERIFIED END TO END BEFORE PINNING, as a council and not seat by seat.** The full panel ran through `runPanel` on the real prompt: `votesCast 3/3`, `degraded false`, `resolution majority`, 23.8s wall, winner Pro 66.5 to 54.8, agreement 0.67. **That run also corrected a claim I had already written into the season comment**: single-shot, sonnet-5 and grok-4.3 both said Con, so I recorded that the new bench flips the round to Con. Assembled, it returned Pro 2-1. The honest statement is that the seats genuinely disagree and are not stable between runs on a close round, which is a reason to write the measurement down rather than the conclusion I expected from it.
  - **Kimi and DeepSeek were both asked for and NEITHER could be seated.** Sent the real ballot prompt: `deepseek-v4-flash` ran 73s and spent all 8000 tokens reasoning without writing a ballot; `kimi-k2.6` and `kimi-k3` returned nothing in 90 seconds, then nothing in seven minutes, while a trivial prompt answers in 1 to 5 seconds, so the route is fine and the task is what defeats them. Both direct Moonshot keys on this account are rejected by `api.moonshot.ai` AND `api.moonshot.cn`, and `GROQ_API_KEY` is rejected too. **Cheap per token is not cheap in practice when a model reasons without bound.** Both are wired as standby families in `judge-jurors.mjs`, so seating one is a season edit once it can produce a ballot inside a usable budget.
  - **xAI and OpenRouter joined the juror dispatch**, with a rule worth keeping: **a router is not a family.** OpenRouter can serve any model, so a season must pin the full upstream model id; pinning `openrouter` alone would let the model behind a published seat change without the season changing, which is the quiet swap this charter exists to prevent.
  - The integrity guard's live-council assertion is re-pinned to the new season id on purpose, so re-benching is a deliberate edit there too rather than something a season swap carries silently. The two-vote substance is unchanged. `xai` gains a bench persona, since a pinned family without a face is a seat a debater cannot read.

- **The judging panel had no window it could finish in, so a scheduled sweep judges the rounds the request path cannot** (2026-09-03, from spectators on a live round: "the panel hasn't been able to deliberate"). Their reading was exact. Measured against the round they were watching (`SparMatch-Ky3Q2opK`, four speeches, ~2.6k tokens of transcript) by re-running its real prompt against production keys with a generous budget: **claude-fable-5 took 25.9s, gpt-5.5 34.2s, gemini-3.6-flash 23.3s, and every one of them SUCCEEDED.** Nothing was broken. All six brains probed healthy twelve seconds earlier. The jurors are simply slower than the 22 second cap the request path gives them, and that cap cannot be raised, because a synchronous invocation dies at roughly 26 seconds anyway. So all three were aborted, the panel recorded `votesCast: 0`, the client scheduled a retry into the identical wall, and the round sat on "Ballot is generating" through five attempts.
  - **This is the shape the log keeps recording, one layer further out.** Every previous version of it was a component returning a plausible answer to a slightly different question. This one is a component that is entirely correct and is simply never given long enough to answer at all, and the failure presents as an infinite spinner rather than as an error. **A timeout is a product decision, not a tuning constant: 22 seconds is a claim about how long a ballot takes, and nothing was re-measuring it as the pinned models changed.**
  - **The second half is that recovery depended on an open browser.** `ballotPending` was read by `live-judge` and by nothing else, and `live-judge` is only ever invoked by a page. A round whose debaters both closed their tabs was orphaned with no ballot, and nothing anywhere would ever try again. The retry loop was designed for a transient failure; this one is systematic, so it retried forever and landed never.
  - **`scheduled-ballot-sweep` runs every minute in a background invocation**, which has a fifteen minute ceiling rather than twenty six seconds, and hands stuck rounds back to the SAME handler with a 90 second juror budget. **Deliberately the same handler and not a second judging implementation**: the season pin, the lease, the immutable audit row, the rating write and `verdictSource:'server'` all have to stay identical, and a parallel copy is exactly how those drift apart on the one surface where a quiet change is a legal problem. The sweep only decides WHICH rooms get another go.
  - **It authenticates with `INTERNAL_JUDGE_KEY` and is a WATCHER, never a participant.** It waits out the same recovery grace, takes the same lease, and cannot claim a room whose own debaters are mid-request. **An unset secret means the door does not exist rather than standing open**, which is the single most dangerous thing in this change: read the other way it would hand panel access to any caller with no App Check and no sign-in.
  - **The lease is now stamped per claim.** A 90 second run cannot be protected by a 45 second lease: a second sweeper would claim a room mid-panel and pay for the same three jurors twice. A forged lease is capped and a document written before the field existed falls back to the short one.
  - **Bounded, because a backlog must not become one enormous provider bill:** three rooms per run, sequential (three in parallel is nine concurrent provider calls, which is how a sweep becomes a rate-limit event), oldest first, and a round stops being retried past fifteen attempts or twenty four hours.
  - `scripts/test-ballot-sweep.mjs` runs in the pre-commit hook. 28 assertions, and per the standing habit the suite passing on its first run proved nothing: ten mutations were applied one at a time and all ten were caught, including the unset-secret one.
  - **Still open and deliberately not fixed here:** the request path keeps its tight budget and will keep failing for these jurors, so the first ballot now arrives via the sweep, roughly two minutes after a round ends rather than seconds. That is the honest trade against a spinner that never resolves, but the real fix is judging that starts the moment the round does instead of after it.

- **The round type is chosen in the room: two doors at the start beat** (2026-09-03, Aidan off a live screenshot: "start conversation or start formal speeches", and separately that the opening line was painted through by the camera bar). **This supersedes the half of the Just-argue entry below that said `/spar` picks the mode.** It still queues `open`, but the choice is now made where the two of them are looking, by either of them, in one press.
  - **Two equal doors replace the single Start** at Speech 1 while the clock is at ready: Start conversation, Start formal speeches. Either one publishes the format so both screens agree, then starts it. The mode the room is already in reads as current, and neither is demoted to a link, because neither is the advanced one. Hidden for spectators, hidden the moment a speech has run, and hidden when a tournament fixes the round type.
  - **Formal speeches has a first speaker and it may not be you**, so the second door relabels to "Switch to formal speeches" and names whoever starts, rather than offering a Start that cannot start. Nothing auto-starts in either mode; the 2026-08-27 rule is untouched.
  - **The opening line moved below the camera bar.** `.cam-modes` is absolutely positioned at `top:10px` and centred, and the card sat at `top:12px`, so the two overlapped and the bar painted straight through the headline. Measured gap after the move is 4px with the bar present.
  - **Two defects found by driving it rather than reading the diff, and both would have shipped.** `publishRoundMeta` lives inside the motion-editor closure and is not in scope at the start beat, so the first version threw a ReferenceError on the switch: the format changed locally, the round never published to the peer, and the clock never started. It writes the one field it needs from the top level instead, which also avoids stamping over an open motion-change proposal. And the secondary door used `var(--ui-surface)`, which resolves near-black on the light theme while this panel is paper, so it measured **1.14** against its own text: the same trap as `#audFab` and `#kickoffGo`. Repainted on `--text` and `--border`, and measured after at 16.58 light, 19.78 dark, 21 crimson, 19.78 stone.
  - **Standing note: this page defines `--bg`, `--text` and `--accent`, and `--ui-surface` is not safe here.** That is the third control on this page to be caught by it.

- **Just argue: one open conversation, and it is the default for a stranger match** (2026-09-03, Aidan: "yep yalla lets go do it", on the two-modes-of-one-room recommendation). A live round no longer has to be four timed speeches. The new format key is `open`, it is what `/spar` queues by default, and timed speeches (`quick`) stay one pick away inside the room under Round details, Options, Style. **Two modes of one product, never two products**: the same room, the same judge, the same ballot, the same leaderboard entry.
  - **Nothing detects the mode from the audio.** The 2026-08-27 rule stands, that a live mic proves words are being said and not what they are, so the mode is a choice made before the round and every mechanic branches on `openMode()` rather than on a signal. A countdown still starts nothing.
  - **The format carries ONE container speech.** Every consumer walks `f.speeches` (the log, the ballot builder, the progress line, the sequence strip, the leaderboard floor), so a format with an empty speech list would have broken all of them. The container keeps them working and `open:true` is what the mechanics read.
  - **Both seats hold the controls.** `isMyTurn()` answers true for both, so either person starts, pauses and ends. The timer sync is therefore symmetric: whoever presses Start publishes the clock, and the other seat adopts it and turns its own mic on rather than mirroring a clock it cannot drive.
  - **Each seat transcribes its OWN mic**, the same path a speaker uses in a timed round. The new part is the interleave. Every 2.5 seconds a seat commits the sentences that landed since the last tick as a timestamped segment and publishes its list under its own key; a tail that has not grown for two ticks commits as it stands, so a trailing clause is never stranded until the end. The seat that ends the round merges both lists by time, correcting the peer's stamps by the skew measured on their writes, which is the same rebase `applyRemoteTimer` already does for the clock and exists for the same reason: real client clocks have been measured 22 seconds apart.
  - **`openLive` is keyed per side, and that is load-bearing.** Two people publish captions at once here. A single `currentTranscript` would be the two of them overwriting each other a few words at a time, which is the shape of the bug rather than a race worth tuning.
  - **The clock counts up.** No target, no progress bar, no overtime note, no `[TIME EXPIRED]` cutoff. There is no allotment to run past, so a deduction for length would be scoring a rule nobody agreed to.
  - **The aides that elect an idle bench are skipped**, because this mode has no idle bench: the fact-check driver, the room-audio backstop and POI capture all pick the non-speaking participant, and here there is not one.
  - **Storage consent still gets only this seat's own lines.** The log entry holds the merged exchange, so `capPushSpeech` is handed a rewritten entry carrying this uid's segments alone. A person's consent covers what they said, never what the other person said.
  - **The leaderboard floor asks a different question.** "Most of the format missing" has no meaning in one open exchange, so the test is whether both people are actually in the transcript. Everything else in the 2026-08-24 opt-out posture is unchanged.
  - **The judge gets the exchange as one tagged conversation** plus six rules: no speech structure to reward or punish, drops still count as drops, interruptions are the format rather than misconduct, no time rules at all, and decide it on who answered what was actually said. `live-judge.mjs` scores it 1-100 like `quick` rather than falling to the 30 scale.
  - **`/spar` sends `?format=`, and until now `/live-round` ignored it** and hardcoded `quick`. It honours the two casual keys now and only those; the competitive formats stay dormant per the casual-1v1-only rule.
  - Verified in a browser against the real engine rather than by reading the diff: sentence-boundary commits, the stable-tail flush, a mid-round rewrite resetting cleanly instead of double-counting, and a two-sided merge landing in time order with both speakers tagged. **Not yet verified: two real seats in one room.** The interleave is proven against a driven peer segment list, not against a second human.
- **The avatar gets a head rig and a body that flexes, and the site RAM question is answered with a measurement** (2026-09-03, Aidan: "fix the avatars they dont move or follow eyes, body movement, etc", then off a live screenshot of a splayed face, "dont allow this to happen - allow the entire body to be flexible like this"). The screenshot was the diagnosis: tracking was WORKING (the MediaPipe FaceLandmarker in `cam-avatar.js` has driven the mask since 2026-08-15) and the renderer was what was broken. A turn slid every feature sideways across a rigid skull, so at thirty degrees the far eye left the silhouette and the brows floated over the temple, while the head rolled alone on a torso that moved 22% of a lean. **Every feature now lives on an ellipsoid at a longitude and latitude and is projected through the tracked yaw and pitch** (`makeRig`): the far ear narrows, fades and goes behind the head, the near one swings forward and widens, the nose stands proud of the surface for parallax, glasses grow temple arms that appear on the near side. **The body is a chain**: the torso leans and rolls with the lagged pose, the shoulders turn (near side forward and lower), the chest breathes, and the head carries only the remainder of the roll about its own centre, drawn inside the body frame so a lean moves the whole figure. The anonymous mask rides the same rig as one foreshortened plate. Two smaller things were wrong underneath and are worth the record: **the cartoon face had vertical gaze inverted** (the tracker reports positive as up, the face drew it down), and gaze now reads off the iris LANDMARKS rather than the eyeLook blendshapes, because an iris offset between the eye corners is image-space geometry that mirrors exactly the way the pose mirrors, while the blendshape names carry a left/right convention that is easy to get backwards. Tracked and idle poses cross-fade over ~400ms instead of cutting, a still tracked face keeps a slow drift, the frame gate no longer starves on a stuck `currentTime`, a failed tracker download retries, and `DebateCam.warm()` pre-fetches the ~13MB tracker on first hover of the Avatar button (live-round, casual-room) and automatically for anyone with a saved look. **That last one is the "does not follow me" half**: on a cold cache the tracker takes 10 to 20 seconds to arrive, and for that whole window the mask ran on mic energy, which reads as broken.
  - **Verified in headless Chrome with a fake camera driven by a consented still panned and rolled by ffmpeg**, not by reading the diff: eyes stay inside the skull through a full turn, mask, 2D, 3D and the designer overlay render with zero console errors. The harness lives in the session scratchpad and `mask-lab.html` exposes `window.__cam` for it.
  - **RAM, measured rather than guessed, and the first reading was wrong in an instructive way.** A renderer probe that read every Chrome renderer on the machine reported the avatar page climbing from 331MB to 1064MB in twenty seconds; scoped to the test browser's own child processes it is flat at 180 to 196MB over 30 seconds of full tracking, so the gigabyte was one of Aidan's own tabs. **A process probe that is not scoped to the process under test measures the machine.** The site survey (12 public pages, same scoped probe) puts every page at 131 to 182MB of renderer RSS with JS heaps of 3 to 14MB, against a Chrome floor around 120MB: the cost is decoded media and the decorative full-viewport canvas, not code. The landing was the heaviest and one third of its 3.3MB transfer was a 1.1MB PNG decoded at 1672x941 for a 395px card; the three quick-door cards now ship 800px variants (49KB, 89KB, 101KB), the PNG is deleted, and the originals the story pages use are untouched. The neural canvas stays at its 1.5 DPR cap, which was Aidan's own settled call on 2026-08-25. **Left alone, on the record:** /community at 6,842 DOM nodes and /leaderboard's 57 running animations are each ~20MB over the lightest page and already under the animation governor; neither is worth a pass without a screenshot of it being slow.

- **The free round runs only into a queue with someone in it; the desk opens on a face; Apple is back on web** (2026-09-04, later the same day, Aidan: "when there is NOBODY let someone go through questionare - first question has to HIT HARD - then press skip to opponent button - both then trigger google sign in ... we really need to get this right to optomisze securing users while not scaring them away forever bc they open a room to nobody and then dont come back"). This narrows the one-guest-round entry directly below, hours after it shipped, and the narrowing is the point: a stranger dropped into an empty room leaves and does not return, while an account can be pinged. So `/spar` now asks `/api/spar-queue` before it mints a guest. Someone waiting: the free round runs. Nobody: the desk still runs (answers saved to the device), and the door after it is the sign-in card reading "Nobody is free this minute. Sign in and we ping you the moment someone is." The padded live count stays above it; the card no longer claims a room is ready. A failed count reads as empty, because the safe direction is the door. **The first question is a person with a picture**: "Who do you actually want to argue about?" over six portraits (Charlie Kirk, Ben Shapiro, Hasan Piker, Candace Owens, Jordan Peterson, Mehdi Hasan) plus None of them. Every portrait is a Wikimedia Commons file whose licence is recorded in `img/creator-watchlist/README.md`, the same bar as the watchlist band; the Kirk portrait was added under it (Gage Skidmore, CC BY-SA 2.0). The answer is stored locally as `figure` (profile v4 stays readable, missing reads as skipped) and never enters the queue doc or the private server profile. ArcadeFlow options gained an optional `img`. The desk's skip and launch buttons both read Skip to opponent. **Doors: Apple returns to the web chooser and to the spar gate** (reversing 2026-08-26 at Aidan's call, with the Hide My Email caveat unchanged: until he registers the sender domain under Sign in with Apple for Email Communication in the developer portal, a relay-address account cannot be emailed), and **Discord is built behind `DISCORD_SIGNIN_READY = false`** in auth-modal.js. Discord publishes OpenID discovery at discord.com/.well-known/openid-configuration, so it rides Identity Platform as the generic OIDC provider `oidc.discord`; the project has no OIDC configs yet and the provider needs a Discord application's client id and secret, which is his account to create. Flip the flag once the config exists.

- **The competitive-or-new question moves to five visible minutes and a second page, and leads with the casual answer** (2026-09-04, Aidan, off a screenshot of the corner card a minute into a visit: "only ask this later bc it crowds the site and makes ppl think the site is for comp debaters. its not. rlly leave it to later - so default to a structural casual language in explaining debate and whats rewarded."). The second half was already true in code and is now the stated rule: `audience-mode.js` treats anything but an explicit "competitive" answer as plain, so every unanswered visitor reads the casual register, and `/practice` is plain for everyone. The card therefore exists only so people who do compete can opt into the vocabulary, which is a question for someone who has used the site. It now waits for five cumulative visible minutes AND a second page in the same session (one page is a visit, two is use), and reads "Ever done organized debate? Most people here have not." with "No, just here to argue" first. `?experienceask=N` still shortens the wait for QA and drops the page requirement. The landing's own inline chooser sits in the Full-tour row below the gate and was left alone; onboarding's version belongs to account setup and was left alone.

- **A page never paints itself empty before the data has answered** (2026-09-04, Aidan, watching pages load: "it shows an image of it empty then loads. just load the leaderboard even if slower. smart about it plz"). On /leaderboard the deferred picture-set script repainted the board with an empty list whenever it landed before the data, which on a cold load is most of the time, so the "nobody on the board yet" pitch showed until the rows arrived; and a failed ladder fetch returned an empty ladder, so a network blip painted the same pitch as a settled answer. The rule, applied there and standing for every data-driven surface: **the empty state is a claim, and it may only render when a request has resolved with zero rows.** While a request is pending the surface shows its own shape (a skeleton in the real row grid, so nothing shifts when rows land), a repeat visitor sees the last good payload from localStorage at first paint and the fresh load replaces it silently when it differs, and a failed load retries (1.2s, 3s) behind whatever is on screen before a failure state with a Retry button renders. Skeleton rows opt out of the row entrance animation, which the animation governor otherwise leaves at opacity 0. Audited the same day and left alone because they already hold the rule: /friends (empty hidden until ready, error flashes and retries), /profile (spinner, then renderError on failure, renderEmpty only on a resolved empty), /watch (server-rendered stills stand in), /live (empties hidden by default), /community (snapshot error retries). The remaining known gap is the leaderboard's own "N watching" on a live room card, which is a random number and should go.

- **One anonymous spar round comes back, the Match Desk runs first, and The Commons can be messaged from** (2026-09-04, Aidan, after watching session replays: "let anonymous first time joiners spawn into an omegle thing one time and for second time its a google sign in. its detering ppl. and do matchmaker questionare make sure to save the results and then go to the google thing", plus "make it possible to dm ppl from chat and then they get email notifications"). **This reverses the 2026-08-27 Google-only door at exactly one round**, and the reason is on the record this time: replay showed strangers leaving at the Google card before they had seen anything. The flow for a new visitor is now Match Desk (saved to the device, `da-spar-match-profile-v4`), then one guest round, then the Google gate, whose copy says that was the free round and the answers come with them. A returning guest with a saved profile skips straight to the gate. The lane is the 2026-08-19 design unchanged underneath: `spar-pair.mjs` meters against `guest_rounds/{uid}` from the verified token, `GUEST_FREE_ROUNDS` in the Netlify env moves it with no deploy and 0 closes it, `firestore.rules` lets an anonymous owner write only its own `waiting` doc with `authProvider:'anonymous'`, and the pairing transaction accepts an anonymous seat while still refusing one whose allowance is spent. The background "Spar live" pill stays Google-only. **The DM half needed less than it sounded like.** Email nudges on a new private message have been live since 2026-09-01 (`notify-dm.mjs`, one per recipient per six hours; 34 recipients nudged as of today, the last one this afternoon), and /chat and /spar have shown a Message button on named posters to named readers since 2026-08-23. What was missing: /community never passed the token or a DM handler, so names there were plain text; a signed-out reader saw no button at all; and a guest poster was never told they could not be reached. Now `chat-feed` returns `named:true` (a boolean, never the uid) to every caller, a signed-out reader's press on Message opens the sign-in chooser with the reason on it, /community routes a DM to `/spar?dm=`, and a guest in the room sees one line under it: you are posting as guest_1115, sign in so people can message you and you get an email when they do. **The honest limit, stated rather than hidden:** the two people in the screenshot were both guests, and a guest can never be DMed or emailed, because there is no account and no address. The fix for that is the sign-in nudge, not a DM to an anonymous uid that its owner could never read.

- **The pre-round panel is the record bar, the resolution, and the two doors** (2026-09-04, Aidan, off six screenshots of a live room: "get rid of these tbh. put record button at the top actually", "we just want a ui thats super simple here", and "Kill both" on the ready strip and the prep card). Retired from `/live-round` before Speech 1, on every width: the "I am ready / Say you are here" strip, the green prep-time card and the prep offer after the formal door, the one-judge card ("The AI judge / Set / How it judges"), the Round notes and fact checks switches, the Switch sides control and the PRO/CON legend, the small duplicate clock with "Neutral context" and "Generate context", the "Starts the clock and both mics" line under the start button, and the points bet panel for debaters. What is left is one full-width red **Record this round** bar as the first thing in the panel, the resolution with Change it / Roll one, one sentence saying which way you argue, and the two doors. **What did not change underneath:** presence is still proven by the seat heartbeat every listing reads, so the ready strip was a second button in front of the only one that matters; round notes and the live fact check still run by default and paint into the same block once they have something to say; `readyChecks`, `showPrepNudge`, `startPrepBanner`, the side-swap consent path and the judge state all stay in the file behind flags (`READY_BEAT_ON`, `PREP_OFFER_ON`) so a restore is one line each; the judge picks are still set so the speech-1 judge lock passes; spectators keep the bet panel. **Mobile riders:** the two roster cards stack on phones so names stop truncating to "Jo..." and Message / Report / AI? stop falling off the card. **Same day, /spar's Match Desk lost its jargon** ("Viewpoint clash", "Closest rating", "Nearest public Glicko rating", "Where do you lean on political speech online?") for plain questions ("Who do you want to argue with?", "Someone at my level", "Should social media take down more political posts?"); the stored values and the matching rules are unchanged, only the words.

- **GPU: blur only where it can be seen, layers only while they move, and the animation governor goes sitewide** (2026-09-02, Aidan: "gpu heavy on app fix optomize a lot!"). Measured on /app at 1440x900 rather than reasoned about: 38 elements running a live backdrop blur and 46 elements holding a permanent compositor layer. A backdrop blur is only visible when the element is translucent AND the backdrop has detail; blurring a flat colour returns the same flat colour at full price. `.cases-sidebar` and `.depth-card` are opaque, 24 form controls sit on a flat panel fill, and `.output`, the streaming ballot surface, re-rasterized a 12px blur on every appended token. All off, at every width. Blurs over real page content (nav, dock, overlays) stay. `will-change` was permanent on `.tab-pane` and on every `.cta` / `.nav-btn` / `.topbar-pill`, so 46 always-live GPU allocations paid for effects that last 400ms; it is scoped to the transition and to `:hover` now. **A composited layer loses subpixel antialiasing, so this also made body text in the app visibly sharper**; the softness was the layers, not the font. `anim-governor.js` (2026-08-18) was hand-included on five pages, so the other ~80 ran their keyframes forever in background tabs (/judge measured 19 still running while hidden); `topbar.js` injects it sitewide, idempotently. **One documented call is reversed with the measurement behind it:** the 2026-05 mobile pass downgraded `.panel` blur to `saturate(1.1)` instead of removing it. At 375x812 the panels compute to a transparent background, so a 10% saturation of a flat page is invisible (before/after screenshots pixel-identical) while still costing a layer per panel on the phones that rule was written for. Removed. Same push fixed `/pricing`'s hero glow, which hardcoded `rgba(255,255,255,.86)` under near-white dark-theme text and erased the headline; it is a per-theme token now, dark being a scrim that raises contrast rather than a glow that washes it out. Frame timings were deliberately not quoted: the browser harness ran hidden at 0x0, which throttles timers, and a number from there would be fiction. The layer and blur counts are structural and are the evidence.

- **The Watch conversion door shares the compact masthead; live rows show the room** (2026-09-03, Aidan: "also have a button for 'get in a debate yourself - join a live room!' button thats big and red at the top to get conversions in users - have live debates show up in a list - and capture thumbnail screenshots of the room to make the room feel like theres something on the other side"). The top remains one shallow row: the title on the left and one large red **Get in a debate yourself. Join a live room!** button on the right. This supersedes only the no-CTA clause immediately below, not the compact-masthead or two-source call. When `/api/watch-live` returns public rooms, they render as a real list with motion, people, state, and a 16:9 preview. **No new capture or privacy rule was invented:** `/live-round` already posts the exact Camera or Avatar canvas the opponent receives to `/api/room-shot`; Off mode posts nothing, private rooms are refused, only a seated signed-in UID can post, and the still expires in 75 seconds. Watch uses that existing fresh frame and falls back to a neutral Pro/Con room tile when none exists.

- **Watch opens on the choice, not a billboard** (2026-09-03, Aidan on the rebuilt page: "this is too big on the top - split left right between the youtube debates and our debates. just brand it as watch the debates"). This supersedes the large social-surface hero added later the same day. The masthead is one line, **Watch the debates.**, with no eyebrow, pitch, or CTA stack. The live-status strip follows, then two equal source panels: clearly attributed YouTube debates on the left and Debatable debates on the right. Both are horizontal shelves, so a growing replay feed does not turn either side into the whole page. The replay player sits above the split when opened rather than appearing inside the outside-video column. Under 900px the panels stack with Debatable first; each shelf remains horizontally contained.

- **Watch is a viewing surface again, not an essay after a video shelf** (2026-09-03, Aidan from the full-page screenshot: "this page needs reinvention lowk - overwhelming detail!"). The merged page had accumulated a hero, live status, six outside videos, nine replay placeholders, a four-photo culture argument, a stance study, four source cards, three ways to watch, five live-room steps, three round-mechanic cards, and nine open FAQ answers. At 1280px it measured 5,301px tall before a drawer was touched, and the five explainer sections in the screenshot repeated facts already demonstrated above them. **The default path is now the decision a Watch visitor came to make:** See what is live, browse four honest replay placeholders, or pick one of three outside editor choices. The page then gives the whole mechanism in three open beats, Choose, Watch, Call it. Account rules, recording, eligibility, duration, and three official outside archives stay as crawlable plain HTML behind one closed New here? drawer; its FAQPage data matches the six answers word for word. The decorative culture band and its second audience thesis are gone. The two APDA editor cards and the public live-room format badge also leave under the casual-one-on-one rule; legacy format fields stay stored but render simply as Round. Phone shelves scroll sideways instead of becoming seven stacked cards. Measured after at 1280x900: 2,048px closed under the later social-surface frame, no horizontal overflow; 390px keeps the action, status, replay shelf, and bottom tabs inside the viewport.

- **The live-round panel says less: one transcript line, tabs at the bottom, the opener over the video** (2026-09-03, Aidan off four screenshots: "to simplify UI get rid of these showing just say 'capturing transcript...'", "get rid of this concept of who controls", "just say 'you speak first' and put it on the left side over the video", "just have round notes and fact checking tab at the bottom, and include the recording button", "make the chat tab much bigger and notable - make it red"). The "X controls this speech" bar with its YOU / THEM badge and YOUR TURN / LISTEN + PREP pills is retired; the speech header already names who is speaking. The transcript header reads "Capturing transcript…" and nothing else: Auto-mic, the language picker, spoken translation and the storage note moved into Round details with their ids intact, the word count is hidden, and the cold "Not debating in English?" strip is gone while the peer and browser-language nudge stays. "Round notes and fact checks" is one tab under the transcript and carries the Request recording button, with the summary saying when a recording is running or requested. Below it, "How the judge adjudicates" opens to the method in plain words (reads the transcript only, decides on the flow, then weighs, six axes to 100, ballot shows its work, panel split stays unresolved, human appeal in 72 hours) with the floor judge switch as its last row. **This supersedes the 2026-09-02 opening card**: the beat is now one line over the video, "You speak first." for the opener and "Jordan speaks first." plus the nudge button for the waiter, hidden in mini, pop-out and spectator modes. The Chat tab is solid red at roughly twice its size. The rebase reproduced the sw.js dedupe trap again (origin landed on v3284 mid-flight); caught by the standing check, re-bumped by hand to v3285.

- **The round starts on the motion it arrived with. The negotiation is a door, not a gate** (2026-09-02, Aidan, off a screenshot of the "Your call. Step 1 of 2" modal: "get rid of this shit entirely ... make the debate start immediately - make it easier for users to see resolution, offer to change it or propose a strike if they dont know what to talk about"). **This supersedes the "Motions are negotiated now, not struck" entry below on the one point that matters: nothing runs BEFORE the round any more.** The offer / send-back / counter tree is unchanged as a mechanism and the server still owns every decision in it; what changed is that `/live-round` no longer asks the server to open one when the pair arrives. A spar-matched pair lands on the motion the queue gave them, both seats are on screen, and Start my speech is live. The modal, the 16s clock, and the two-step "your call" beat that stood between two strangers and their first word are gone from the default path. **Three doors sit under the resolution instead**, in a row a person can read rather than the 0.62rem chrome nobody found: **Change it** (the existing propose-and-consent flow, unchanged), **Roll one** (a random proposal through the same consent), and **Draft one together**, which opens the negotiation on demand for a pair that does not know what to argue. The draft therefore keeps every rule it had (server-stamped eligibility, one veto, the offerer decides the motion and the responder the side, the ready check standing down while it runs, Speech 1 refusing while it is open) and loses only the assumption that everyone wanted it. **The server refusal is spoken now.** A room that was never stamped eligible (a /live challenge, a direct link) used to be a silent no-op on open; the button says so and points at Change it, because a button that does nothing is the exact "is it broken?" beat the 2026-09-02 recordings entry measured. `dfOpen` also stopped latching `dfOpened` on failure, since a one-shot guard that was fine for an automatic open would have made a manual button dead after its first refusal. **Watch `live_round_draft_open_manual` against rounds started.** If nobody opens a draft by hand, the negotiation is dormant code and the next pass should say so; if a meaningful share do, the door is earning its row.

- **Nothing has ever automatically invited anybody back, because `user_profiles` is not where an account's email lives** (2026-09-02, found while asking why 76% of people who play a round never return). Every scheduled lifecycle sender reads `prof.email`. **Measured against the live project: of 215 people who sat in a real paired live round in 60 days, 193 have a profile doc and FIVE have an email on it. 87 have one in Firebase Auth (77 google.com, 10 password).** Nothing writes the address onto the profile at a Google or phone sign-in, so the win-back has been addressing 2% of its own cohort. `config/winback_state` has been recording the result in plain sight for weeks: **`candidates: 31, eligible: 0, sent: 0`**, and exactly two win-backs have ever been sent in the product's life. The manual campaigns never had this bug because they enumerate Firebase Auth instead (`admin-open-rally` reached 259 accounts that way), which is why the blasts work and the automatic ones do not. `scheduled-winback` and `scheduled-wau-digest` now fall back to `getAuthUserByUid(uid).email` when the profile carries none. **Deliberately unchanged: a uid with no profile doc at all is still skipped**, which is the 2026-08-11 rule that mailing an address whose preferences we cannot read is the wrong side to err on; this only fixes the case where the profile exists, says nothing about opting out, and simply has no address on it. `send-first-round-email` was audited and is fine, it reads the address off the ID token. **The run now records `noEmail` separately from `skipped`,** because "nobody is lapsed" and "nobody is reachable" are different failures that were indistinguishable from the outside and one of them had been true for months. Effect: 32 people are lapsed 7-28 days and reachable right now, against 0 before.

  **The retention numbers this came out of, and they are the real state of the product.** Over 60 days, 215 people sat in a paired room: **76% sat exactly once and 11% ever came back on another day** (mean 2.2 rounds, median 1; Policon's public leaderboard shows its top players at 95, 74 and 65 matches). Splitting by what happened in a person's FIRST room, testers excluded: **a room that died with no speech returns 10% (n=158), a room that ran a speech returns 18% (n=40).** So fixing the opening beat is worth roughly 1.8x on return and is necessary, and it is not sufficient: **82% of people who got a complete working round still never came back.** Retention is not downstream of the start button alone.

  **Also found and NOT fixed: `scheduled-winback` did not run on 2026-09-02** (cron `0 16 * * 3`, checked at 20:42 UTC, state doc still stamped 08-26), and `scheduled-spar-night` has not run since 2026-08-19 despite a weekly cron, across a schedule change on 08-24. `scheduled-wau-digest` DID run on its last scheduled day (Sunday 08-30), so the scheduler is not globally dead. Watch the state docs; a missed weekly run is invisible everywhere else.

- **Two silent refusals on the Start button, and the speaker's own transcript never scrolled** (2026-09-02, from five of Aidan's screen recordings analysed end to end: three different real strangers, on three different days, all failed to start a speech, and one round was abandoned without starting). The recordings are the evidence and they agree: a Dutch user ("it's not working because you've already started your speech"), Liam ("you have to start it bro, I don't know what to tell you", "we should reload"), Paolo ("now, it's not starting. what's happening?"), and Aidan's own promo video ("did you press start speech or just start typing? it doesn't show you").
  - **`startSpeechTimer` has four refusal paths and two of them returned bare.** The judge-not-settled branch already writes a hint and opens the draft, which is why that one gets diagnosed and fixed; the MOTION-DRAFT branch returned with no toast, no hint, and nothing on screen. That is not an edge case: `spar-pair` stamps draft eligibility on every casual pair it makes, so it is the normal path for every matched stranger, and pressing Start during it did nothing and said nothing. It now writes the same kind of hint the judge branch writes, toasts, and scrolls the draft board into view. The refusal itself is unchanged, per the standing rule that nothing may start a speech while a draft is pending.
  - **The speaker's own transcript box had no scroll handling anywhere in the file.** `specCaptions`, the SPECTATOR pane, has sticky auto-scroll with a considered comment about not yanking the reader; `speechText`, the textarea the mic writes into, had none, so the mic wrote past the fold and the text froze on an early line for the length of a speech. Caught on camera in the promo for over a minute while the word count climbed 95 to 270.
  - **The spectator pane's rule was tried here first and MEASURED FAILING, which is the part worth keeping.** Bottom-proximity ("follow only when already near the bottom") is right for a pane that is READ and wrong for one that GROWS under a speaker who starts at the top: after the first overflow the gap is thousands of pixels, "near the bottom" is false forever, and the box stays frozen. Verified in a browser against the real element: no-scroll and bottom-proximity both end 109,928px behind, the shipped rule ends at 0. Position alone cannot separate "they scrolled up" from "the mic outran the viewport", so the rule is instead: keep following while the box is still where WE last put it, yield the moment the speaker moves it, resume when they return to the bottom. No scroll listener, so no race with the async scroll event our own write fires. **Do not "simplify" this into the spectator rule; that is the version that was measured broken.**
  - **The first test of the scroll fix was VACUOUS and passing**, the same trap this log keeps recording: the textarea sits inside a `display:none` ancestor, so `clientHeight` and `scrollHeight` were both 0 and every comparison trivially passed. Forcing the ancestor visible produced real numbers and immediately failed the fix, which is how the wrong rule was caught before it shipped.
  - **Flagged, not fixed:** `updatePlayPauseBtnCore`'s `else` branch renders an enabled "Start my speech" for `timerState === 'ended'`, a state where `startSpeechTimer` returns bare at line 1. It is largely transient (`prepareTimer` resets to ready), so it was left rather than guessed at, but a button that looks pressable and does nothing is the same defect class as the two above.

- **Clash Hour is retimed to measured demand: midnight, 3 PM and 7 PM ET** (2026-09-02). The 2026-08-24 sessions (7 AM, 3 PM, 8 PM ET) were picked off a map, one per side of the world, and never checked against what happened. **Measured over 14 days of real `live_rounds` with test rooms excluded** (36 of 168 docs were `uxcheck` / `lentest` / `prodcheck` QA rooms from one 08-24 session, and they were skewing every hour-of-day read): by PAIRED rooms, where two people actually met, **15:00 ET produced 13, 07:00 ET produced 1, and 20:00 ET produced ZERO.** The real peaks were one hour off and half a world away: **19:00 ET with 15 paired rooms and 00:00 ET with 13.** So Europe keeps its hour, the US session moves an hour earlier onto the peak it had been sitting next to, and the Asia session moves to midnight ET, which is the middle of the Asian working day (Delhi 9:30 AM, Tokyo 1 PM, Sydney 2 PM) rather than its late evening. **The cron moved with them**, 09:00 to 02:00 UTC: with the first session now at 04:00 UTC, a 09:00 UTC email would land five hours after the session it announces, which is exactly the bug 13:00 UTC had before the 7 AM slot existed. Swept together because an hour that appears in one place and not another is the one that gets quoted back at you: `js/spar-night.js` (the session table drives every countdown), `scheduled-spar-night.mjs` (hours, cron, email rows), `admin-clash-hour.mjs` (subject, three calendar links, table), `/how-it-works` (four visible strings plus two FAQPage JSON-LD answers), `/landing`, `/tournaments`. **Re-measure before moving these again:** n is 116 paired rooms over 14 days, enough to rank the hours and not enough to split them finely.

  **The larger finding from the same measurement, and it is not fixed here.** Of 132 real rounds, 116 seated two people, **50 ran a single speech, and 39 reached a ballot.** The matcher is not the problem: 88% of rounds pair. **57% of rooms where two real people met produced nothing at all** — 35 died inside three minutes, 20 lasted past ten minutes with both people present and zero speeches, and **37 of the 66 had already finished the motion draft**, meaning they struck motions, learned their sides, and then stalled at the one button. That is 2.8 completed rounds a day. The opening-beat card shipped the same day against exactly this; the number to watch is `started / paired`, which was 43% when the card landed.

- **The Match Desk was invisible to every returning stranger, because an anonymous uid is not a signed-in visitor** (2026-09-02, found by opening the shipped page rather than by reading the diff). The Match Desk was reopened for signed-out visitors that morning, and the boot path routed a sessionless arrival to it correctly. It never fired. **Measured live on production:** `isAnonymous: true`, no providers, no stored profile, and the sign-in gate on screen. A previous visit had left an anonymous Firebase user in the browser, so `onAuthStateChanged` took the GUEST branch (`state.user && !state.named`), and that branch called `renderGate()` directly. With `GUEST_FREE_ROUNDS` at 0 the guest can never queue, so every returning anonymous visitor met the account wall and the desk they were supposed to see first was never rendered. **This is the third entry in this log for the same trap and the sharpest one: `!!currentUser` on this site means "has a browser", and an anonymous uid IS the anonymous profile the feature was built for, not a lesser case of a signed-in one.** The guest branch and the stale-mirror correction now both route through `renderSignedOutEntry()`, which falls through to the gate on its own once a profile exists. Verified after: SKIP writes the neutral profile with `mode:'viewpoint'`, so a visitor who skips every question still gets viewpoint-clash matching rather than first-available.

- **The school Program license drops to $200 a season, and the page says the price is the API bill** (2026-09-02, Aidan: "revise our pricing page to be cheaper for schools like 200, say api costs are what is the reason"). Supersedes the $550 figure from the 2026-08-11 pricing decision. No school had bought at $550 in three weeks of it being on the page, and the competitor set (Policon, free, solo-built, subscriptions "planned") makes $550 read as an enterprise number on a product a coach can trial for nothing. $200 is stated everywhere as the cost of a season of rounds for a roster, flat and not per seat, with no margin on schools; that framing is the pitch and it is also the constraint. **Known exposure, on the record:** "unlimited judged rounds" at $200 loses money if a real roster runs voice rounds heavily (voice is ~80% of per-user variable cost per the 2026-06-27 audit; a 30-person roster running 20 voice rounds each is roughly $300 of Realtime usage). Acceptable while the goal is the first paying school; revisit to a typed-unlimited plus voice fair-use shape once one exists. Swept together: pricing.html (JSON-LD, band, FAQ, beta-copy strings), landing + landing-classic + landing-full + practice JSON-LD and FAQ, terms section 6, schools.html, and `scripts/check-prices.mjs` now blocks `$550` so the old number cannot drift back. Program stays invoiced by email; it is not in `lib/plans.mjs` and has no Stripe price, so this was a copy change, not a billing one.

- **face66 joins the consented real-still bank** (2026-09-02). A webcam still cropped from a live round the founder recorded, added at his direction with consent and 18+ confirmed by him on record (age 20, per his relay). Same standing and same process as face63 and face65 two days earlier: one frame chosen for a clean, front-facing read, cropped so the camera controls and the other debater's self-view are outside the frame, since the opponent consented to nothing. It is the first real still in the women's pool, so `FACE_W_REAL` now exists and deals ahead of the generated set the way `FACE_M_REAL` does, and it rides the `.rot-cam` weighted pool at the same five copies. No hover clip exists for it; the board shows the still. The standing bar is unchanged: no real face reaches any surface without explicit consent and an 18+ confirmation from Aidan, and none of it is Claude's to infer from a recording.
- **Phones get the app, not the website** (2026-09-02, Aidan: "we need to fix up mobile, its overwhelming. i want an entirely different user interface for mobile, similar to the app. a very simple 'join debate' and then organize the UI better"). Two changes, both gated under 720px and both inert on desktop. **The landing on a phone is now one screen**: `#mhome` in landing.html mirrors app/native.html (headline, one red Join a debate door carrying the live count, Debate the AI and Watch cards, sign-in, a row of text links) and every desktop section from the example board to the FAQ is `display:none` at that width. Nothing is removed from the document, so search and deep links are unchanged; the phone simply never paints the tour. The hide list is explicit rather than "everything but", so a new desktop section shows up on the phone instead of vanishing. **Every topbar page gets a bottom tab bar** (Home / Watch / Debate / Board / Me, `.da-tabbar` in topbar.js, styles in ui.css) that is the same idea native-bridge.js already draws in the iOS shell; web only, since the app has its own, and never on a round surface. `body.has-tabbar` reserves the height and lifts the fixed-bottom floaters that already ship (feedback pill, live popup, signup pill, sign-in wall CTA, experience ask). Verified at 375 and 360, and at 1280 where neither node renders. **Editing this on a phone width:** /god at the 390 preset renders `#mhome`; the copy-edit session was asked to tag exports with the preview width so a phone style edit lands inside the media query.

- **Friends takes Home's tab, and Debate becomes Home** (2026-09-03, Aidan: "lets add friends feature somewhere here and replace it with the home button. have the debate button be the home button basically"). This narrows the five-tab decision immediately above. Both phone web and the native shell now read **Friends / Watch / Debate / Board / Me**. Friends opens `/friends`, owns the active state for direct messages and chat, and carries the unread-message badge that previously sat on Me. The raised microphone keeps the label Debate and its visual weight, but tapping it returns to the home surface (`/` on mobile web, `/native` in the app); the home surface and debate routes keep that middle control active. Me is now account territory only: profile, settings, and brain. `/friends` and `/messages` load the native bridge so the new app tab does not drop into website chrome after the first tap.

- **The first-screen board's faces move on hover** (2026-09-02, Aidan handed over 57 silent three-second loops generated from the existing face stills, one per `face*.jpg`). They live beside their posters at `/img/round/faces/<face>.mp4` (440x244, H.264, ~35KB each, 2MB total) and the board plays the two on screen only while the pointer is over the card or the card holds focus; leaving pauses and rewinds, `prefers-reduced-motion` never plays them, and a face with no loop (the seven fictional screenshots, the creator portraits, face63 and face65) keeps its still and costs no request. `preload="metadata"` on the two current faces only. The service worker got the same branch narration has: a `<video>` asks for byte ranges and the default branch would have cached a 206 as the whole file, so ranges pass straight through and only a full 200 is cached. **Flag, not a reversal:** the loops for the consented real stills (face46 to face54) are AI-animated likenesses of real people, which is a step past the still they agreed to. They shipped because Aidan produced the whole batch deliberately; dropping those nine is one edit to the `HOVER_CLIPS` list in landing.html if that consent turns out narrower than the animation. Verified in real Chrome with a genuine hover, not in the hidden preview pane, which reports the tab hidden and never loads media. **v2 the same day:** 40 of the 57 loops regenerated with varied listening, hesitation, reaction and interruption behaviour, five carrying a brief connection stall; 17 calmer v1 loops kept. Same paths, same dimensions, so it was a file swap plus a hand-bumped service worker (the hook skips media-heavy commits).
- **The before-Speech-1 recording ask is gone, and you can put your own motion on the strike slate** (2026-09-02, Aidan: "remove the ask to record the room thing its a blocker" and "when striking motions add a 'add you own motion' option"). Two changes on /live-round. **(1) Recording.** This narrows the 2026-08-26 "every seated person is asked about recording before Speech 1" entry. The dialog no longer opens on its own in an ordinary room: it appears only when the OTHER seat presses Request recording, or in a tournament room, where recording is a condition of entry and the mandatory hold is unchanged. Recording is still unanimous, off by default, and consent-versioned; only the unprompted ask went. The setup and round notes now say so instead of promising a choice before Speech 1. **(2) Add your own motion.** During the strike beat either debater gets one "+ Add your own motion" row under the dealt cards. What they add is a card like any other: it is published to both sides with its author labelled, either side can strike it, and it runs only if it survives the same two strikes. That is what keeps it fair without a second consent flow; the strike IS the veto. Three rules hold it: the window closes the moment ANY strike is in (a motion added after the opponent struck is one they never got to veto, which would hand the adder a guaranteed survivor of their own choosing); an add restarts the strike clock so the other side has time to read it; and the text passes the site motion boundary in `content-guard` before it is stored, because a free-text field is the one place a heavy subject can enter a round that every seeded bank already refuses. A stamped tournament pool refuses adds, published as `poolLocked` so the board hides the row. One per side, 12 to 200 characters, duplicates refused. `scripts/test-round-draft.mjs` covers it and three guards (window, pool lock, one per side) were each shown to fail when deleted. Verified in a browser against a stubbed draft: the row, the guard error, a half-typed motion surviving an opponent's snapshot, the "Yours" and "Added by" tags, striking the custom card, and the row hiding once the opponent has committed.

- **Founder state, verbatim: the Sam Lessin update Aidan drafted on the subway and almost sent** (drafted 2026-08-19, surfaced 2026-09-01, recorded at his request). Kept here as a record of how the founder was actually thinking mid-August, before the $2M media-company ask and deck v81. Do not quote it externally; the numbers in it are stale (477 named accounts as of 2026-09-01, the Open ran as a week-long ladder, the ask is $2M). Context: Sam's last reply was 2026-07-19 ("narrative crispness i do think is key ... what you are / what you aren't & some proof of what really works"); Aidan's 2026-07-30 raise-process email got no answer. One confidential sentence about a teammate's equity is deliberately left out of this doc.

  > hi
  >
  > alive and well. Been going back and fourth between NYC-SF. Lots of insightful convos that has me on pause regarding the vision and how I want to move forward (if you and Joe are still interested??)
  >
  > While I've been dabbling in other VC/angel talks, In all transparency, I'm kinda banking on you/Joe(I'll write him independently this week) playing a serious role.
  >
  > I am waiting on hearing back from this program that would change fundraising and how I go about the year. Ive progressed in the application but nothing secured yet.
  >
  > Im bootstrapping. I need (am finding, I'll be successful) an engineer who understands the world well. Muku is still involved out of god will and focus on being a teacher for debate. I would join him.
  >
  > Now I ponder (btw I'm stuck on a subway that broke down I'm typing on the phone)
  >
  > What does an update mean for an early stage idea?
  >
  > Like did you ghost me or is it incumbent on me to reach out again
  >
  > Here's where we are;
  >
  > got traction. 220+ google sign ups, a tournament being hosted on the 29th, and constantly iterating the judge evaluation system (we're think judge avatars/diff paradigms) gathering more recordings of rounds too. The upcoming tournament is to create content.
  >
  > The truth is I love debate. The debate community has mixed feelings in response to my cold outreach. I've gotten 'we hate your project' to 'how can we scale this in Africa'
  >
  > That difference is interesting.
  >
  > The vision.
  > - gamified media company
  > - let Polymarket/kalshi price our debates like they do sports
  > - pay in tournaments / XP potential
  > - creator sponsorships / we bring a high ELO debater to a streamer. I saw Slow has the creator fund now, not sure how much that may overlap with us but thought I'd mention it
  > - data data data. Frontend voice, reasoning, evals of human disputes + expert domains (hoping this makes sense and backend the polling of audience, how ppl respond to certain arguments, micro message testing
  > - masterfully orchestrate the social media campaign. Happy to extrapolate on the how here.
  >
  > I want it to be multi generational. Not young annoying gamer bros only.
  >
  > The raise rn: 750k
  >
  > Raised? Nothing. I've been gathering

  **Why it is worth keeping.** Three lines in it are the honest version of things the polished decks smooth over: the "we hate your project" to "scale this in Africa" spread is the real market read; "multi generational, not young annoying gamer bros only" is a product constraint that should bind the media-company pitch; and "what does an update mean for an early stage idea" is the question every investor update since has been trying to answer. The rest of it (six vision bullets, the ask, the pause) is what Sam's July note warned against, which is why it did not go out.

- **The main AI round no longer depends on the browser shipping Web Speech** (2026-09-01, Aidan, off a `/practice` screenshot saying "Speech recognition not supported in this browser": "this is a huge problem - fix this universally - fix this tool bc its the main AI to debate against"). The canonical AI-opponent surface had one mic engine, `SpeechRecognition`, while `/live-round` already had the universal answer: record short segments with MediaRecorder and send them through `/api/transcribe`. Firefox, Brave, many in-app browsers, and unreliable iOS Web Speech therefore got a typing box in the product whose moat is spoken argument. `/practice` now prefers browser speech recognition when it works and falls back to server transcription when the API is missing, refuses to start, errors, or produces no words for 18 seconds. The fallback reuses the stream already opened for the orb instead of asking a phone for a second simultaneous mic capture, records eight-second segments, skips measured silence, retries one failed segment, preserves speech order when responses return out of order, and **awaits the final segment before the AI rebuts or the judge reads the speech**. That last wait is load-bearing: MediaRecorder emits its closing blob after `stop()`, so the synchronous path dropped the user's closing sentence on every fallback browser. While backup mode is active the round says exactly what changed: audio goes to OpenAI for transcription and is not saved by Debatable. Typing remains the last fallback only when the browser cannot record or two transcription segments fail before any words land. `scripts/test-practice-transcription.mjs` executes the real hook with Web Speech absent and out-of-order mocked segment responses; the pre-commit hook blocks if the recorder path, borrowed stream, final wait, or ordered transcript regresses.

- **The resolution gets the room's widest, loudest type beat** (2026-09-01, Aidan, off the live-round screenshot: "this should widen", use "a new font that stands out always when showing resolution", and give it "its own pop up animation"). The active resolution band is the one child of `#roundView` exempt from the console's 1100px reading measure, because the proposition is the shared object of the whole round rather than supporting interface copy. Resolution text now has its own `--resolution-font`, Oswald with a local condensed fallback, and that face follows the proposition into the setup field, change proposal, collapsed-video console, and broadcast stage so it does not revert to ordinary UI type when the surface changes. The band is a raised full-width stage with a restrained accent flare and a larger fluid headline. Every newly applied resolution gets one 620ms rise-and-settle beat. That beat is transform-only Web Animations with no persistent fill and a visible final base style, not a CSS opacity entrance: `anim-governor.js` can pause off-screen CSS motion, and the draft board already proved that visibility tied to a paused first frame can hide the one sentence the round depends on. If the draft resolves while its modal covers the band, the animation waits and fires when the board leaves.

- **The casual motion pool becomes a live argument page, not a civics worksheet** (2026-09-01, Aidan, off the room draft: "ive been seeing the same motions - make them the ones like AI will lead to socialism. high pertinent and interesting ones"). The 34-motion Quick pool was large enough to avoid duplicate cards inside one slate and still small enough that the same slogans kept coming back across rounds. It also mixed sharp theses with narrow policy chores, so a five-card draw could feel like five forms of homework. The canonical `practice.html` pool is now 117 plain-English claims across AI and ownership, work and housing, democracy and media, geopolitics and trade, energy and climate, and culture and everyday life. **"AI will lead to socialism." is in the bank verbatim and sets the register:** consequential, current, provocative, and still honestly winnable from either chair. The generated room pool moves with it through `scripts/gen-draft-motions.mjs`; it is never edited by hand. `scripts/test-motion-draft.mjs` now refuses a casual pool under 100 motions and pins the plain declarative form, so the variety cannot quietly collapse back to six valid cards while the existing no-repeat test stays green. The sensitive-motion boundary and the five-motion, two-blind-strike draft are unchanged.

- **Debatable is the arena and the media company around the sport** (2026-09-01, Aidan on `/future`: the vision is a media company too; extrapolate it through the streamer and leaderboard mechanics). The vision page now names both halves instead of treating streaming as one downstream feature. The arena creates judged one-on-one rounds and a public ladder. The media company uses that ladder as a living cast list: movement creates rising names and rivalries, high-ranked people can earn a shot at a streamer, featured rounds become live programming, and replays plus key-moment clips give every matchup an afterlife. The loop is rounds to ranking to matchup to broadcast to clip to audience to more people entering the arena. Claims discipline from §2 still governs the streamer beat: the opportunity is real and shipped, but no creator round has happened, so copy says someone can earn the shot and never implies one already occurred. The public distribution row on `/future` follows the current outward posture, Debatable live plus TikTok clips plus Discord community; retired public Twitch and YouTube doors came off that page rather than being revived as part of the media-company framing.

- **One judge, a Google-only video room, daily Clash Hour, and the first "wants to debate" door for strangers** (2026-09-01, seven calls from Aidan in one afternoon, recorded together because they are one product posture: fewer choices before a round, a real account across the table, and more reasons to show up at the same hour).
  - **One judge.** The three-archetype judge draft on /live-round (Casual viewer / Standard / Experienced, each bench nominating, lock on agreement) is OFF behind `SINGLE_JUDGE` in live-round.html ("for now lets just have one judge - not 3 options that confuses users"). The lock key is always Standard, nominations are no-ops, the setup card and the in-round panel render one fixed judge, and the Speech 1 "settle the judge first" gate cannot trip. Nothing about the 2026-08-18 consent machinery is deleted; flip the flag to bring it back. The 2026-08-12 paradigm lenses on /judge are a different surface and untouched.
  - **Talking into a stopped clock now keeps the reminder up.** The 2026-08-27 reminder showed once per speech for nine seconds. It now stays while the mic hears speech, returns every time that happens, and carries a Start my speech button in the banner. It still never starts the speech. Same day, the resolution band runs the full width of the panel with the context tools beneath it, and a person whose stale connection is still in the Daily room no longer paints as a second black tile (records sharing a user id collapse to the local one, else the one with a camera, else the newest).
  - **Clash Hour, three times every day.** Open Spar Night became three daily sessions at 7 AM, 3 PM and 8 PM ET, and was renamed Clash Hour because two of the three are not at night. All four copies of the schedule math moved together (spar-night.js, the landing community band, spar-rsvp.mjs, scheduled-spar-night.mjs); calendar links recur daily. The reminder email stays weekly on purpose: a daily reminder to every account is a daily email. Same evening, the "come back, three slots a day" campaign went to 433 accounts (family included at Aidan's call; the five notables and six educators held out), 0 errors, stamp `clashHourSentAt`.
  - **The video room is Google-only, with no anonymous option, and the site says so at the moment someone wants to debate.** Reaffirms 2026-08-27 and closes the door that entry left open: /live-round minted a guest session on arrival, so a visitor with a room link could sit anonymously. `create-daily-room` now refuses a debater token unless the verified Firebase token carries the google.com provider (viewers stay open, tournament rooms keep their admission gate), /live-round gates the seat at mountDaily and opens the shared modal Google-only, and casual-room sends its token. The sitewide popup's WAITING source is armed again as "<name> wants to debate"; a visitor without a Google account taps it into the Google-only modal and lands on /spar signed in. Replay stays retired.
  - **Schedule and challenges are a pair, with Schedule first.** The Explore Debate column's combined "Schedule & challenges" door opens `/live`, not `/challenges`, and both pages order the shared switch Schedule | Challenges (2026-09-01 follow-up, Aidan: "make it go to schedule page first"). The organic challenge page stays live and indexed; it is the second view, not removed.
  - **Find people.** A search box on /friends backed by `/api/find-users` (named accounts only, rate limited) over non-private public profiles and real leaderboard names, never account display names and never the Anonymous alias. Each result links to the profile and offers Add friend and Message; an empty search browses recent people on the board.
  - **Measured, not fixed:** in the last 24 hours before these shipped, 8 spar matches seated two people and 5 got past speech 0. Both stalls after the reminder deployed had the same person as Pro, and speech 1 is Pro's to start; the opponent had no control that could start it. The next build on activation is the waiting side nudging the speaker, not another reminder on the speaker's own screen.

- **The /spar searching screen is stripped to the search** (2026-09-01, Aidan on a screenshot of the waiting state: "gert rid of all these it overwhelms the /spar page tbh"). Gone from the searching state, all in one cut: the "Use the wait" Voice-AI offer card, the quiet-hours line and Open Spar Night card, the async-round line, the "Open Realtime Voice AI" link, the Matchmaker-profile recap card, and the entire Round setup card (judge type, language, Meet your judges). What remains is the search itself: status, Cancel, the get-pinged card, the waitlist stats, the watch panel, and the mic/camera reassurance line. Nothing behind the cut changed: the matchmaker profile still applies (the full-screen build at entry is untouched), the judge lens defaults and rides the queue doc as before, and Spar Night keeps its other surfaces. Every JS reference to the removed ids was already null-guarded, so the wiring stands down without errors. Restore is git history at the removal commit; if any single block earns its way back, bring back ONE, not the stack, because the stack is what he photographed.

- **The organic doorway pages get the waiting-card pop-up, and the strategy reading behind it** (2026-09-01, Aidan, off a Search Console screenshot showing 640 clicks in 28 days: "so what does this mean for how we should design site/ focus SEO / etc - bring pop ups to the pages that are to redirect ppl into a live omegle debate etc"). The reading first, because it decides the build. `/debate-online` takes 324 of the 640 clicks and `debate omegle` is the top query at position 1 to 2 with a 55% CTR: the omegle head terms are WON and small, so there is no ranking headroom left there (the 2026-08-22 stranger-vertical entry said the same and it still holds). The lever now is CONVERTING the ~23 arrivals a day who already come, and the next ranking work is the NSDA topics cluster, which is seasonal and peaks in September, plus off-page authority for the category terms per the late-September re-measure the 2026-08-26 wall entry scheduled.
  - **The pop-up already existed and was disarmed.** `js/live-popup.js` has carried a WAITING source since it shipped ("Maya is looking for a round, and 1 other in the queue, Debate them" into /spar, with the real self-filtered queue count), and the 2026-08-31 live-only re-arm deliberately left it dormant because the REPLAY source was the thing that had put a recorded person's face on unrelated pages. That objection is about replays. So the waiting source re-arms on exactly four pages, `INTENT_PATHS` (`/debate-online`, `/debate-strangers`, `/omegle-alternative`, `/online-debate-platforms`), which are the pages whose visitors typed a query the card literally answers. Replay stays retired everywhere, and on an intent page the chain never even fetches `/api/recordings`.
  - **Real inventory only, per the 2026-08-31 real-numbers rule.** No live round and an empty queue means NO card: the page's own CTAs already point at /spar, and inventing a waiting stranger is the exact fabrication that rule closed. Measured with a stubbed API: a two-person queue fires the card at ~6s of visible dwell on /debate-online; an empty queue with a replay available fires nothing on /debate-strangers and never calls the recordings endpoint; a full queue on /pricing fires nothing, because the slow chain still never starts off the intent list.
  - **The dwell is shorter on intent pages (6s against the sitewide 15s) and it stays a VISIBLE dwell**, which is the pogo-stick guard: a searcher who bounces back to the results in three seconds never sees a card, and bouncing is the one visitor behaviour that costs the ranking that delivered them. Everything else the card's restraint list carries is unchanged on these pages: at most two cards a session six minutes apart, a dismissal snoozes four hours, hidden tabs never poll, someone mid-round in another tab is never poked, and a taller corner card keeps right of way.
  - **Two honest limits.** The /spar door behind the card is Google-only with no anonymous preview (2026-08-27), so the card hands an organic visitor to a sign-in gate rather than a queue; that gate shows the same real waiting count, so the promise survives the hop, but `spar_gate_view` from these referrers against Google sign-ins is the number that says whether the door is too heavy for this traffic. And the Browser harness reports `document.hidden`, so every measurement above was taken with the visibility getter overridden, the same artifact three earlier entries record. Watch `live_popup_shown` with `kind:wait` on the four pages against queue joins; if the card shows often and converts nothing, the answer is the gate, not a louder card.

- **A DM emails you once, not once per message** (2026-09-01, Aidan, off his own inbox: "i dont need an email every time i have a message on debatable"). `/api/notify-dm` emailed every recipient on every message, with dedupe only per message id, so a normal back-and-forth produced identical "You have a new message" nudges a minute apart. The email is deliberately generic (no sender, no text, just "open your inbox"), so one nudge covers a whole burst. Now `notify-dm` claims a per-recipient GLOBAL cooldown across all threads before sending, in `dm_email_state/{uid}` (admin-SDK only, unlisted in rules so the default deny covers it, the guest_rounds posture), inside a transaction so two messages landing at once cannot both email. The first message of a burst emails; everything after it inside the window is suppressed with reason `cooldown`. A failed send reverts the stamp so it never spends the window, and a stamp from the future can never mute someone forever. `DM_EMAIL_COOLDOWN_MS` (default 6h) is env-overridable with no deploy and `0` disables it. The client still calls the endpoint on every send; suppression is one transactional read, which is cheaper than the profile plus Auth reads the old path spent per message. `scripts/test-dm-email.mjs` grew to 11 checks; four mutations applied one at a time, and the fourth ESCAPED the first version of the ordering guard because `claimCooldown(db, recipientUid` matched the function definition rather than the call. Matching the `await` call fixed it. Same lesson as every guard entry in this log: green on the first run proves nothing.

- **The "live right now" floor goes to 3, and a signed-in visitor can finally see the line** (2026-09-02, Aidan: "bring back the thing for 'live right now' add 3 as the minimum"). The "bring back" half was the real finding and it was not a floor problem. The pill lives inside `.fs-signin--side`, which `body.signed-in` hides, so the line existed for a signed-out desktop visitor and for every phone (`#mhome` carries its own copy inside the Join button) and **for a signed-in desktop visitor it rendered nowhere at all**. Measured on production in all three states before touching anything, which is the only reason this was not "fixed" by rebuilding a line that already worked. A second instance, `.fs-live-now--signed`, now sits above the first-screen CTA row and renders ONLY under `body.signed-in`, so exactly one is ever on screen; it carries `data-live-now`, so the existing `querySelectorAll` in fsLiveNow paints it with the same number and there is still one place the arithmetic lives. `LIVE_FLOOR` is 3 and all four JS-off fallbacks in the markup ship "3". Everything the entry below says about the exception still binds: the measured inputs stay raw, nothing drifts or randomises, the displayed figure is a conversion label and never traffic data, and the floor does not travel to another surface without its own entry here. Verified at 1440 and 390, guest and signed-in: one pill per state, never two.

- **The landing's "live right now" line shows 2 plus the measured count, always** (2026-09-01, Aidan, off a screenshot of the first screen: "say the number of live right now (always have 2 minimum) and add the actual live amount on top"). fsLiveNow, the guest nudge above the first-screen Google button, becomes the SECOND founder-called display exception to the 2026-08-31 real-numbers rule, the same shape as the /spar gate's "4 + real waiting": displayed = 2 + max(presence online30, 2 x live public rounds), and the line no longer hides at zero (the markup ships "2" so a JS-off visitor sees the floor rather than an empty tag). The measured inputs stay raw and nothing drifts; the displayed figure is a conversion label, never traffic data. The floor is `LIVE_FLOOR` in fsLiveNow; do not widen it to any other surface without its own entry here.

- **The first-screen odds strip is hidden again** (2026-09-01, Aidan off a screenshot of the Owen/Grace percentage bar: "get rid of this thing on the landing page it makes the page ovefrwhelming / too much"). This re-retires the 2026-08-28 "spectator-heavy first screen built around betting tokens" reveal and returns the board to the 2026-08-22 posture: `.fs-board .fs-odds{display:none}`, one CSS rule, markup and JS untouched because the strip's tick drives the board's clock. The card now reads faces, motion, then the AI judge's decision. The 2026-08-31 header compression ("Spectators bet free tokens on who wins") is moot while hidden. Do not restore `display:block` without a fresh founder call recorded here; the strip has now flipped twice on founder sight of the live page.

- **face64 is pulled from the board, and removing a face turns out to be four surfaces** (2026-09-01, Aidan off the first-screen ROUNDS board: "get rid of his image on the landing page its not attractice"). The tile was Pascal, the middle still of the face63-65 batch supplied the day before: dark, underexposed, shot against a bare wall, and the weakest of the three at tile size. **Consent was never the question**, so this is a quality call and not a consent one, and a brighter crop of the same person would be welcome. The file is deleted and the number stays out of every pool, the same posture as face05/09/14/39/55.
