  - **The removal was bigger than the ask, and that is the part worth keeping.** He named the landing board; the same still also rode the `.rot-cam` rotator pool and the /spar signed-out gate, which is the site's one Google-conversion surface, so the image he called unattractive would have kept greeting every signed-out visitor. A face lives in FOUR places and only ONE of them spells it as a filename.
  - **Two of those pools hold it as a bare number**, `FACE_M_REAL` for the board and `REAL` for the rotator, so `grep face64` returns nothing and the deleted file simply 404s with no error anywhere. **face64 survived the first pass of its own removal inside the rotator list for exactly that reason**, and was caught only by grepping for the pool variables rather than for the name. `scripts/test-spar-google-gate.mjs` now parses both numeric pools and asserts 64 and 55 are absent from each, plus the gate's filename and its tile count; every guard was shown to fire by re-adding the number one pool at a time.
  - **The gate's fourth tile slot went with it.** The 08-31 batch occupied a four-position ring (left-top, right-top, left-bottom, right-bottom) and three tiles in a left/right/left run read lopsided, so slot 18 moved to the free bottom-centre position and slot 19's rule was deleted. Restore the paired rule only if a fourth tile ever joins that ring.
  - **The roster still deals cleanly:** 30 male characters against 32 male faces (10 real, 22 generated), so nobody goes uncast, and the real-first `mPool` concat means all 10 consented stills still deal before a generated one. Every number in every pool was verified to resolve to a file that exists.

- **Structural de-vibe of the named surfaces, and the AI features stop billing flagship rates for advisory work** (2026-08-31, Aidan: "we've been advised to do a structural simplification on UI design to not feel or look vibe coded... esp the UI for the live debating feature and the messages/community page/voice ai stuff - reduce token use on that too - so it charges me less"). Two halves, three pushes.
  - **The /voice-debate setup screen was the sharpest offender and its own CSS comment admitted it** ("a video-game loadout screen"): animated perspective grid floor, glowing laser line, scanlines on the active card, hover sheens, pressure/depth meter bars, an "Arena loadout" eyebrow, a "One round type" counter chip, and — because VOICE_MISSIONS holds a single entry — a mission card that repeated the h2 and subtitle VERBATIM. All chrome stripped to one flat quiet card in both themes; with one round type the chooser grid no longer renders at all; the meters and chip row collapse to one line (mode + AI tier); the copy reads "Voice round" and "Round options". Layout, ids and handlers untouched; net -56 lines, verified in a browser light/dark at 1280 and 375. **Do not re-add decorative animation to this screen**; the rewritten CSS header says the same.
  - **/community and /messages moved to one system per page** (one card recipe, one spacing scale, one filled-red primary per screen, accent color only where semantic: side tags, trust tiers, live green, warn amber). Community lost the doubled background glow, panel gradients, glow shadows, five per-kind feed pill colors, and two boxes-in-boxes; messages had every button styled against a `--accent-solid` token NO THEME DEFINED (falling back to a literal — the phantom-token tell), now defined per theme. **Merge note: this pass landed the same day as the upstream "Refresh community around real people" palette work, and the merge rule was: upstream's palette, cat-tag helper, and persona-grid removal stand; the one place this pass overrides it is role badges (judge/coach) going NEUTRAL — roles are labels, not states; only the honesty tiers (verified green, AI violet, mod red) keep color.**
  - **/live-round was deliberately NOT blind-restyled.** Its structure got three founder-directed simplification passes in the last five days (Start-my-speech, Round-details fold, recording consent), its AI-aide cards already share one recipe, and its in-round state cannot be rendered outside a live round — a sweeping cosmetic pass there would be unverifiable. Its radius census (eight radii in play) is on the record for a future targeted pass with a founder screenshot of the offending screen.
  - **Token cost, the "so it charges me less" half.** The two per-speech ADVISORY calls on /live-round — the floor judge's sideline color and the round notes — moved Sonnet to Haiku (they never score and the aides' rules forbid calling a winner; the BALLOT and the deep RFD stay on Sonnet, per the 2026-08-18 long-RFD decision which is Aidan's own). motion-context's classification defaults to Haiku behind `MOTION_CONTEXT_MODEL` (it fails open, so a wrong read costs a redundant question, never a round). **And the big dial: `OPENAI_REALTIME_MODEL=gpt-realtime-2.1-mini` is set in the production env** — the documented cost lever on the surface the 2026-06-27 audit measured at ~80% of per-user variable cost, roughly 3x cheaper on audio tokens ($10/$20 vs $32/$64 per M). **This is a quality trade made on the founder's explicit cost instruction; reverting is deleting one env var, no deploy.** Watch voice-round completion and ratings after the flip: if rounds get worse in a way people notice, the env var is the dial.

- **Replays clip themselves, and a live round reaches every arrival within seconds** (2026-08-31, Aidan: "setup an audio clipping system on all the debates shown on the site rn to filter the relevant dialogue and title so ppl actually watch this shit", plus mid-turn: "when a user joins if someone else is live allow them to anonymously join that debate and get the notification asap - user experience is key here"). Two coupled ships, one push.
  - **Every published recording now carries AI-cut key moments, and the pipeline is built around a measured problem: the dead air is enormous.** Read off the live corpus before building, the first spoken word lands 35s to 364 SECONDS into the video (a full six minutes of two people adjusting laptops on the Electoral College round). New pure `lib/highlights.mjs` aligns each stored speech to video time (`roundStartedAt + speech.atMs - recordingStartedAtMs`), renders a timecoded transcript, and validates what one Sonnet call picks: 1 to 3 windows of 12 to 90 seconds, each with a title and a VERBATIM quote. **The quote gate is the design, same discipline as the fact checker and the clash map:** a moment whose quote the host speech's transcript cannot prove is dropped, never repaired, because a titled clip pointing at a moment where nobody said the thing is the one failure a front-of-store surface cannot absorb. **The alignment fence is the other half:** rooms get REUSED, and one live round's reset clock mapped its speeches to second 2799 of a 1667-second video, so any speech that does not fit inside the recording is dropped and a round with nothing aligned yields NO clips rather than wrong ones. `scripts/test-highlights.mjs` (33 assertions) runs in the pre-commit hook; four mutations applied one at a time (quote gate, alignment fence, title lint, moment cap) were all caught, and the moment-cap guard was found vacuous on its first pass (the overlap dedupe was doing the trimming) and rewritten until the mutation failed it.
  - **Generation is bounded the way recording-thumb is:** `GET /api/recording-highlights?id` does its expensive work AT MOST ONCE per recording (done/none answers return without a model call, failures cap at 3 attempts, per-IP limiter behind that), `scheduled-highlights.mjs` sweeps one unanswered published recording per 15 minutes — deliberately its OWN cron, because riding on scheduled-recordings-sync would put a 20s model call inside a function already spending its ~26s execution budget on Daily reads and thumbnail warms, and a timeout there kills the sync that publishes recordings at all. The 16 live published recordings were backfilled the same hour: 7 got real moments ("Con concedes the monarchy costs too much, undermining her own case"; one round's best quote is in Dutch, and the cross-language title says so honestly), 9 correctly answered none (no speeches, stub-length recordings, the reused-room corruption).
  - **On /watch the moments are the merchandising:** key-moment chips in the player (each one seeks and clamps through the SAME activeClip machinery human clips use), one "Key moment" card beside each round in the feed, and **full replays now open at the first speech** when the setup runs past 25s, with the meta line saying so and the scrubber still reaching 0:00. A moment card's deep URL is the round's `/watch?r=` (it has no clips-collection id), which is why openClipById branches on `hl`.
  - **The sitewide live card returns, LIVE-ONLY, which PARTIALLY REVERSES the 2026-08-25 dormancy on that entry's own terms.** The retirement reason was the REPLAY source parading the same recorded participant on unrelated pages; the founder's ask is about live rounds, which name nobody not already live in public. So topbar.js loads live-popup.js again and the file ships `LIVE_ONLY=true`: the waiting and replay cards stay dormant, and only a round actually in progress fires — checked ~2.5 seconds after arrival instead of after the old 15s dwell, then a once-a-visible-minute watcher for the rest of the visit (watch-live is CDN-cached 12s, so that is one cached GET per visible minute), with a live card allowed 75s on screen against a nudge's 30s. **What the fast lane deliberately keeps:** hidden tabs never poll, a dismissal's 4-hour snooze holds, someone mid-round in any tab is never poked, and another corner card gets right of way (retried at 15s, not 60, so the experience chooser being answered does not cost a live round a minute of invisibility). **What it bypasses, on purpose:** the per-session card caps and gaps, which exist for unsolicited nudges, and a genuinely live round is the one thing this site can least afford to sit on. The card hands an anonymous visitor straight into `/live-round?room=…&spectate=1`; spectating has needed no account since the watcher-token work, so "join anonymously" was already true and the missing half was only the telling. Re-arming the replay/waiting chain is one flag, but reread the 2026-08-25 note first: the inventory-depth concern is what has to have changed.
  - **Verified before ship, not after:** the /watch flow end to end in a browser against a stubbed API and a generated 700-second mp4 (skip-the-setup seeked to 178s of a firstWordSec-182 round, chips seeked and clamped at the window end, the no-highlights round hid the row, the moment card opened its round in clip mode), and the fast lane on a topbar page (card at 2.2s, dismissal snoozed, nothing reappeared). One harness note for the next agent: the Browser pane reports `document.hidden`, so the lane correctly refuses to fire there until visibility is patched — the same artifact that faked a negative on the presence gate.

- **Overtime goes off the flow, an extension takes both debaters, and the judge finally hears the back-and-forth** (2026-08-31, Aidan, three asks off live rounds: "what happens when speeches go over time? judge stops considering. allow users to extend debate with a mutually agreed to button", and "it should capture interruptions like POIs and a dialogue and listen in to the back and forth and judge it fairly"). Three coupled changes to /live-round, one push.
  - **Overtime is now a cutoff, not only a deduction.** The 2026-08-29 time-discipline rule docked points for an overrun but everything said after time still argued on the flow, so running long literally bought arguing time. When the clock crosses the 15 second grace mid-speech, the speaker's own client records where the transcript stood; endSpeech splits the committed text at that word boundary and inserts a `[TIME EXPIRED]` marker. Every ballot builder (both client builders and live-judge.mjs) carries the same sentence: everything below the marker is OFF THE FLOW, it cannot win an argument, extend one, or count as an answer to one, and the RFD may mention it only to note it came after time. The point deduction stays; the marker is what makes "the judge stops considering" true rather than aspirational. The on-screen overtime note says exactly this while it is happening, so it is a disclosed rule, not a stealth one.
  - **+1:00 by mutual agreement, the consent shape this page already speaks.** An extension is a change to the contest both sides prepared against, so it uses the resolution-change pattern: either debater proposes from the timer row (the offer surfaces at under 45 seconds remaining), proposing implies your own agreement, and the minute lands only when the other side accepts. Accepted seconds move `targetSec` itself, so an extended speech that stays inside the new window carries no overtime tag and no deduction, and the cutoff recomputes against the extended target. Rides `timeExt.{speechIdx}` on the round doc with an accepts map; a solo room with no seat uids self-accepts because there is nobody to ask.
  - **Interjections: the judge reads the dialogue instead of two monologues.** The transcript pipeline only ever heard the CURRENT speaker, so a POI, a question shouted across the room, and the answer it drew were invisible to the ballot. While the other side speaks, the idle debater's client now level-meters its OWN mic, borrowed from the camera pipeline the way the backstop borrows tracks, and never via a fresh getUserMedia, because a permission popup landing mid-opponent-speech is worse than a missed POI. Only segments with sustained speech (the same RMS gate the server engine uses) are transcribed, so silence ships nothing and spends nothing. Captures publish as `interjections` arrayUnion rows tagged with the speech index and a round-relative offset (both sides of the subtraction share `roundStartedAt` as origin), and both client ballot builders plus live-judge.mjs weave them back under the speech they landed in as `[Interjection at +m:ss — Name (SIDE)]` lines.
  - **The fair-exchange rule is general now, not tournament-only.** live-judge.mjs carried an interaction rule only inside the tournament block; a version of it now rides the base prompt and both client builders: a concise relevant question that exposes a gap lifts the ASKER's responsiveness and strategy, a clean answer under pressure lifts the speaker, heckling or talking over an answer lowers the INTERRUPTER's scores, and a speaker is never penalized merely for being interrupted.
  - **Bounds, because an open mic is a cost and an attack surface:** 10 rows a speech, 300 chars a row, two-word minimum (a cough is not a POI), 1v1 seated debaters only (a 2v2 partner cannot write the round doc, per the backstop's seat rule, and four open mics would transcribe the room's cross-talk four times), and an echo guard drops a capture that already sits verbatim inside the speaker's live transcript, which is what the speaker's voice arriving through this side's speakers looks like when echo cancellation loses. Unit-driven both sides (server weave 5/5, client gates 6/6); watch `live_round_interjection` in GA4 against real rounds, and if the judge starts quoting phantom interjections the first suspect is speaker bleed-through, which means raising the echo guard, not removing the feature.

- **The random judge reveal is retired; the three visible judge types are the whole choice** (2026-08-31, Aidan, off the Warrant judge popup: "its always warrant judge get rid of this just have the 3 selections"). `/newvoice` and `/voice-debate` no longer load the random roster or open its full-screen persona card during connection. It duplicated the live product's actual judge contract, Casual viewer, Standard, or Experienced, and made a random internal lens look like a fourth choice the person never made. New AI-voice rounds use the standard ballot. Legacy saved judge fields remain readable for compatibility, but they do not create a new selection or reveal. `/spar` and `/live-round` keep exactly the three shared judge types, and their server-applied ballot lens is unchanged.

- **The landing conversion pass makes the account ask immediate, compresses the public signup figure, and sharpens the example board** (2026-08-31, Aidan, off three live screenshots). This is a direct founder call and supersedes three same-day details below.
  - **Live gate:** "have a sign in with google pop up" restores the once-per-session `/spar` auto-prompt, but as a Google-only mode of the shared auth card because live video rejects every other provider. The same-evening follow-up, "bring up this pop up after a few seconds like chill," puts five calm seconds between the gate appearing and the modal opening; an intentional click on the Google button remains immediate. The full gate remains behind the modal if the visitor closes it. "Have it say 4 live and add the number of actually live" makes this signed-out conversion label the one explicit exception to the same-day real-public-number rule: it displays `4 + queueWaiting`, while `/api/spar-queue`, matchmaking, and operational surfaces remain raw. GA4 keeps both `live_display` and `queue_waiting`, so the conversion number is never mistaken for the measured queue downstream.
  - **Signup caption:** the provider split comes off the globe. It now says only "N sign-ups so far." The endpoint may keep returning provider totals for internal analysis, but the public caption neither names nor sums them.
  - **Example board:** resolution headlines are italic and alternate red, then black, from one generated card to the next. Approved photos now render from their source pixels with the artificial grain and colour filter removed. The lead creator challenges prioritize the sharpest licensed portraits already in the repository; HasanAbi's 960px portrait replaces the 428px xQc portrait in that lead bucket and carries an authored conflict-disclosure motion. No new portrait or license class was introduced, and the sensitive-motion boundary is unchanged.

- **The landing point-of-view note is short, text-only, and unattributed, and the real-round thumbnail returns to its original image** (2026-08-31, Aidan, off four live screenshots: "get rid of image of me just say note from founder", "how can we improve and shorten this quote", "bring it back to what it was originally", then "get rid of it saying quote from the founder"). The landing copy is now two short beats: "A good argument is a beautiful thing. Debatable gives anyone a place to test an idea, take a side, and enjoy the game." The portrait, name, title, and attribution label are gone; only the quiet "Why this exists" link remains. This narrows the same-day identity return on the landing only. The founder's name and photo remain sanctioned on the public identity surfaces where they still appear, and the credential stack remains retired everywhere. Separately, `landing-shot-live.jpg` is restored to the image that preceded the second consented-stills swap; the new face batch remains in the round and gate pools, so reverting the thumbnail does not remove those consented people from the product.

- **/story recentres on sharing ideas and arguing them, on three pillars: dialogue, open, broadcast** (2026-08-31, Aidan: "make this less about debate nd the idea of sharing ideas and arguing about them with a bit of structure. need a dialouge, open, and broadcast"). The page opened on "Debate practice should not depend on who happened to show up" and ran six modules of prep, coaching, and format framing. Now: h1 "Ideas deserve a real argument.", lede stating the three pillars in one sentence, and the letter rebuilt as 01 the internet lost the back-and-forth (Her reference kept), 02 dialogue (one question, one person a side, a clock, a decision), 03 open (no background needed, plain language, strangers are the point), 04 broadcast (public rounds, spectator picks, replays, ladder, live streams), then the timeline, open problems, and his beliefs paragraph. The letter CTAs move to the pillars: /spar primary, /watch secondary. **Two rule violations fixed in passing:** the timeline named APDA and Policy in public copy (banned since the casual-1v1 call) and the coach module was training-first framing on a social app; both are gone. The founder card, name, photo, "built from inside debate" trust line, and "a good argument is a beautiful thing" all stay per the 2026-08-31 identity entries. Same push, per "delete these" on a screenshot: "The team" and "Watch real rounds" came off the landing founder sig row; both pages stay reachable from the footer.

- **Debating without a camera now costs event rating, and the open-week email says so over the real stills** (2026-08-31, Aidan on the email draft: "just say you can debate with the avatar (though you lose points in the system) - actually have that have impact btw the point loss for not using camera", then "use these images instead ... the real ones are better"). Two halves, and the system half exists so the email half is true.
  - **The penalty was disclosed and weightless.** The 2026-08-29 camera-presence rule ("dark only they lose points, avatar they lose less points, video they gain points") shipped scoped to the speaker-points TIEBREAK, and the same day's ladder decision made standings rank on EVENT RATING, with speaker points the second tiebreak behind wins. So the one number the ladder ranks on never heard about camera choice, and the rule was published on four surfaces while moving nothing. **This supersedes the "adjusts only the tournament speaker-points tiebreak" sentence of the 2026-08-29 disclosure entry**, on the founder's explicit instruction that the point loss have impact; the sentence that survives from that entry is the load-bearing one, that camera choice can never change who won.
  - **The rule: after the Elo update, camera on costs 0, Avatar subtracts 10 event rating points, Off or a dark tile subtracts 20, weighted by speech time like the points adjustment.** Sized against K=32: an avatar round costs about two thirds of a win's gain, a dark camera costs more than the win pays. Deduction-only by table construction, and DELIBERATELY NOT ZERO-SUM: the deduction burns rather than paying the opponent, so nobody profits from someone else's camera choice. The speaker-points table (+2/−1/−3) is unchanged, and the winner, the argument ballot, and the global Glicko ladder are untouched — camera choice must never corrupt the sitewide skill rating, only the event it was disclosed for.
  - **Plumbing:** `TOURNAMENT_CAMERA_RATING` in `lib/tournament-scoring.mjs` rides the scorecard as per-side `ratingAdjustment`, `live-judge` hands it to the ledger seat-mapped, the pairing stores `govCamRating`/`oppCamRating`, and `tournamentRatings` applies them on every rebuild, clamped to [−20, 0] at BOTH write and read so a forged positive value can never buy rating. Director-entered results carry no camera data and read as 0. Disclosed on every surface the 08-29 rule already rode: the recording-consent dialog, the pre-round setup note, the ballot's standings card (which now prints the per-side rating cost), the avatar button's own tooltip, /tournament-rules, and the day reminder email. Guarded in `test-tournament-round` and `test-tournament-pairing`; four mutations (drop the application, drop the clamp, weaken the table, stop passing it) applied one at a time and each caught by the intended assertion.
  - **The open-week email (admin-open-week.mjs) rebuilt around it.** The round screenshot and the six fictional-face strip are OUT (his call, twice: first "dont include these photos", then, shown the /spar gate wall, "the real ones are better esp the black guy... the ones with me"); the imagery is now the eight consented real stills (face46-49, 51-54) in one grid, which keeps the fictional seven where the 2026-08-27 decision put them, atmosphere on the gate and not evidence of rounds. The avatar paragraph makes the honest offer with the real price: play behind the mask if you want, it costs 10 rating a round, camera off costs 20, camera on costs nothing. Not sent; the /admin card's dry-run/testTo/SEND flow is unchanged.

- **The first screen goes back to "The chess.com of debating.", hours later** (2026-08-31, third pass, Aidan off a screenshot of the live hero: "no bring this shit back to the chess.com of debating"). Everything else in that entry stands — the founder's name and photo stay public, /story and /team stay first person, and the taste sentence ("a good argument is a beautiful thing") survives in his own words on /story. The identity is the person and what he built, not the resume; do not restore "APDA Pro-Ams champion" or "UChicago" from pre-08-22 copy, and do not re-anonymize from the 08-22 entries, which this supersedes.
  - **The first screen opens on the founder's own line.** "The chess.com of debating." is replaced by "A good argument is a beautiful thing." with one support line ("Not a shouting match..."), from his stated brief: "the beauty of a good argument is what draws me to debate. not arbitrary dispute." A page that opens by naming another product reads as a template; this one states a taste. Its text colors ride the theme tokens, measured in all five themes; a `:not([data-theme="light"])` guess painted light-on-light under the "dark" value and is warned against in the markup.

Clara and Karthik's photos stay in the repo, unlisted, since he named only those two. **/story is indexed, first person, and de-staled:** the "9 formats" tile (format-count ban + casual 1v1), the "4 people" tile, and "0 investors, intentionally" (he is fundraising) are gone; the timeline's "Next: multiplayer infrastructure" became "Now: the arena," and "debate-first" softened to "built around the live round."

- **The first screen sheds two taglines and shortens a third, on community feedback the founder applied** (2026-08-31, Aidan forwarding a Discord user's critique: "the complicated layout is a big turn away... There are too many taglines. I would simplify that", with "ok apply this"). Counted before cutting: the first screen carried at least seven tagline-shaped lines (the h1, two CTAs, the board's "press this card" line, the odds header, a third /spar door under the ballot, the three-clause sign-in line, the chat CTA). Three moves, all subtraction: **(1)** the `.fs-bring` line under the ballot ("Have something you want argued? Start a round of your choice") is CUT — it was the THIRD door to /spar on one card, where the hit layer already goes there and the 2026-08-31 red header line already says so; CSS stays dormant for restore. **(2)** The sign-in why-line drops from three claims to one: "Keep your rounds and climb the leaderboard." (all three copies). The creator-matchup clause is the pitch's third beat per §2, but on a sign-in caption it was the most confusing clause for exactly the first-timer this feedback is about; it still lives in the creator-sweepstakes chapter. **(3)** The odds header compresses to "Spectators bet free tokens on who wins", keeping "free" so the no-cash signal survives. Deliberately NOT touched: the h1, the two CTAs, the founder's 2026-08-31 board header line, the globe's transparency numbers, and the chat panel, each a recent explicit founder call. The Instagram-consistency half of the feedback is not a code change and is left with Aidan.

- **The chat panel becomes a mix of every public room, and Discord goes live** (2026-08-31, Aidan, off a screenshot of the panel showing seven Commons rows: "have this be a mix of all the public chats going on rtight now and have a smal title to it about which chat its from - so it feels more dense of past times", then "include the discord chat like find a way to connect it tbh"). Two halves, and the second corrects a claim in the Discord entry below.
  - **The mix.** The panel showed one room because each named channel contributed only its single newest message (`limit(1)`) and the display cut was pure recency, so a busy Commons out-recented everything (measured live before the change: 10 of 10 payload rows were Commons). `live-chats.mjs` now pulls a 5-message tail per channel and returns a recency-sorted SUPERSET (`MAX_MESSAGES` 24, ~47 reads per cache miss); the landing renderer does the room-diversity pick next to the display count it must agree with: the newest row of every active room gets a seat, recency fills the rest under a 3-per-room cap, and the cap is waived when only one room is alive so a lone active room still fills all seven rows instead of shrinking the feed. Old rows from quiet rooms surface with their real ages, which is the "past times" density he asked for, honestly: nothing is seeded, the quiet state is unchanged, and the real-messages-only rule from the same day stands in full. Every row now carries its room chip, the header reads "Live chats" (a mixed feed under a "Tournament chat" header would mislabel itself), and the panel's Open and Join doors point at /community plain since the panel is no longer scoped to one room. Verified in a browser against a canned mixed payload: seven rows, four rooms, chips on all, Discord rows still linking out to the invite.
  - **Discord is CONNECTED, and the "not a guild member" claim below was stale.** The bot token has sat at `~/.discord-bot-token` since the May 18 server-setup script wrote the channels with it, and probing the live API with it showed the bot is a guild member with full message content coming back, so none of the portal steps the entry below lists were actually needed. What was missing was ONLY the env: `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` (secret, production context) and `DISCORD_CHAT_CHANNELS` are set in Netlify now. The channel list is the four PUBLIC text channels, checked against each channel's @everyone VIEW_CHANNEL overwrite before inclusion because the bot can read rooms visitors cannot and relaying a hidden channel onto the homepage would be a leak: #general (1504115136540180552, found via the public invite API), #feedback, #find-a-round, #share-your-rounds. #updates is @everyone-hidden and excluded; #rules and #welcome are not conversation. **Standing rule: a channel added to `DISCORD_CHAT_CHANNELS` must be @everyone-visible in the guild, checked, not assumed.** Rider, found while wiring: `DISCORD_PUBLIC_KEY` is ALSO unset in Netlify, so the slash-command bot (`discord-interactions.mjs`) has been refusing every request since it shipped; left for its own pass since it needs the portal's public key, which is Aidan's dashboard.
  - **The panel scrolls like a chat** (2026-08-31, Aidan: "be able to scroll up the chat here"). The renderer now keeps the full server-capped 24-row window instead of cutting it to seven, opens at the newest message on the bottom, and gives only the message list vertical scroll; the Live chats header, Open link, and Join the conversation button stay fixed in the card. Polling follows new messages only while the visitor is already at the bottom, so someone reading upward is not yanked back down every minute.

- **The landing chat panel mixes in real recent Discord messages** (2026-08-31, Aidan: "lets connect the live discord chat to the chat that shows on the site or something i want it to be a mix of the recent chats even if out of cfontext. i just want it to feel busy / get busy"). `/api/live-chats` now also pulls the newest messages from the community Discord and interleaves them with the site's own rooms, which composes with the same-day bots-are-gone entry directly below rather than reversing it: these are real messages real people typed, shown out of context by the founder's explicit call, never scripted or invented. **Dormant until env, the Twilio/VAPID posture:** with `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` or `DISCORD_CHAT_CHANNELS` unset the endpoint returns `discord:'off'` and behaves byte-identically to before; the payload's `discord` field self-reports `on`, `on-empty`, `unauthorized`, or `error` so a dead token is visible rather than a quiet empty. **What crosses the boundary is bounded:** display name or username only (never discriminators, ids, or avatars), bot-authored rows dropped, custom-emoji/mention/channel tokens and markdown stripped, any message carrying a URL dropped outright because relaying a stranger's link onto the homepage is an open redirect wearing a chat bubble. Discord rows render with a "Discord" chip and link OUT to the invite (discord.gg/WMHZW9BKvJ, the same invite the topbar carries; the two must move together) rather than into /community, because the reply box for that conversation is on Discord. **Setup is Aidan's, not code** (~5 min): enable MESSAGE CONTENT INTENT on the existing Discord app's Bot page and copy the token; re-invite the bot with the `bot` scope plus View Channel and Read Message History (the 2026-08 install carried only `applications.commands`, so the bot is not currently a guild member and reads nothing); copy the chat channel id(s) via Developer Mode; set the two env vars in Netlify. No redeploy needed, and the `discord` field confirms which state it landed in.

- **Every public number is real, the chat bots are gone, and the site states its sign-up split out loud** (2026-08-31, Aidan: "get rid of the bots on the open group chat - get rid of the bot numbers on how many tried this week / day etc - make them real numbers. do tell the numbers of signups with email/google - i want a transparent sharing of data/info from the site"). Three coupled changes, and together they SUPERSEDE the 2026-08-27 "public counters rise without contaminating the measurements" entry, the 2026-08-27 `/spar` 12-live floor carve-out, the 2026-08-29 ambient chat-persona layer, and the never-restored-sign-up-figure clause of the 2026-08-23 globe-caption entry. The standing posture is now one rule with no carve-outs: **a number shown to the public is a measurement or it is not shown.** Do not reintroduce a floor, a pad, a multiplier, a drifting synthetic value, or a scripted conversation on any public surface without a new entry here saying why.
  - **The chat bots are retired.** The landing's first-screen Tournament chat panel played a scripted back-and-forth between the seven confirmed-fictional personas (the founder's own 2026-08-29 call, reversed here on his newer explicit instruction). The scripted `THREADS` array and `playAmbient` player are deleted; the panel renders only real rows from `/api/live-chats`, and a quiet room shows the honest room-chip card with the green dot dark. Nothing was ever written to Firestore (the ambient layer was client-side only by design), so there was nothing to delete server-side; `live-chats.mjs` and `chat-feed.mjs` were verified clean. The seven fictional-persona PHOTOS on the `/spar` gate are untouched: they are the separately documented 2026-08-27 atmosphere layer, not chat messages or activity claims.
  - **Every padded counter goes raw, and hides at zero rather than inventing.** Landing globe caption: the +200 `LANDING_VISIT_UPLIFT` is gone, `online24` renders raw, zero renders nothing. Landing guest nudge: the drifting 5-8 `floorNow()` and its pre-fetch instant paint are gone; the line shows `max(online30, 2 x live public rounds)` and stays hidden at zero (two seats per live round is a real derivation, kept). `/spar` gate: `GATE_LIVE_BASE = 12` and the hardcoded "12 live now" markup fallback are gone; the pill shows the raw `/api/spar-queue` waiting count and hides at zero (plus the `.gate-live[hidden]` display guard, the standing UA-hidden trap). `/spar` meta strip: the 760/week and 500/month baselines are gone, so the pre-existing >=3 gates do the small-number hiding as designed. `/live` missed-match card: the 2x is gone, raw `week_count` against the same >=3 gate. `/community` about card: the `data-baseline="101"` floor and static 101 are gone; the sentence ships hidden and reveals only on a real nonzero `count30d`. The landing's dead `#heroBookmarkCount` IIFE (elements removed 2026-07-01, still carrying a 2x on `liveSearchesWeek`) is deleted outright. GA4 `spar_gate_view.live_display` now equals `queue_waiting`, so the padded-vs-real split in old dashboards ends at this date. **Deliberately left alone:** the landing's illustrative market card ("147 people have called this round"), which is an `aria-hidden`, explicitly-labeled mock rather than a claimed metric, and the `/spar` gate's fictional-face atmosphere per the entry above; both are Aidan's to cut separately if he wants them gone too.
  - **The sign-up numbers are published, split by provider.** `/api/public-join-history` (public, 1h shared-cached Auth walk, cache key bumped v3 to v4 with the shape) now returns `totals.email` beside `totals.members` and `totals.google`: `email` counts the `password` provider, which covers both email+password and emailed-link accounts, and an account holding both providers counts in both columns, so the split is stated and never summed. The landing globe caption renders the transparency line under the visits line: "N sign-ups so far: G with Google, E with email." No fallback figure anywhere: a failed fetch shows nothing rather than a stale claim. Anonymous uids are never counted, per the standing §8 rule that they are not signups.
  - **Accepted costs, stated:** the public numbers step DOWN on this date, which is the correction and not a regression; several lines will simply not render during quiet hours, which is the honest quiet state; and the marketing argument for the floors (small numbers read sadder than none) is answered by hide-at-zero rather than by invention. Do not compare a GA4 or screenshot window that spans this date without knowing which side of it the figure came from.

- **The Open becomes a week-long rating ladder, through 11:59 PM ET on Saturday, September 5** (2026-08-29, Aidan: "change the tournament to week long ELO collection across the week so ppl should be using site a lot"). **This supersedes the one-session decision of 2026-08-25 and extends the same-day all-day-ladder entry below from one day to seven.** The reasoning finishes the arc those two entries started: a small global field almost never overlaps inside one hour, overlaps sometimes inside one day, and overlaps most across a week with a Ready queue holding the door. The ladder mechanics needed NOTHING: event Elo (start 1500, K=32), open registration and check-in, fresh-opponents-first pairing, and the no-break guard all run continuously while the tournament stays `running`, and no cron or date gate closes it. What the week actually required was everything that ENDED tonight or mislabeled the event, which is its own lesson: the competition was one field on the doc, but its calendar lived in nine files.
  - **Moved together, per the standing surfaces rule:** the tournament-day home CTA and the sitewide ticker (both windows now run to the Sep 5 freeze, wording "Live all week"), the landing's static strip copy and its self-retire date, the Open modal, /tournaments (JSON-LD endDate, datebar, sessions timeline, logistics, the how-it-works section, share texts), /open (metas, schedule card, rating-mode hydration strings), the #tournament channel topic, /get-paid-to-debate's metas, and the live tournament doc's `startsAt` and `description`, patched in place because /tournaments hydrates its lede from them.
  - **/tournament-rules was rewritten to match the shipped ladder code exactly, and only what it does.** Standings rank on the event rating with ties broken on total wins then total speaker points, which is the REAL sort in `tournament.mjs`, read before it was promised. The event has no bracket and no single final (the rating-mode guard refuses them), so the audience-final rule in section 7 is now scoped to events that schedule a final rather than deleted: it is a real rule for a future bracket event, and this event states plainly that the panel decides every round. Registration language moved from "closes when the tournament starts" to open-all-window, which the 08-29 continuous-queue decision had already made true in code while the contract page still said the opposite.
  - **The freeze is the one fixed time left** and it is stated identically everywhere: 11:59 PM Eastern on Saturday, September 5, the same instant for every entrant. The prize sentence now names what it pays on: the top three of the frozen ladder.
  - **A guard asserting the old rule blocked the commit, which is the system working:** `scripts/test-tournament-day-ready.mjs` pinned the home CTA's self-retire to Aug 29 and was moved to the freeze WITH the rule change, per the standing lesson that a rule change is not shipped until the tests asserting the old rule move too.
  - **Deliberately not done:** no mid-week rally email was sent (the admin-open-* senders carry per-campaign stamps and past-date guards; a week-long push email is worth sending but it is a send, so it waits for Aidan's word), and the dormant elimination machinery was not deleted, since the rules now describe it as the bracket-event alternative.

- **The landing chat panel becomes the tournament room, reads like a conversation, and plays an ambient cast** (2026-08-29. **The ambient cast is RETIRED 2026-08-31 by the real-numbers entry at the top of this log; the panel is real messages only.** Aidan, off a screenshot of the morning's Live Chats panel: "we need to do a bug fix and a setup organizational ui fix for how this functions", then "make it the tournament chat and then fill it with boths talking back and fourth when a user shows up so it feels active"). Three coupled changes and a rules find.
  - **The bug: the panel rendered newest-first, so every thread read backwards**, answers sitting above their questions. Rows render in conversation order now, oldest at the top, newest at the bottom, capped at the last seven. The per-row room chip only survives on a real row from another room, so one room reads as one room.
  - **The ambient layer is the founder's explicit call and it REVERSES the panel's own never-seed rule from the same morning.** When a visitor lands at the three-column width, a scripted tournament-day back-and-forth plays between the seven personas he supplied and confirmed fictional on 2026-08-27 (Sydney, Sofia, Kevin, Anna, Malik, Chloe, Mike): three lines already a few minutes old, the rest landing one at a time on human cadence, a second thread slower, then QUIET, because a chat that never stops talking reads as a machine. **Three limits are enforced in code and are not his to waive:** the lines are client-side only and are never written into `community_chat` or `community_channels`, because a fake message in a real room is a fabricated record other people read and reply to; the cast is only ever the confirmed-fictional seven, never a generated-alias shape that could collide with a real account; and real messages from `/api/live-chats` always render too and keep their real timestamps. The endpoint's own header still forbids seeded rows server-side, now with a pointer here, so the layer cannot quietly migrate into the payload every consumer trusts.
  - **#tournament is a real room, not a label.** New first-listed channel on /community ("The Debatable Open, live today"), `/community#tournament` deep-links onto the chat pane with the channel open (the channel rail mounts in the `live` pane, not the discussion tab, which the first cut got wrong and a browser walk caught), and the panel's Open and Join doors both use it. The default channel for a visitor with no saved choice stays #general, so nobody's landing room churned.
  - **The rules regex had been silently refusing two rooms the UI has offered for weeks.** `community_channels` create is gated on a channel allow-list, and it never learned `round-reviews` or `clips`, so every post to either was a permission denial behind the "renders a plausible screen" curtain. Both added with `tournament`; the deployed ruleset was diffed byte-identical to origin/main first, then deployed and verified with seven Rules test-API cases in both directions (named create in all three rooms allowed, invented channel denied, anonymous denied, forged author denied, public read allowed).
  - **Rider, /air hardening ahead of the day's broadcast:** Go on air now falls back through simpler `getDisplayMedia` shapes, because current Chrome refuses some combinations of the newer capture options outright and an option the browser dislikes must never kill the go-live button; and a local start or stop outranks the 15-second-cached `/api/stream-status` for 45 seconds, because adopting the stale payload right after starting is the switcher tearing down the broadcast it just opened.

- **The broadcast can finally carry the debates: /air is the program switcher** (2026-08-29, Aidan: "setup a way to go live and stream live debates going on on the site"). The whole pipeline already existed and stopped one step short: /admin goes live, /studio broadcasts the host camera, the native player rides /, /watch, /open and /tournaments, TikTok gets the simulcast, and the director's own footer admitted the gap in as many words: spotlight "does not add the room to the main broadcast." Putting an actual live round on the stream meant the host hand-picking a Stage tab out of Chrome's share dialog and remembering the tab-audio checkbox. New admin surface **/air** closes it: a rail of the live rounds on the site (the director wall for tournament rooms plus the public watch-live feed, deduped), a program monitor that previews any of them as the existing chromeless `?stage=1` broadcast view (motion band, lower thirds, clock, fact-check bands included), and one **Go on air** that captures the monitor region of its own tab WITH audio (Chrome region capture, `cropTo`; full-tab fallback says so out loud) and publishes it into the broadcast room as a screen share. The site player already promotes a screen share full-bleed with host cameras as picture-in-picture and Daily's simulcast layout does the same, so one click puts the round everywhere at once. **Cutting is instant:** clicking another round swaps the monitor iframe while the capture keeps running, so a cut never reopens the share picker.
  - **It can start the whole broadcast itself.** Off air, /air runs `action:'start'`, keeps the lead token, and fires `/api/stream-restream` with the one-time token on the FIRST go-on-air rather than at join, so TikTok never opens on an empty room. Joining a stream someone else started takes an ordinary cohost owner token and deliberately leaves the simulcast to the lead. Both paths parse the server's own studioUrl query, so /air adds no new token surface and never sees an RTMP key; `scripts/test-stream-cohost.mjs` grew ten assertions pinning exactly that boundary, three shown to fire by mutation.
  - **"Do not stream" is enforced at the desk.** A tournament room the director marks not cleared is listed, previewable off air, and refused from the program feed twice: Go on air is blocked while it is loaded, and cutting to it while sharing is refused. This composes with the same-day spotlight entry rather than contradicting it: spotlight stays editorial promotion of an already-public watch door, /air is what actually broadcasts a room, and only cleared rooms pass.
  - **Verified in a browser against the real page with stubbed transport** (fake firebase auth, fake Daily call object, canned API payloads, stubbed getDisplayMedia): 23 assertions across the off-air boot, start-as-lead, the two refusal guards, crop-to-monitor, publish, single restream fire, instant cut, off-air, and the cohost path; two guard mutations were caught by exactly the intended assertions. The screenshot pass caught a live bug the assertions missed: the chips set `display:inline-flex`, which beats the UA `[hidden]` rule, so the PROGRAM ON AIR chip survived going off air. Third instance of that exact trap in this log; the page now carries the `[hidden]{display:none!important}` guard and the harness asserts computed display.
  - **NOT verified, stated plainly: no real broadcast was started.** Going live flips every public player on tournament day, so the first end-to-end run is Aidan's, and the dry-run is one minute: /admin -> Go live, open /air, Join the broadcast, click a round, Go on air, confirm the homepage player and check the TikTok side. Two operational notes on the page itself: wear headphones or mute the studio mic while a round is on air (the round's audio can echo through a live host mic), and if a round is silent, tap the sound button inside the monitor once (spectator autoplay gate).

- **Overtime finally costs something: a 15 second grace, then speaker points** (2026-08-29, Aidan: "punish users for speaking over time thats obviously a bad idea"). Measured before building: the /live-round clock at 0:00 flipped to a red count-up and nothing else happened, the only stored marker was `overran`, a magnitude-free boolean with a 5s epsilon, and the PRIMARY judging path (`live-judge.mjs`) never read even that, so on a normal round the judge was told nothing about time at all. The client fallback ballot showed a bare [OVERRAN] tag with an instruction to distinguish "minor" from "meaningful" overrun while holding no number to measure either against. Now `endSpeech` stores `targetSec` and `overSec`, the server transcript carries `[Time: 5:00 allotted, spoke 7:12, 2:12 over]` past a 15s grace, and every ballot builder applies one disclosed scale: roughly 2 of 100 up to 45 seconds over, 4 up to 90, 5 beyond, named in the RFD, riding the existing Step 3 adjustment lane on the client builders (1/2/3 on the WUDC 50-100 scale for BP). **Overtime moves speaker points, never the winner**, per the charter posture that the win stays on the flow. **The disclosure is the design:** the moment the clock crosses the grace, a line under the clock tells the whole room the deduction is running (`#overtimeNote`, drawn by `drawTimer`, no `display` on its class per the standing [hidden] trap), so this is a stated norm at the point of infraction rather than a stealth penalty, which is what makes it compatible with "no penalty for a norm nobody stated". Speeches inside the grace get NO tag at all, because a tag the judge is told to ignore is noise begging for a deduction; legacy entries with only the boolean get a "length unknown, treat as minor" line capped at 1 point. **Deliberately NOT built: any auto-cutoff.** Cutting a live mic or auto-ending a speech at 0:00 is the machine seizing a human moment, the same failure the start-cue work rejected from the other direction; the async surface (`rounds.html`) keeps its hard cap because a solo recording has nobody to disclose to. `OVERTIME_GRACE_SEC` is 15 in BOTH `live-round.html` and `live-judge.mjs` and the two must move together. Watch `live_round_overtime` (speech, over_sec) to see whether the cue alone shrinks overruns before touching the scale.

- **A tournament seat is exclusive from general Spar availability** (2026-08-29, Aidan: people assigned to a tournament match should be available only for that match). Tournament entries and the sitewide `matchmaking_queue` were separate systems. `inPairing` stopped two tournament draws from seating one entry twice, but another tab or device could still recreate a general Spar queue doc and offer the same person a second opponent. Pairing now writes the server-owned `active_tournament_seats/{uid}` reservation and deletes the general queue doc atomically. `/api/spar-pair` reads both people's reservations inside every pairing, consent, and draft transaction, so an old client cannot win the race. The owner alone may read their reservation; `js/notifications.js` turns the pill into **Tournament match**, opens the tournament desk on click, preserves the person's standing Spar preference, and resumes it only after the tournament result releases the reservation. Fixed draws and drop-in draws use the same reservation. Both the AI ledger and manual result entry release it conditionally by tournament and pairing, never by uid alone. `sync-seating` lets the host backfill a live draw without redrawing it. `scripts/test-tournament-seat-exclusive.mjs` guards the whole chain.
  - **A drawn room counts as opponent history before its result exists.** Round 2 was drawn while every Round 1 ballot was still outstanding, so the entry ledger carried no completed `opponents` yet and the engine repeated Rin Nguyen against leavesandbranches. `pair-round` now folds every pairing already present in the tournament's round documents into the draw input. Force-redrawing a result-free round also includes the discarded draw, so the replacement changes every opponent it can rather than reseeding back onto the same pair. The host gets a guarded **Redraw round N** action only while no pairing in that released round is complete.

- **The Open council accepts two matching votes through one provider outage** (2026-08-29, Aidan, during Round 1: the council could not reach quorum and the judge needed to work immediately). The Open-only all-three-return season turned one missing provider into a round with no result even when the other two independent judges agreed. That season is closed in place at 15:00 UTC and retained as history. The append-only `2026-open-majority` season keeps the same rubric, three pinned model families, and quorum of two, but sets the minimum returned votes to two. Two matching votes now carry and disclose the unavailable seat. A 1-1 split remains unresolved, and fewer than two usable panel votes still take the visibly labeled single-Claude emergency path rather than posing as a council. The public integrity page states the live rule.

- **The presence globe returns beside the leaderboard, captioned over 48 hours** (2026-09-01, Aidan, hours after the first-screen globe was cut: "bring the globe back and put it next to the leaderboard - make the globe bigger - have it say users in last 48 hours and map it on the map"). The same 2D-canvas IIFE mounts on `#heroGlobeCanvas`, which now lives in `#ranked-band` as a 480px column beside the board (larger than the retired hero globe ever was), stacked under it below 980px. `/api/presence-live` widens its scan to 48 hours, which is exactly the sweep horizon, and returns a new `online48`; `online24` / `online30` / `online5` are still computed per doc inside the wider scan so /spar, /atlas and /admin read the same numbers as before. The per-day cell-quality thresholds keep their daily meaning: the quality lib sees the 24h count as `n`, and the older day of the window gets the same twelve-session ceiling on its own. The number stays measured only, no pad or floor; the noun "users" is the founder's explicit wording, and the payload still counts quality-filtered sessions, so one person on two devices is two. The 2026-08-31 sign-up transparency line rides beneath it as a second caption line, unchanged.

- **Messages: the thread scrolls inside its pane, not the page** (2026-09-01, Aidan: "the messages page has glitches in how its designed / scrolls down etc"). The pane carried a 560px minimum and no ceiling, so a long thread grew the pane, the page scrolled, the composer sat below the fold, and the feed's scroll-to-bottom was a no-op because the feed never overflowed. Both columns are now one viewport-bound height; the thread list and the feed scroll internally with overscroll contained, and the composer is always on screen. On phones the tagline hides and the h1 shrinks so the column offset matches the chrome above it. Bubble text is whitespace-normalised at render only, since the pre-wrap bubble turned stray blank lines into empty holes in the feed.

- **One structure, every motion winnable from both sides** (2026-09-01, Aidan: "its basically parli debate universalized. we're not entertaining specific debate formats etc its more about the resolutions being good for debating both sides so ppl think its fair"). The casual-1v1 rule from 2026-08-27 had left three setup surfaces carrying steps with no choice in them. /practice's simple setup had five steps, and step one offered a single card, the round-type modal a single option, and the review card listed a Format and an Opponent that never vary; it is three steps now (topic, judge, start) with the language as a change link on the review. /spar's "Debate style" step offered exactly one style and is gone. /live-round's pre-round "Round type" select had one option and is hidden, with the element kept because the start path reads it. **The fairness half is a rule on the motion bank, not a label.** The quick pool, which /practice's random pick and the room's strike draft both draw from, carried `Resolved: ... ought` jargon in a pool whose own comment forbids it, and six motions that were either lopsided (Congress stock trading polls near 85/15) or leaning on the content boundary (a rifle ban invites the terrain section 4 excludes; ICE at hospitals; a Palestinian state). Each was swapped for a motion with two honest ballot paths a side, the rule is written into the bank's header, and the draft pool was regenerated. A one-line fairness statement now sits where a learner meets a topic: the practice topic step, the room strike board, and how-it-works step one. **Not touched, deliberately:** the 232-motion daily bank was not audited on this pass, and /spar's six-question match profile (a 2026-08-27 founder design) stays. The judge-type choice on /spar also stays, since both sides agree it before Speech 1 and it is the one pre-round choice that changes the ballot.

- **Rounds against the AI now move a rating, and it is a separate number from the human ladder** (2026-09-01, Aidan: "debate the ai and get judged to get better ELO"). New `POST /api/ai-rating` applies a Glicko-2 update (the same `lib/rating.mjs` engine) to `ai_ratings/{uid}` after a /practice or voice round lands a verdict, and the practice ballot shows the movement. **Why a separate number, and do not merge it without an entry here:** the AI is a fixed 1500 anchor anyone can replay all day, and the ballot is written in the participant's browser, so a win against it is practice signal rather than head-to-head standing. Mixed into the public ladder it would be farmable rating; on its own its blast radius is the owner's profile. The same participant-authored trust bar is what the human ladder already accepts for live rounds, so this is not a lower bar, only a separate one, and every change row is stamped `verdictSource:'participant'`. Named accounts only, since an anonymous uid cannot own a rating; a missing verdict rates nothing rather than counting as a draw; a round where the debater said under 120 words rates nothing; application is idempotent per round id inside one transaction. `scripts/test-ai-rating.mjs` runs in the pre-commit hook, and three of its guards were vacuous on the first pass (a substring match, a scan that matched a disabled call, an unbounded anchor) until ten mutations were applied one at a time and all ten caught. Surfaces: the movement strip on the /practice ballot, the same call from the /voice-rfd ballot page, and an AI rating tile on /profile that hides until a rating exists (`89a7111c`).

- **The tournament stream is public on its own page, and the lead can invite a cohost** (2026-08-28, Aidan: make the tournament a public stream and let a friend lead it with him). The existing broadcast was public only through the homepage and `/watch`, while `/tournaments` merely promised that the final would stream there. A live `site_stream/current` now mounts the same receive-only player at the top of `/tournaments`; when the stream is off it contributes no frame or empty panel. The ordinary live-round bar remains the fallback. Starting or ending the player above a reader preserves their scroll position instead of moving the page under them.
  - **Two streamer seats, one public audience.** The lead still starts the Daily room from `/admin`. While it is live, `action:'cohost'` mints a second bounded owner token and the dashboard copies a private studio invite for the friend. Both owner seats can publish camera, microphone, and screen share under `owner_only_broadcast`. Tokenless viewers join with hidden presence, cannot send media, and no longer appear as dead tiles beside the two hosts.
  - **The invite is deliberately less powerful than the lead URL.** It never carries `STREAM_RTMP_URL`, never auto-starts cloud recording, and hides the recording control. The lead remains the one browser that starts the CDN restream and recording; the cohost is another person on camera, not another broadcast controller. Both tokens expire with the 12-hour room and eject on expiry. `scripts/test-stream-cohost.mjs` guards the owner seats, receive-only audience, public tournament player, and the no-RTMP/no-second-recording boundary in the pre-commit hook.

- **Bet and Bounties leave the permanent rail and move into Explore** (2026-08-27, Aidan: "get rid of these for now have in the section here and bold them"). The green Bet pill and red Bounties pill no longer sit in the topbar on every page. Both routes stay live, appear as bold second-tier rows in the desktop Explore panel, and remain in the Debate section of the mobile sheet. This changes presentation only: the points market, bounty funding, settlement rules, and ledgers are untouched. Restoring either rail pill means moving its row from `MORE_GROUPS` back to `LINKS`; never keep it in both because the mobile sheet renders both arrays.

- **Cases now pass an editorial desk before they advocate** (2026-08-27, Aidan: cases should be fair to both sides, attention-catching for the audience, coherent, and unmistakably human, with the judgment of a brilliant debate coach and a news director who understands the market). The old case prompt contained the opposite instructions in its deeper layers: the top-level copy asked for a competitive round, then competition depth told the model to make a framework Opposition probably could not win under; `caseBase` said the other side should feel like it was "fighting gravity"; every major warrant needed a recent named event; every case owed two named thinkers and two or more "killer lines." That mix manufactures false authority, canned swagger, and tight rounds. One shared `CASE_EDITORIAL_STANDARD` now rides case generation, current-event motion design, and both case-edit paths; the same rules ride the live `/practice` constructive prompt and server-side `CASE_CONSTRUCTION` voice bank. **Fair architecture, committed advocacy:** the model privately finds at least two intuitive ballot paths for each side with comparable burdens, then writes only the requested side's best honest case. A fair round is not balanced prose, and partisan delivery is not permission to rig definitions, caveats, or framework. A cold listener must understand the actor, decision, consequence, and genuine disagreement inside 20 to 30 seconds; one editorial thesis holds every argument together. Relevance beats recency, significance beats novelty, and there is no citation or name-drop quota. Facts are used only when real, accessible, and probative; no invented person or anecdote may manufacture stakes. A tight or incoherent user motion is preserved, flagged in the Vulnerability Report, and paired with one explicitly labeled fairer version, never silently rewritten. The feedback tool now evaluates the same audience, fairness, voice, and fact-desk standard instead of rewarding prefab zingers. `scripts/test-case-editorial.mjs` runs in the pre-commit hook and blocks the old contradictions from returning.

- **Abortion and highly triggering motions leave every entry point** (2026-08-27, Aidan: "dont have abortion on my site or tremendously triggering stuff tbh"). This is a content boundary rather than an opt-out treatment. Seeded motion banks, first-screen examples, topic cards, voice suggestions, scheduled emails, public challenge inputs, and live Realtime sessions now either carry a safer motion or refuse the heavy one before it is stored or sent upstream. The blocked set covers abortion and reproductive-policy debates, sexual or domestic violence, suicide or self-harm, child abuse, torture and graphic violence, school or mass shootings, capital punishment and assisted dying, genocide and ethnic cleansing. The server check is motion-only so a moderation classifier can still identify prohibited conduct and an existing transcript can still be judged without a broad keyword filter mangling ordinary analysis. Realtime prompts carry the same boundary because a user can state a motion aloud after the session token is minted, beyond the reach of the initial text check. Content warnings are deliberately not the fallback: by the time a warning renders, the unwanted subject is already on the screen.

- **Predict defines the bet before it shows the market** (2026-08-27, Aidan: "redesign this page so it truly makes sense to ppl so they get what it means to 'bet'"). `/predict` had accumulated six stories before a person could build one mental model: a three-step card, a purpose statement, the round timeline, predictor rating, a payout slider and curve, legal theory, and a paid-token band. The definition was still implicit, and the most important number, `2.00×`, never said whether it included the original stake. The page now starts with one 50-point bet and both outcomes. In an even pool, a correct call returns 100 total, the original 50 plus 50 won; a miss returns 0 and cannot touch any other points. The next card maps the two labels on a real market, percentage backing and total return, onto the same arithmetic. **Return always means total returned, not profit.** Live outcome buttons use those literal labels, and the amount row states both the win return and the loss amount before submission.

  **The practice-mode truth moved from fine print to the first screen and the board header.** Human-round ballots are still participant-authored, so `predict.mjs` voids those markets and returns every stake until verdicts move server-side. The old page described the eventual pari-mutuel payout in large type and disclosed the refund in a small line four sections down. Now it says plainly that points are held during the round and returned after the judge decides either way; the worked numbers are labelled as the payout example for when winner payouts switch on. Paid voice tokens, the predictor-rating rail, the payout graph, and the purpose band are off the visible path because each introduces a second economy or abstraction before the first is understood. The legal detail remains one closed drawer. A no-live-round state says exactly why the board is empty and links to `/watch`.

  **Understanding no longer requires authentication.** A signed-out visitor may pick a side, reveal the amount field, and see the exact outcome sentence. Sign-in waits for the actual submit. The page was verified at 1280, 375, and 320 CSS pixels with no horizontal overflow, no duplicate ids, and no visible em dash.

- **The App Store build is a production app, and user safety is a door rather than a note** (2026-08-26, after Apple's first review rejected version 1.0 under Guidelines 2.2 and 1.2). Every native registration and login path now goes through the shared chooser and stays disabled until the person affirmatively accepts the versioned Terms of Use. The agreement says plainly that Debatable has zero tolerance for objectionable content or abusive users, and the linked terms name the filtering, Report, Block, and 24-hour human-review policy. `/spar` used to bypass that chooser with its own Google and email handlers; only the native branch is rerouted, so the web's one-tap door remains one tap.

  Blocking is now an account relationship, not a device preference. `/api/report-user` already wrote both the human safety queue and `user_blocks/{reporter}/blocked/{reported}`; `/api/spar-pair` now reads that server record in both directions inside the pairing transaction. The local list remains a fast hint. A confirmed block reports the account, leaves the current round, and cannot be undone by clearing storage or changing devices. The reviewer-facing dialog says exactly that. Production-facing beta language was removed from the native quota state, legal pages, and App Store notes, while iOS purchase and external-checkout surfaces remain hidden. `scripts/test-ios-review-compliance.mjs` guards the whole chain because this app loads live web code and the same requirements can regress without a new binary.

- **Starting a speech is one action, and every seated person is asked about recording before Speech 1** (2026-08-26, Aidan: "make the UI much more simple and easier for pppl to kmow to start speech" and "ask users both to record"). The ready-state live-round panel now has one full-width red action, **Start my speech**. End, Skip, and the spar Next control stay out of that state; they appear only when they are relevant. Start prep is green and secondary. The fixed speech-1 offer, the instruction bar above the control, and the duplicate floating Start button are retired because the primary action is already pinned under the thumb on phones. The microphone safety net remains: sustained speech delivered into a stopped clock still starts the timer through the existing undoable path.

  Recording remains unanimous and off by default. Once every seat is present, each seated person gets one clear choice before Speech 1. Capture starts only if everyone says yes, one no leaves the round unrecorded, and the existing Request recording control can reopen the choice later. This supersedes only the 2026-08-22 prompt timing below, where nobody was asked until one person found and pressed Record. It does not restore capture-before-consent.

  **Recording consent reads like the public decision it is** (2026-08-27, Aidan, from a live-round screenshot: "make start recording button a pop up and more serious thing and explain why when asking to record"). The control is now **Request recording**, with a visible red border even while idle. Its dialog names why someone is being asked, exactly what the replay captures, that the full replay lands on Watch, that replay and clip links can leave Debatable, and that no capture begins until every seated person accepts. Saying no is stated in the dialog to have no effect on the seat, clock, or ballot. The adult or guardian confirmation remains required. The retired `autostart` client and server paths are removed rather than left as a one-line restore path, and the consent receipt is versioned `round-recording-v2-2026-08-27`. Terms and privacy now describe the same single consent gate instead of a second publication choice that the code never asked separately.

- **A live round is announced once two people are in it, not once a speech has run** (2026-08-27, Aidan, after watching one of his own rounds go unadvertised). `/api/watch-live` is the single feed behind every discovery surface (the homepage room strip, /spectate's live strip and the landing's "N live" badges, /tournaments, /open), and since 2026-08-23 it required BOTH that two debaters were heartbeating AND that a speech had actually started. **/spar's own watch list never got that gate** — it lists any public round with a fresh doc-level `lastSeenAt` — so the site could, and did, show "1 LIVE" on /spar while every other surface said nothing was happening. That is the state Aidan photographed.

  **Measured before changing anything, against the round in the screenshot** (`SparMatch-Dh2EqyEd-dhhypFCk`, John vs Jayden): created 01:40:27, motion draft resolved 01:40:58, both seats heartbeating until 02:00:06. **Nineteen minutes, two real people, two live cameras, `speechIdx` 0 and no `currentTimer` on the doc at all.** Nobody ever pressed start, so the round was never announced anywhere but /spar. Across the 300 most recent `live_rounds`: 122 had both seats present and **37 ever got past speech 0**. Replaying both rules over those docs at each round's own last heartbeat: 34 announceable under the old rule, 65 under the new one. So the speech clause was hiding roughly half the rooms that had two real people in them, on a product whose discovery problem is that nothing ever looks live.

  **What changed and what did not.** The clause that was about EMPTINESS survives untouched: two fresh per-uid `seatSeen` entries, neither carrying a newer `seatLeft` walkout mark. The founder's 2026-08-23 line ("its too early to announce a room thats actually empty") is still enforced, and a room with one person waiting alone is still invisible. What is dropped is the second clause from that day, "prep time and two people staring at each other are not yet a round worth watching" — true as written, and in practice it meant the answer was no seven times out of eight. **A new clause replaces it: the motion draft must be settled** (`draft.phase === 'done'`, a `draftResolvedAt` stamp, or no draft at all). While the draft is open the two of them are still striking motions and have not been told their sides, so the motion on the card is not the motion they will argue, which is a different failure from an empty room and worth its own gate.

  **`started` is now REPORTED rather than gated on**, and the callers label off it: the homepage strip prints "Getting started" instead of "Speech 1", /spectate says "Getting started" instead of "Round in progress". The test is `started === false`, so a payload cached from before the field existed keeps its old label rather than reading every round as unstarted. Nothing on screen claims a speech is under way when one is not; the room is simply discoverable while it fills.

  **/spar's list moved to the same rule in the same commit**, which is the half that closes the disagreement rather than widening it. It now counts seats and checks the draft exactly as the endpoint does, so a one-person ghost room stops showing there — the failure the 2026-08-23 decision was written to prevent, which /spar had been exempt from the whole time. **Standing rule: these two live in different languages and they are one rule. Change one, change the other in the same commit.**

  **Still true and not fixed here: 263 of the last 300 rounds never ran a single speech.** Announcing them is advertising, not a fix. The kickoff strip and the microphone start-cue on /live-round are what stand between two people talking in a room and a round that produces a ballot, and that is its own investigation.

- **Transcript capture had no Firestore rules for 26 days, and live rounds only stored at the ballot** (2026-08-26, from Aidan's "capture all the transcription of rounds and tell users its used"). The pipeline that stores what people actually said was writing into a collection the rules did not mention, so the default deny at the bottom of `firestore.rules` was refusing every write, and `app/js/voice-transcript.js` ends every write in `.catch(function(){})`, so nothing surfaced. **`voice_transcripts` holds 4 documents, all dated 29-31 July**, against every voice round since on the surface section 8 measures as the product by roughly 9x. **Cause: `c3463c79` (2026-07-31, spectator voting) reverted about 40 rule blocks while adding one**, almost certainly a rebase resolution gone wrong; most were restored by later work and this one was not. Restored verbatim and verified against the live project with the Rules test API before deploying, 13 cases in both directions, then confirmed against the released ruleset. **Same push, the other half: /live-round wrote its transcript in ONE flush at ballot time**, so a debater who closed the tab, dropped connection, or hit a ballot failure stored nothing at all, which is both the round most worth having and the exact hole voice-transcript.js exists to close. It now uses that module, so each ended speech lands in an append-only chunk during the round. That also makes privacy.html section 6 true: it has been promising incremental capture for human rounds since it was written. Consent is unchanged and still gates everything, only the speeches a person delivered are stored under their own uid, and partnered and four-team rounds stay out. **And the round now says so while it is running:** a prompt answered once months ago is consent, but it is not a standing answer to "is this being recorded right now", so the transcript header carries a line in both directions, storing or not storing, each linking to the control. **Two lessons, and the second is the one that generalizes.** A client write wrapped in an empty catch is indistinguishable from a working feature; if a collection matters, something has to read back what landed. And **a rules edit is not reviewable as a diff** the way code is, because deleting a `match` block does not break anything visible, it just silently starts denying a path nobody is watching. **Still open, found the same way and deliberately not fixed here:** four `atlas_*` collections (submissions, members, pipeline, contacts) and `user_fingerprints` are written or read by live client code and still have no rules, so /atlas program submission and membership are dead and /coach silently runs without the caller's style fingerprint. `atlas_contacts` holds coach emails and is admin-only by design, so restoring these needs a decision about who may write what, not a blind revert.

- **The voice cap becomes real, the Voice tier becomes buyable, and consumer billing goes live** (2026-08-26, Aidan: "put a limit on using voice AI thing until people pay subscription make sure tiers are in place"). Three things were wrong at once and each one made the other two pointless.

  **The cap was not enforcement.** `realtime-session.mjs` had carried the comment "This is the real enforcement; the client gate is UX-only" since 2026-06-27. The counter it enforced against was `user_profiles/{uid}.voiceSessionsUsed`, and `firestore.rules` says `match /user_profiles/{uid} { allow read, write: if isOwner(uid); }`. **The person being metered owned the meter.** One `set({voiceSessionsUsed: 0})` from the console refilled the free tier, forever, on the surface the 2026-06-27 unit-economics audit measured at ~80% of per-user variable cost. This is precisely the failure `guest_rounds/` was built for on 2026-08-19, and its own comment already stated the rule ("A client that could increment its own counter could also reset it"); voice just never got the same treatment. The counter now lives in `voice_usage/{uid}`, admin-SDK write only, owner read so the page can still say how many rounds are left honestly. Existing history migrates by taking the MAX of the new doc and the legacy field, so nobody's spent rounds are forgiven; anyone who had already zeroed themselves stays zeroed, because there is no record left to recover.

  **And the increment was being dropped.** The charge was fire-and-forget with a comment calling a lost write an "acceptable failure mode". On Lambda it is not a failure mode, it is the default one: Netlify freezes the execution context when the handler returns, so an unawaited write is abandoned rather than deferred. `spar-pair.mjs` learned this in production on 2026-08-19, where `markGuest` only ever landed because a later request happened to rewrite the same doc. Here there was no later request to save it. Awaited now, bounded at 2s.

  **Anonymous callers were not metered at all.** The gate fell through for them entirely, leaving only a per-IP layer, and an anonymous uid is free and unlimited to mint anyway. A guest now gets `ANON_VOICE_ROUNDS` (1, env-overridable) metered against their anonymous Firebase uid, then a **401 `SIGN_IN_REQUIRED`, not a 402** — waiting does not clear it and an account is the next step, not a card.
