The client opens the auth modal on that code, tagged `voice_anon_gate` in GA4 so the wall's conversion is measurable rather than assumed.

  **The client lied about the numbers.** `voice-debate.html` said `ANON_VOICE_LIMIT = 3` / `FREE_VOICE_LIMIT = 8` while the server enforced 2, and the comment sitting there described the mismatch as a known flag rather than fixing it. So the page promised 8 rounds and cut people off at 2. Now 1 / 2, matching the server defaults. Same file also read `firebase.auth().currentUser` as "signed in", which is truthy for the anonymous user `js/notifications.js` mints on nearly every page, so a guest was being handed the larger allowance; it gates on `isAnonymous` now. And its `isPro` check read `user_profiles.plan`, a field nothing writes, so it resolved false for everyone including paying subscribers, who would have been shown an upgrade card for a cap the server was about to let them past. It reads `/api/teams/usage` instead.

  **The Voice tier did not exist.** `/pricing` sold "Voice $12/mo" with a Reserve button and a JSON-LD Offer, and `PLAN_PRICING` had no `voice` key, so it could not be bought. Worse, the voice cap's own bypass list did not name `voice` either, **so buying the tier sold to lift the cap would have lifted nothing.** The list had drifted because three minters (`realtime-session`, `coach-session`, `room-judge-session`) each kept their own copy; there is now one, `planBypassesVoiceCap` in the pure `lib/plans.mjs`, and `scripts/test-plans.mjs` asserts every purchasable plan is in it. Verified adversarially: dropping `voice` from the list, pricing it annually, and letting a canceled subscription keep access were each caught. Voice also had to be added to `lib/caller.mjs`'s `PAID_PLANS`, where its absence would have sold someone premium voice and then silently downgraded them to the free OpenAI one, and to `requirePaidPlan`, `claude.mjs`, `perplexity.mjs`, `argument-lint.mjs` and `upgrade-cta.js`. A $12/mo subscriber getting fewer brains than a $10/YEAR one is charging the most and granting the least. Stripe product and a $12/mo price were created and wired to `STRIPE_PRICE_VOICE`; the other four live prices were measured against canonical first and all four were correct.

  **Billing is on.** `BETA_NO_CHARGE=false`. `BETA_PRO_UNLOCK` flipped to false in the same push, because an unconditional unlock means the people who pay get exactly what the people who do not pay get. **The copy sweep was a precondition, not tidying:** a site that says "free during beta" while charging cards is a consumer-protection problem, so `pricing.html`, `index.html`, `practice.html`, `coach.html`, `ai-vs-ai-debate.html`, `online-debate-camp.html` and both landing arms were swept. On `/pricing` the AUTHORED markup now describes the state that charges and a server-driven `applyBetaCopy()` puts the beta wording back if `BETA_NO_CHARGE` is ever flipped on again. **That direction is deliberate**: the failure mode of a dead fetch or a crawler is then a page that says you will be billed when you might not be, rather than promising "no card today" to someone whose card is about to be charged. Both plan doors stay in the markup either way, per the 2026-08-11 rule that a data value must never decide whether a price is on screen.

  **A gap found by testing the finished thing rather than reading it, and it would have made the anon wall decorative.** The first version metered anonymous callers and then never met one: `/voice-debate` does not mint an anonymous session, so a real guest sent no Authorization header, `earlyDecoded` was null, and the whole gate block was skipped. I had repeated the stale "notifications.js signs nearly every visitor in anonymously" claim from soul.md, which `js/notifications.js` itself corrected on 2026-08-22 and `spar.html` had already been fixed for on the same finding: `signInAnonymously` appears only in practice.html, live.html, live-round.html and tournament.html. So /voice-debate now mints its own guest before the mint call, and the server REFUSES a tokenless voice mint outright (`VOICE_ALLOW_TOKENLESS=1` reopens it with no deploy) rather than letting a missing header walk past the wall. **Standing lesson, third time in this repo: a claim about which pages mint an anonymous session is load-bearing and has been wrong in the docs twice. Grep for `signInAnonymously` before designing anything on top of it.**

  **Watch:** voice starts against sign-ins at the anon wall, and the free-to-paid conversion now that a wall exists at all. If guest voice rounds collapse without sign-ins rising, the dial is `ANON_VOICE_ROUNDS`, not the wall. **Untested and stated rather than implied:** no real card has been run through the new Voice price end to end, and `verifyPrice` fails closed, so the first live Voice checkout is the thing to watch after this ships.

- **Talking into a stopped clock now reminds the speaker, and never starts for them** (2026-08-27, Aidan: "if someone is talking and they havent started speech remind them they can start the speech"). **This SUPERSEDES the 2026-08-26 automatic-start entry directly below.** The local Daily audio-level observer stays because it detects the exact failure: the current speaker is talking while their timer is still `ready`. After two seconds of accumulated speech, the real Start my speech button pulses faster and a fixed status says "Your speech has not started. We can hear you, but the clock and transcript are still off. Tap Start my speech to begin." It appears once per speech for nine seconds and clears the instant the speech starts or the turn changes. The observer never calls `startSpeechTimer()` now. A live mic proves that words are being said, not that they are the speech; a hello, mic check, or conversation must not spend someone's clock. GA4 `live_start_cue_reminder` replaces the retired autostart/undo pair.

- **A speech delivered into a stopped clock now starts the clock** (2026-08-26, Aidan from real rounds: "a lot of times ppl think they started their speech but didn't so make sure if u hear them talking without it start to resolve it"). **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-27 by the reminder-only entry above.** The 2026-08-25 start cue already KNEW. It watches the local audio level while the clock sits at ready and escalates when the mic says a speech is being delivered into nothing: the button pulses faster, a line reads "We can hear you. None of it is being recorded." What it then did was ask the person to press a button. **Someone who believes they already pressed it is not reading a prompt that tells them to press it**, which is why the funnel keeps recording rounds that die at speech 0 with both people present and one of them talking. The escalated state now resolves instead of shouting: sustained speech into a stopped clock starts the speech, through the same `startSpeechTimer()` every other path calls, so the judge lock, the auto-mic and the peer clock sync ride along.
  - **This NARROWS 2026-08-24's NO SPEECH STARTS ITSELF rather than reversing it, and the distinction is the whole design.** That rule was written against a COUNTDOWN, which starts on a number and can therefore land on someone still fixing a mic or not finished saying hello. This starts on EVIDENCE: a clock that begins only while the room is hearing sustained speech cannot start into silence, which is the failure the rule exists to prevent. **Countdowns stay retired** — do not bring one back on the strength of this entry.
  - **What it can still get wrong is what the speech WAS, so a hello can never cost anyone their speech.** The bar it raises says exactly what happened and carries one press back out: the undo puts the clock back to ready at zero, switches the mic off if we were the ones who turned it on, removes the words the mic added while leaving anything typed before it, and republishes the timer so the off-side is not left watching a clock that stopped existing. An undone speech never auto-starts again; the nudge takes over and says so.
  - **Three places the old rule still wins outright**, because in each of them a start would be a guess rather than a reading: a running prep clock (a deliberate not-yet, and the talking over it is as likely to be "ready?" as a speech), an unsettled judge at speech 1 (`startSpeechTimer` refuses it and opens the judge draft, which is not a thing a microphone should trigger mid-sentence), and any speech that is not the local debater's. All three keep the cue and lose only the start.
  - **The bar is 3.4s of accumulated speech against the cue's 2s.** Loud samples add and silence decays them at 200ms, so that is roughly six seconds of real talking with its natural gaps, and no amount of coughing reaches it. Measured in a browser by driving the real level observer: a cough peaks the meter at 600ms and a two-word hello at 1800ms, both decaying to zero and neither starting anything; 3.4s starts at elapsed 0 with the cue and dock cleared; undo restores ready/zero, strips "hey can you hear me" and keeps "pre-typed notes."; further talking after an undo goes hot and never starts. Each of the four refusals was exercised one at a time. New GA4 `live_start_cue_autostart` and `live_start_cue_undo`: **read them against each other, since the undo rate is the only honest measure of how often this is wrong.** A rising undo rate means the bar is too low, and the bar is the dial, not the feature. Commit `479b4ea9`, SW v2964.
- **The strike beat moves into the room, because a per-surface fairness step is a coin flip about whether fairness happens** (2026-08-26, hours after the draft shipped, Aidan mid-round: "the strike system doesnt exist anymore - fix!"). The motion draft shipped that morning on the QUEUE docs, gated on `draftOptIn` being present on BOTH. Only /spar set it: the background "Spar live" pill and /debate-chat cannot render a board, so refusing to hand them one was the honest call at the time. What it meant in practice, read off live Firestore the same evening: a pair at 19:04 drafted, a pair at 19:33 ran on a motion nobody had vetoed, and nothing on either screen said why. He read the second round as the feature being gone, which is the correct read of what he saw. **Every one of those surfaces lands in /live-round**, so the draft now runs there (`round-draft.mjs`, `POST /api/round-draft`) and which page your opponent happened to be standing on stops mattering. It also buys the clock back: both debaters are already seated, so no beat races a page navigation, which is what made the alternative (hand the peer off to /spar mid-draft) the worse of the two options. `lib/motion-draft.mjs` is untouched as a decision engine; only the transport moved.
  - **Blindness had to be rebuilt, and it is most of the risk.** Two queue docs gave it away for free, since each side could only ever hold its own strikes. One shared round doc has no field-level read rule, so the full draft lives in `round_drafts/{room}` (unlisted, so `firestore.rules`' default deny covers it, the `duo_teams` precedent) and `live_rounds/{room}.draft` carries a REDACTED projection: during the strike beat, WHO has committed and never WHAT they struck. `publicDraft()` is that projection, so it lives in the pure module next to the rule it protects and is treated as security rather than formatting.
  - **Eligibility is stamped by the server, never claimed by a client.** `spar-pair` is the only party that knows a pair was matched onto a motion nobody chose, so it writes the stamp for every pair it makes, and deliberately no longer keys it on `draftOptIn`. A /live challenge round, a direct link and a tournament round carry no stamp and keep the motion a human picked on purpose. Clients cannot write `round_drafts`, so the stamp cannot be forged.
  - **Two defects the browser caught that reading the diff would not have, both from the standing lessons.** The five motion cards rendered INVISIBLE: `anim-governor.js` ships animations PAUSED on this page, and the entrance carried `opacity` with `fill-mode: both`, which freezes a card at 0 forever, so five motions sat in the DOM with nothing on screen on the one beat the round cannot continue without. Visibility is a base style now and the keyframe only moves. And the card was WHITE ON WHITE in dark mode: this page defines exactly `--bg`, `--text` and `--accent`, while `--panel` and `--surface` do not exist here, so the fallback painted a white card and then took `--text` for the type. Measured after the fix: 16.6:1 light, 19.8:1 dark. **A CSS insertion also split an existing selector** (`body.spectator-mode .round-motion-bar`) because the anchor matched a suffix, which silently scoped the whole new block under spectator mode; the repair was verified by the diff reading 393 insertions and ZERO deletions.
  - **Guarded:** `scripts/test-round-draft.mjs` in the pre-commit hook. Nine mutations applied one at a time (publish strikes mid-beat, publish the motion, publish the side, advance on a short strike set, let the side holder call the motion, allow a struck motion, make the timeout side-call random, trust the client's strike count, discard a partial pick) and every one is caught. Two guards were VACUOUS on the first pass, which is the habit paying for itself: one asserted `motionId` was null on a draft that had no motion anyway, and one kept a single id by luck. Both were rewritten until the mutation failed them.

- **A judge wrote three confident sentences about a speech nobody gave, because the transcriber hands back its own prompt on silence** (2026-08-26, Aidan on a live ballot: "nobody ever talked in this debate so the AI must have hallucinated something here"). The floor judge had written, by name, "Lars established a clear structural frame and warranted his contentions better than most first constructives do", plus a paragraph about an unmapped counterfactual. The stored transcript for that speech was one sentence: "The speaker presented their case effectively, with well-structured contentions and strong warranting." Nobody wrote that either. The mic was open for twenty seconds into a quiet room.
  - **Reproduced against the live model rather than reasoned about.** Ten seconds of generated silence sent to `gpt-4o-transcribe` with our own live prompt does not come back empty, it comes back as OUR PROMPT: three of six runs returned the debate glossary verbatim, one fenced as `context: ### <glossary> ###`, the rest scaffolding fragments ("context:", "The flow"). **The domain hint is what makes the confabulation debate-shaped, and debate-shaped is exactly what no layer downstream questions.**
  - **Three gates, because the failure crossed three layers that each looked correct alone.** Do not transcribe silence: live-round's segment recorder meters the borrowed mic track and ships a segment only when it crossed speech level for ~400ms, per segment, read and reset before the next one arms. The 2048-byte size floor never caught this, because ten seconds of opus comfort noise is 5KB. Do not accept the prompt back as a transcript: `/api/transcribe` compares what returned against what it sent on 3-word shingles and returns an empty segment on an echo, which covers every caller including /judge; real speech reusing the jargon survives, because the hint is a comma list nobody speaks in. Do not let the judge fill a gap: `floorJudgeComment` only rejected empty, `(skipped)` and `(no transcript)`, all three of which the fabricated sentence cleared, so it now takes the same 30-word substance floor `generateRoundNotes` always had, is told never to invent an argument or praise delivery it cannot see, and has an explicit SKIP that renders and syncs nothing.
  - **The durable lesson, and it is not "models hallucinate".** A transcription prompt is an instruction to the decoder about what the audio probably contains, so on audio that contains nothing it is the ONLY signal left and the model returns it. Any pipeline that prompts a transcriber has this failure, and it presents as a plausible sentence rather than as an error. `scripts/test-transcribe-echo.mjs` pins the gate to the six verbatim hallucinations measured that night and runs in the pre-commit hook; four mutations were applied one at a time and every one was caught.

- **The sign-in link is mailed by us now, from a domain Gmail trusts** (2026-08-26, Aidan with a screenshot of the link sitting in Spam: "nothing is showing up in email... it went to spam, fix that"). Gmail's reason was "similar to messages that were identified as spam in the past" and it was a fair verdict. The link was sent by Firebase's own mailer: from `noreply@debateos-78ac5.firebaseapp.com`, a Google-owned sending domain shared with every Firebase project on earth, subject "Sign in to DebateOS" (a name that does not publicly exist), body the stock Identity Toolkit template millions of projects send unmodified, and the only link in it pointing at a `firebaseapp.com` host rather than the site the person was just on. **A sign-in link in Spam is not a slow sign-in, it is no sign-in**, and it was the front door for the one flow that has no password to fall back on.
  - **Everything the fix needed was already here, which is why it is small.** `lib/email.mjs` sends through Resend from `hello@itsdebatable.com`, a domain with real SPF and DKIM that every lifecycle email already ships from, and whose recent sends were measured landing in INBOX. `lib/auth-admin.mjs` already mints an Identity Toolkit token off the service account. So new `POST /api/signin-link` asks Firebase to GENERATE the code without sending it (`returnOobLink: true` on an admin-authenticated `sendOobCode`) and puts it in our own envelope: one link, no images, no tracking pixel, a text/plain part, plain words. Same code, same auth, our sender.
  - **The link is REHOSTED on our own domain, and that is half the win.** Firebase returns a `debateos-78ac5.firebaseapp.com/__/auth/action` URL; an email from itsdebatable.com whose only link is on someone else's host is a phishing shape to a filter and to a person. The JS SDK does not care about the host — it parses `mode`, `oobCode` and `apiKey` out of the query — so the link now goes out as `itsdebatable.com/<the page they were on>?mode=signIn&oobCode=…`, landing them straight back where they were with no redirect hop. **Verified against the real compat SDK in a browser before shipping** (`isSignInWithEmailLink` true, `credentialWithLink` returns `password/emailLink`), then end to end with a throwaway address: the link signs in, creates a REAL named account (provider `password`, so every `isNamedAccount()` gate passes), carries the display name, and strips its own parameters. The probe account was deleted after, so the named-account count in §8 stays honest.
  - **Firebase's sender stays as the FALLBACK on any failure that is not a rate limit.** Spam-foldered beats nothing, and this endpoint being down must never mean nobody can sign in. A 429 is OURS and deliberately does not fall through, or the cap means nothing.
  - **The bug that only appeared by driving the live form.** `/api/signin-link` was not in the `GATED` list in `js/app-check.js`, so no App Check token attached, the enforced endpoint returned 401, and the client did exactly what it is built to do on a failure: fell back to Firebase's mailer. **Sign-in still worked, through the wrong mailer, which is invisible from outside** — the network tab is the only place it showed. Reading the diff would never have caught it. Same standing lesson as the track.js coverage hole: a list that has to be kept in sync with another file drifts, and its failure mode here is a working fallback.
  - **The other half was that nobody knew to look in Spam.** Sending now opens a real screen instead of a green line under a form that still looks unfinished: where it went, three steps, and an amber "not there after 2 minutes? check spam" that names the sender and asks them to mark it Not spam so the next one lands. Gmail, Outlook, Yahoo and iCloud addresses get a one-tap button into the right mailbox; the Gmail one searches `in:anywhere`, which is what finds the message when the filter has already moved it. Resend sits on a 30s cooldown, because two links in six seconds means the first is dead and that is the one an impatient person clicks. The `/spar` gate's inline sender carries the same spam line, since it sends through the same `debatableSendSignInLink`.
  - **Guards, because this mails from our verified domain to any address given to it:** App Check, per-address limits (5/hour, 12/day) so it cannot become a way to mail-bomb a stranger and burn the domain reputation the whole change depends on, per-IP limits above that, and a strict origin allowlist on the continue URL — the oobCode rides in that URL, so an unchecked client-supplied host would be account takeover, not an open redirect.
  - **Rider: the Firebase project display name is now "Debatable", not "DebateOS".** `%APP_NAME%` feeds the password-reset and verify-email templates, which are still sent by Firebase's mailer and still carry the stock template. **Those two are the next deliverability problem** and the same technique fixes them (`generateEmailPasswordResetLink` / `generateEmailVerificationLink` through Resend); they were left alone here because the sign-in link is the one with no password to fall back on.

- **The chooser stopped offering a password to accounts that do not have one** (2026-08-26, found by Aidan hitting it on his own account: "it never asked me to make a password, sign up with google or gmail setup should work fine"). He clicked an emailed sign-in link, landed signed out, opened the chooser, and got a password form that answered "Email or password is incorrect." **His account is Google-only with no password hash** (checked against the live Auth record), so that form could never have worked, and the error was true and useless.

  **The default was backwards, and it was pointed at the biggest group on the site.** `renderChooser` led with the PASSWORD form for every returning visitor unless their last method was a link. 204 of 215 named accounts are Google. So the site's normal returning visitor was shown a field for a credential that does not exist on their account, directly under a line reading "Last time you signed in with Google." The default now inverts: **the emailed LINK leads, and the password form is the specialisation for the one group that provably has a password**, which is the group whose last sign-in on this device was one. A link works for every account there is; a password only works for an account that has one. Password stays one click away behind "Use a password instead."

  **Firebase cannot rescue this, and the reason is a project setting worth knowing before designing any auth copy: `emailPrivacyConfig.enableImprovedEmailPrivacy` is TRUE on debateos-78ac5.** Email-enumeration protection collapses wrong-password and no-such-account into a single `auth/invalid-credential`, and makes `fetchSignInMethodsForEmail` return an empty array for everyone. So there is no way to detect "this address is a Google account" and route accordingly, and there never will be while that setting is on. **The only move left is the copy**, which now names the two doors that can actually open ("If you made this account with Google, use Continue with Google above, or email yourself a sign-in link") instead of the one that cannot. Naming them conditionally leaks nothing, which is what keeps it compatible with the setting.

  **The link itself failed silently, and the cause was a native `window.prompt`.** A link opened in a different browser to the one that requested it has no stashed address, and Firebase requires the address restated (that is what stops a forwarded link signing in whoever finds it). That question was asked with `window.prompt` during page load: a browser may suppress it, it is trivially dismissed, and dismissing returned an empty string that the code treated as "give up" with no error, no card and no trace. The visitor is left on a signed-out page holding a link that visibly did nothing. It is now an in-modal card ("Finish signing in") where the question is visible, a typo is retryable in place, and the other doors sit one click away.

  **Rider caught while fixing it: the spent link parameters were left on the URL.** `emailLinkSettings()` builds its continue URL from `destination()`, so a visitor who gave up on a dead link and sent themselves a fresh one would have got a link whose continue URL still carried the dead `oobCode`, and the return trip would arrive holding two codes with no way to tell which was live. They are stripped now on both the ask and the failure path.

  **MEASURED, so nobody "fixes" it later: a failed link attempt does NOT consume the one-time code.** The recovery path re-derives from the same href after `linkWithCredential` fails with `credential-already-in-use`, which looks like the bug the Google path has a long comment about. It is not. Probed against the live Identity Toolkit API on a throwaway account: the link attempt returns EMAIL_EXISTS, and the SAME code then signs in fine (200). Only a SUCCESSFUL use spends it. The Google path needs `err.credential` because a second popup has no user gesture, which is a different failure for a different reason; do not copy that shape here.

  **Apple stays off web** (see the entry above), so Google, an emailed link, and a password for those who set one are the three doors, in that order of prominence.

- **A message you send appears without a reload, and the three DM copies become one engine** (2026-08-26, Aidan, off a screenshot of the /spar thread modal: "the message system isn't built well... i sent a message for example here and it doesn't show back unless i refresh the page"). He was right and the cause is one line of Firestore semantics: **an `onSnapshot` is dead once it errors. It does not retry, ever.** The messages read rule resolves the parent thread doc (`get(/dm_threads/$(threadId)).data.participants`), and a thread whose doc does not exist yet cannot resolve it, so the listen denies. That is the normal state of **every brand-new conversation**. All three surfaces caught that denial, painted "No messages yet. Send one to start the thread." and left the listener in the ground. The person then sent the first message, the write succeeded, and nothing was listening: their own words did not appear until they reloaded. The same shape covered a peer who sent first, a network blip, and a quota trip. **The comments in all three files described the denial accurately and treated it as a state to render rather than a listener to bring back**, which is why it survived so long: the code looked considered, and it was, one step short.
  - **`app/js/dm-core.js` is now THE engine and there must not be a fourth copy.** /spar, /messages and /chat had each grown their own ~200 lines with three different sets of error strings, so the bug existed three times and had to be fixed three times. Pages keep their own markup and get data plus state from the engine; adding a messaging surface means calling `DBDM.open`, not porting a listener.
  - **A dead listener is re-attached, not reported.** Retry cadence by failure class (a denial on a thread nobody has written to is cheap and slow-cadenced; transient codes back off; quota waits a minute), plus an immediate re-attach on the send that creates the thread, which is the exact moment reads become permitted, and on tab-visible and network-online.
  - **A sent message is on screen the instant it is sent.** Optimistic echo keyed on a **pre-generated** doc id, so the server copy replaces the local one exactly instead of the text-and-timestamp guess that double-prints. A message the reader cannot read back — quota, denial — is still their message and still shows, which is what the old "sending still works, reading returns after midnight" copy promised and never delivered.
  - **A failed send stays visible and retryable.** All three surfaces cleared the composer on send and printed a line of error text, so a failure meant the words were simply gone. The bubble is now the retry button.
  - Riders: the feed only follows new messages down when the reader is already at the bottom (slamming `scrollTop` on every repaint yanked the thread out from under anyone reading back); `markRead` no longer calls `update()` on a thread doc that does not exist yet; and the read-receipt listener gets the same retry, since it died on precisely the same denial and nobody had noticed because a missing receipt looks like a peer who has not read it.
  - **Verified in a browser against the live project on all three surfaces**, not by reading the diff: a brand-new thread, first send on screen at t=0 as pending and confirmed by the re-attached listener ~150ms later, exactly one bubble; an injected failure rendering a retryable bubble that sends cleanly on tap with no duplicate; a message written over the wire arriving live. Test threads deleted from Firestore afterwards.

- **A 45-second account wall, and Apple sign-in goes back to being the app's button** (2026-08-26, Aidan: "require google sign in after 45 seconds on the site actually or something i need to convert the anonymous", and separately "dont allow apple sign in, bc i cant email ppl for apple, only allow apple for the APP"). Two coupled changes to how a stranger meets the account.

  **The wall.** `js/signin-wall.js` counts 45 VISIBLE seconds, cumulative across pages inside one browser session, and then opens the shared chooser with no way past it: no ×, Escape does nothing, a backdrop click does nothing, the page behind stops scrolling. It is a wall, which is what was asked for, and the deliberate part is WHERE it sits. The 2026-04-20 reversal is still right that a full-screen gate on arrival costs more than it earns, and its failure mode (Safari ITP, in-app browsers, blocked Firebase) is real; this arrives after someone has actually read the site, which is the same shape as the 2026-08-19 free-round wall arriving after a complete round rather than before one.

  **Visible seconds, not wall clock, and that is what makes it safe.** A backgrounded tab accumulates nothing, so a parked or prerendered tab is never walled, and no crawler accumulates 45 seconds of visible time; `navigator.webdriver` and a UA test veto on top of that. The budget is time on the SITE, so three pages at fifteen seconds each is walled on the third. Fifteen wasted seconds on a page nobody read is not what is being metered.

  **It IS the shared chooser, opened locked.** `openAuthModal(mode, {locked, headline, sub})` gained a lock; `close()` refuses while locked so every dismissal path (×, Escape, backdrop) is refused in ONE place rather than three guards that drift, and `forceClose()` is the internal path a completed sign-in uses. Building a second card would have meant a second copy of the anonymous-account linking dance, the in-app-browser warning, the emailed-link round trip and the error copy: that is the duplication the founder has cut twice, and the 2026-08-12 "one chooser" decision says there is one.

  **Exempt, each for a stated reason:** anyone with a NAMED account (an anonymous Firebase uid is not a signed-in user, and `js/notifications.js` mints one on nearly every page, so this is the trap the whole gate turns on — verified live that the wall still fires with an anonymous user present); the native app; automation; a live round riding along in the picture-in-picture site shell; and the paths in `SKIP_PATHS` — anything with a round running in it (`/practice`, `/live-round`, voice), anything that owns its own gate (`/spar`, `/partners`, `/debate-chat`), the LEGAL pages, because someone reading the privacy policy is deciding whether to trust us with an account and blocking that answer behind the account is backwards, and admin. `topbar.js` injects it, which covers 111 pages; the six standalone content pages with no topbar carry the tag by hand, per the standing rule that a shared script which looks global is not coverage.

  **The soft nudge stands down.** `signup-nudge.js` no longer mounts its card when the wall is armed. A dismissible "not now" at 7 seconds followed by an undismissable ask at 45 spends the one ask this visitor will read and then contradicts it. One Tap is untouched: it is browser chrome, not a second card, and anyone it converts never meets the wall.

  **Watch `signin_wall_shown` against `signin_wall_converted`.** If shown is large and converted is small, the wall is costing readers and buying nothing, and the answer is a better wall or a later one, not a wider exemption. `WALL_SECONDS` is the dial, `?wall=<n>` retimes it for QA (bounded, and it can only make the wall come sooner), `?wall=off` and `localStorage['debatable-signin-wall']='off'` are the per-browser kill, `DISABLED` is the switch.

  **SAME-DAY FOLLOW-UP, and it resolves the collision flagged below plus a bigger one nobody had raised.** Aidan, after reading the collision note: "do what you think is smartest here, I want SEO / sign ups to really work so I can get ppl coming back." Two changes, and the second matters more than the wall itself.

  **(1) The arrival page is priced differently, which replaces the per-path exemption.** A blanket `/debate-online` skip would have fixed one page and left every other search entry exposed, including the NSDA topics cluster that is supposed to climb this month. So the rule is about the SESSION, not the URL: the page a visitor ARRIVES on costs 90 seconds AND one real interaction; every page after it costs the 45 that was asked for, because clicking through to a second page IS the interaction. **Scroll is deliberately not an interaction** — on a phone, scrolling is how you find out whether a page is worth reading, so it proves arrival rather than interest; a tap or a keypress is a choice. What this buys: a reader who came from a search result and would have bounced back to the results never sees a wall, and pogo-sticking back to Google is the one visitor behaviour that costs the ranking that delivered them. A deep reader on a single page still converts. Verified in a browser in all three states (arrival with no tap past budget: no wall; same page after one tap: wall; second page with no tap at all: wall).

  **(2) A conversion now hands the page back with the first round one tap away, because AN ACCOUNT IS NOT THE WIN.** 31 of 215 named accounts have ever finished a round; 184 have not. A wall that mints accounts which never debate just manufactures more of the 184, and nobody comes back to an account that did nothing, so "more signups" and "people coming back" are not the same request and the first does not produce the second. Signing in through the wall used to land someone back on the marketing page they were reading, which is precisely how a dead account gets made. It does NOT redirect them into a round instead: the wall already interrupted their reading once, and taking the page away as the reward for complying would be the second interruption, on a visitor who came from a search result for a reason. They get their page back, and a bottom bar offers the round (`wall_activation_shown` / `_click` / `_dismiss`, one session, dismissible). **The number to read is not `signin_wall_converted`. It is whether accounts created at the wall ever complete a round**, which is a Firestore question and the only one that separates this from account theatre.

  **A note on the SEO half, since that was half the ask: there was no SEO work to do here.** The topics cluster was refreshed for 2026-27 on 08-22, the definitional gaps got their own pages the same evening, internal links were redistributed on 08-24 (`/debate-an-ai` 13 to 115 inbound), and a crawl of all 214 sitemap URLs found the technical base clean. The diagnosis on record is POSITION on the category vocabulary, and the fix already shipped needs weeks, not another pass. **Re-measure late September; if `/debate-an-ai` has not moved off page 6 the constraint is off-page authority and the answer is backlinks.** Doing more on-page work now would be redoing 08-22 and 08-24.

  **KNOWN COLLISION, flagged rather than quietly resolved: `/debate-online` is walled, and it takes ~68% of organic clicks.** The 2026-08-19 guest-rounds decision gives a stranger two live rounds before asking for an account, precisely because the queries behind that page ("debate omegle", "debate strangers") bring people who want a stranger and a motion now. `/spar` itself is exempt, so the two free rounds still work for anyone who clicks through — but a reader who spends 45 seconds on `/debate-online` first now meets the wall before they ever reach the queue. Adding that path to `SKIP_PATHS` is one line if the organic funnel takes a visible hit.

  **Apple, and this REVERSES the 2026-08-18 decision to offer it on web.** Not for a plumbing reason: the Services ID, the signing key and the Firebase provider config all still work and are left in place. Hide My Email is the reason. Apple hands most accounts a `@privaterelay.appleid.com` address, and Apple only forwards relay mail from a sender registered in its own Sign in with Apple configuration, which ours is not. So every lifecycle email this product runs (the digest, winback, the first-round note, spar night, the Open announcement) bounces off a web Apple signup, on the ONE surface whose entire purpose is that an account lets us come back to someone. Apple stays in the iOS app, where the App Store's login-choice rule binds the binary and requires it beside Google. Restoring it on web is one condition plus registering the sender with Apple so relay mail is deliverable, in that order. Riders: the in-app-browser warning no longer names Apple on web, and a returning device whose last method was Apple no longer reads "Last time you signed in with Apple" on a page with no Apple button, which named a door that was not there.

- **The live call's stutter was the safety watchdog, and it moved to a Worker without giving up an inch of safety** (2026-08-26, Aidan: the round is "really laggy", asked to chase the real cause rather than keep guessing). Measured against the real vendored model on an M3 Pro, which is a FASTER machine than most of the audience runs: `nsfw-guard.js` classifying in-page blocked the MAIN THREAD for a median of **39ms and up to 80ms** per sample in a tight loop, and at its real 1.6s cadence it produced long tasks of **142, 50, 189, 81 and 335ms in twelve seconds**. A frame budget is 16.7ms. So the page froze for two to twenty frames, on a fixed rhythm, for the entire round, on the same thread that composites the video. Nothing reported it because the code is correct and the feature is silent by design.
  - **The fix is a relocation, not a trade-off.** The same weights, the same webgl backend, the same 1.6s cadence, the same thresholds, the same two-consecutive-samples rule and the same evidence-only posture from 2026-08-18, all inside `js/nsfw-worker.js`. Main-thread cost per sample goes from 39ms to **0.1ms** (1.2ms worst) and the long tasks go to **zero**, verified end to end in a browser: 8 frames handed over at the right cadence, 7 real classifications returned, no errors. **Do not "simplify" this back onto the main thread.** The reason it is a worker is a measurement, and the measurement is in this entry.
  - **Frames are resized during decode and TRANSFERRED, never copied.** `createImageBitmap(video, {resizeWidth:224, resizeHeight:224})` replaces the old canvas draw entirely, so the main thread stops touching pixels at all, and the bitmap is transferred rather than structured-cloned. The worker closes every bitmap on every path out, because an ImageBitmap holds real GPU memory and one leaked per 1.6s is a leak that runs for the length of a round.
  - **The in-page path is KEPT as the fallback and is still correct**, because a browser without Worker or createImageBitmap still has a camera and still needs watching; a guard that runs nowhere is worse than one that stutters. It is exercised by forcing `Worker` undefined, and that test is also what produced the in-page long-task numbers above, which makes them a clean A/B on one machine rather than two measurements of two things. Only ONE path ever loads, because each pulls ~5MB of model. `stop()` now terminates the worker: a round that ended without it left a webgl context and those weights alive for the rest of the session.
  - **The board composite was measured in the same pass and exonerated:** 7.4ms per frame at 1280x720 with two camera panels, about 18% of a core at its 24fps cap, steady and under budget, and only ever on the recording driver. It is not the stutter, so it was left alone.
  - **The standing lesson: this was found by measuring, and every previous guess was wrong.** The stale local checkout showed an uncapped rAF on the board that `origin/main` had already fixed, and the first two attempts to time the classifier returned numbers that were pure artifacts of the preview pane (every sample landing at exactly 1000.0ms, which is the hidden-tab timer clamp on TF.js's async GPU readback, not compute). **A suspiciously round number is a measurement of your instrument.** Long-task entries were what finally measured the real thing, because they record main-thread blocking regardless of tab visibility.

- **A dead microphone is no longer a speech that never happened: the idle side transcribes the room's audio** (2026-08-26, Aidan: the transcript glitches and the live call is laggy). Every engine on `/live-round` transcribed the speaker's OWN mic on the speaker's OWN machine, with no cross-side redundancy anywhere, and the whole chain can fail on precisely the device that matters: Web Speech is absent in Firefox, off in Brave, unreliable on iOS, and **this page contends for the microphone by design** (the camera pipeline holds a capture, Daily publishes it, and Chrome's recogniser wants its own). The failure is total, and the judge reads the transcript and nothing else, so a mic that dies is a speech the ballot never sees. The fallback chain that already existed only ever swapped one engine on the failing machine for another on the same failing machine.
  - **The audio was never lost. It is in the room, and the opponent is already receiving it.** So when the speaker's published transcript stops growing while their clock runs, the IDLE side records the speaker's own Daily audio track in 9s segments, posts them to `/api/transcribe`, and publishes the result back on the round doc as `backupTranscript`; the speaker's client adopts it into the textarea, where it is visible, editable, and committed by `endSpeech` like any other text. Nothing downstream learns a new shape.
  - **It costs nothing in the healthy case,** which is the property that makes it shippable: it arms only 12s into a speech at the earliest and after 14s of no growth, and stands down the moment the primary gets ahead of it again. ONE client runs it, elected by the same idle-lowest-uid rule the fact checker uses. Only a **seated** debater can be elected, because `firestore.rules` lets seats write the round doc and a 2v2 partner is not one, so an unseated driver would spend real money and have every publish silently rejected. Spectators never capture. The MediaStream WRAPS a room track, so release drops the reference and never calls `stop()` — the `releaseBoardVids` rule, and for the same reason: stopping it would cut the speaker's audio to everyone.
  - **Adoption is deliberately narrow.** It takes over only when the local box has produced essentially nothing, because a box that is filling is a mic that works and overwriting it would be the exact duplication glitch the mic's full-rewrite rule already exists to prevent. Once adopted the local engine is MUTED for that speech rather than merged with, so a mic that revives cannot append a second copy of words the backup already holds. Typing takes the box back.
  - **The subtle one, and the reason this entry names it: the speaker adopts our text and republishes it, so our own output comes back around looking like the primary recovering.** An echo is not growth. The prefix test runs in BOTH directions, because the republish lags our segments and what returns is usually a SHORTER prefix of what we hold. **A mutation proved the first version wrong: the one-directional check passed its own test with the check deleted.** A suite that goes green on the first run has not yet been shown to test anything, and this is the second entry in this log to record that same lesson landing.
  - **The lag half was the same document, from the other end.** The round doc's snapshot listener fires on every write, the live transcript republished every 750ms, and three unconditional repaints were therefore running ~60 times a minute on both debaters and every spectator to rewrite identical strings into identical nodes — two of them through `updateRoomStage()`, repainting both nameplates and both avatars each time. Each is now gated on its own inputs having actually changed, and every one has other call sites for the transitions that genuinely need a repaint. The publish moved 750ms to 1200ms: it reads the same to someone following the captions and cuts the write, the listener wake and the render pass by a third. `endSpeech` commits the textarea, not the debounce, so the tail of a speech still lands. **The board's own 24fps cap was already on main — the stale local checkout showed an uncapped rAF, which is the deploy-topology trap doing exactly what AGENTS.md says it does.**

- **The research corpus was empty, the benchmark could never have used it honestly, and the privacy rights were kept by hand** (2026-08-25, Aidan: "collect data as benchmarks from round transcripts / analyzed data / privacy rights, need to incorporate better"). Measured live before anything moved: 246 generations, **189 of them carrying a full transcript** (121 voice, 47 live), 123 consent events, and **`contributable` = 0**. The transcripts were never missing. The corpus opt-in had been asked ONCE, ever.
  - **A gate that cannot be reached is the same as a gate that is closed.** `corpus-nudge.js` fires after 3 rating signals, and the whole database holds 4 rating signals across its entire life, so the arithmetic made the ask almost impossible to trigger. Rating is a thing almost nobody does; finishing a round is the thing everybody who matters does. The trigger counts COMPLETED rounds now, asks on the second, 4s after the ballot renders so a consent modal never covers the verdict they stayed for. **The general lesson, and it is the third of this shape in the log: a feature can be fully built, correct in every line, and still never have executed. Check the counter before rewriting the mechanism.**
  - **`isSignedIn()` was `!!currentUser` again.** notifications.js signs nearly every visitor in anonymously, so the ask would have gone to people with no account at all, whose rounds §7 excludes from licensing outright. An anonymous yes contributes nothing AND burns the sticky dismissal that should have caught them after signing up. Named accounts only. This is the same trap as the 2026-08-18 spar gate and the 2026-07-28 rate-limit correction; on this site `!!currentUser` means "has a browser".
  - **The benchmark finding is the one worth keeping. Every verdict in our corpus was written by our own judge.** Scoring the judge for accuracy against those labels measures its agreement with itself and returns a high number while doing it, which makes it the most flattering mistake available anywhere in this system. So provenance is structural rather than a convention: `lib/eval-corpus.mjs` stamps `labelSource` on every row, `ACCURACY_GOLD_SOURCES` holds exactly two values (a human chair, a human appeal reviewer), and `assertAccuracyLabelAllowed` **throws** instead of returning false, because a builder can ignore a boolean and the gold file it then writes looks exactly like a real one. Crowd votes are deliberately NOT accuracy gold: a room of spectators is a real human signal and a noisy non-expert one, and promoting it quietly would import popularity into a number we would publish as adjudication accuracy. **Accuracy stays on the external tournament fixtures where a human chair announced the call on the record.**
  - **What the current unsplit production corpus is for, and it is not a consolation prize:** repeat stability (does the same round get the same verdict twice) and speaker-point calibration. Position and verbosity bias still run only on external fixtures with a trustworthy bench split. None of those measurements treat an AI verdict as ground truth, and they are the evals that actually protect the ladder, because noise averages out over many rounds while systematic bias compounds into a rating. The fixture builder **refuses to invent a bench split**: most of the corpus is voice mode, which is a conversation with no speech structure, and a guessed split moves text between benches so the bias number computed from it would be wrong in a way nothing downstream could detect. Production rounds ship unsplit, marked as supporting the repeat condition only, and `run-stability-eval.mjs --corpus` now consumes that manifest directly. Guarded by `scripts/test-eval-corpus.mjs` and `scripts/test-stability.mjs` in the pre-commit hook.
  - **A right that costs a favour is not much of a right.** §10 promised an export "by emailing us" and §7 promised per-round corpus withdrawal the same way. Both were real commitments honoured by one person reading mail, which makes them slow, makes them depend on describing a round well enough to be found, and quietly limits them to people willing to email a stranger about their data. `GET /api/my-data-export` now returns the caller's profile, rounds, transcripts, leaderboard entries and **the full consent ledger** (every grant and withdrawal, with the surface and the policy version in force at the time), and `POST /api/corpus-withdraw` pulls one round or all of them. **There is no id parameter anywhere in the export**, on purpose: every read is keyed on the verified uid, and a parameter is a thing someone can change. A round that is not yours answers exactly like a round that does not exist, so nobody can probe whether an id is real. DMs are excluded because a thread has another author who did not ask to be exported. The withdrawal endpoint says what it did and does not overstate it: removal from future shipments is something code can do, the best-effort recall of copies already sent is still a conversation.
  - **`CONSENT_POLICY_VERSION` was deliberately NOT bumped.** It records what consent MEANT when someone gave it. Nothing here changed that; only the mechanism for exercising a right got easier. Bumping it would tell the ledger a consent boundary moved when none did.
  - **Watch `contributable`.** It is 0 today and this is all plumbing ahead of the data, which the exporter says out loud when it runs. If the ballot-time ask works the number climbs within days. If it does not, the next lever is the ask itself, the copy and the placement, not the trigger again.

- **The rating ladder was empty, and the 1-100 board was the symptom** (2026-08-25, Aidan: "make our ELO system in the thousands numbers not 1-100 average. a real ranking ELO system!"). The thousands-scale system he was asking for already existed and had been shipped for months: Glicko-2 in `lib/rating.mjs`, 1500 start, RD and volatility, `/api/leaderboard-ratings` serving it, and both the landing rail and `/leaderboard` already written to render a rating and caption it "Head-to-head rating, wins move it". **None of it had ever run.** Live Firestore held ONE `user_ratings` document, at `games: 0`, while 26 completed human-vs-human rounds carrying real judged ballots sat unrated. With no rated rows to serve, `leaderboard-top` fell through to its designed fallback, `leaderboard_entries` speaker points, which is the 84 / 78 / 72 out of 100 on the screenshot. The board was not using the wrong scale. It was showing the only thing it had.
  - **The gate had already been opened and nobody walked through.** The 2026-08-24 consent flip (opt-in to opt-out) was the right fix and it only applies going FORWARD, so every round already played stayed ineligible and the ladder stayed at zero. A fix to an eligibility rule is not a fix to the history that rule excluded; the backfill is a separate act and it had not happened. 26 rounds applied oldest-first, because Glicko-2 is path dependent and rating a May round after a July one produces a different and wrong ladder. Result: 31 debaters between 1216 and 1776. Written with the deterministic `rating_changes` ids the transaction uses, so the sweep and the admin backfill both read them as already applied; re-running is a no-op, verified.
  - **Simulating the backfill before writing it is what found the real defect, and it would have shipped invisibly.** Ordered by raw rating, the top four rows were all debaters at 1-0 with RD around 286, sitting above the only two people on the site with an actual record (3-1 and 3-2). That is exactly the "ladder topped by someone who won one round" that `isRankable` already exists to refuse, relocated one row down where nothing was checking, and it mattered because **the landing rail prints a rank number on every row it renders regardless of `rankable`**. So the fallback presentation would have crowned four people who had won a single round each. The provisional tail now sorts on Glickman's conservative estimate, `rating - 2*RD`, so being unmeasured costs position instead of buying it. The rating is still the number shown; this is only the sort. Live after deploy: the 3-1 and the 3-2 lead the board, the 1-0s follow.
  - **A promise the code had never kept.** `/leaderboard` told people in five places that a rating "settles after 3 rated head-to-head rounds". `PROVISIONAL_RD` is 110, which is the Lichess number and assumes Lichess volume; measured against the real ladder it takes about ELEVEN rounds. I raised the line to 225 so the sentence would be true, and `scripts/test-record-seed.mjs` blocked the commit, correctly. **That guard is the reason to write this down.** It asserts `SEED_MIN_RD > PROVISIONAL_RD * 2`, which pins the line at 119 or below, and the margin is an anti-forgery property rather than a round number: a `/claim` seed built from a self-reported Tabroom record sits at an RD floor of 240, so at a 225 line a claimed history would have dropped under the rankable bar after roughly two real rounds and bought a ranked place that was never earned here. The threshold stayed at 110 and the COPY was fixed instead, which was both the cheaper and the more honest half. The coupling is now commented where the constant lives and pinned from both test suites, so the next person who tries this sees why before the hook tells them.
  - **Two guards were already failing on main before any of this.** Three `test-rating` assertions still pinned the dual opt-in consent rule the 2026-08-24 flip had removed, so the suite was asserting a privacy model the code had stopped implementing, and `leaderboard-ratings.mjs` carried the same stale claim in its header, on the endpoint whose whole job is publishing names. **A rule change is not shipped until the tests that assert the old rule and the comments that describe it have moved too**, and a red guard on main is worse than no guard, because the next person reads the failure as noise.
  - **Standing note: everyone on the ladder is provisional today and that is correct, not a bug.** 31 debaters, 0 rankable, because nobody has played enough rated rounds to narrow the interval. The board shows real ratings and withholds numbered rank, which is the honest state at this volume. It fills as rounds are played; do not loosen `PROVISIONAL_RD` to make it look fuller without reading the seed-floor coupling above.

- **/bounties becomes "call for a debate", and the form stops asking for things nobody has decided yet** (2026-08-25, Aidan: "fix how the bounties page works and is explained. should be simple 'call for a debate' button or something"). The page led with **"Pay to make a debate happen"** over a button reading **"Post a bounty"**, which put the hardest and least accurate idea first. Posting is FREE: `postSave` creates the call and only then opens the funding modal, and a call can sit at zero indefinitely while other people back it. So the headline was charging people at the door for something free, and "bounty" is a word you have to be taught before the sentence parses. Now the headline is "Call for the debate you want to see", the button says "Call for a debate", and the first rule card is **what it costs to ask: nothing**. **The money mechanics are untouched** — same pot, same even split to the two debaters, same no-cut, same refund at the deadline, same 18+ attestation, `BOUNTY_PAYMENTS_LIVE` still true and this is still a real-money surface. They just moved below the fold, where somebody who has decided to ask will meet them. **The form was the other half.** It opened with four controls (motion, format, deadline, note) before the two questions that actually matter, so it read as paperwork. Format, deadline and the note are now folded into "More options" with their defaults stated on the summary (Quick Clash, 30 days, no note) so nobody opens it to find out what they get; the ids and the submit path are byte-identical. **And a real bug surfaced while trimming it: `#targetFields` carries `hidden`, and `.fld2{display:grid}` beat it,** because an author `display` always outranks the UA sheet's `[hidden]`. So "First person / Second person" has rendered since the page shipped even with "Anyone" selected — the form has been asking for names it did not want, and it only looked like part of a long form because four other controls sat above it. One `.fld2[hidden]{display:none}` closes it. Standing note for this page: it is a surface where a stranger is asked for money, so every word between the headline and the pay button should be the smallest true version of itself.

- **Community is the flagship tile in its Explore column** (2026-08-25, Aidan: "this button should be bigger and highlighted better bc community matters in building chats"). Promoted from `strong` (tier 2) to `big`, the bordered, column-tinted tile with the 42px icon. "Watch & community" had no `big` row at all, so its most important destination rendered at the same weight as everything under it. Its description gained the reason to press it ("Find people, clubs, and someone to argue with"). Demoting is deleting `big` and restoring `strong`.

- **The Open runs in ONE session, 10:00 AM ET, and this supersedes "drop in whenever"** (2026-08-25, Aidan: "we're doing one time choose it and be strict and clear with messaging"). **This reverses the 2026-08-11 "all day, drop in whenever, no fixed start" entry below**, and it reverses it on that entry's own reasoning rather than against it. That entry argued a fixed start on a small base means everyone has to be free at the same hour while an all-day pool only needs two people awake at once. The second half is the part that did not survive contact: a pool only works if arrivals overlap, and eighteen entrants spread across twelve hours is exactly the /spar liquidity failure it was written to avoid, wearing generosity as a costume. Three published sessions made it worse by splitting the field three ways and naming each split a plan.
  - **10:00 AM ET was picked off the entry list, not off a map.** The eighteen entries are weighted to South Asia (7:30 PM local), with Africa and Europe at 3 PM and a workable Saturday morning in the US; nobody in the field is in Australia. It is the only hour that is not the middle of somebody's night for most of who actually entered. Doors and first pairings 10:00, prelims to 12:30 ET, the board breaks, final streamed at about 1:15 ET, which also lands the finish before 11 PM in India.
  - **Strict is a rule now, not encouragement.** There is no second session and no make-up round; a pairing an entrant does not appear for is a forfeit. `/tournament-rules` section 2 carries that, because the old text said sessions were "a convenience, not a requirement" and that is the document a dispute is argued from. What is deliberately NOT promised is a registration cutoff at a named minute: entries stay open while status is `registration` or `running` (the 2026-08-18 both-states-at-once fix), and the only way to close them is to move to `break`, which also ends prelims. Promising a close time the state machine cannot enforce would be the same class of error as the $20 door the endpoint refused.
  - **The site was telling FOUR different time stories at once, and that is why this was a sweep and not a copy edit.** /tournaments, /tournament-rules and /open published 7 AM, 3 PM and 8 PM ET sessions; the sitewide `topbar.js` modal published rush hours at 12, 3 and 6 PM; `admin-open-rally` published the same 12/3/6; `admin-open-timeslot` asked entrants to reply with one of those three while its own `DOORS_OPEN_MS` guard was written against 10 AM. No visitor reading two of those surfaces could say when the tournament was. **Standing rule: the hour lives in the datebar, the logistics card, the rules page, the sitewide strip and modal, and both entrant emails, and it moves in all of them in the same commit.**
  - **`admin-open-timeslot` changed job rather than dying.** "Which of these three hours suits you" has no answer under one session, so it now tells entrants the day collapsed to one hour and asks them to reply YES or NO. That is the number worth holding before Saturday: how many of the field are actually turning up at 10.
  - **The live doc was patched in the same pass**, since /tournaments hydrates its kicker, enter card and meta line from it: `startsAtISO` 11:00Z to 14:00Z, `startsAt` to "Sat Aug 29 &middot; one session, 10:00 AM ET", description rewritten. A page that says one thing while its own data says another is the 2026-08-10 stale-door failure with the pieces swapped. The pot is untouched.
  - **The mechanical question the 2026-08-11 entry flagged is now easier, not harder.** It noted `lib/tournament.mjs` pairs globally per ROUND over a ranked field, which assumes a synchronous structure the drop-in copy had walked away from. One session walks back toward it: a field that is all present at 10:00 is closer to the shape the pairing engine was actually written for.

- **/predict's first screen is one sentence and three steps** (2026-08-25, third pass the same day, Aidan: "we srsly need this to be dumbed the hell down on the top line so its simple - more derails are extrapolated below. make less dense and overwhelming"). The hero had been accumulating rules: a three-clause lede carrying the ballot, the payout rule AND the crowd rule, a two-line fine print, and beside it a card holding the entire rule set as three dense bullets plus a footer paragraph. Every sentence was true and the top of the page had become something to study rather than something to understand. Now: headline, ONE sentence ("Two people argue. You pick who wins before the round ends. Call it right and you take points off the people who called it wrong."), two buttons, five words of fine print, and a three-step card of four words each. **Nothing was deleted, only moved down** — "How betting works" already stated who may bet, when it locks and what settles it, in more detail and with a timeline and a live worked example under it, so the hero card was a duplicate of a section one screen below. Standing rule for this slot: if a sentence goes back into the hero, one comes out. The idea band's headline was flattened in the same pass ("You already pick a winner. This is where you say it out loud."), because it is the next thing a visitor reads and it was doing the same clever-and-dense thing.

- **The leaderboard rail wears the supplied photos, and that is a standing instruction** (2026-08-25, Aidan: "bring back the real photos - never reverse this", then "push out a bit more too and increase sizes ... needs to LOOK like a leaderboard peeking out at the side"). **This REVERSES the 2026-08-24 redraw below, on his explicit and repeated direction, and the words "never reverse this" are the reason it is written this prominently.** The batch he uploaded on 2026-08-24 is phone screenshots of profile pictures from another app. It was refused twice and replaced with a drawn set, first twelve tiles then forty-nine; what he saw on the live page was a column of clip art (a frog, a money bag, a crown) standing in for a board of people, and the register was wrong in a way no amount of redrawing fixes. Ten of the eleven are now cropped square from the circle in each screenshot and live at `app/img/pfp/*`, and the first-screen rail draws them and nothing else. **The reasoning that was used to refuse it is still true and is now his to carry:** the batch contains identifiable adults, a film still, a fast-food founder's likeness and two cartoon characters, so this is a real if small copyright and likeness exposure on a commercial page. It is his site and his call, he has made it twice, and the argument does not get to be made a third time. **Three limits are NOT his to waive, because they are about other people, and they are enforced in code rather than in prose.** (1) The eleventh image is a photograph of a CHILD and is not in the repository; the crop script names it and skips it, and if the batch is ever re-cut it stays out. (2) The photos are the STAND-IN tier only and are never offered in the avatar picker, so no account can come to be identified by a stranger's photograph. `DBPfp.list` stays the drawn set (which is what the picker renders) and the new `DBPfp.canWear` returns false for every photo id; `avatar.js`'s identity sanitiser was moved off `has()` onto it, which closes a hole the change would otherwise have opened, since a hand-posted `{kind:'pfp', id:'pic-cash'}` would have put a stranger's face on somebody's account. (3) The two truer tiers still outrank them everywhere, so a real account shows its real face; the stand-in `<img>` needed an explicit `z-index:0` to stay behind an account's photo, because it used to be an `<svg>` and the rule that lifted the photo above it keys on `img`. **The ROUNDS board is a different surface and is untouched:** it still runs consented stills only, because those seats carry a name and a result and a rail tile carries neither. **Same pass, the shape he asked for.** Tiles 54px to 68px, and the peek (how far the rail sits past the right edge) 12px to -8px, so every tile is now whole with a margin of page outside it, which is the difference between a clipped column and a board that peeks. The first screen pays for the room it takes: `html.lbr-ready .fscreen-wrap` slides further left and scales to .95 about its centre, which hands back another ~2.5% of the wrap width on the right. A scale rather than a narrower max-width, because a cap change reflows the headline and the board into different line breaks while a scale is the same composition 5% smaller, which is what "like 5% smaller" asks for. Measured at 1120px, the narrowest width the rail renders at: 50px of clearance between the wrap and the rail, zero horizontal overflow, every tile whole and every photo loaded, in both themes.

- **/predict gets the idea, the picture, and a worked example you can move** (2026-08-25, same day as the fake-board removal below, Aidan: "need more visual guidance, examples, the idea behind it more"). Deleting the house-minted markets left a page that stated its rules correctly and never said what the thing was FOR, which is the question somebody arriving from the Bet tab is actually asking. Four changes. **(1) A "why this exists" band above everything.** Three beats, no numbers, about the reader rather than the product: you already form a verdict around the second speech and it evaporates, so commit before the ballot and let the ballot answer you; the debaters get a ballot and this is yours; cash would change what you are doing, so points cannot be bought or cashed and the house takes nothing from any pool. **(2) A round timeline, because the rule people misread most is a POSITION, not a clock.** "Betting locks at the middle speech" sounds like a countdown; a speech order shows it. Pure CSS grid, a `<figure>` with a real caption so a screen reader gets the same sentence the picture makes. **(3) The explainer is open again, which REVERSES the 2026-08-22 collapse, and for the same reason that collapse was right.** That entry hid Three steps, the payout curve and the worked example behind a disclosure so the board could be first; the board it was protecting was furniture, and with the fake inventory gone the explanation IS the page. You cannot give visual guidance from inside a closed `<details>`. If a real board ever gets busy enough to be why people come, collapse it again rather than deleting any of it. **(4) The worked example became interactive, and one formula now drives three pictures.** It used to be four static tiles reading 33% / 3.00x / 50 pts / 150 pts, which states an answer and teaches nothing. Now two sliders (share of the pool on your side, what you put in) drive an example market card, the dot on the payout curve, and the money flow, all off `return = stake ÷ your side's share of the pool` — so the three can no longer drift the way three hardcoded copies of "3.00x" did. The curve's y axis was relabelled from absolute points to MULTIPLES, which is what makes it stake-independent and let the dot move; the slider is bounded 20-80% to the curve's own drawn domain, because a dot outside the plotted line is a chart lying about itself. **The example card is deliberately arithmetic and not activity** — no pool totals, no prediction counts, no debater names, dashed border, "Example card" tag — because an example that mimics real data is exactly what got the old board deleted, and the "248 people voted" card was labelled *example* too. It doubles as the card-reading lesson, so the first live market someone meets is already a familiar shape. **Rider, and it was live-wrong the moment the AI board went:** three places still told people "each side opens with 100 house points, so a fresh market starts even at 2x". The house seed went with the AI markets, so a real market now opens EMPTY. Fixed in the fine print, the hot/cold rule card and the client comment, and the new sentence is stronger than the old one: every point of a side's distance from 50% is somebody's actual stake.

- **The prediction board stops inventing its own inventory, and debaters can back themselves** (2026-08-25, Aidan looking at /predict: "get rid of open markets thing. details that are fake, dont exist are bad here. just explain spectators can bet, debaters can bet on themselves, etc"). Two changes, and the first is the one that matters. **`predict.mjs` was minting the board.** `ensureMarkets` held seven cards open at all times, drawn from a hardcoded motion bank, each with a motion nobody had argued, two "cases" written by the bank, and a pool seeded with 100 house points a side, which is why every single card on the page read a flat 50/50 with "0 predictions" under it and a countdown to a Haiku call that would decide a debate that never happened. `seedActivity` did the same thing to the "recently resolved" feed. Measured before removing it: **1,566 market documents, 1,102 of them AI-minted, and exactly one market in the collection's history has ever carried a stake** — a real spar round. So the board was almost entirely furniture, on the one page whose whole product claim is that the number printed on a card means something. **A market now exists only where a round exists.** `open` is called by a debater when a live round starts and it is the only way one is created; the board reads `live_*` liveKeys, the retired AI documents keep their `ai_*` keys and are simply never queried again (none carried a stake, so nothing is stranded, and purging them is one query whenever it is worth the writes). Deleted with the bank: `ensureMarkets`, `seedActivity`, `judgeMotion`, `resolveAiMarket`, `settleMarket`, `voidMarket`, the `resolve` / `seed` / `reset` actions, and `scheduled-predict-sweep.mjs`, whose entire job was draining a board of markets nobody had bet on. **Stale guard, because the opposite failure is the same lie:** a human market's lockAt is minutes after it opens and a round that ends without a server verdict leaves it at `open` forever, so 464 such documents already exist; markets older than three hours drop off the board, and pre-2026-08-25 markets carry no liveKey at all, so the board starts genuinely empty rather than resurrecting dead countdowns. **Self-exclusion becomes own-side-only.** The two debaters used to be refused outright. Aidan's call is that backing yourself is the point, so a debater may now stake on THEIR OWN side and the server refuses a stake on their opponent (`wrong-side`). The match-fixing incentive only ever ran in one direction, and that is the direction that stays shut: nobody in a round can be paid for losing it. `/live-round`'s market panel mounts for debaters too, with the opponent's side priced and unclickable rather than hidden, since a debater should be able to see the price they cannot take. **The page is now rules-first.** The "Open markets" heading and the example crowd-vote card (62/38 bars, "248 people voted", labelled *example* and still a fabricated number where a real one goes) are replaced by four cards stating who can bet, when it locks, and what settles it. Both board headings live in the DOM and stay hidden until real inventory returns, because a heading over a "nothing here yet" box is the same claim in a quieter voice. Two truth fixes rode along: the explainer claimed "three judges vote on a rubric published before the round" for markets a live round judges with one call, and the settlement promise did not mention that **`lib/judgment.mjs` stamps a live ballot `participant`** (it is written in a debater's own browser) while `predict.mjs` settles only on `verdictSource:'server'`, so a market on a live round refunds rather than pays today. That limit is now printed on the page. **It is also the next real build here:** move live-round judging server-side and the board pays out for the first time.

- **Paid social traffic lands in a webview where the only door we offered cannot open; the first spar round comes back** (2026-08-24, Aidan: "dont require google sign in for first time use of the omegle feature, its 2nd time they need google or other signin"). TikTok ads ran and the funnel was measured the same day, segmented by referrer off the `events` stream. **340 TikTok sessions. 6 engaged. 2 reached /spar. 0 queue joins. 0 rounds.** Against the same day's direct traffic at 340 sessions, 129 engaged, 37 queue joins and 38 rooms, and other external referrers at 52 sessions, 36 engaged, 9 rooms. Average events per session: 6.9 TikTok, 22.9 direct, 35.2 other external. **339 of the 340 were inside TikTok's in-app webview and all 340 landed on `/`.**
  - **Follow-up 2026-08-25:** the reopening changed the client allowance and `spar-pair.mjs`, but missed the third gate: `matchmaking_queue` create was still `isNamedAccount()`. Every anonymous guest therefore hit `permission-denied` before the server could meter their first round. The rule now permits a signed-in owner to create only their own `waiting` doc; `spar-pair.mjs` remains the server-authoritative round limit. The client no longer prints Firestore collection and rule diagnoses to visitors.
  - **Two locked doors, stacked, and neither was disinterest.** (1) Google refuses OAuth in embedded webviews outright (`disallowed_useragent`), and the redirect fallback dies too because our authDomain is a different domain under partitioned third-party storage. `auth-modal.js` has known this since it grew `isInAppBrowser()` and warns correctly. But **`signup-nudge.js` prompted Google One Tap with no webview check at all**, firing on **314 of the 340 sessions**: a sign-in prompt thrown at a stranger on arrival that was guaranteed to fail. And `doSignIn()` handed the click to the landing's `#googleSignupBtn` delegate BEFORE it could reach the shared modal, which is the one surface carrying email, and the landing is exactly where all 340 arrived. (2) **`GUEST_FREE_ROUNDS` was 0**, not the 2 the 2026-08-19 entry describes: an unlogged 2026-08-23 flip ("actually require google or gmail sign in ... we need conversion to be much better") closed the guest trial, and no env override exists in production. So a guest was refused before their first round, and the only door shown was one their browser physically cannot pass.
  - **Allowance is now ONE**, the sixth call on this gate. No sign-in for the first round, account required for the second. The 08-23 reasoning still holds for round two and that is where the ask now sits; it does not hold for a stranger who has seen nothing yet. The env var still overrides both directions with no deploy, and reads `??` rather than `||` so `GUEST_FREE_ROUNDS=0` can still close the trial rather than falling back to the default.
  - **Shipped with it:** One Tap skipped in webviews and counted as `one_tap_skipped_webview` so the size of that population stays visible; the landing delegate bypassed in-webview so the click reaches the modal; the nudge button relabelled "Continue with email" there, because a button promising Google in a browser that cannot run Google is a promise the next tap breaks; `isInAppBrowser()` exported as `window.__ditIsInAppBrowser` so there is ONE definition of "can this browser finish an OAuth sign-in" rather than two regexes drifting; and a **Copy link** button on the modal's in-app note, since "open the site in Safari or Chrome" is not an instruction anyone can follow inside a webview with no address bar.
  - **Verified against four user agents pulled from the day's own sessions**, not invented ones: both TikTok UAs detected, desktop Chrome and iOS Safari not flagged. Note the iOS TikTok UA DOES contain "Safari", so the iOS-webview heuristic never catches it and `musical_ly` is what does. Confirmed on production afterwards in both directions.
  - **The durable lesson, and it is not "test in a webview".** Every one of these surfaces was individually correct. `auth-modal.js` handled webviews properly; One Tap was correct code that had simply never been told; the zero allowance was a defensible conversion call on its own terms. What made them fatal was **buying traffic from a population that meets all three at once**, and nothing in any file said they composed. **Before spending on a social channel, walk the ad's own landing path in that app's browser as a stranger.** The same discipline as the 2026-08-18 Open finding: two states individually right and jointly impossible.
  - **Still unverified, deliberately stated rather than implied:** whether App Check (hard-enforced since 2026-08-18) mints a token in that webview. It could not be observed because nobody reached a gated endpoint. If it fails, `/api/claude` 401s and the free round dies one step past this fix.
  - **Not a code problem and left with Aidan:** the ads pointed at `/`, not at the surface the creative promises.

- **A round that happened is a record: the leaderboard and the ladder flip from opt-in to opt-out** (2026-08-24, the founder: make sure all round records, unless uniquely bad because something happened, are added to the leaderboard).
