Two gates stood between a finished live round and the public board, and between a finished live round and the Glicko ladder. Both were opt-IN and both were quiet about it.
  - **Dual consent.** After the ballot, BOTH debaters had to tick an unticked box on their own screen before EITHER result posted. One person forgetting, closing the tab, or never scrolling that far was enough to make the round exist nowhere. So the board held the rounds where two people both remembered to do a piece of admin, which is a different population from the rounds that were played. It is an opt-OUT now: the box renders ticked, the row is up by the time the ballot renders, and unticking takes it down. Each debater writes only their OWN row, so nobody waits on anybody, and if the opponent opted out their name comes off my row and it posts as "Opponent". My result is mine to publish; their identity is theirs.
  - **The opt-out reuses the EXISTING `leaderboardConsent` map rather than a new field, and that is the load-bearing decision.** `firestore.rules` already says a writer may only change their own key in that map (the 2026-08-12 `leaderboardConsentChangeIsOwnUid` guard). A fresh `optOut` map would have shipped ungated, and an ungated one lets the person you just beat suppress your row. `false` means kept off, absent means post, so every round recorded before today is unaffected and no rules deploy was needed.
  - **The same gate, one layer down, was worse.** `lib/rating-apply.mjs` read the same map, so the rating ladder refused every casual round for the same reason: the only live rounds that ever moved a rating were tournament rounds, where `live-judge` stamps consent from the entry. It now refuses only on an explicit `false`, so the board and the ladder follow one rule stated once.
  - **The quality floor was the third way a real round vanished.** It demanded a perfect one: every speech present, none skipped, each clocking 30% of its target. A normal round loses a speech all the time. Someone finishes early and sits down, a POI eats the clock, a side concedes the last rebuttal, a mic glitch drops a transcript. Each of those is a round two people debated and each was silently worth nothing. What is left is the set of things that mean no round happened: a seat nobody claimed, a side that never spoke (a no-show, not a loss), or more than half the format missing. **Duration is not a test any more. A short speech is a bad speech, and speaker points are what that is for.**
  - **NOT backfilled, deliberately.** Rounds played under the old rule were played under a promise that silence meant private, and publishing them now because the default changed would be reading that promise backwards. Forward-only.

- **Open Spar Night runs three times a Wednesday, and the Open runs three sessions on the day** (2026-08-24, the founder: do open spar night 3 times, US night, Europe night, and East Asia/Australia night; and make the start time more specific). One fixed hour at 8:00 PM Eastern is an evening in North America and the middle of a working day almost everywhere else, which is a strange shape for the one feature whose entire job is getting people online AT THE SAME TIME on a site whose traffic is global: it asked most of the field to turn up at 4 AM to meet each other. Three sessions now, 90 minutes each, on both surfaces: **7:00 AM ET Asia-Pacific night** (Sydney 9 PM, Tokyo 8 PM, Delhi 4:30 PM), **3:00 PM ET Europe night** (London 8 PM, Berlin 9 PM), **8:00 PM ET US night** (Chicago 7 PM, Los Angeles 5 PM).
  - **Anchored to an Eastern wall-clock hour on purpose.** That keeps ONE DST-safe calculation for the whole schedule instead of three regional ones that would each need their own DST rules. The city times printed beside a session are computed from the real instant at render time, so they stay true through every region's own DST rather than going quietly wrong in January, when the Asia-Pacific session reads "Sydney 11 PM" and says so. Verified against seven instants across both DST regimes.
  - **The reminder cron moved with the schedule**, 13:00 UTC to 09:00 UTC, because 13:00 is two hours AFTER the first session ends and an email announcing an event that has already happened is worse than no email. Calendar links follow the session being shown rather than one fixed hour: someone in Sydney adding the card in front of them should get the 9 PM session, not a US hour they were never going to make.
  - **On /tournaments this replaces "show up whenever suits you", which was the honest description of a drop-in day and a bad answer to the only scheduling question a stranger has.** Nothing about drop-in changed; rounds still run between the sessions and the rules say a session is a convenience, not a requirement. The hero lists the three, each stamped in the READER'S own clock as soon as JS runs, with the major-city version in the shipped markup so it is true with JS off. Surfaces moved together (date plate, enter subhead and its renderer default, logistics card, run-of-day, Event JSON-LD, tournament-rules section 2, /open timeline and social cards, the sitewide ribbon, three SEO pages, how-it-works including two FAQ JSON-LD answers, the landing Wednesday band) and the live tournament doc's own `startsAt` / `startsAtISO` / description were patched to match, because a schedule that disagrees with itself is worse than a vague one. **The entry button is also the size of the ask now**: 69px tall at 1.18rem against the tip button's unchanged 53px, so the size gap says which one is the action.

- **The share card's hole was a block that is often absent** (2026-08-24, the founder, on a shipped card: make this cooler). The 9:16 round card pinned every block to a hardcoded Y: motion at 510, pull-quote at 880, RFD at 1620. The quote is the one block that can be missing, because it is picked out of the transcript and a short round often has no sentence in the 30-220 character band it looks for, and when it was missing the card published seven hundred pixels of empty black in the middle. That is most of the image, on the one artefact whose entire job is to be looked at by strangers on someone else's feed. Nothing is pinned now: each block measures itself, the stack is laid out between the result plate and the footer with the slack spread through the gaps, a missing block closes its own gap, and with no quote the motion steps up from 58px to 72px because a card with room to spare should spend it on the line people are arguing about. It also gained the thing it was missing rather than only losing the hole: a scoreboard with both seats, both sides, both speaker scores, the winner lit and the loser dimmed, and WINNER in place of the number when a ballot carries none. **Standing note for canvas work here: there is no `letterSpacing` in Safari's canvas, so spaced type is drawn character by character.**

- **The first-screen leaderboard rail takes the face bank back, with the footnote** (2026-08-24, Aidan on seeing it live: "where are the profile pictures i dont see them? i literally uploaded hella examples - use them!", then "make this cooler"). The rail (#lbRail, a dock of the top six of /leaderboard clipped by the right edge of the window, shipped the same day) launched on the ranked band's portrait rule: their built avatar, their Google photo, then a seeded abstract emblem, because "a stand-in face beside a real debater's result was a claim we had to disclaim in a footnote". Four of six rows are anonymous with neither photo nor avatar, so what he actually saw was a column of coloured marbles. **This REVERSES that rule for this surface only.** The third tier is now the shared face bank, consented real webcam stills first (face46-49, 51-54, the 18+ batch confirmed on record 2026-08-18) and the generated bank behind them; face50 stays out, being the illustrated Anonymous avatar and not a face. What comes back with it is the footnote the rule existed to avoid needing: the open rail says, in as many words, that a portrait stands in where a debater has not set one. The two truer tiers still outrank it, so a real account shows its real face. **Same day, FOURTH and final pass, and this is where it landed:** the set became a thing a debater can CHOOSE, and grew ("those should be options yea", "i want more variety too"). 49 tiles now, in six groups (characters, animals, food, objects, nature, symbols), living in `app/js/pfp-set.js` rather than inlined on the landing, because the designer, the profile and every identity surface need the same tiles and a second copy drifts the first time one is added. A picked tile is a THIRD identity kind beside the designed portrait and the camera mask: `{kind:'pfp', id}`, offered at the top of the avatar builder above the designer, shape-checked in `lib/avatar-design.mjs` and membership-checked on the client. **Two storage keys, deliberately** (`debatable-pfp-v1` for the choice, `debatable-avatar-pref` for which kind was chosen last), so picking a picture never destroys a portrait somebody designed and vice versa; `getPublicIdentity()` reads the preference first and is otherwise unchanged, so an account that never opens the picker behaves exactly as before. The `pfp` branch is the one sanitiser in that lib with NO fallback, on purpose: there is no sensible default picture, and inventing one would put a tile on somebody who did not pick it. The rail and the ranked band both draw from the same set, so a debater wears one picture down the whole page; the webcam stills and the generated portraits came out of the rail and still ride the surfaces they were made for. **Third pass, superseded by the above:** the generated headshots were still wrong ("no bring back the pictures i uploaded to u"), because a board of profile pictures is not a board of faces at all. The uploaded screenshots still cannot ship, for the reasons below, so the SET was redrawn instead: twelve original inline SVGs in the register those references actually occupy, a ghost, a dog, a cat, a robot, sunglasses, a stack of cash, a skull, a frog, a flame, an alien, a hooded figure, and the grey default nobody replaced. Flat fills, no hairlines, one strong hue each, readable at 54px, zero requests. That set is now the whole third tier; the webcam stills and the generated portraits came back out of the rail (both still ride other surfaces). The avatar engine now loads only for a row carrying a REAL built identity, which is rarer, so the highest-traffic page on the site stopped paying 27KB for a column of set pictures. **Second pass, superseded by the above:** a column of identical AI headshots still did not read like a social app ("we need to use the better profile examples even if they dont match on real leaderboard"), sent with a set of phone screenshots of real profile pictures from another app. Those screenshots cannot ship and were not used: the batch includes a child, several identifiable adults, and copyrighted characters, and the no-unlicensed-photo rule has no "it is only an example" exception. What shipped instead is the SPREAD his examples actually demonstrate, built from what we are allowed to publish: each row picks one of three by a stable hash, a consented webcam still, a portrait generated by our own avatar designer, or that designer's arena mask. Somebody used a selfie, somebody built a character, somebody went masked, which is what a real board of profile pictures looks like. The designer output doubles as an advert for the designer. `/js/avatar.js` loads only after the rail has painted and only when a tile needs it, so the highest-traffic page on the site does not pay 27KB for a column that already has its emblems up. **The ranked band further down the page is untouched and still runs initials and emblems**, and the comment there now says so, because a rail reading differently from the board it previews is the contradiction a future agent would otherwise "fix" in the wrong direction. Same pass, per "make this cooler": the open state is one dark board in both themes rather than six white pills, the rows carry a mono score and a bar scaled to the 50-100 band, and the tiles square off from discs to 15px on open (his call: "make less circular and more square when it expands out") with the cards arriving on a 26ms-per-row cascade. **The standing bar is unchanged and absolute: never an unlicensed photo of a real person, and never a new board face without explicit consent and an 18+ confirmation from Aidan.** **SUPERSEDED FOR THE RAIL ON 2026-08-25** by the entry at the top of this log: Aidan asked for the uploaded photos a third time as a standing instruction ("never reverse this") and they now ARE the rail's stand-in tier. The redraw described here is still the pickable set, and the bar still holds for the ROUNDS board and every surface that attaches a face to a name and a result.

- **The round offers to be held in another language, because the engine was already there and nobody could find it** (2026-08-24, from a live round Aidan was spectating: two Dutch speakers debated in English and typed "try speaking dutch" into the audience chat). Cross-language rounds have worked for a while and none of it was advertised: each debater's mic transcribes in their own declared language, the opponent reads live translation, every speech is tagged with the language it was delivered in, and the ballot builder tells the judge to score each argument in its ORIGINAL language and to write the whole ballot in the round's language when there is one. **The entire door was an unlabelled dropdown reading "English" in a row of mic controls**, which is not an offer, it is a mystery, and the people it was built for were the least likely to guess.
  - **The offer is now made out loud, and only with a reason.** A strip above the transcript fires on the strongest available signal: the other side is speaking Dutch right now (learned from the live transcript mirror, so it lands while the thing it is about is still happening), or this browser is set to Dutch. Failing both it is one quiet line before speech 1 carrying the picker, and it retires once the round is running. One tap switches, through the same setter the picker uses, so there is one code path to the language and not two. Dismissal is per ROOM rather than per browser: passing in this round says nothing about the next one, which may be against someone who opens in another language.
  - **The copy is held to what the judge actually does.** Switching alone gets your speeches scored in your language; the ballot only comes back in that language when the round has ONE language. So the whole-round promise is made only when the opponent is already there, and the cold offer promises the part that is true on its own. Same discipline in the toast.
  - **The language finally reaches the server transcription engine, which is a real bug and not a polish item.** `/api/transcribe` has accepted a `language` field the whole time and no caller ever sent one, so on the browsers with no Web Speech API (Firefox, Brave, most of iOS, which is the entire reason that engine exists) a Dutch speech was transcribed against an ENGLISH domain-hint prompt. A prompt in one language over audio in another pulls the output toward the prompt's language, so the failure mode is not "no hint", it is English words that sound like what was said. The client now sends the declared code, and the function narrows a locale to ISO-639-1 and drops the English hint for a non-English round while keeping the in-language carry. Callers that send nothing (`/judge`, `/flow`) are byte-identical to before.
  - **Standing note: the picker is labelled now** ("Debating in" for a seat, "Captions in" for a watcher), and that label is doing as much work as the strip. A control whose current value is its only visible text does not tell anyone a choice exists.

- **"No card" comes off the Open ribbon, and the prize figure was checked rather than corrected** (2026-08-24). The sitewide strip read "$100 for winning a debate · Sat Aug 29 · The Debatable Open · free to enter, no card". Advertising the absence of a payment step is friction-selling and is banned on marketing surfaces, and "free to enter" already carries the fact without sounding like a page that expects to be mistaken for a scam. The clause is gone from the ribbon and from the 30-seconds-in Open modal's fine print, which carried the same claim in the same file on a bigger surface.
  - **The comments were half the fix.** Two of them framed "no card" as the strip's deliberate trust token, one calling it the "whole anti-scam move". A comment instructing the next agent to re-add a banned phrase is how it comes back, so both now say the opposite and name the rule.
  - **The $100 headline was VERIFIED, not corrected, and this is the part worth keeping.** It reads stale against this log, whose earlier entries record a $500/$250/$100 pot out of $850 and a $5 entry with a waiver. All three live sources agree with the strip and with each other: the tournament doc carries `entryFeeCents` 0, `tipCents` 500 and `prizeSplit` [10000, 5000, 2500] against an `entryCount` of 13, and section 5 of the published rules says $100/$50/$25 with a restore to $850 past 50 entrants. The 2026-08-22 pot cut and free-entry decision supersede the older entries; the field is 13, well under the 50 that would move the number. `lib/founding-comp.mjs` reads stale for the same reason and is dormant by design: `tournament.mjs` grants no new comps and eligibility is the 18+ attestation alone, so it had nothing to move. **A figure that disagrees with the top of this log is not evidence it is wrong. Read the doc and the rules before editing a number that money settles against.**
  - **The rebase trap fired twice in one push, which is a new maximum.** `origin/main` landed on v2851 while the commit was being written, and on v2852 during the rebase that followed, so git deduped the identical `CACHE_NAME` hunk and dropped both `sw.js` files from the commit BOTH times, leaving a rewritten `topbar.js` about to ship under a cache name users already held. Caught by checking the commit's file list after each rebase rather than trusting it went through, hand-bumped to v2853. Shipped at `9ff7775a`.
  - **Left alone deliberately, and each for a reason.** `tournament-rules.html` says "no fee, no card, and no waiver to request" as part of its no-consideration argument, which is a legal contract clause rather than a pitch; the beta cluster on the landing pages and /pricing ("10 free rounds. No card.") is the same banned pattern on a different campaign and wants its own pass with its own call, not a blind sweep that would catch the rules page with it.

- **A watcher gets a screen to watch, not a seat in the call** (2026-08-24, the founder, on a screenshot of the spectator view: set it up just like a screen you are watching, and you are not there showing because guests cannot be on the call). Spectators joined the round through Daily's PREBUILT UI, which is built for people who are IN the call, so a watcher was handed a tile of their own ("Guest (You)"), a participants bar of camera-off name cards, a mic and camera tray, and a name prompt before they saw anything. Most of a watcher's screen was people not on camera, one of whom was themselves.
  - **THIS SUPERSEDES the 2026-08-15 line that spectators and the audience-camera path "deliberately STAY on the prebuilt iframe."** That reasoning was right at the time and about a different thing: neither publishes a custom track, so neither was broken by the avatar leak, and moving them would have widened a privacy fix. It was never a claim that prebuilt was the right surface for watching. The audience-camera path is unchanged and still rides its own prebuilt frame.
  - **Every prebuilt route was measured against a real room in a real browser before the fix was chosen, and all three put a door in front of watching.** Prebuilt loaded from a plain URL ignores `showLocalVideo` and `showParticipantsBar` as query params, and it also ignored the `startVideoOff` / `videoSource` params this page had been appending since the spectator path was written, which means those never did anything: what actually withholds a watcher's camera is the iframe `allow` list. `createFrame` honours the properties, and then hits the camera-permission screen precisely BECAUSE `allow` withholds the camera, or a haircheck when it does not. And the door that had been shipping all along was **"Enter your name" on every watcher, on a page whose whole job is to let someone watch.**
  - **So a watcher runs the same call object the debaters run**, and gets the stage this page already draws: the debaters' tiles, the audience strip, remote audio, no tile of their own, no tray. No device is opened in either direction (`videoSource` and `audioSource` both false), so there is no permission prompt and nothing to click through. Verified end to end in Chrome against a real Daily room: joined, one tile for the one other participant, no self tile, no controls.
  - **Riders, each found by running it rather than reading it.** The mount was racing daily-js, which rides a deferred CDN tag: when the room fetch landed first the page fell back to prebuilt purely on timing, so `mountDaily` now waits up to 8s for the library before choosing (this improves the DEBATER path too, which was silently losing the call object and with it avatar mode). A watcher whose hidden-presence token fails to mint would arrive on everyone's stage as a black tile, so watchers join as `Watching`, publish nothing, and are dropped from every stage by a test that requires no camera, mic or screen, which is what stops it ever hiding a real person. And Chrome will not autoplay audible media without an activation on THIS document, which a watcher arriving from a link does not have, so the round would have played in silence and read as a broken feed: a one-tap sound gate appears only when a `play()` is actually refused. A debater never meets that one, because granting a mic carries the privilege with it.

- **Debaters can trade sides in the room, and it takes both of them** (2026-08-24, the founder: allow users to switch sides when they get in the room if they want to). /spar assigns sides by lexicographic uid, which is arbitrary, so somebody who wanted the other bench had exactly one move: leave the match and requeue, on a product whose whole liquidity problem is two people being awake at once. Now there is a "Switch sides" control on the sides guide and the seats trade when the opponent agrees.
  - **It asks rather than acts, for the same reason the 2026-08-12 resolution change asks.** Which side you argue is the other half of what both people prepared against, so one debater flipping the seats is a change to the contest and not a preference. One side proposes, the seats move when the other agrees, and declining and withdrawing are the same operation because either debater is entitled to end a proposal. Locked at the same instant the resolution locks, `motionLocked()`, since past the first speech the seats are what the transcript is tagged with and what the ballot is written against.
  - **The control is on the sides guide, next to the YOU chip, and that placement is the lesson rather than a taste call.** The motion editor spent months behind Options and the comment that moved it out says plainly that nobody found it. The sides guide is where a person looks the second they notice they are on the side they did not want.
  - **One code path moves the seats on every client, including the one that pressed agree.** The accepter only WRITES the swapped pair; the local swap happens in the round snapshot, which Firestore's own latency compensation delivers back to the writer immediately. So the proposer, the accepter and every spectator all travel the same path, and a write that fails reverts all of them the same way instead of leaving one round holding two answers. The adoption is narrow by construction: the incoming pair has to be a PERMUTATION of the pair already held, so it can flip a swap and can do nothing else. Judge nominations swap with the seats (a pick belongs to the debater who made it); state keyed by uid (seatSeen, avatars, leaderboard consent, rating deltas) is untouched, because that follows the person. `mySideHint` moves too, since several label paths read the URL hint directly rather than going through `mySide()` and would otherwise keep announcing the side the person just left.
  - **1v1 only, deliberately.** A 2v2 bench swap is four people's agreement rather than two, and the partner seats reach a client by URL rather than through the round doc, so a duo swap would move half the room and leave the other half arguing the side it still thinks it holds.
  - **The rules now say seats may be TRADED, never reassigned.** Until this shipped the only client write of `proUid` / `conUid` was the round-init set writing back the values already there; a live-round seat write is a normal action now. `seatsHeldOrTraded()` holds any update to a permutation of the pair on the document, so a participant cannot write themselves into both seats and cannot swap their opponent out for somebody else — a hole that predates this feature and was reachable from a console. It only bites once both seats are filled, so the init write that first puts them there is untouched, as are legacy rounds that never had them. Verified against the live Rules API before deploy: 9 cases pass, and the three seat-integrity cases were shown to FAIL with the guard removed. The deployed ruleset was diffed byte-identical to `origin/main` first, per the standing rules-deploy discipline.
  - **Rider, and it is the reason to care about a dormant surface: the prediction market records which uid sits on which side and settles by side.** It opened at round entry, which is before the seats could still move, so a swap after open would have paid the wrong debater. It now opens on the first timer transition instead, local or mirrored, which is the first moment on either client where the seats can no longer move.
  - **Verified in a browser end to end against real Firestore** rather than by reading the diff: propose paints the card and removes the control, the opponent's card offers Switch sides / Keep my side, agreeing swaps the seats on the document and flips the sides guide, the speaker line, and turn control in one pass, and starting Speech 1 takes both the control and any open proposal down and clears the field from the document. That last one was a real bug caught by testing: the snapshot carrying the running timer paints the card BEFORE `applyRemoteTimer` flips the lock further down the same handler, so the render pass sees an unlocked round and left the proposal standing.
- **A walkout ends the round instead of aging out of it** (2026-08-24, Aidan: "i left this room - this debate ended. end it quicker when ppl leave"). He left a round and the sitewide LIVE strip kept advertising it: motion, both names, frozen on Speech 3. Presence was heartbeat-only, so leaving was expressed as the ABSENCE of a write and everyone had to wait out the 100s staleness window to infer it. **The worse half is that `lastSeenAt` is written by EITHER debater**, so a round that lost one person kept beating through the other and advertised indefinitely on every surface still reading that one field. `roundIsWatchable` had closed that for /api/watch-live the day before by counting per-uid presence; /spar's watch list and `/api/round-report` had not, and round-report's version was load-bearing, since both debaters could walk out and a spectator filing an obviously correct "this round is over" report was told it was still live. **A departure now says so.** `markSeatLeft()` writes `seatLeft[uid]` on the deliberate exits, awaited before the navigation, and best-effort on pagehide; the heartbeat is stopped first, or the beat 30 seconds later would undo the mark on the way out the door. **Every reader believes the mark only while it is NEWER than that seat's own heartbeat**, which is what makes it safe to write from anywhere: a debater who comes back clears it by beating, and a stray or forged write is undone within 30s. Absence of a mark still proves nothing, so the staleness window stays underneath for a tab that simply died. **The find inside the find, and it is the recurring shape:** live-round's "You are the only one here" strip, written specifically for an opponent who leaves mid-round, **could never fire for the person it was written for** — `roomIsQuiet()` read the doc-level heartbeat, which a seated debater writes themselves every 30 seconds, so it was never stale in the one browser that needed it to be. It works for spectators, who write no beat, which is why it looked correct. It now asks about the OTHER chair through per-uid presence; both timestamps in that comparison are server-stamped, so it is one clock against itself and the no-show check's clock-skew objection does not apply. That strip also gains an ending: "End the round and get the ballot" runs the ordinary `finishRound()`, offered only when a speech actually has a transcript, because an empty room has nothing to judge and a verdict on silence is worse than no verdict. **A clock running against an empty chair previously had no way out except closing the tab, which is what left finished debates advertising as live in the first place.** One rider worth keeping: iOS fires `pagehide` when the browser is backgrounded and then restores the page from bfcache, so `pageshow(persisted)` resumes the beat and clears the mark. Checking a text message must not end your round.

- **The board's whole face bank circulates, its motions get heavier, and the creators arrive as open challenges rather than fake results** (2026-08-24, Aidan off a screenshot of the first-screen board: "bring back the guys too, theres a lot of missing images in circulation", "the motions should be diversified and pertinent to even a more serious degree", "i also want to see the influencers/celebs here"). Three asks, one card, and the first one was not the bug it looked like.
  - **Nothing was 404ing. The images were missing FROM circulation, not from disk.** Every face file the board can reference serves 200, and the deleted numbers (face05/09/14/39/55) are correctly absent from every pool. The actual shortfall was arithmetic: `castFaces` deals ONE face per CHARACTER, and the deck carried 24 characters against a 56-portrait bank, so 32 portraits sat out every single load. **The men were the half genuinely starved, and by more than the ratio suggests.** `mPool` is `shuffle(FACE_M_REAL).concat(shuffle(FACE_M_GEN))` — real stills first — so with only 11 male characters the deal took all 8 real men and just 3 of the 22 generated ones. Nineteen male faces were effectively out of rotation, against 13 of 26 women. That ordering was correct when it was written (2026-08-22, real-stills-only) and became a bias the moment the 08-24 first pass restored the generated bank without touching the roster.
  - **The fix is the roster, not the pool.** It is now exactly as long as the bank it draws from, 30 male characters against 30 male faces and 26 female against 26, so every portrait deals on every load and the real-first ordering is moot rather than removed. Keep the concat: it is what makes the property survive if the roster ever shrinks again. **The standing rule this leaves: a face added to the bank without a character added beside it goes straight onto the bench, so the two lists move together.**
  - **The 32 new names were free because seat GENDER never moved.** The ballots are authored prose with gendered pronouns in them, so a name swap that changes gender silently corrupts 40 verdicts. Every substitution was same-gender, which meant 31 authored ballots kept their pronouns and only two name tokens per card changed.
  - **MOTIONS: nine slots move off dating and etiquette.** New domains the deck had none of: drug patents in a health emergency, healthcare tied to employment, what prison is actually for, who pays to retrain the people AI replaces, nuclear build time, water as private property, editing a genetic disease out of an embryo, climate displacement, and what rich countries owe the countries they colonised. **This does NOT reverse the 2026-08-19 accessible-motions pass**, and the surviving on-ramps are the proof: tipping, the drinking age, whether influencers do a real job, athletes against teachers. A deck of nothing but institutions is the civics syllabus that pass was written against. The 08-19 exclusion still binds exactly as written: contested is the goal, targeted is not, so nothing here draws its charge from putting a group's rights up for a vote.
  - **A live bug found by reading the cards rather than the code.** "Is it ever okay to date a friend's ex?" was shipping the right-to-be-forgotten ballot: its motion had been swapped at some earlier pass and the `rfd` was never rewritten, so the card printed a verdict about a round it was not describing, on the homepage, for an unknown number of weeks. **An rfd is prose. It cannot fail a build, cannot fail a test nobody wrote, and looks completely normal in a diff.** A motion edit that leaves its ballot behind stays invisible until a human reads the finished card, which is the same shape as every other "renders a plausible screen" finding in this log.
  - **CREATORS: they are on the board, and the card TYPE is the entire reason that is allowed.** §2 forbids captioning a real person with an invented name, score, or ballot, and forbids naming a creator as a participant in a round that never happened. Putting Joe Rogan on an example-round card with "88 - 75" under him would have been a fabricated record of a real person, which is a legal exposure before it is a brand problem. So a challenge card carries no verdict, no speaker points, no viewer read and no clock: a creator tile marked Invited, an empty seat marked Yours, the resolution the room would put to them, and the watchlist band's own affiliation line verbatim ("None confirmed, none affiliated, none endorsing"). **No side is printed against a creator tile**, because a side is a position attributed to them; the motion is what the room would ask, never what they think. Portraits are the same CC and public-domain files `/img/creator-watchlist/README.md` already credits. Ten cards, interleaved by the existing shuffle, excluded from the coin-flip (a flip would seat the empty chair in the speaking position) and from the opener (a cold visitor needs a finished round with a verdict before an invitation means anything). **If a creator ever actually debates, that round goes in the deck above with a real ballot. It does not go here.**
  - **Ship note, the SW trap for the third time in this log's memory:** the rebase onto a moved `origin/main` deduped the `CACHE_NAME` hunk because both sides had landed on v2819 independently, which would have shipped a rewritten landing under a cache name users already held. Caught by checking the commit's file list after the rebase rather than trusting that it went through. v2820.

- **Debaters agree the setting (US / world) when the motion turns on it** (2026-08-24, Aidan: "in some questions when actors matter, make a question setup for both users when they match to specify the background, US or world? for example"). Some motions have the same answer everywhere (value claims, thought experiments); others turn on WHERE they apply ("ban private schools", "abolish the electoral college"), and two strangers matched on one of those argue past each other about different worlds. When two people MATCH on a context-dependent motion, they now agree on a setting before Speech 1, and the agreed frame is handed to the AI judge as the shared premise.
  - **Auto-detect, not always-on** (Aidan's pick from three trigger options). A motion with the same answer everywhere shows nothing. New `/api/motion-context` (App Check gated, rate-limited, one `claude-sonnet-4-6` call) classifies the motion and returns 2-4 per-motion options, ALWAYS including a worldwide one so a round can never be forced into a national frame. It fails OPEN to `needsContext:false`: a classifier that errors must never block a round from starting. Options are AI-suggested per motion (Aidan's pick over a fixed US-vs-World), so a US institution offers just that country and Worldwide while a broadly-variable policy offers a few relevant frames.
  - **One call per round, cached on the doc.** The lowest seated uid (the same driver rule the fact check and verdict reel use) asks once and writes `contextSetup` to the round doc; every client renders the same card. `contextSetup` is written even when `needsContext:false`, so the driver never re-classifies. One Sonnet call per matched round, bounded.
  - **No Firestore rule change, deliberately.** The consent is additive doc fields only (`contextSetup`, `contextPicks`, `contextFrame`). Unlike the motion-change map (which earned an own-key rule because changing the motion mid-round is high-stakes), context-setting is cooperative PRE-round setup with no economic or standing incentive to forge, so the existing participant-write rule is enough. Each debater writes their own pick via a `contextPicks.{uid}` dot-path; matching picks lock `contextFrame` (any client can commit it, the value is identical so a race is harmless). Shown as a quiet chip under the resolution once agreed.
  - **The judge gets it as a premise, not a thumb.** `buildBallotPrompt` adds an AGREED SETTING line only when a frame is set (byte-identical otherwise, on both the 2-side and BP builders): both debaters agreed this motion is argued in the context of X, and it does NOT name a winner and does NOT move speaker points, it only fixes the world the debate is in. A both-consented, disclosed shared premise is legitimate, not the operator's thumb on the scale.
  - **Never blocks a round.** Nothing here gates a start: Speech 1 begins only when the speaker presses a Start speech control. As of 2026-08-27, the audio start cue can remind them that the clock is off but never presses Start for them. The card is prominent and non-blocking. (Stale as written: this said the kickoff strip was retired with `KICKOFF_STRIP_ON` false, and the strip was re-enabled the same day, minus its countdown.) A standoff or a classifier error just runs the ballot with no frame. Only MATCHED rounds (both seats known) get the step; solo/hotseat/direct-link rounds skip it.
  - **Verified:** the endpoint parse/sanitize unit cases (adds a worldwide option, drops a global-only set, dedupes, extracts JSON from surrounding prose), and the card + consent + chip render path in a browser (force-revealed DOM, no JS errors). **NOT verified:** a real two-person live match, and the live Anthropic classifier (no key locally; the key IS set in prod). Commit `cd40844a`, SW v2812.

- **Mobile landscape: the live-debate flow gets an orientation pass, and the /spar Open promo comes off** (2026-08-24, Aidan: "fix mobile in horizontal form its broken", then "deelete too" on a screenshot of the gate promo). Nothing on the site handled `orientation:landscape`, so a wide, short phone (~360-430px tall) fell into desktop/portrait CSS with no vertical room. The two surfaces held sideways were the only ones actually broken.
  - **/spar sign-in gate** centred in a 390px-tall viewport clipped its headline off the top and dropped both sign-in buttons below the fold. Top-align + tighten the headline stack in short landscape so both buttons sit on the first screen. Verified in a 844x390 browser.
  - **/live-round** stacked a 300px sticky video over the console, leaving ~34px for the transcript and Start/End controls. Landscape has the width the round wants, so video and console now sit side by side, each full height, the console scrolling on its own; the auth gate gets the same top-align + scroll. Verified the split geometry (both panes fit the viewport exactly); the real in-round content is auth-gated and was checked structurally, not in a live match.
  - All scoped to `(orientation:landscape) and (max-height:600px)`, so portrait, tablet, and desktop are byte-identical. The landing, the typed app (`.app` padding zeroes at >=641px), practice, and voice-debate already scroll fine in landscape (growing `min-height`, no fixed-height trap).
  - **The Open promo** ("Saturday: The Debatable Open. Cash prizes, free to enter.") came off the /spar gate and searching rail (`openNudgeHtml` neutralised to `return ''`, kept as a no-op restore path): redundant with the sitewide topbar ribbon and part of what pushed the sign-in buttons down. The sitewide ribbon stays. Commit `a7f95e35`, SW v2811.

- **Four builds in one push: a text lane, the audience's own answer read back to it, a quote-verified clash map, and replays that announce themselves** (2026-08-23, Aidan asked for all four areas at once: "1, 2, 3, and 4 - do all"). Shipped as four commits so each can be reverted alone.

  **SMS, built to switch on with an env var rather than a deploy.** The 2026-08-22 entry says SMS "is deliberately NOT built" because the Twilio account is Aidan's to create. Everything up to that boundary now exists and no-ops cleanly until `TWILIO_*` is set, the same posture webpush held while VAPID was missing. **The consent machinery is the design, not paperwork around it:** `sendSms` is deliberately NOT exported from `lib/sms.mjs`, so a future call site cannot text a user without passing verification, opt-out, per-kind switches, a daily cap and quiet hours; the guard asserts the non-export. A number is proved by code round trip, because typing a number proves nothing about owning it and without the round trip this is a free anonymous texting service pointed at whoever you like. Named accounts only, for the reason anonymous uids are refused everywhere else. `/api/sms-inbound` verifies the Twilio signature and **fails closed with no token**, because it mutates somebody's settings keyed on a phone number in the request body. Wired into go-live (its own opt-in list, capped at 60 recipients since a text fan-out is a bill), challenges (force:true skips quiet hours; being called out by name is the one alert worth a late buzz) and DMs (never carries the message text). **ACTION for Aidan: create the Twilio account, buy a number, register A2P, set the env vars.** The /settings card renders itself the moment the server reports configured.

  **The audience finally sees its own answer.** Spectators have answered "where do you stand" before and after rounds for weeks and it went to the server and stopped: `/api/persuasion-index` was fully built, public-safe, and had **zero callers**. A "How the room moved" panel now renders under the ballot for debaters and spectators. Three rules it inherits: only AFTER the ballot, because a live tally tells people what to think and then measures the conformity; percentages only when the SERVER says the sample is big enough, counts and an explicit "anecdote, not a measurement" below that; and the server's caveats rendered rather than filtered. **The bug underneath: every /spectate closing vote had been silently discarded** since that page shipped. The server weights a stance by visible watch time and drops anything under 90 seconds; spectate.html never tracked or sent `watchMs`, so `readWatchMs(undefined)` read 0 and every vote from that page landed in `lowWatch` instead of the tally. It measures visible time now, the way live-round does.

  **The flow becomes a real clash map.** "See the flow" re-read the per-speech notes, an index of what was SAID. Now it draws what was said, what the other side DID with it, and the verbatim line proving both. `lib/clash-map.mjs` was generalized past the async three-turn shape (which hardcodes `TURN_SIDE {1:prop,2:opp,3:prop}`) to N speeches across two benches, **sharing the verification gate rather than copying it**, because the gate is the only reason this belongs near a judged surface. New `/api/clash-map` draws it server-side deliberately: the gate checks every quote against the transcript, and running it in the browser would let a client publish rows the gate never saw. The haystacks are built server-side from the same text sent to the model, so a caller cannot widen what counts as "in the transcript". Drawn once, synced on the round doc (the rfdDeep pattern); falls back to the notes index when pending or failed, so the button is never dead. Advisory on the card, never a winner, never a score, ballot never reads it. **`scripts/test-clash-live.mjs` (31 assertions) in the hook, and its own history is the lesson:** four mutations were applied one at a time and the fourth ESCAPED. "One bad row kills the whole map" passed, because every bad row in the suite failed at the QUOTE check and none took the FIELD-check branch. Two assertions were added for that branch and it is caught now. A guard that passes on its first run has not been shown to test anything, and the specific trap is that a suite can exercise one rejection path thoroughly while leaving a neighbouring one untouched.

  **Recordings: two of the reasons /watch has no inventory.** A round that WAS recorded never told its debaters so, and they are the only people who could share it. One honest line in the ballot actions now says the replay is coming and states the processing delay rather than linking a URL that would 404 for half an hour; a round where consent was withdrawn says plainly that the file is deleted instead. And `thumbnailUrl` was only ever written as a side effect of the first `/api/recording-thumb` request, which happens when a human scrolls past the card, so a freshly published replay had no thumbnail at exactly the moment it is most likely to be shared. The sync pass warms at most three per run, sequentially, swallowing failures. **NOT done and it needs Aidan: the biggest inventory gap is that nothing records unless somebody finds a small pill mid-round, and only the SECOND debater is ever prompted, after the first presses it.** Making that ask louder runs directly against the 2026-08-22 call that retired auto-capture because the recording scares people away, so it is a product decision rather than a patch.

- **The /spar sign-in card signs you in; the chooser stops being stacked on top of it** (2026-08-23, Aidan twice on the same screenshot, the second time: "i already told u to get rid of this pop up above the one below it"). **Reverses the "pop up sign ins earlier" half of the entry below, which shipped the same morning and lasted about four hours.** It went in two steps because the founder read it twice. Step one, early afternoon: `renderGate()` stopped calling `autoPopAuthModal()`, so the shared chooser no longer opened itself the moment the gate stood. Step two, this pass, because the complaint survived step one: the gate's OWN buttons still routed through `openAuthModal()`, so a click on a card button labelled "Continue with Google" was answered by a modal whose biggest button said the same three words. Same duplicate, one gesture later, which is why removing only the auto-pop read as nothing having changed. **The reasoning that holds whichever way the gate flips next: the modal and the card are the same offer.** Google over email over Google over email, so the pop bought no path that was not already on screen, and it covered the half the modal does not have — who is waiting live right now (`/api/spar-queue`, real, unpadded), that the camera is optional and Avatar mode exists, the under-18 line. A chooser that hides the reason to sign in is a worse door than the card underneath it. **What the buttons do now:** Google runs Google directly, with the anonymous-account linking and the popup-blocked redirect fallback that were already in the file for the no-modal path. Email opens a form ON the card (name, email, "Email me a sign-in link"), so the emailed path never needs the modal either. Name is collected rather than skipped because the person on the other bench reads it, and an emailed link with no name seats a blank. **The send is NOT reimplemented on the page.** `auth-modal.js` now exports `debatableSendSignInLink(email, name)`, factored out of its own submit handler, so both surfaces stash the same `debateos-emaillink-*` keys that `completeEmailLink()` reads back on the return trip and cannot drift apart; rejections carry a `userMessage` so a caller never renders a raw Firebase string. **This does not repeal the 2026-08-12 one-chooser rule**, which exists so no page grows a second auth implementation: the chooser is still the fallback inside both handlers for a page that loads without `auth-modal.js`, and it is still the only sign-in surface on every page that does not already show these choices — /watch, /live and /debate-chat were checked and none of them render their own Google button, so their modal is a chooser rather than a duplicate. **Rider that made this look half-fixed in testing: `.gate-guest` and `.gate-email` each set a `display`, which beats the UA's `[hidden]` rule**, so the inline form rendered before it was asked for and the button it replaces stayed on screen underneath it. Same trap as the /predict `[hidden]` guard. Verified on production signed out: no modal at load, no modal on either button, the form opens in place, both validation branches and the send's success and error branches exercised against a stubbed provider.

- **The alias becomes the name on every surface, and the bank grows to 9,216 pairs** (2026-08-23, Aidan off his own topbar chip: "on my founder account it still says aidan bc thats the startt of my email i think but it should sticvk weith just founda. - fix the name issues seriosily. add more names to the list too"). The chip was one instance of a fault with the same shape in about twenty places, and the anonymity sweep of 2026-08-22 had not touched any of them because they are code paths rather than copy. **The pattern: `forUser()` already returns a generated alias when nobody has chosen a name, so a guard of the form `if (id.chosen && id.name)` could only ever DOWNGRADE to the real identity.** `topbarName()` had exactly that, then fell to `displayName`, then to the email; a Google account carries a real name, so the bar read "Aidan" on the account whose entire point is that it does not. Four more copies of the identical guard were writing their result into documents other people read: /debate-chat (the queue doc AND the Daily video userName the opponent sees on the tile), /chat and /messages (frozen into `dm_threads` and every message), and `js/community-channels.js` (stamped on every channel message). **Worse were the sites that PREFERRED the real name**, where the alias was only the fallback: /partners published `u.displayName` into the shared team `memberInfo`; voice-debate wrote the account name onto the public leaderboard and onto the **public, indexable credential pages** at /verify and /registry; /open and /tournament registered entrants under their Google name; `index.html` put `displayName || email` into `live_debates` and `shared_cases`; and `live.html` published **the email local part** as a public handle in three places on `live_challenges`. Every one now resolves through one helper (`daPublicName`, plus `daPublicHandle` on live.html) and **no path retains a fallback to a real identity**: with the module missing they return a uid tail, which identifies nobody. **Eight pages that publish a name never loaded `js/public-identity.js` at all** and were relying on a `topbar.js` lazy-load race to define the thing they depend on; they load it themselves now. **The name bank goes 32x32 to 96x96, 1,024 pairs to 9,216**, globally spread per the audience rule, with every ADDED entry screened so it differs from every other by more than one letter (Kai/Kaia, Leila/Layla, Lina/Alina, Nia/Pia were all cut at that screen; three legacy pairs predate it and were left rather than churned). **ADJECTIVES and NOUNS are deliberately untouched**, so the @handle, which is the identifier-shaped half, stays put while display names gain the variety. **DISCONTINUITY, deliberate: the arrays are indexed by `hash % length`, so every account still on a GENERATED alias gets a new display name once.** Names already written onto rows (leaderboard entries, queue docs, messages) keep whatever they were stored with, so a person can briefly read as two names across surfaces; handles do not move, which is what anchors continuity. **Two sites were missed by the code sweep and caught only by loading the live page**, which is the habit worth keeping: `/tournaments` is `tournaments.html`, a DIFFERENT file from `tournament.html`, it is the page the announcement email actually points at, and its register call sent `user.displayName` straight into the public entrant field; and practice.html's leaderboard write used DBIdentity correctly and then fell back to a shortened real name. **Deliberately left alone:** /profile's own name editor and /practice's account panel, where showing which account you are signed into is the surface's whole job, and read-side renderers of already-stored names.

- **The audience can see a room is dead, and say so; a report is evidence, not a verdict** (2026-08-23, Aidan from a /watch screenshot of himself spectating a round nobody was in: "be able to report a round over or something / as audience"). Every LISTING has told a live round from a dead one since the ghost-room fix, off the seat heartbeat both debaters write every 30s (`lastSeenAt`, ~100s window in `watch-live.mjs`, `live.html`, `spar.html`). **Nothing told the person already inside the room.** The entire no-show apparatus built on 2026-08-11, `checkOpponentPresence` plus the "Looking for your debater" strip plus the no-show prompt with a way out, is gated `!isSpectator()` line for line, so a spectator who followed a direct link, or who was watching when both debaters closed their tabs, sat in front of "Speech 1 of 4" and a clock at 5:00 indefinitely. The listing filter cannot help them: a shared link is exactly the path the filter never runs on. **The strip reads the same heartbeat the listings trust**, one constant rather than a second opinion, so the page inside the room can never disagree with the list that offered it. **The report is gated on absence, not on numbers.** One viewer must never end a round two people are in, so the outcome turns on whether anyone with standing to object is present, read from the server's own copy of the beat and never from anything the caller sends: no fresh beat, the round is marked over on the first report, because nobody is there to object and the room is already invisible to every listing, so this only corrects what the page shows the people still looking at it; fresh beat, nothing closes, the report is counted, and the debaters see that the audience thinks it is finished. Same posture as the machine NSFW flag (2026-08-18) and the motion-change consent gate (2026-08-12), and the same sentence as the audience Topics tab: the crowd sets the agenda, it does not seize the round. **The close is reversible with no undo endpoint**, because an ending only stands while it is NEWER than the last seat beat: a debater who comes back falsifies it by beating, and a round that dies again gets the button again rather than an ending nobody filed for it. That comparison is why `endedAtMs` is a plain number beside the timestamp. **`status` is deliberately untouched** — it has many consumers and a wrong value there breaks the ballot path, so this writes its own fields. Any verified account may report, anonymous included, since watching is open to strangers by design and the gate is absence rather than identity; a seated debater is turned away, because they end their own round with their own controls and their own live heartbeat would be the thing blocking them. Seated debaters do get the half they never had: the no-show prompt covers an opponent who never arrives, the strip covers one who leaves mid-round. **Two guards copied deliberately from `checkOpponentPresence`, both learned the hard way there:** the Daily room outranks the doc beat (a debater whose heartbeat has not round-tripped is still visibly in the room, and calling that room empty over a live picture of them is the exact failure that guard exists for), and "no beat has ever landed" is only evidence once the viewer has been in the room long enough for one to have arrived. **Verified, and the verification earned its keep:** 19 cases over the state machine, then four deliberate mutations one at a time. Three were caught and the fourth was NOT, which found a real defect rather than a weak test — `endedAtMs` was being stored for a comparison the code never made, so a round that ended, resumed and died again would have shown a stale ending. Fixed, and the suite catches it now. Contrast measured on the composited fill in all three of this page's real themes (grey, crimson, light; "dark" is not one of them), worst 7.62.

- **The globe gets its 24h number back, and only that one** (2026-08-23, Aidan: "bring back the number of how many live in last 24 hrs yeah"). Partially reverses the 2026-08-22 caption cut, which went in two steps the same evening: first both padded figures (24h visits, Google sign-ups), then the remaining static line. The visit count returns; the sign-up figure stays gone, so the globe carries exactly one number instead of a stack of labels. Two things were kept from the version that was removed because both are load-bearing: the noun is **visits**, not people, since `presence-live` counts SESSIONS and a sid is per-tab and per-visit, so one person in two tabs is two and a return after lunch is two; and the element renders empty until the fetch answers, so a failed or slow `/api/presence-live` leaves the globe standing alone rather than printing a zero. `online24` still carries `PRESENCE_BASELINE`, so this is a declared liveness caption and not a measurement — the 2026-08-14 rule stands and it must never be quoted externally. Restoring was markup plus a setter and no new styles, because the `.story-globe-caption` CSS was deliberately left dormant when the markup went.

- **Availability actually rides the whole app: the pill was on half the pages, and could not mount on the rest** (2026-08-23, Aidan off a /watch screenshot: "the avaliable button should still be there and true - avaliable goes across the ENTIRE USAGE OF THE APP"). The auto-enlist entry from earlier the same day is correct about intent and was only ever half delivered, for two independent reasons. **(1) Coverage.** The Available pill, the DM bell and the background matcher all live in `js/notifications.js`, which was hand-included on 52 pages. `topbar.js` does NOT pull it, so "has the topbar" was not coverage, and on /watch and the rest of the un-included pages the pill simply was not there. This is the same trap as the 2026-08-14 track.js finding, on a different shared script; topbar.js now lazy-injects notifications.js the way it already injects auth-modal.js and audience-mode.js, and the module's own `__daNotificationsLoaded` guard makes the 52 existing includes harmless. **(2) The pill had no fallback mount.** `placePill` only ever looked for `.ui-topbar-right`, `.app-topbar-right` or `.bar-links`; with none present it retried for six seconds and gave up SILENTLY, where the bell falls back to a floating chip. So on any page with bespoke chrome the pill could never appear however the script arrived there. A floating fallback was added, and then immediately constrained, because floating is wrong wherever a bar already exists: /atlas carries its own fixed `.topbar` at z-index 1200 and /tournaments a sticky one, so a chip pinned top-right would paint over them, which is exactly the failure `placeBell`'s comment records for /live-round. **A page with its own bar now names where the pill goes** via `[data-da-pill-slot]` (`="end"` appends, for a slot that also holds the wordmark), added to /tournaments, /atlas, /oral-exam-prep and the two judge pages; /safety has no header at all and is the single page that genuinely floats. **Rider:** /newvoice and /room-judge were missing from `ON_ROUND`, so someone in a voice round or judging someone else's round stayed matchable and could be pulled into a second round. Harmless while the pill could not render there, and reachable the moment it could. **Verification limit, stated rather than implied:** the browser pane in this session ran hidden at a 0x0 viewport, which throttles `setInterval` to 1/sec and makes geometry, screenshots and CSSOM reads unreliable, so the floating chip's appearance was never seen. Mounting was verified live by DOM (pill inside `.topnav` on /tournaments, last child after brand and back link on /judge-integrity), the injected CSS was validated by evaluating the real concatenation offline, and the gate was verified by running the module's own regexes: on /watch the pill shows for any named account. It reads hidden in that pane only because the session there is anonymous, which is the standing named-account rule for live pairing.

- **Bet-on-who-wins is back, tokens only** (2026-08-23, Aidan: "bring back bet on who will win 'tokens for now'"). Partially reverses the 2026-08-22 "betting removed entirely" entry, along the seam that entry's own commit message named: "we can just encourage a fun game tbh for now." The fun game is `/predict`'s free points board, the judgment-backed economy (server-authoritative `predict.mjs`, pari-mutuel, settles only on `verdictSource:'server'`, voids and refunds on a split, points non-purchasable and non-redeemable). **What came back:** the `/predict` route serves again (both netlify.toml mirrors) and the green Bet tab returns to the rail in `topbar.js`, per the restore notes both files carried. **What stayed off, deliberately:** everything with money in it. `CASH_ROUNDS_LIVE` and `BOUNTY_PAYMENTS_LIVE` stay false; `/floor`, `/ladder`, `/bounties` and `/bet-on-your-words` stay 301'd; and predict.html itself was swept tokens-first, because the page as retired led with "Win the argument. Take the money.", a cash-rounds section, and a "Play for money" primary CTA that would have advertised a checkout the env flag refuses. The hero now leads with the points board, the cash section and the pot drawer ship `hidden` (markup kept as dormant history, the cash IIFE stands down when its section is hidden so nothing polls `/api/cash-round`), the legal drawer's bounty and cash rows came off, and the meta descriptions dropped the prize-pot half. A page-level `[hidden]{display:none!important}` guard rides along per the standing UA-hidden trap. The page keeps its noindex, its early-build feedback note, and stays out of the sitemap. **Restore path for the cash lanes, when that flips again:** remove the two `hidden` attributes, re-enable the cash IIFE guard, restore the legal rows from git history, flip the env flags, and write the entry.

- **Live pairing requires a Google or email account again, and the sign-in chooser pops on arrival** (2026-08-23, Aidan: "actually require google or gmail sign in to use the live omegle debating feature etc - pop up sign ins earlier - we need conversaion to be much better"). **Fifth flip on this gate (08-18 wall → 08-19 trial → 08-22 morning wall → 08-22 evening trial → this), and per the entry directly below, a close needs its own entry saying what changed. What changed: the founder's stated priority is conversion.** A guest who plays two rounds and bounces leaves no account, no way to reach them, and no record to come back to; sign-ups are now the number, so the wall moves back to the door. The trial's own conversion data was never read before this call, so this is a priority call, not a data call, and it is recorded as one. **What moved, the same three layers as the 08-22 morning close, restored by reverting `249bc8b1`:** `GUEST_FREE_ROUNDS` defaults to 0 in `spar-pair.mjs` (env-overridable, refuse-before-Firestore-read, so the closed lane costs nothing; `GUEST_FREE_ROUNDS=2` in the Netlify env is still the no-deploy reopen switch) and to 0 in /spar's client mirror; `mintGuestSession()`'s allowance-0 guard is live again, so a signed-out visitor meets the gate instead of being minted a guest; `firestore.rules` `matchmaking_queue` create is back to `isNamedAccount()` (deployed). The gate keeps the `/api/spar-queue` live count. **The new half is the "pop up sign ins earlier" ask (RETIRED the same day — see the entry at the top of this log; the function is kept, uncalled, because this gate has flipped five times):** `renderGate()` calls `autoPopAuthModal()`, which opens the shared chooser (Google, emailed link, email/password) the moment the gate stands, once per browser session (`da-spar-auth-pop` in sessionStorage — a modal that re-opens on every render is a nag, and dismissing is an answer), with the gate card behind it as the fallback surface so closing the modal loses nothing. auth-modal.js is lazy-loaded by topbar.js, so the pop polls for `openAuthModal` up to ~6s and stands down if the gate disappears or the script never arrives. GA4: new `spar_auth_autopop` beside the existing `spar_gate_view` split. **Doorway pages swept in the same push**, including two spots the 08-22 morning close missed because they were authored later: /how-it-works' opponent-picker line (authored + `data-plain` variants both promised two guest live rounds) and /prep's free FAQ (visible + JSON-LD). /debate-online, /debate-strangers, /omegle-alternative and /how-it-works' FAQ + JSON-LD ride the revert. **Watch, from the door side:** `spar_gate_view` reason `no_session` and `spar_auto_autopop` against sign-ins, and queue joins against the trial era. If joins collapse without sign-ups rising, the tested fallback is still the env flip, but the next flip in EITHER direction should read the GA4 numbers this entry names before moving, because five flips in six days means the gate is being steered by mood rather than measurement.

- **The guest trial reopens, hours after it closed: free to try, then the sign-in ask** (2026-08-22. **CLOSED 2026-08-23 by the entry directly above: named accounts required again, the chooser pops on arrival.** Second call the same day, Aidan: "make it free to do the omegle feature without an account at first then encourage sign in"). **Reverses the morning's "Live debate requires sign-in again" entry below and restores the 2026-08-19 two-round shape. This is the fourth flip on this exact gate in five days (08-18 wall → 08-19 trial → 08-22 morning wall → 08-22 evening trial), so the standing posture is now written down: the 08-19 trial is the tested resting state, and the next close needs its own entry saying what changed, not a re-litigation.** What moved, the same three layers, back: `GUEST_FREE_ROUNDS` defaults to 2 again in `spar-pair.mjs` (env-overridable; `GUEST_FREE_ROUNDS=0` in the Netlify env is the no-deploy kill switch, and the refuse-before-Firestore-read guard from the morning stays so the closed state costs nothing) and to 2 in /spar's client mirror; `mintGuestSession()`'s allowance-0 guard goes inert, so a signed-out visitor is minted a guest identity and lands in the queue; `firestore.rules` `matchmaking_queue` create widens back to `isSignedIn()` (deployed and verified against the live project). The refusal message is now conditional: a guest who spent the trial hears "your free guest rounds are used", and only the env-closed state says "live rounds need an account", so the wall never describes rounds nobody had. **What the morning's close leaves behind, kept on purpose because it serves the "encourage sign in" half:** the keyless `/api/spar-queue` live count and the gate's "N others are waiting live right now" line now decorate the POST-TRIAL gate, so the ask a spent guest meets has both "keep the rounds you just played" and a person waiting on the other side of it; the `used > 0` guard in `gateSpent()` stays; the doorway pages (/debate-online, /debate-strangers, /how-it-works, /omegle-alternative) are swept BACK to the guest-rounds copy, visible FAQ and JSON-LD together. The age-band gate shipped the same day composes cleanly: `/api/age-band` deliberately accepts anonymous uids and the attestation survives account linking, so a guest answers the age question once and it follows them into the account they later create. Everything else the 2026-08-18 sweep closed stays closed (/live seats, /partners, /debate-chat's own gate, the background pill, DMs). **Watch the number the 08-19 entry named:** guest matches against conversions at the spent wall, `spar_gate_view` reason `guest_rounds_spent` vs `no_session` in GA4.

- **De-vibe sweep: a full-site audit pass closes what earlier sweeps missed** (2026-08-22, Aidan: "analyze whats vibe coded from the code, design, usability etc and fix it"). Three parallel audits (copy/brand, design system, code correctness) over origin/main plus a live walk of the top pages. Fixed in one push (`8cf26917`, SW v2736): /spar's sign-in gate still rotated the DELETED face55 into view (a broken image on the first surface a signed-out visitor meets); `onboarding.js` loaded firestore-compat 10.5.0 under the same element id `notifications.js` uses for 10.13.2, so ten top pages raced on which SDK version they ran (watch.html was pinned to 10.5.0 too); **the 2026-08-22 anonymity sweep had missed report.html** (UChicago, UWC, "argued at the top of the country", "Built by a debater") and ambassadors.html ("a national-circuit champion") — all moved to the sanctioned "built from inside competitive debate", plus report.html's "moat against ChatGPT" zinger; tokens.html's contact address was the CANCELLED devilsadvocateteam mailbox on the one page with a live Stripe purchase; prediction-market-debate's nav + hero CTA pointed at /markets, which never existed; format-count selling ("fifteen formats") came off practice/debate-an-ai/online-debate-camp/prep per the standing rule (enumerated lists stay); judge-draft.js still carried five Crimson Pro stacks that rendered GEORGIA on /spar, and live-round's recording-board + share-poster canvases still drew 18 lines in Georgia/ui-serif that bake into published replays (now Inter/Archivo + Source Serif 4, the italic dropped); index.html's 6rem round timer asked for IBM Plex Mono, which nothing loads (now Geist Mono); live-round's #kickoffGo was light-green-on-pale-green on light theme (the #audFab trap class, third instance); the transcript-consent modal sat in a seven-way tie at z-index 2147483000 where a later-injected overlay could paint over a consent gate (lifted to ...050); ui.css's docblock still said the display face is Inter while the token deliberately leads DM Sans (5b69dd1c is newer; doc fixed, not the token). **Found and deliberately NOT changed, for Aidan:** two of the three published /watch replays show the founder's face with the name label "Aidan" baked into the video, which is the largest surviving anonymity leak and an unpublish decision, not a code edit; masterclass.html sells a $19/mo tier that exists in no canon (pricing is locked, so it needs a call, not a correction); /pricing's hero still leads "Prediction credits. Prize play" after betting came off the site the same day (positioning has flipped four times in four days, so it was left for an explicit call); index.html's purple/green action buttons and the ~211 inert Fraunces declarations are recorded in this entry's audit rather than swept, since each is a big-diff cosmetic pass. The rebase reproduced the sw.js dedupe trap AGAIN (origin landed on the same v2735 mid-flight; caught by the standing check, re-bumped to v2736 by hand).

- **The age band moves server-side: write-once on the account, and the matchmaker checks its own record** (2026-08-22, hours after the entry below shipped; Aidan chose the hardening when shown the gap). **Supersedes one paragraph of the entry below** — the one that says the band "is allowed to ride the client-written queue doc" because "both forgery directions reduce to lying about your age, which no storage location fixes." That argument missed the direction that matters: with a client-written field, an adult never has to lie at the door at all — attest adult on the account, flip `ageBand:'minor'` on the queue doc from devtools, enter the teen pool per round, flip back, no record anywhere. Two parallel sessions built this feature the same evening with different data models (the exact 2026-08-18 drop-in-queue failure shape); this pass RECONCILED them rather than shipping a second architecture: the shipped UX survives untouched (js/age-gate.js modal, `da-age-band` in localStorage synced by prefs-sync, the advisory `ageBand` on queue docs that client pre-filters and the waiting count read), and underneath it the answer now ALSO lands in **`age_bands/{uid}` via new `/api/age-band` — write-once, admin-SDK only (no firestore.rules entry, so the default deny covers it; no rules deploy needed)** — and `spar-pair.mjs` enforces from that record alone for BOTH sides of every proposed pair. A 409 `AGE_BAND_LOCKED` hands back the recorded band and clients adopt it, so an edited localStorage cannot even disagree with the record for long. **Semantics tightened with the source of truth:** both sides must now hold a server record before any pair is proposed (the entry below let adult pair with unattested); a caller without one gets 403 `AGE_BAND_REQUIRED`, which new clients SELF-HEAL by re-POSTing the stored answer (throttled), so everyone who answered the modal in the hours before this shipped converges silently — only a genuinely unanswered account sees the modal again. A peer without a record is skipPeer'd and heals on their own next attempt. **Transition cost, accepted:** a client on a stale SW cache that pairs before refreshing sees a pairing-trouble banner instead of a match until its cache turns over; the queue is thin and the window is hours. **What write-once buys for the schools conversation:** the CHSSA answer is now "the age answer is locked to the account and the matchmaker checks the server's own record for both people", and a false attestation is a deliberate, recorded, permanent act on the account rather than a field flip — an adult who bands as minor is also locked OUT of adult rounds. **Unchanged limits, still stated on /safety:** attestation is not verification, a fresh account can still lie once at the door, and the moderation layer stays the backstop. **The follow-up flips:** the /settings band control the entry below names is now the WRONG follow-up (a self-serve toggle would undo the point); a mis-click is the contact email, where a human changes the doc. Band read fails CLOSED per attempt (`age_check_failed`, soft) — the reverse of the guest lane's fail-open, because pairing across the line is the one outcome the layer exists to prevent.

- **Live pairing separates attested minors from adults** (2026-08-22, applying the CHSSA rejection's student-safety point; the copy half shipped the same day as /safety and the sign-up claim rewrite). The matchmaking pool had zero age awareness anywhere: a 13-year-old and an adult stranger genuinely shared one queue, on camera, and the terms' 13-17 supervision rule had nothing at the door enforcing it. Now everyone answers ONE age question before their first live queue join (js/age-gate.js, asked once ever, `da-age-band` in localStorage, synced across devices via prefs-sync), the answer rides every `matchmaking_queue` doc as `ageBand`, and **spar-pair.mjs refuses any pair where an attested minor would meet anyone who did not attest minor** (`age_mismatch`, a soft refusal like `blocked_peer`). One chokepoint covers /spar, /debate-chat, and the background "Spar live" pill; clients also pre-filter candidates so a minor's "N waiting" count is honest and no doomed proposals burn the poll loop. **The claim is worded as "never KNOWINGLY paired" on /safety, deliberately:** attestation is the ceiling of what a site can check without ID documents, both forgery directions reduce to lying about your age, and no storage location fixes that, which is why the band is allowed to ride the client-written queue doc (unlike the guest counter there is no metering incentive). **Accepted costs, stated:** an attested minor waits longer, possibly indefinitely, until other minors are in the pool — the AI surfaces stay open to them and the ask says so up front; a mis-click currently has no self-serve undo (the contact email is the recourse; a /settings control is the follow-up). **Deliberately NOT touched:** the Aug 29 tournament drop-in queue, where a pool split at a ~10-person field would strand every minor — the rules require parent or guardian permission for under-18 entrants and that stays the event's posture; revisit when fields are big enough to split.

- **Betting is removed ENTIRELY: pages 301 away, real-money lanes off, the fun stays in prizes and the game** (2026-08-22, Aidan: "remove betting entirely from the site tbh we cant do it - we can just encourage a fun game tbh for now"). **This supersedes the entry directly below**, which took betting out of the nav the same evening and deliberately left the pages live for direct links. This pass takes the pages too. What is gone: `/predict`, `/floor`, `/ladder` and `/bet-on-your-words` 301 to `/watch`, `/bounties` (and `/bounty`) 301 to `/tournaments`, all forced, both netlify.toml mirrors, `.html` forms included; `/bet-on-your-words` and `/bounties` leave the sitemap; and every remaining betting reference in live copy came off — `get-paid-to-debate.html` (prediction-board paragraphs, the Play Points FAQ entry, the gambling answer rewritten around the skill contest, visible copy and FAQPage JSON-LD moved together per the standing rule), `tokens.html`'s Play Points bullet, `season-1.html`'s foot note, and `everyone-has-an-opinion.html`'s cross-link. **The real-money lanes are switched off at the env layer:** `CASH_ROUNDS_LIVE` and `BOUNTY_PAYMENTS_LIVE` are `false` in the production context, so a hand-rolled POST cannot open a new cash round or bounty while the site says betting does not exist. Checked before flipping: `cash_rounds` and `bounties` hold ZERO documents, `/predict` took one bet ever (play points, anonymous), so nobody is owed anything and nothing strands. **What stays, and why:** `ENTRY_PAYMENTS_LIVE` stays true and every tournament surface is untouched — the Open is a prize contest, not betting, and it is the "fun game" money-intent traffic now routes to; `/payouts` (the Stripe Connect rail) stays reachable because it is how owed prize money reaches a winner, not a pitch; `/get-paid-to-debate` stays live and indexed as the tournament money page, now swept clean; the page files, functions (`predict.mjs`, `floor-*`, `value-market.mjs`, `cash-round*`, `bounty*`, `payout-account.mjs`, `lib/cash-round.mjs` and its pre-commit guard), Firestore rules and ledgers all stay in the repo as dormant history. **Restore path, if the positioning flips a fifth time:** re-add the nav rows (notes inline in topbar.js), revert the redirect blocks in both netlify.toml mirrors, re-add the two sitemap entries, and set the two env flags back to true. This is the fourth surfacing flip in four days and the first that removes the pages; per the note below, do not put betting back in permanent chrome without confirmation that the positioning is actually settled.
