- **The week-of rally email went to the whole list: 259 sent, zero errors, and the "no verified Resend domain" claim is corrected** (2026-08-22, Aidan: "send out an email to all sign ups ... announce the 29th tournament, the award, and wanting direct feedback, ppl using at once so the spawning works. bc your early, we'll note your role for when it grows into a company"). New `admin-open-rally.mjs` at `/api/admin/open-rally`, the same dry-run / confirm / batch / per-user-stamp posture as `admin-open-announce.mjs` with its OWN stamp (`openRallySentAt`), so the two campaigns can never block or double each other. The copy carries the five asked-for beats inside the published truth: Saturday Aug 29 with 10 AM ET doors, free entry, $100/$50/$25 scaling to $850 past 50 entrants (the scale-up doubles as the forward-this line), the three rush hours as the "everyone on at once so pairing works" mechanic, a reply-for-feedback ask with `Reply-To` pointed at the founder's gmail (the standing contact-email exception; From and sign-off are anonymous per the same-day founder sweep), and the early-member note written as a record being kept, never a grant of anything. **Correction to the two entries below that say no Resend domain is verified and outbound mail is failing: sending WORKS.** The prod `RESEND_API_KEY` is a send-only key, so `GET /domains` answers 401 (previously read as "empty list") and `verifiedSenderDomains()` falls back — but a real send from `hello@debateai.com` returns 200 and lands in a Gmail INBOX, verified end to end with a single-address `testTo` mode on the new endpoint before any batch ran. `EMAIL_FROM` in the env points at `hello@itsdebatable.com`, which is NOT known to be verified; `OPEN_RALLY_FROM` is set to `hello@debateai.com` in the prod env and that is what the run used. Result: 1,572 Auth accounts, 259 with an email and no opt-out, 259 sent across 13 batches, 0 errors, run log at `config/open_rally_state`. Do not re-run it (the stamp makes a re-run a no-op anyway); the next campaign gets its own stamp field.

- **The landing goes light for everyone, the board goes real-stills-only, and the verdict card speaks plain English** (2026-08-22, Aidan off a screenshot of the dark first screen: "rethink this page, make it the light mode as well. change images used bc they look ai right now. use the real ones. and increase sizing of buttons / images to guide to the omegle featrue", followed mid-session by "really improve this page bc its confusing to read ... not bet against the pot type language. its the AI ballot etc (also dont use phrasing ballot explain it as decision justification or something)"). Four coupled changes, one push. **(1) Theme: the 2026-08-19 70/30 light/crimson bucket is retired.** Every unset visitor gets light, in both the landing early-paint script and topbar.js; visitors the split AUTO-bucketed into crimson (da-theme-ab === 'crimson') migrate back to light exactly once, gated by `da-theme-light-v3`, so a later deliberate toggle to dark sticks and is never re-flipped. `?themeAb=crimson` survives as the QA force for the dark surface, which keeps full token coverage. Verified both migration cases in a browser: auto-bucketed flips once, deliberate toggler keeps crimson. **(2) The example board is REAL STILLS ONLY.** All 34 ROUNDS entries were re-cast onto the eight consented webcam people (Marcus, Mateo, Luka, Miguel, Timothee, Emeka, Dev, Danylo) plus the illustrated Anonymous avatar on its one motion; the generated bank read as AI to Aidan and is dormant, not deleted. RFD names and pronouns moved with every seat (validated by script: winner matches, no absent name, no leftover she/her), and the opener rule is now "never open on the Anonymous avatar" since the old mixed-gender rule has nothing left to match. **Accepted cost, on the record: the visible cast is all male until a new consented batch exists. Do not fix that by reintroducing generated faces; fix it with a new batch.** (3) **The board sheds its market clothes for newcomers.** The "Example round" label is back (cut 2026-08-12, restored because a cold visitor needs telling what the card is), the who-viewers-think-is-ahead percentage strip is hidden (`.fs-board .fs-odds{display:none}`, engine intact since it drives the clock), and the payoff card reads "AI judge's decision" with the winner plus a plain-words justification pulled from the rfd strings, whose winner-stem is stripped so it answers why ("Because he proves...", "By defining..."). This resurrects the why-line cut that same morning, on Aidan's explicit evening ask. The winner line is always green now; the old amber "room disagreed" tint was market signalling on a strip that no longer renders. (4) **"Ballot" is swept from this page's reader-facing copy and JSON-LD**, ~50 strings, in favour of decision language ("the AI judge's written decision", "decides who won, and says why"); the one testimonial quote keeps its author's own words, and CSS class names / ids are untouched. The Debate CTA and the board column also grew (wrap 1200→1280, columns .82/1.18) so the stranger-match door and the two real faces are the biggest things on the screen. Commit `c9fb44ca`, SW v2730 after two rebases in one hour, the second of which reproduced the sw.js dedupe trap exactly as the AGENTS.md warning describes (origin landed on the same v2729; caught by the standing check, re-bumped to v2730 by hand). NOTE for future passes: this page's word for the judge's output is now "decision"; the rest of the site still says ballot and sweeping it is its own pass, per surface, not a blind sed.

- **"Get pinged when someone is live" was fully built and dark: two missing env vars lit it, and the waiter now gets the real numbers** (2026-08-22, Aidan: "lets setup a 'get a text when someone is live' feature where we text ppl / ping them when someone is live to match - and encourage the other peson to wait"). The whole chain already existed — `go-live.mjs` fan-out, `notify_prefs` opt-in, `push-subscribe`, the sw.js push handler, the /spar "Get pinged when someone goes live" card — and none of it had ever delivered a single notification, because `VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY` was never set in the Netlify env: `pushConfigured()` was false and every layer no-opped cleanly, exactly as its own comments promise. **18 people had already clicked Turn on into a void.** A fresh keypair is in the env now (the baked-in public key's private half never existed anywhere recoverable), and those 18 self-heal into real subscriptions on their next visit, because `daRegisterPush()` already runs on load for named users and previously had nothing to subscribe against. **Guests can broadcast too, and the gate that makes that safe is global, not per-uid.** Anonymous uids are free and unlimited to mint, so a per-uid cooldown on anonymous broadcasters is no cooldown at all; all anonymous callers share ONE 5-minute gate (`live_now/_anon_broadcast_meta`, claimed in a transaction, failing closed) on top of the per-uid 10-minute one, and a guest's notification text is fully server-constructed ("Someone is live on Debatable") so there is no name to spoof. Verified on production with minted-then-deleted anonymous accounts: first guest broadcast reached 18 recipients, the same guest hit the per-uid cooldown, a second fresh guest hit the global gate. **CORRECTED 2026-09-04: "reached 18 recipients" counted sends, not deliveries; the VAPID public key in the env did not match the private key and every one of those sends was refused by the push service. See the 2026-09-04 entry at the top of this log.** One false negative on the way was a warm pre-deploy function instance answering with the old code minutes after the deploy activated; re-run before diagnosing. **Note the same-day sign-in reversal above makes the guest lane dormant on /spar** (guests cannot reach the queue at `GUEST_FREE_ROUNDS=0`), which is fine: every current joiner is named and broadcasts through the per-uid path, and the guest lane reactivates with the trial's env flip, already tested. **The waiter's side is honest numbers, not the invented stat.** The ask suggested "90% of matched rounds took 30 seconds or more"; measured over the 124 matched queue sits on record it is 58%, median wait 43s, 87% inside two minutes. The wait rail now says that ("Most matched rounds waited over 30 seconds before connecting, and nearly nine in ten matched inside two minutes. Leaving early is how rounds get missed."), and when the join broadcast lands it shows "We pinged N people who asked to be told when someone goes live. Give them a minute to arrive.", which is the strongest true reason to keep the tab open. The chime already existed (sparChime + daAlert); the rail copy carries it. `spar_match_found` / `spar_match_made` now carry `wait_sec` so the stat is recomputable from GA4 instead of a one-off Firestore read (the queue doc's `joinedAt` is a heartbeat, not a join time — do not derive waits from it), and new `spar_golive_pinged` records fan-out size against conversions. **SMS was the literal ask and is deliberately NOT built:** there is no Twilio anywhere in the repo, and the account, phone number, and A2P registration are Aidan's to create, not code. When it exists, the fan-out point is `go-live.mjs`, beside the push send. An email lane was considered and skipped: no Resend domain is currently verified (see the anonymity entry), so it would be dead on arrival.

- **Betting comes off the site again, hours after the Bet tab went green** (2026-08-22, Aidan: "for now remove betting from website"). Reverses the 2026-08-20 restore and this morning's green-pill entry directly below; same posture as the 2026-08-19 de-surfacing, and "for now" is again the operative phrase. What came off, all in `js/topbar.js` (the only chrome carrying betting; landing, /home and /leaderboard were checked and already clean, and the landing's Floor band has been `display:none` since 2026-07-31): the rail's green Bet tab (/predict), the Explore Bounties row (/bounties), and the Explore Money row (/get-paid-to-debate), which returned on 08-20 explicitly as part of the betting positioning and so leaves with it. Restore notes sit inline on all three rows; the `money` green-pill render branch and the ICONS entries stay as dormant code so restoring is re-adding one line per row. **What deliberately did NOT move:** the pages themselves (/predict, /bounties, /floor, /ladder, /get-paid-to-debate) stay live for direct links, matching every prior nav demotion; the cash-round, bounty and payout backends and their env flags are untouched (`CASH_ROUNDS_LIVE` etc. stay true, so a held link still works); tournament surfaces (Tournaments, Enter a tournament, the Open ribbon and modal) are prize events, not betting, and keep their entries; and the SEO cluster (/bet-on-your-words, /prediction-market-debate and their cross-links) is left as it was after the 08-19 pass. This is the third surfacing flip in four days (off 08-19, on 08-20, green 08-22 morning, off 08-22); whoever restores it next should note the cadence and confirm the positioning is actually settled before putting Bet back in permanent chrome. (2026-08-22, Aidan: "make the bet button on nav green actually, so it screams money" plus "3 sections minimally highlighted ... the past rounds like youtube thumbnails on the left, leaderboard in middle, and debate the AI on the right (use the image of the her movie)"). Two small coupled changes. (1) The rail's Bet link moves off the faint-red `hot` treatment onto a new `money` flag in `js/topbar.js`: a filled green pill (#16a34a, white text, green pulse dot), deliberately the ONLY green in the bar so money reads as money at a glance; red stays the brand-action color everywhere else, and `hot` stays available for a future headline tab. (2) The landing's three quick doors above the Full tour button (shipped as plain pill anchors earlier the same day, see the entry below) are now preview cards: `/landing-shot-live.jpg` with a video-style play badge for Watch past rounds, `/img/leaderboard-portraits.jpg` for The leaderboard, and the Her still for Debate an AI, with the film credit riding the image per the standing attribution rule. Hrefs, data-cta tags, and the hashchange gate-opener are untouched; under 700px the cards go horizontal (small thumb left, label right) so three tiles don't eat the phone fold.

- **Live debate requires sign-in again, and the gate shows who is waiting** (2026-08-22 morning. **REVERSED the same evening — see the guest-trial-reopens entry above: the two-round trial is back and the named-only layers below are re-widened. The /api/spar-queue live count and the gate survive as the post-trial wall.** Original entry, kept as the record: Aidan: "require google sign in for using live debate feature actually - i want sign ups, and say '4 others waiting live' or something before people sign up so they feel encouraged"). **Closes the 2026-08-19 two-round guest trial below and restores the 2026-08-18 named-account posture, ahead of a paid push.** The reasoning flipped with the job: the trial existed to hook organic omegle-intent visitors who would bounce off a wall; an ad push is paid traffic, and a guest who plays two rounds and bounces leaves nothing — no account, no way to reach them, no record to come back to. Sign-ups are the point of the spend, so the wall moves back to the door. **What moved, all three layers together:** `GUEST_FREE_ROUNDS` defaults to 0 in `spar-pair.mjs` (a guest is refused with `SIGN_IN_REQUIRED` before any Firestore read, so the closed lane costs nothing) and to 0 in /spar's client mirror; `mintGuestSession()` refuses at allowance 0, so a signed-out visitor goes straight to the gate instead of being minted an anonymous session; `firestore.rules` `matchmaking_queue` create is back to `isNamedAccount()`. The guest machinery is KEPT dormant, not deleted: re-opening the trial is `GUEST_FREE_ROUNDS=2` in the Netlify env plus the client constant plus widening the rules create — the three move together or guests get a doc nothing will pair. `gateSpent()` now also requires `used > 0`, because with the allowance at 0 an anonymous session minted by another page would otherwise be told "that was your last free round" about rounds it never had. **The new half: the gate is no longer a wall in front of an unknown room.** New keyless `/api/spar-queue` returns the REAL count of queue docs heartbeated in the last 120s (single-field range on joinedAt, status filtered in memory, shared-cached 15s, no floor, no padding — someone who signs in off "3 others are waiting live" has to actually meet somebody). The gate renders it with a green pulse above the sign-in buttons; when the queue is quiet it falls back to the last-24h visit figure from `/api/presence-live`, worded as visits per the globe-caption honesty rule, with the dot dimmed grey. GA4 `spar_gate_live_count` carries the waiting figure so conversion can be read against what the visitor was shown. **Doorway pages swept in the same push, both copies:** /debate-strangers, /how-it-works, /omegle-alternative and /debate-online all still promised guest rounds in visible FAQ and JSON-LD (the 2026-08-18 lesson: a product gate is not shipped until the pages advertising the ungated behaviour move too); /debate-online's hero "play a couple of rounds as a guest first" became "see who is waiting in the live queue". **Watch the same number as both prior entries, from the door side:** gate views (`spar_gate_view` reason `no_session`) against sign-ins. If the ad traffic bounces off the gate without converting, the answer per the 2026-08-18 entry is a better gate, and the trial's env flip is sitting there as the tested fallback.

- **The payout rail: cash rounds pay out through Stripe Connect, and "by hand" stops meaning what it meant** (2026-08-22, Aidan: "setup real money system for betting im ready i got lawyers to approve etc"). **First, the disambiguation, because "betting" names two things here and they sit on opposite sides of the line the cash-round entry below draws.** Real money has been moving on rounds since that entry: `CASH_ROUNDS_LIVE=true` in the production context, on `sk_live_`, alongside entry, bounty and tokens. So nothing needed switching on. What was actually missing was the half after the verdict, and the code said so in as many words: payouts were "paid by hand until Connect and KYC exist." **This is Connect and KYC existing.** The OTHER reading, spectators staking cash on somebody else's round, was NOT built and is still refused for the reasons in that entry; `/predict`, `/floor` and `/ladder` stay points-only. A verbal approval does not change that, because what makes third-party wagering lawful is a licensing posture rather than an opinion, and because we run the judge that would settle it. Aidan picked the payout rail from a three-way choice.

  **What "by hand" actually cost, and what replaced it.** Paying a winner meant obtaining their bank details out of band, which is a data-collection problem wearing a logistics problem's clothes. Now the winner onboards to a **Stripe Express** account at `/payouts`; Stripe collects the identity documents, the bank details and the tax form and runs the KYC itself. What comes back to us is an account id and two booleans, and a payout is one API call. We never see a bank number.

  **What deliberately did NOT change: who starts it.** The lib's decision 4 says money never moves on an AI ballot alone and an admin sends it; the endpoint comment framed the human as a stopgap until Connect existed. Those two readings conflict, and on real money the conservative one wins: `pay` now sends a genuine Stripe transfer instead of recording that somebody sent one, but an admin still presses it. **The rail makes paying one click. It does not make it automatic.** No cron was added. Escalating to auto-release is a separate decision and it needs an entry of its own.

  **Two independent double-send defences, because each alone leaves a window.** A Firestore transaction claims the payout under a status guard, so two concurrent callers cannot both reach Stripe; a deterministic idempotency key derived from the round id ALONE collapses a retry from a cold start onto the same transfer. The claim stops a race, the key stops a retry, and neither covers the other's case.

  **Failure leaves money owed, never lost.** A winner who has not onboarded is `blocked`, not failed: the round stays `settled`, the pot stays owed, and it sends when their account is ready. A Stripe refusal is `failed` and retryable. Nothing in this path voids a round, pays a loser, or clears a debt. The admin queue reads the winner's account state so a stuck round shows "WAITING ON WINNER" rather than refusing when Pay is pressed.

  **The lesson, and it is this repo's own habit catching a real hole.** `scripts/test-payout.mjs` (36 assertions) passed on its first run, which proves nothing, so seven deliberate mutations were applied one at a time. Six were caught. **The one that escaped was the most important guard in the file:** the idempotency assertion compared two calls to each other, and two calls land in the same millisecond, so a `Date.now()` mixed into the key still compared equal. It is pinned to the literal string now, which is the only form that catches a clock, a counter or a random. A guard on the double-send defence that does not detect a clock is worse than no guard, because it is read as coverage.

  **Honest limits, stated rather than discovered later.** Stripe cannot pay out to every country, and this audience is global; an unsupported country is now named to the user instead of retried forever, but it does mean cash rounds are unavailable to some winners and that is a real ceiling. Buy-in limits are UNTOUCHED at $50: that is a blast-radius limit, and raising it belongs in its own change after this rail has moved real money at least once. And the rail has not yet paid a live winner, so it is verified by its guards and by a deployed endpoint, not by a completed payout.

- **Real people top the leaderboard: the seed bank re-scored below them, and the landing gets a leaderboard teaser of real names only** (2026-08-22, Aidan: "advertise the leaderboard here ... use the real ppl on the leaderbaord though"). Two coupled changes. (1) A minimal strip under the landing first screen (above the Load more card, deliberately not in the one-pager hide list) renders the top real, named people and links to /leaderboard. It reads a new `realRows` field on `/api/leaderboard-top` that excludes the seed bank and unnamed rows SERVER-SIDE, and the strip ships hidden until at least three such rows exist, so the surface can never invent texture; the API cache key moved to `leaderboard-top-v2` because the shared cache survives deploys and would have served the old payload shape for a TTL. Do not wire the teaser to the mixed `rows` field. (2) **The 48 seed docs were re-scored in place, order preserved, from 67.2-97 down to a 46-60.5 band**, below the top real scores. This is not a reversal of the 2026-07-18 seeding decision; it restores its stated intent. That entry seeded at 26.3-29.5 on the 30 scale precisely so "a real 29.6 tops the board", and the 2026-08-18 scale migration mapped the seeds to 65-97 while real rounds score 31-84, which quietly buried every real person under the bank on /leaderboard, the tour's ranked band, and the API. Each doc's pre-demote score is stashed at `scorePreDemote` for revert; seeds keep their "Example" label on /leaderboard. Verified live after: the top 12 of the collection by score are all real entries. Note the /leaderboard LIVE tab is still seeds-only, since every real entry today is voice-kind; that changes as live rounds land, not by code. **The teaser half was RETIRED the same day** (Aidan, off a screenshot of the strip: remove it). The landing now carries three quick doors above the Full tour button instead (watch past rounds, the leaderboard, debate an AI), each a hash anchor that deep-links into the gated tour through the existing hashchange opener; the leaderboard door lands on #ranked-band, where the re-score above already puts real people on top. Change (2) stands in full. `realRows` on /api/leaderboard-top now has no consumer but stays as built: it is one loop over an already-fetched snapshot, and it is the correct field for any future real-names-only surface. Do not build one on the mixed `rows` field, and do not re-inline a board strip under the first screen (tombstone comment at the old spot in landing.html says the same).

- **Mission Control re-cut on the priority stack, the drain holes closed, and unfinished rounds finally get a WHY** (2026-08-22, Aidan: "redesign the admin page to match the priorities of the company and what stats matter, also setup system to avoid anyone token draining us, and gather notes of users experiences, esp unfinished rounds, to capture why"). Three coupled changes, one push.

  **(1) /admin leads with the numbers the company runs on.** The KPI strip led with lifetime vanity totals (cases generated, forum posts, teams, shared cases): counts of what exists, not of whether it is working. New `/api/admin/northstar` renders the strip on the 2026-08 stack instead: named accounts (Auth-derived, spark from creation dates), activation (named accounts that ever finished a round; 31/215 is the whole retention story), rounds finished by kind (voice is the product ~9x and the split is the point), spar match rate (17% of queue joins matched in the August window; liquidity is the activation leak), bets in the window against all-time (the pivot's watch number; 766 markets settled against ONE bet ever, an anon on Aug 6, pre-pivot), and the Open's field with days out. New seventh workspace **Money** (key 2, priority order): the betting scoreboard (markets, wallets, bets, cash rounds, latest bets verbatim), the Open's entry counters straight off the tournament docs, and the Cost guard. `admin-signin-errors.mjs`, built in May with its own header calling sign-in the second-biggest funnel leak and ZERO consumers since, finally gets a card. New attention rules: match rate under 25%, zero bets in the window, and both cost-posture flags below.

  **(2) The cost guard is now a system, not a hope.** An audit of every provider-spending endpoint found the 2026-08-19 standing rule ("identify the caller through lib/caller.mjs, meter through lib/rate-limit.mjs") had five compliant endpoints and TWENTY module-scope Map counters still on money paths, plus five real holes, all closed: `docs-agent.mjs` was an effectively open Anthropic relay (read client-settable x-forwarded-for FIRST, so rotating one header minted a fresh bucket per request; now callerIp + shared layers, and the App Check catch fails closed for non-extension origins); `fact-check.mjs` and `perplexity.mjs` handed the per-user lane to ANY verified token, and anonymous uids are free to mint, so rotation bought unlimited Perplexity sonar-pro + Sonnet (both now require isNamedAccount for the uid lane, anon falls to the IP lane, App Check added); `brain-health.mjs` ran six provider probes per unauthenticated cache miss with no lock (per-IP miss metering + in-flight dedupe + stale-serve); `live-judge.mjs` ran a multi-juror Anthropic panel with zero rate limiting (per-uid 20/hr named, anon 6/hr + IP backstop, sized generously for Aug 29); `recording-thumb.mjs` re-ran a 16MB Daily download + ffmpeg per request on its soft-fail path (per-IP limit + the CDN cache header the success path already set). **And the guards became visible:** every deny in `lib/rate-limit.mjs` now increments `abuse_daily/{day}` (buffered, at most one write per isolate per minute, lazy import, failure swallowed — visibility must never take the request down), `claude.mjs` counts its sign-in wall there too, and `/api/admin/abuse` renders it with the posture flags that matter: **UPSTASH_* is still unset, so every limiter on the site is per-isolate memory — the lottery the lib's own header warns about — and that now shouts from the attention strip instead of a console.warn nobody reads. Setting the two env vars is the single highest-leverage drain fix left and needs no deploy.**

  **(3) Unfinished rounds now say why.** The round funnel could say WHERE a round died and nothing anywhere recorded WHY. Two first-class events (`round_abandoned`, `round_exit_reason`, allowlisted anon because two thirds of round activity is anonymous) and a shared widget (`js/round-exit.js`: one-tap reason chips + optional 240-char note + skip, explicit dark styling because theme tokens on unknown surfaces is how two invisible-text bugs shipped). Passive beacons on all four round surfaces fire on pagehide mid-round with the state that matters: practice carries `ai_loading` and `stuck_shown`, so the "they close the tab during the silent AI wait" hypothesis the stuck-speech watchdog comment names is finally measurable; live carries speech index, elapsed and whether the opponent ever arrived; voice reuses stop()'s own counters via a `funnel('abandon')` step. Bail buttons whose reason is knowable record it without asking (no-show "Find another" → opponent_no_show, forfeit → forfeit_next); the one active prompt sits on /spar's cancelQueue, the biggest leak, and can never trap the navigation (answer, skip, or 30s of silence all resolve). `/api/admin/round-exits` aggregates both and renders the free-text notes VERBATIM on the new "Why rounds die" card, because "tech_problem" is a chip and "mic died when my opponent joined" is a fix. The un-instrumented confirms on live-round (end-without-timer, no-transcript, kickoff dismiss, forfeit declined) all got events too — a user hitting those dialogs is telling us something broke, and neither outcome was recorded.

  **Recode-from-notes is the loop, not this entry:** the card fills as real exits land; read the reason mix and the notes before guessing at the next retention fix, and treat a spike of `ai_loading` beacons as the AI-wait leak asking for a streaming/progress fix on /practice.

- **AI-use reports get a one-tap door and a transcript screen; the resolution gets a visible Change control** (2026-08-22, Aidan: "make it more easy to change resolution and to report opponent for using AI. setup a system to detect AI use"). Three coupled changes on /live-round. **(1) The resolution band carries its own ✎ Change and 🎲 controls** instead of hiding the editor behind Options ▾: same inline editor, same both-debaters consent flow from 2026-08-12, same speech-1 lock, one click closer. The buttons hide for spectators and disappear when the motion locks (synced off the timer-transition hook, so the off-side agrees). **(2) Reporting AI use is one tap:** a 🤖 AI? button on the opponent card opens the existing safety modal with the new `ai_use` reason preselected, and the modal states the posture in as many words before anyone sends: the screen is advisory, a human reviews, the judge never sees it. **(3) The detection system, and its fences are the design.** New pure `lib/ai-use.mjs` + a report-triggered path in `report-user.mjs`: on an `ai_use` report from a round both parties actually debated (checked against the round doc's seats, server-side), the accused bench's transcript gets a mechanical heuristic pass (markdown/citation remnants in a "spoken" transcript, AI self-reference, more words than the clock allows — ASR never emits any of these) plus one fenced Sonnet read, combined into none/weak/moderate/strong and attached to the `safety_reports` record as `aiAnalysis`. **The fences, all pinned by `scripts/test-ai-use.mjs` (49 assertions, in the pre-commit hook):** a machine read is EVIDENCE for the human reviewer, never a verdict — no strike, ban, or eject path exists off it (the 2026-08-18 machine-flag posture, applied to conduct rather than safety); **style can never convict** — a model verdict of 'strong' is capped at 'moderate' unless the heuristics found a mechanical artifact, because fluent structured speech is what practice produces and what second-language debaters get accused over; the AI judge never sees the analysis (it rides safety_reports and stops there — an accusation with a thumb on the scale is what the charter forbids); Policy spread pace is explicitly not a signal; and a rate-limited or failed screen still FILES the report with the skip on the record, because human review must never be blocked by the machine's meter. The reporter gets the verdict back, calibrated honestly ("no clear AI signal; a human still reviews") but never the raw evidence list — naming the accused's phrasing back to the accuser mid-round invites a confrontation the reviewer has not had yet. Detection is report-triggered only, never a cron and never per-speech: one metered Sonnet call per report (4/hour, 10/day per reporter through lib/rate-limit) is the whole bill.

- **Round notes: the AI flows every speech for both sides, and the live panel simplifies around them** (2026-08-22, Aidan: "setup a system where you can see AI notes of the other sides arguments to help you get ready for your response. other side can see that as a bit of an aide. but simplify how we organize the experience of debating"). After each finished speech on /live-round, the acting debater's client (the same election the floor judge uses; it holds the freshest transcript) asks Claude for 2 to 4 structured notes on that speech: a short argument tag, one sentence of what was actually argued, and one sentence naming what a reply has to engage. They land on the round doc (`roundNotes`, arrayUnion rows like `factChecks`, `atMs` not serverTimestamp because serverTimestamp is illegal inside arrays) and sync to every client. **Symmetry is the fairness argument and it is why the feature is safe to ship on a judged surface:** both debaters and every spectator read the IDENTICAL notes, so it can never be one side's coach; the prompt names the point of clash and is told never to supply the counter-argument, never call a winner, never score, and the ballot never reads the field (same posture as the fact check and the clash map, whose quote-gate discipline this inherits in lighter form: only arguments actually made, invent nothing). Skipped speeches, sub-30-word speeches, and the final speech generate nothing (the ballot covers the last one). Fails soft; a device can hide the card with one tap (`lr-round-notes`), and hiding is per-device, generation continues for everyone else. Cost: one Sonnet call per non-final speech, roughly the floor judge's footprint, on by default where the floor judge stays flagged off. **The simplification is the second half of the ask:** the standing chrome that used to sit above and below the clock at all times, the two-card roster, the 4-team roster, and the full speech-sequence strip, folded behind one collapsed "Round details" line at the bottom of the panel. Every id and renderer inside is untouched, so `renderRoster` and the sequence painter write into the same nodes; the existing `body.speech-live` collapse still hides the roster during a speech. Between speeches the panel now reads motion, clock, transcript, notes, which is the moment the notes exist for: the person about to speak is looking at the AI's read of exactly the speech they have to answer. Verified in a browser against the worktree (real renderer run with injected rows: latest speech open, older collapsed, HTML-escaped, both themes on tokens) and live after deploy. Watch `live_round_notes` in GA4 against speech counts; if debaters hide it more than they open old speeches, the card is furniture and should get quieter, not louder.

- **Second dry run of the Open's day, seven days out: 19 checks pass, one real hole found and closed** (2026-08-22). The entry path changed shape today (free entry, eligibility = the 18+ attestation, tips), so the whole day was driven again through the REAL production endpoints against an isolated throwaway tournament with minted test accounts, per the 2026-08-20 method, then torn down to zero. **What held:** register without the box enters a competitor at `prizeEligible:false`; re-registering with the box upgrades the same entry in place; a later call without the box does NOT downgrade it; the tip checkout mints a Stripe session and a recorded tip refuses a second ask with `ALREADY_PAID`; `running` keeps entries open; the drop-in queue pairs two ready entrants into a `d`-keyed room and refuses an anonymous token; `break` closes both entries and tips. **What did not hold: `register` itself accepted an anonymous token, and granted it `prizeEligible:true` on the client-sent flag.** The queue one door later refuses the same token, the client never sends it (withUser gates on a real sign-in), and that gap between layers is exactly the 2026-08-18 shape: the visible surface gates, the endpoint does not, and nothing errors. Anonymous uids are free and unlimited to mint, `entryCount` renders in the hero as "N debaters have signed up", and the cash-eligibility list should never hold an unaccountable uid, so register now refuses anonymous with the same 403 the queue uses. Closed same hour, re-verified against production: named register still passes, anonymous gets 403 at the door. **The standing habit this re-proves: a changed entry path gets a full day-path dry run before the day, because both dry runs to date have found something silent.**

- **The stranger vertical is the product, so the front door says it and four pages stop denying it** (2026-08-22, the founder: "the basic product vertical is debate omegle, really emphasize, direct user, have seo lean that way"). **The measurement changes what "lean the SEO that way" means, so it goes first.** In Search Console the omegle head terms are already WON and they are SMALL: `debate omegle` position 1.6 on 55% CTR, `debate strangers` 3.6 on 72%, `omegle for debates` 1.8, and the whole niche is ~300 impressions in 28 days. There is no ranking headroom left to take. So leaning that way cannot mean chasing those queries; it means CONVERTING the traffic already won and taking the page-2 siblings at positions 11 to 17. **The front door was the leak.** Everyone gets the stranger arm of the first screen (the A/B was called 2026-07-27 and forced), and that arm has carried its h1 screen-reader-only since 2026-08-19, so the visible page said the product in one four-letter button: a visitor arriving on the query the site ranks FIRST for met a page that never used the word. The headline is visible again with one supporting line, which is precisely the fix the 8c9255de revert named on its way out ("a headline added back to THIS composition rather than a re-revert of the whole pass") rather than a re-litigation of the layout. It is sized off VIEWPORT HEIGHT the way `--fs-globe` and `--fs-rhythm` already are, because the screen is pinned to 100svh and a headline can only cost scroll; measured at 32-800 at both 1280x800 and 960x800 with the globe whole, which only fits because 25ebec32 had just taken the Open card off that column. The primary button is deliberately still "Debate": "Debate a stranger" wrapped the CTA pair to two rows at 941px, which the CSS comment there names as a hard constraint, and the headline above it carries the noun. **Four pages were telling this exact traffic there is a wall that is not there.** The 2026-08-18 named-account sweep put "a live round takes a free account" on `/debate-strangers`, `/omegle-alternative`, `/how-it-works` and `/prep`, and the 2026-08-19 guest-rounds reversal made every one of them false. The pages built to catch "omegle alternative" were answering the account question with the old gate. All four now say the first rounds need no account; `/prep` was additionally still quoting five anonymous AI rounds against an `ANON_LIMIT` of 1. **The page-2 siblings got real answers, not keyword shims:** `anonymous debate online` (pos 15.7) had ZERO coverage anywhere on the site and `debate random people` (11.8) sat on one page, so `/debate-strangers` gained two FAQ entries in the searcher's own words (Avatar mode plus the guest lane; the queue mechanics), with the visible copy and the FAQPage JSON-LD diffed question-by-question afterwards and matching in order, 14 to 14. `/online-debate-platforms` takes 345 impressions for 14 clicks, which is a snippet problem rather than a ranking one, so its title and description now lead with the intent it actually ranks for. `/debate-online` wins those queries outright and opened its h1 on the leaderboard; the h1 now opens on the stranger and its title and meta description are deliberately untouched, because they are what is winning. Nav: the desktop rail carries Explore only, so the LINKS label is what the MOBILE SHEET shows, and "LIVE" named a state next to /watch, /spectate and /live; it is "Debate live", and the Explore spotlight titles itself "Debate a stranger". **Standing note for the next agent handed "grow organic": do not spend another pass on the omegle head terms.** They are at position 1-5 with nothing left to gain. The untapped volume is the NSDA topics cluster at positions 34-61, which is seasonal and peaks now.
  - **Found in passing and NOT fixed: `debateprep.com` serves the landing page, not `prep.html`.** The 2026-07-23 keyword front door is a `status = 200` rewrite from `https://debateprep.com/` to `/prep.html` in both netlify.toml mirrors, and the live domain 301s and lands on `/` instead, so the one unique page that door exists to serve is unreachable and `/prep` on the canonical host 301s away to it. That is a redirect-topology question rather than a copy one, so it is recorded here rather than guessed at. **CLOSED the same day by the entry directly below.**

- **prep.html gets a URL that resolves, and the keyword domain is recorded as never having been connected** (2026-08-22, closing the note directly above). The config was never wrong. The host move it was written for never happened, and nothing on the site was in a position to notice.

  **Three things measured before touching anything, because the note above declined to guess.** The apex answers on GoDaddy **Domain Forwarding** (`15.197.225.128` / `3.33.251.168`, AWS-hosted, `x-request-id`, and a `405` on `HEAD` that is not Netlify), which 301s to itsdebatable.com **before Netlify ever sees the request**, so no host-scoped rule could have matched no matter how it was ordered. `debateprep.com` is **not a Netlify domain alias** (`getSite` lists only itsdebatable, debateai and debatethedevil), so `www.debateprep.com` reaches Netlify's edge and is served the wildcard `*.netlify.app` cert: there has never been a certificate for this host. And the ordering theory is **disproved**: rules 1 to 29 are all scoped to other hosts, and inside the block the apex exact-match and the asset passthroughs correctly precede the catch-all. The rules are byte-identical across both mirrors.

  **The repo had already diagnosed this correctly and then waited a month.** `sitemap.mjs` carries a STAGED note from 2026-07-23 withholding the sitemap entry for exactly the right reason, and `app/netlify.toml` says in as many words that the host "was previously on GoDaddy Domain Forwarding" and that "moving the host onto Netlify is what fixes that." That move was never made. **The DNS has since moved further away, not closer:** the staging note recorded a correct `75.2.60.5` sitting in the zone beside the parking records, and that record is now gone entirely, leaving only forwarding.

  **The one genuine defect, and it is not the DNS.** `/prep` and `/prep.html` on the canonical host 301'd to the keyword domain, so a misconfigured second host made the page reachable at **no public URL at all**. A keyword domain is a convenience; it must never be able to take the page down everywhere. Both 301s are deleted and Netlify's pretty-URL handling serves `/prep` in place, the same way `/india` and `/report` already resolve. The page's canonical, `og:url` and JSON-LD `url` move to `https://itsdebatable.com/prep`, because a canonical naming a URL that 301s to the landing page is worse than none. **The debateprep.com host block is left exactly as written**, correct and inert; it costs nothing and starts working the moment the host reaches Netlify.

  **`/prep` also joins the sitemap.** Nothing on the site links to it, so it was an orphan on top of being unreachable, and the staging note's only stated objection (a submitted URL would soft-404) is void now that it is a 200 on a host that has a cert.

  **NOT done, and it is deliberate rather than blocked-on-nothing: the domain was not forced into working.** Adding a Netlify alias whose DNS does not resolve to Netlify can stall cert renewal for every domain on the site, and this repo has already paid for one DNS outage. The safe order is DNS first, alias second, which is what the staging note says too. **Both remaining steps are Aidan's, at GoDaddy and in the Netlify UI, and neither is a code change.** Until then the page is served, indexed and self-consistent on the canonical host, which is the outcome the keyword domain was only ever a nicer address for.

- **The guest lane on /spar was unreachable for fifteen weeks, because nothing on that page ever minted a guest** (2026-08-22, Aidan: "allow ppl who arent signed in to debate up to 2 times and then sign in"). He was describing a feature that shipped on 2026-08-19 and has been advertised on four SEO pages since. It works exactly as designed and almost nobody could get to it.

  **The premise under it was false, and the log states the premise in as many words.** The guest-rounds entry below says the allowance is metered "against the anonymous Firebase uid `js/notifications.js` already mints on every page". It does not mint one. Grepping the shipped tree for `signInAnonymously` finds it in `practice.html`, `live.html`, `live-round.html` and `tournament.html`, and nowhere else, and not in a single one of the eighteen shared scripts /spar loads. So a visitor whose first stop was /spar had **no user at all**, fell through the guest branch to the signed-out branch, and met "Sign in to join the live queue" permanently. Not a flash, not a race, and not for a moment. The lane worked only for somebody who had happened to load /practice or /live earlier in the same browser and picked up a session there, which is nobody's first visit and, given /debate-online takes 68% of organic clicks, nobody's typical one either.

  **Everything downstream was already correct**, which is what made this so quiet: `firestore.rules` accepts an anonymous owner's `matchmaking_queue` create, `spar-pair.mjs` meters `GUEST_FREE_ROUNDS` against `guest_rounds/{uid}`, the gate has separate copy for a spent allowance, and GA4 splits the two gate reasons. Four layers of a five-layer feature, and the missing one was the front door.

  **The fix is that /spar mints its own guest session** when the auth listener reports nobody, then falls into the guest branch that already exists. A boot state stands in for the moment in between, deliberately NOT the searching state, because painting a search with no queue doc behind it is the exact failure the listener already guards against. If anonymous auth is blocked or disabled the mint rejects and the sign-in ask stands, so the gate remains a statement of fact rather than of policy. It is skipped entirely for `?dm` / `?thread` / `?inbox` arrivals, where the named-only ask is the honest one and a queue is not what the visitor came for.

  **Verified against the real project with a URL-armed stub so nothing reached the live queue:** signed out to `currentUser: null`, loaded the page, and watched it mint a fresh anonymous uid, show no gate, and reach `joinQueue` exactly once. The failure path was forced separately and put the gate up with both sign-in buttons. **Two earlier attempts at this test paired me with a real person before I could stop it**, which is the reason the stub exists and the reason to use one next time: /spar is a live queue with humans in it, and loading it is not a read-only act.

  **The durable lesson is the sharpest version of one this log keeps relearning.** The 08-14 entry says a metric that renders a plausible number is not evidence it works. This is worse: a FEATURE can be fully built, correctly reasoned about across four layers, documented in this file, advertised on four public pages, and still be reachable by nobody, because one sentence of load-bearing infrastructure in a code comment was not true. Nothing errored. Nothing logged. The gate it produced is a real screen that says a plausible thing. **When a decision rests on "X already happens", grep for X.**

- **The Open's first-screen card is retired for the second time, and the ad becomes a modal 30 seconds in** (2026-08-22, Aidan: "get rid of this bc the line at the top resolves it - advertise it as a pop up 30 seconds in to all users"). The card was removed by the 08-19 composition pass on exactly this reasoning, put back by a later session, and is now gone again. Two promotions of one event on one screen, stating the same date and the same price, is duplication rather than emphasis. **The `.fs-open*` CSS is deliberately left in place, unused**, so a third restoration is re-adding markup rather than rebuilding styles, and the removal comment says so.

  **What replaced it is one interruption, spent once.** A modal is the most expensive thing a site can spend on a visitor, so the rules in `js/topbar.js` are all about spending it on the right moment and never again: once per person **ever** (localStorage, not session, because a modal that returns tomorrow is a nag and dismissing is a real answer); never on a surface where somebody is mid-task (a round, a voice call, the queue, the tab room, checkout, and the tournament pages themselves, a wider skip list than the strip's because the strip is a line you can ignore and this takes the screen); never to someone who already entered, which `/tournaments` stamps locally on confirmation; never to someone who dismissed the strip about this same event in this session, checked at fire time rather than at load because that dismissal usually happens during the wait; never while the visitor is typing, because a modal that steals focus mid-sentence costs a sentence; and never in the iOS shell, same Apple 3.1.1 reason the strip is hidden there.

  **The 30 seconds are VISIBLE seconds.** A backgrounded tab accrues nothing, so a page left open in another window does not "read" for half an hour and get interrupted the instant it is looked at. Same discipline as the 2026-08-14 presence gate, and it has the same consequence for anyone testing it: **the Browser pane reports `document.hidden` as true, so the modal correctly never fires in that harness.** That is the artifact that has already faked one negative result on this codebase. Verified by overriding visibility and measuring: fires at 29s, focus lands on the primary action, body scroll locks, Escape closes and restores focus, and a second load with the flag set produced no modal across 42 seconds of patched-visible dwell. The once-only guard was proven by waiting it out rather than by reading the early return, and the skip list by running the shipped regex against 41 real routes.
- **The site asks a stranger who they are, and /how-it-works stops explaining the same round twice** (2026-08-22, Aidan: "make this page more logical to new users - also when entering site ask ppl if they already debate or dont and are new (no worries if your not!)"). **The answer had plumbing and no front door.** `audience-mode.js` has read `debateos-experience` since 2026-08-18, stamps it on `<html>`, swaps every `[data-plain]` string on the page and feeds /practice's own register fork, and the only two things that ever SET it were one quiet line docked beside the Full tour button, below the fold on the landing, and the post-signup onboarding card, which by definition arrives after the copy it would have changed. So the fork existed and almost nobody was ever forked. New `js/experience-ask.js`, injected by `topbar.js` alongside audience-mode, asks once from the bottom-left corner on every topbar page and never again either way.
  - **Not the first screen, and not a modal.** The chooser shipped over the foot of `#first-screen` on 2026-08-18 and Aidan moved it down the same day asking for a clean first screen; that call stands, so this does not touch the hero and does not stack a second strip under the Open ribbon. A modal in front of someone who has seen nothing is a toll booth, and the entire point of the answer is to make the page behind it easier to read. It waits ~2s, asks from the corner, and a dismiss stores only "we asked" so the authored debate-register copy stays as written.
  - **It stands down where a page asks inline** (any `[data-exp]` control in the DOM), which is how /how-it-works owns the question without anyone being asked twice, and it skips round surfaces outright: asking someone mid-speech what kind of debater they are is worse than never asking.
  - **/how-it-works was reordered, not rewritten.** It opened on a 1080px video, then the plain-English version of a round, then a dense paragraph carrying motion / speaker points / ballot at a reader who had just been told none of them, then the CTAs, then a mock ballot with no caption, and then "The loop in three steps", which explained the same round a SECOND time in different vocabulary. The three-card grid is deleted rather than reworded (its HowTo JSON-LD anchors moved onto the five plain steps), a five-term glossary goes before the first paragraph that uses any of the terms, the mock ballot gets a heading and a plain reading of itself, and the video moves below the steps. Answering "I am new" swaps the jargon and reorders the two doors so the AI round leads. Its ballot numbers also moved off the retired 30 scale, which they were still on.
  - **Footer ranked rather than trimmed** (same day, Aidan: "revise this to match what the site rlly should be - ai exhibition not needed for example"). The visible rail was a flat alphabet of 33 links where a doorway page written for one query sat at the weight of the product, /practice was labelled "Debatable", an AI-versus-AI demo was listed as a feature on a social app, and the live cash tournament was not in it at all. Visible is now 21 links in the order someone wants them; everything demoted moved into the expander under three new rails, because the landing carries the most inbound equity on the site and the expander's "site index" turned out not to be the complete index it claims to be, so a dropped page would have been orphaned outright. **Nothing was deleted.**

- **The Open's signed-up number is the real one, and the pot has a stated way back up** (2026-08-22). Two entries above record the pot going to $100/$50/$25 against a field of nine and entry going free. This is the same day's third pass and it is about honesty in the number beside them. **The hero printed 51 against 10 real registrations**, because `renderEntrants` was a floor of 48 plus growth above a baseline of 7, and the markup shipped a hardcoded 48 that painted on the first frame and survived a failed read. Aidan, reading it off his own page: make the number accurate. **The distinction worth keeping, because a floor is defensible elsewhere on this site:** `PRESENCE_BASELINE` pads an ambient dial nobody can count. A roster for an event with a register button is countable, and someone could in principle ask to be shown the 51. It also fought the ask, since a stated field of 51 makes "help us pass 50" read as already done. Do not reintroduce a floor there; if the number needs to be bigger, get more entrants. **Paired with it, the threshold that makes a smaller pot honest rather than just smaller: past 50 entrants it goes back to $850 ($500/$250/$100)**, stated together on the rules page, the pot card, the logistics card, the JSON-LD and the live tournament doc, because a prize figure that appears in one place and not another is the one that gets quoted back at you. The reminder email reads both the field size and the pot off the same doc the page renders, so it cannot quote a figure the site contradicts, and it now mails ENTRANTS too: a pot that moved is the news the ten people it affects have the most standing to hear, and the old skip would have excluded exactly them.

- **Cash rounds: real money, paid at the door, and the fee is the whole legal argument** (2026-08-22, Aidan: "we need to seriously rethink the betting section to be more simple, we want real money, so you pay us first"). The betting surface sold three lanes and two markets, one of which (the Ladder) had no inventory and could not be traded, and **not one of the five could take a card**. There are now five economies in this repo (floor_*, predict_*, credits/settle, value_*, and this) and this is the only one that moves real money on the result of an argument, so read this entry before touching any of them.

  **The shape, and why it is this shape.** Two people buy in for the same amount, argue one motion, and the winner takes the pot. That is a CONTEST OF SKILL: the people paying are the people competing, and the outcome turns on which of them argues better. The thing that was NOT built, deliberately and permanently, is the obvious-looking alternative: selling points so spectators can wager cash on rounds. That is unlicensed sports betting in every US state, and it is worse here than generically, because we run the judge that settles it. A house that owns the oracle and takes a cut of the bet is the argument that sank Papaya and the first paragraph of any complaint. **Do not build it, and do not let a future "simplification" collapse the two.**

  **THE FEE IS CHARGED AT THE DOOR, and that is what makes it compatible with the no-rake invariant rather than an exception to it.** The judge charter forbids a rake in `credits.mjs` and `settle.mjs` for a stated reason: "the operator's take cannot depend on who wins." A flat 20% taken from both sides before a word is spoken does not depend on who wins. It is the same number whichever way the ballot goes, so the house is indifferent to the result by construction rather than by promise. `feeOf` takes the buy-in and nothing else, and the guard asserts the arity so nobody can widen it. **What stays forbidden is a cut at SETTLEMENT**: `awardPot` takes the pot and the winner, does no fee arithmetic, and throws if the payout is a cent short. A skim there is a stake in a verdict our own judge wrote.

  **A round that produces no verdict refunds everything, the fee included.** Nobody paid to sit in a queue. `refundPlan` returns gross and the Stripe refund passes no amount, so the whole charge goes back; we only earn on a round that actually gets judged. A split panel voids rather than being tie-broken, because a tie-break would be the house's thumb on a contest the house is holding the money for.

  **Money never moves on an AI ballot alone.** A verdict marks the winner owed; paying is gated a second time on `APPEAL_WINDOW_MS`, the same constant the appeal layer publishes, so the money and the right to object cannot disagree. The winner is read from the recorded judgment, never from the request body, and only on `verdictSource:'server'`. Payouts are sent by hand, same as tournament prizes and bounty pots, because there is still no Stripe Connect and no KYC. **That is the real ceiling on this feature: it works at ten rounds a week and not at a hundred a day.** **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-22 by the payout-rail entry at the top of this log: Connect and KYC now exist, so the send is a Stripe transfer rather than a manual errand. An admin still releases it, which is the half of this sentence that still stands.**

  `lib/cash-round.mjs` is pure and `scripts/test-cash-round.mjs` (299 assertions) runs in the pre-commit hook. Six mutations were applied one at a time and every one was caught. **One hole the guard found on its own, worth recording because it looked harmless:** `canSettle` first resolved the winner by matching the judgment's winner key against a seat uid as a fallback. A judgment names its winner as a side key (`a`/`b`) and carries uids in `participants`; our sides are pro/con and a judgment's may be prop/opp. With no participants map that fallback would have paid whichever seat held a uid spelled like a side key. There is no fallback now.

  **The page was simplified by subtraction.** The "two markets" band and the three-lane band are gone; the hero is two doors (cash round, free board) and the cash band states the fee in the same breath as the price. **The legal table was made FALSE by this and is corrected**, which matters more than the layout: its third row said the pot surface required no payment ("Not required. Free qualifying path in every event"), and a cash round requires paying, so it now names the leg this surface actually removes, which is chance rather than consideration. The fourth proof card said settlement has no fee at all; it now separates a flat fee at the door from a cut of the pot, because only one of those is compatible with running the judge. Leaving either would have put the page's own rules in contradiction with the band above them, on the document a dispute gets argued from.

  **Live as of this entry:** `CASH_ROUNDS_LIVE=true`, alongside `ENTRY_PAYMENTS_LIVE` and `BOUNTY_PAYMENTS_LIVE`, all on `sk_live_`. Bounties took a card for days with no route from the nav and now have one. **Two riders in the same push:** paid plans went unlimited on the text lane per Aidan ("subscription is so you can do everything on app without limits"), with voice deliberately left on its own meter because the 2026-06-27 audit put it at ~80% of per-user variable cost; and `ANON_FREE_CALLS` moved to 12, so a guest gets two AI rounds before the wall, matching the two live rounds `GUEST_FREE_ROUNDS` already allows.

  **One bug worth the space, because reading the diff could not have found it.** `/api/cash-round` answered every request with "error decoding lambda response". The list query filtered `status` with an `in` and ordered by `createdAt`, which needs a composite index; a missing index throws at QUERY time, not at deploy time, and nothing was catching it. The sort moved into memory (the board is capped at 40 rows anyway) and the handler is wrapped, because a crashed lambda reads to the caller as an outage rather than as our bug.

- **The Open is FREE to enter, and $5 is a tip. This supersedes every entry-price entry below** (2026-08-22, Aidan: "make all entries to this tournament completely free actually - advertise as that. tips of 5 dollars accepted"). Fifth flip of the entry model, and the last one this log expects: the price is now zero, so there is no number left to move. Sequence, for anyone reading the entries under this one out of order: paid on 08-10, free on 08-11, paid-with-waiver hours later, $20 with an automatic comp on 08-11, $5 on 08-19, free for everyone today.

  **Why it holds this time.** The $5 door never took money worth having: eight days after entries opened, `paidEntries` was 0, and at the flip it was 1. Against that, every surface on the site had to carry a fee, a waiver, a comp cutoff and a two-door explanation, and the copy went stale in three separate ways in ten days. The revenue being given up is one payment. The thing being bought is a page that a stranger can read in one line.

  **The architecture change is the part that outlives the price.** Cash eligibility used to be a PAYMENT (or a comp standing in for one). It is now the **18+ attestation and nothing else**, decided once in `tournament.mjs`'s register action, which no money can reach. That is what lets the tip be a real tip: `entry-checkout.mjs` mints a Stripe session that grants nothing, the webhook branches on `tip:'1'` and pointedly does NOT touch `prizeEligible`, and the entry stands whether the card succeeds, fails or is never opened. A tip that could buy eligibility would be an entry fee wearing a different word, and the whole free-entry footing of a cash-prize event rests on it not being one. Sessions minted before today carry no flag and keep the old behaviour, because they were sold as prize entry and settling them any other way would take something a person actually paid for.

  **The attestation is an upgrade, not a gate.** Entry is open to 13+, the box is optional, and an unticked box registers a real competitor who plays for the placement and the ranking. Register is idempotent and re-sends the attestation, so ticking it later and pressing the same button is the entire recovery path; it only ever moves toward eligibility, since a stray click that stripped it would cost somebody a prize. **Nothing was backfilled**: the two existing entries without eligibility were not granted it, because asserting that someone is 18 when they never said so is the one thing this field must never do.

  **Surfaces moved together**, per the list this log has kept since 08-11, and it is longer than that list: the enter card, hero kicker, pot note, logistics card, both FAQ copies and every hydrated string on `/tournaments`; sections 3 through 6 of `/tournament-rules` plus the liability and no-consideration clauses; the `Event` JSON-LD collapsed from three offers to one free one; the landing first-screen card; the sitewide `topbar.js` ribbon; the open-band fine print on `/debate-online`, `/debate-strangers` and `/omegle-alternative`; the `/community` and `/spar` nudges; `/get-paid-to-debate` including its FAQPage JSON-LD; the landing money FAQ in both its visible and JSON-LD copies; `/profile`'s entry card; the control room's prize links, which keyed on `entryFeeCents` and would have hidden the rules from a free prize event; all three announcement emails; and the live Firestore doc (`entryFeeCents` 2000... 500 -> **0**, new `tipCents` 500, description rewritten). Two stale claims fell out on the way and were live: the reminder email and `/get-paid-to-debate` still promised **$500 / $250 / $100** against a ladder that has been $100 / $50 / $25 since the pot was cut.

  **The strip lost its price for good.** `topbar.js` rides ~60 pages and is the slowest surface on the site to correct; it has now said free, $20, $5 and free again. It carries the prize and the day and nothing else, with the DATE in the hook rather than the tail, because under 560px the tail is hidden and "Win $100 ->" with no day on it advertises nothing.

  **Nobody is owed a refund, and the check is worth recording because the first read said otherwise.** The tournament doc showed `paidEntries: 1`, which reads as a customer who paid for something now free. It was not: the one `paid` row in `tournaments/{tid}/payments` is `pi_3U6EIFQmgdy6tzIa1KDTMnKk`, and Auth resolves its uid to Aidan's own account, a self-test on the day the $5 door opened. The other seven payment docs are `comp` at $0. **So the $5 prize entry took exactly zero real payments in its entire eleven-day life**, which strengthens the case above rather than complicating it. `paidEntries` is left at 1 on purpose: it is a record of money that genuinely moved, and nothing public renders it. The habit worth keeping: a counter is not a customer, and one lookup against the payments ledger and Auth separates them.
- **The founder goes anonymous: name, credential, school and photo off every public surface** (2026-08-22, Aidan: "get rid of all mentions of my name aidan and the whole apda pro ams champion uchicago stuff. i want to be more anonymous for the site"). **This supersedes §3 wedge #3 and the §2 "bio fact" carve-out**, both of which told agents to SURFACE the credential on the landing. What went: every "APDA Pro-Ams champion, 2025" and "UChicago parliamentary debater" line (24 files, meta descriptions, JSON-LD, footers, FAQ answers, the /judge byline, llms.txt, the extension store listing), the name in the /team and /story founder cards, both founder photos (`img/team/aidan.jpg`, `img/reviews/aidan.jpg` deleted), the decorative corner portrait on /reviews and both landings, the "APDA / UChicago" testimonial chip, the personal handles in the changelog byline and the extension issue link, the "Aidan" sign-off on five admin email templates, the `aidan@` local part on every Resend From default, and ~420 `per Aidan` source comments across 115 files (view-source is a real leak, so the comments were swept too). **/uwc was deleted rather than anonymized**: the page was first-person as "a UWC-USA alum, Class of 2024, now a sophomore at the University of Chicago", so anonymizing it removes its entire premise. It 301s to /schools in both netlify.toml mirrors, which keeps every link in the UWC outreach emails alive. **Two dormant narration MP3s were re-synthesized**, not just re-scripted: /story and /team both SPOKE the credential aloud, and a corrected manifest beside stale audio would have left the claim live at a public URL. Same voice, same settings; `uwc.mp3` deleted with its page.

  **The replacement is product facts, not a vaguer credential.** Aidan's call, given the option of "a college parliamentary debater": drop it entirely. So the trust signal now rides the published rubric, the human appeal, and per-format depth. Where a builder had to be referenced at all, the line is "built from inside competitive debate" with no person attached.

  **The contact email STAYS `aidandavidhollinger@gmail.com`, deliberately, and it still carries the name.** Aidan chose that over standing up a new mailbox; it is one click away in ~140 footers, FAQs and mailto links. **So this sweep is a real reduction in exposure, not anonymity.** Anyone who clicks Contact learns the full name. Closing that gap needs a mailbox at `contact@itsdebatable.com` and is his call, not a code change.

  **Three leaks survive because they need his account, not an edit:** the Donate links point at `buymeacoffee.com/8idanhm`, `index.html` loads `aidanhm31.goatcounter.com/count`, and the Resend From addresses now read `hello@` on domains that **no Resend domain is currently verified for** (the API returns an empty domain list, which means outbound mail is failing for reasons that predate this sweep). Renaming the first two is a settings change in those two products.

  **Standing rule for anyone editing copy from here: do not restore the credential from a stale doc.** It reads as a factual correction and it is the opposite of one.

- **The Aug 29 day was run end to end nine days early, and six things were broken, every one of them silent** (2026-08-20). Nothing here was findable by reading code. All six answered a request with a plausible sentence, logged nothing, and left a screen that looked like it was working. The method was the finding: a real tournament day driven through the REAL production endpoints with five real accounts against an isolated test tournament, then torn down.
  - **(1) The drop-in queue refused every entrant.** `tournament-dropin.mjs` read `decoded.uid`. A Firebase ID token names its subject `sub` and mirrors it as `user_id`; there is no `uid` claim, because that spelling belongs to the Admin SDK's DecodedIdToken and `lib/auth.mjs` is a hand-rolled verifier returning the raw payload. So `myUid` was undefined, `members.includes(undefined)` was false for every entry, and ready / leave / poll answered every registered person with **409 "Register for this tournament first."** The drop-in queue IS the mechanism of a drop-in day. The same one word had `value-market.mjs` answering **401 "Sign in required"** to every signed-in trader, so the Ladder was inert for a second reason nobody had found. Closed at the source: `verifyIdToken` now mirrors `sub` onto `uid`.
  - **(2) A registered entrant arriving on the day had no way in.** `/tournaments` hydrates from the UNAUTHENTICATED list route, so the `me` key never arrived and the page could not tell an entrant from a stranger. The only link to `/open`, where the queue lives, shipped `hidden` and was revealed by exactly one thing: completing a registration click in the same session. The nine people already entered would have opened `/tournaments` on Saturday — the URL in the announcement email, the sitewide strip and the landing card — and been shown the entry doors again, with the only other path to `/open` being a button on `/profile`.
  - **(3) A drop-in round was invisible to the tab and unreportable by the director.** The queue wrote its round with no `status`, and `publicRound` withholds pairings unless released. That gate is right for a prelim draw a director stages and reviews; a drop-in round is released by construction, because it is written the instant two people are seated and they are already in the room. So the tab showed a live round with nobody in it and the control room, which filters on `released`, did not render it at all. It also wrote `roundNo: 0` while naming the document `d{seq}`, though `roundKey()` states the opposite contract in as many words, so the director's report-result resolved every drop-in result to a document called `d0`. Measured: `roundNo:0` returns 409 "That round does not exist", `roundNo:1` returns 200.
  - **(4) No tournament round has EVER verified.** `verifyTournamentPairing` opened with `await import('firebase-admin/firestore')`, and **firebase-admin is not a dependency of this repo** — `lib/firestore.mjs` wraps `@google-cloud/firestore`. It threw ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND on every call, the catch swallowed it as "treating as casual", and every tournament round failed the gate that decides whether a result reaches the board. The repo had already paid for this lesson once: the header of `scheduled-recording-reaper.mjs` records the same package doing the same thing to the same project. `FieldPath` now rides out of `lib/firestore.mjs` and the import is STATIC on purpose, so a missing package fails at bundling where Netlify shows it, not at runtime inside a catch where nobody does.
  - **(5) Every split judge panel died, and the ones that lived could show the wrong reasoning. This one is sitewide, not a tournament bug.** A juror's config names it `id`; the object `callJuror` returns names it `jurorId`; `judge-run` passes the RESULT into `normalizeVote`, which read `juror.id`. So every vote cast in production carried `jurorId: undefined`. `tally.dissent` maps those ids, so the moment a panel actually SPLIT the array became `[undefined]`, Firestore refused it, the ballot write threw, and the request died as **HTTP 502 "error decoding lambda response"** with no ballot, no audit row and no result. Unanimous panels were fine, which is why it reads as intermittent, and `/live-round` falls back to the client-side judge on any failure, so debaters kept seeing a ballot and nobody knew. Worse than the crash: `leadJurorId` could never match a vote, so the ballot fell back to `votes[0]` for its RFD, and on a 2-1 split whose first juror is the dissenter **the ballot recorded the majority's WINNER above the dissenter's REASONING.** A verdict explained by the argument for the other side is the one output this layer must never produce. The 207-assertion judge guard was green throughout, because every existing assertion hands `normalizeVote` a config object while production hands it a result: two shapes, each correct on its own. Three assertions now cover the production shape and all three were confirmed to fail against the old line before it was fixed.
  - **(6) An AI-judged round recorded the win and zero speaks.** `live-judge` read the per-side scores from `ballot.panel.points`, and `runPanel` has never put a `points` key on its panel object; the medians go onto the ballot as `proPoints` / `conPoints`. The ledger records a missing speak as 0 rather than blocking the win, which is right there and is exactly why this failed quietly. Speaks are the tiebreak standings sort on, so a full day of judged rounds would have produced a break ordered on wins with the tiebreak flat at zero, and the break decides who plays for $500. Measured: a real 2-1 panel returned 28.7 and 27.8 and both entries recorded spk 0.
  - **Verified after, on production, in one run:** register, doors open, a latecomer registering into a running tournament, four entrants queued, two rooms seated, a real three-juror panel returning a 2-1 majority with its dissenter named, the result posted to the board (Kai 74 to 102.5, Rin 237 to 264.6), both debaters released back into the pool, consent stamped `tournament_entry`, then break, semifinal, final and complete. Every test account, tournament, round, ballot, audit row and rating written by the run was deleted afterwards.
  - **The durable lesson, and it is sharper than "test your code".** Every one of the six was a plausible answer to a slightly different question than the one being asked: "no such entrant" for a real entrant, "no such round" for a round that exists, "treating as casual" for a tournament round, "sign in required" for a signed-in user, an empty pairings list for a live round, a zero speaker score for a scored ballot. None of them was an error, so none of them logged one. **The only thing that finds this class is running the real thing against the real endpoints and reading what the data says afterwards, rather than what the response said.**

- **Avatars reinvented: one studio portrait engine, and a builder whose options you can actually see** (2026-08-22, Aidan: the avatar system "is quite frankly ... funny", reinvent it from the ground up). The old cartoon renderer in `js/avatar.js` (balloon head, bright traffic-light backdrops, orbital lines, sticker grins) is gone. The whole file now runs on ONE parametric renderer in the CAMEO editorial language the AI personas already used: frontal bust with natural proportions, deep tonal duotone backdrops derived from the stored bright `bg` palette, one warm key light with a rim on the lit side, quiet features, matte everything. Users, AI persona presets, the CAMEO cast, seeded stranger faces, and the coach's talking head all draw from the same `portrait()`; the talking controller (`mountTalking`) kept its interface and animates the new geometry. **The config schema is unchanged plus one optional `face` field** (0-2 jaw shapes, derived deterministically for configs saved before it existed), so every stored avatar, every remote `kind:'portrait'` public identity, and every uid-seeded face upgraded in place with no migration. Hair went 12 -> 14 styles; hijab (index 13) is a deliberate builder choice and is excluded from `randomConfig`, because assigning it to an arbitrary seeded stranger would assert something about them. **The builder was the actual complaint and the actual fix:** the old modal showed each brow/mouth/facial-hair option as a 58px whole-bust thumbnail, so every row was five identical circles. Options now render as viewBox CROPS of the live portrait zoomed on the feature they change (brow chips show brows, outfit chips show the collar), grouped Face / Hair / Expression / Style, with a body scroll lock, a double-open guard, focus restore, and rAF-batched thumbnail re-renders. **Perf note that outlives the pass:** the CAMEO set used three feGaussianBlur filters per portrait, and blur is CPU-side; all soft volume is now painted with two radial "brush" gradients, so there is not a single SVG filter anywhere in the engine and a leaderboard can paint dozens of faces without blur cost. The live-mask identity system (`maskSvg`, cam-avatar.js) is untouched. Verified in a browser: all 14 hair styles, 3 faces x 6 skins, every mouth/eye/brow/facial/gear/outfit/backdrop variant, personas, the cast, 24px chips, old-schema configs, live-mask public identities, the talking head mid-speech, and the builder end to end (pick, save, persist, Escape, scroll-lock restore) in both themes.

- **What Stripe charges and what the site advertises were different numbers, and nothing could see it** (2026-08-24, found while wiring bounty and plan payments end to end). The live Individual price billed **$5.00 per MONTH** against an advertised **$10 a year**, and Team billed **$30.00/month** against **$50/year**. Those are the pre-2026-05-14 figures: the 2026-06-27 repricing swept the copy, as the log records, and never re-pointed Stripe. Anyone who had reached checkout would have been charged **six times** the advertised figure, on a cadence they never agreed to.
  - **Why it survived a guard built for exactly this.** `scripts/check-prices.mjs` hard-blocks a retired price STRING in user-facing HTML, it runs in the pre-commit hook, and it was passing the entire time. It reads words. Money settles against a Stripe price OBJECT, addressed by an opaque id in an env var, and **nothing in the repo compared the two**. Reading `STRIPE_PRICE_INDIVIDUAL` tells you nothing about what it charges. Both halves were individually correct and jointly wrong, which is the third finding of that exact shape in this log.
  - **The fix is that the amount now lives next to the id.** `lib/plans.mjs` is the single canonical price, and `create-checkout` retrieves the Stripe price and refuses to mint a session that disagrees with it. It **fails closed**, deliberately: a checkout that refuses is an outage, a checkout that quietly bills 6x is a refund queue and a chargeback, and those are not the same size of mistake. `scripts/test-plans.mjs` guards the offline half in the hook. Per the standing habit, its 64 passing checks proved nothing until eight mutations were applied one at a time; seven were caught and **one was vacuous** (an env comparison against `undefined`, which the hook always makes true) and now reports that it could not run instead of showing green.
