  - **A repricing is not one edit, and the webhook proved it.** Stripe never re-prices an existing subscription, so the moment the env var moved, every existing subscriber's renewal arrived carrying an id `getPlanFromPrice` no longer recognised, and it warned and returned `'individual'` — a **guess about a paying person's entitlement** that would have silently demoted a legacy Team subscriber on their next renewal. `LEGACY_PRICE_PLANS` now records what each retired price actually granted. Never delete a row from it.
  - **The Stripe products were still named "Devil's Advocate"**, which is the name a customer reads on the Checkout page and on their card statement. The sole-public-name rule had swept the site and stopped at the payment processor. Renamed; verified by minting a real session, which now renders "Debatable Individual · $10.00 / year", then expiring it.
  - **Second half, the same failure one layer up: a 402 was being read as a broken brain.** `requirePaidPlan` answers 402 for a paid brain, and both `/practice` and `/app` caught it in the generic `!r.ok` branch and rerouted to Claude with a `console.warn`. So picking Grok gave you Claude, silently, with no notice and no path to the fix. On `/app` that is the COMMON case rather than the rare one, since `BETA_PRO_UNLOCK` shows all six brains unlocked to everybody while the server gates five of them. Both surfaces now say the swap happened and offer the plan; **the round still finishes on Claude**, because a round that starts always finishes and cutting a live speech to sell a plan is the worse failure. `/practice`'s picker also read `plan` and ignored `status`, so a lapsed or past_due card showed six brains unlocked while the server refused them; it now matches `requirePaidPlan` exactly. The notice is plain DOM rather than React state on purpose: it fires mid-generation inside the component carrying the documented hooks-order hazard, and a banner is not worth the React #300 crash.
  - **Bounties: the endpoint was complete and had no button.** `/api/admin/bounty` has shipped complete/expire/refund/mark-paid for a while and `/admin` had zero references to it, so every one of them was curl-only. A money queue nobody can see is a stranger's card charged for a debate that may never have happened. Card added to the money pane, lazy-loaded like its neighbours, each action confirming with its actual consequence named rather than "are you sure". Payouts stay manual until Connect and KYC exist, so "Mark paid" records that you paid and moves nothing.
  - **`BETA_NO_CHARGE` is untouched and consumer checkout stays dark**, per Aidan's call when the drift was surfaced: wire it correctly, leave it off. Turning it on is one env var and no deploy. **Do not flip it without also resolving `BETA_PRO_UNLOCK`** (index.html), which hands every visitor the pro features a plan is meant to sell, so on the day charging starts, paying would unlock nothing they did not already have.
  - **Standing rule this leaves: a price is three edits that land together** — `lib/plans.mjs`, a NEW Stripe price (never edit one in place), and the env var, with the copy the price guard polices. Two of the three passing is what produced this.

- **You strike the motions before you meet your opponent** (2026-08-26, Aidan: "something similar to OYAPLE where before you meet your opponent you strike motions. 5 options, you strike 2, then get side choice or whatever and then meet opponent. i want it to feel like a video game"). `/spar` now opens a draft board instead of an accept card. Five motions land, both debaters strike two with the other side's picks hidden, the strikes reveal together, and a coin flip splits what is left: one debater calls the motion, the other calls their side.
  - **The arithmetic is the design, and it inverted the obvious build.** Two strikes each from five, BLIND, means the strikes can overlap, so survivors is 1, 2 or 3 and can never be 0 or 5. Measured over 4000 fuzzed drafts it is one survivor only ~29% of the time. So the motion-call beat is the COMMON case rather than an edge case bolted on, and both headlines ("One motion left standing" / "N motions survived") had to be written as first-class copy. A build that assumed 5 minus 2 minus 2 equals 1 would have been wrong seven times in ten.
  - **Side choice is the price of the motion call, and that is the whole fairness argument.** Last word on what you argue is real power, so the flip pays for it with last word on which side you take. Handing one debater both would make the coin flip decide the round rather than the debate. When a single motion survives, the motion holder has nothing to decide and the beat becomes a lock-in moment instead of a choice.
  - **Blind is enforced on the server, and the first version of this leaked.** A queue doc is readable by its owner, so mirroring the draft to both docs handed whoever struck SECOND the first striker's picks in devtools, which is the entire draft. Each doc now carries only its own owner's strikes until both sides commit; the peer's set is read inside the pairing transaction and the merged set is written to both docs only once neither side can still act on it. Do not "helpfully" mirror them back.
  - **It subsumes the ready check, which is the second reason it is worth building.** The 2026-08-11 finding was that a record written at intent time is not evidence anyone arrived. Striking is an ACT, so it proves presence the way a Ready button never did. The asymmetry that follows is deliberate: during the strike beat a silent peer still unwinds the pair through the existing ghost path and their strikes are NEVER auto-filled, because striking for an absent person is exactly how a room opens onto an empty chair. Past that point both people have proven they are there, so either side may expire a pick clock and the round survives a slow click.
  - **The pure layer owns every decision.** `lib/motion-draft.mjs` computes the slate, the flip, the phase, and the resolution; `spar-pair.mjs` applies it inside the EXISTING consent transaction, so the draft inherits ghost detection, mutual-skip, the reaper and guest metering rather than growing a second state machine. The client renders what the doc says and posts intent, never a phase or a side: a client that could pick its own side would pick the winning one. Timeout resolution is SEEDED rather than random at call time, so two clocks expiring at once cannot land the two browsers on different motions.
  - **Rollout is per-surface by construction.** A draft is only built when BOTH queue docs carry `draftOptIn`, which `/spar` sets and `/debate-chat` and the background "Spar live" pairs do not. A client that cannot render a board must never be handed one; it would sit on a card it does not understand until the reaper swept it, which is precisely what `/debate-chat` did when the consent gate went universal on 2026-08-10.
  - **The AI fallback drafts too**, so a thin queue is a different opponent rather than a worse ritual. Stateless endpoint, two calls, and the AI's strikes are computed only after the human's are committed; everything after the reveal resolves client-side because there is no opponent left to protect. A failed slate hands off to the plain AI round rather than costing anyone their round.
  - **Guarded and mutation-tested.** 28,901 assertions in the pre-commit hook cover the strike cap (four strikes from one side would leave one survivor of its own choosing), pick authority, timeout determinism, and a 4000-draft fuzz asserting every reachable draft opens a legal round with two different sides. Eight mutations were applied one at a time and all eight caught, but **two guards were found VACUOUS first and are worth remembering as a pattern**: comparing two `autoResolve` calls let a `Math.random()` side pass half the time, which is a flaky test rather than a guard, and the struck-motion fallback is unreachable through the public API so the fuzz never touched it. Both were rewritten to fire every time.
  - **`?draftdemo=1` renders the board against a synthetic pair with every POST short-circuited** (`&overlap=0` forces the clean split, `&role=side` holds the side call). Same precedent as the landing's `?first=` arm. The draft is a sequence of timed beats, so the only honest way to judge the feel of it, or to change it later, is to watch it run without needing two signed-in humans and a live queue. Browser verification through that view caught three real defects: the face state labels inherited the name's ellipsis clamp ("you called the m..."), the reveal's attribution badge was absolutely positioned and painted over the longest motion (which is the one most likely to survive), and the sitewide `#daExpAsk` audience chooser floats at z-index 99990 straight over motions 4 and 5 on a 375px screen, so it is suppressed while a board with a 14 second clock is up.
  - **Open, and it is Aidan's call:** the clocks are 14s to strike and 9s per pick, chosen to keep the worst case near 30s of pre-round. If the strike beat reads rushed on real rounds the number is the dial to turn, not the feature to remove. Nothing yet reports how often a draft is decided by a clock rather than a person; `spar_draft_strike` carries an `auto` flag, so that is one GA4 read away.

- **Guests spar twice before the queue asks for an account** (2026-08-19, Aidan: "allow every user to use omegle feature when they join once or twice then require google sign in after, gotta get ppl hooked first"). Partially reverses the 2026-08-18 named-account gate below, and along the axis that entry itself named: it closed by saying that if joins collapsed without signups rising, "the fallback is a one-round guest trial before the wall, not a return to unlimited anonymous queueing." This is that fallback, at two rounds. **What changed and what did not.** A guest gets `GUEST_FREE_ROUNDS` (2, env-overridable) live rounds against real people, then the sign-in card. The three layers that refused guests come down to one: the /spar client gate now renders only when the allowance is spent, `firestore.rules` accepts a signed-in owner's `matchmaking_queue` create again, and `spar-pair.mjs` — the chokepoint — meters instead of refusing. Everything else the 2026-08-18 sweep closed stays closed: `/live` still refuses to mint a guest into a debater seat, `/partners` still reads named-only, `/debate-chat` keeps its own `isNamedUser` gate (it routes through the same metered spar-pair, so even if that gate moved it would be inside the allowance), the background "Spar live" availability pill stays named-only (being silently matchable sitewide while browsing is a different decision from trying a round on the page that offers one), and DMs stay named-only because the rules refuse an anonymous writer. **Why the door and not the wall.** /debate-online takes 68% of organic clicks and the queries behind it are "debate omegle", "debate strangers", "debate random people". Those visitors want a stranger and a motion, now; a sign-in wall asks them to open an account for a thing they have not seen. The wall moves to after the round, where it can point at something they just did. **The metering is the whole safety argument.** The 2026-08-18 objection was correct and is not answered by wishing: anonymous uids are free and unlimited to mint, so an ungated guest lane is an unbounded queue-filler and an unaccountable opponent. What bounds it is the allowance, and the allowance only means something if the client cannot reset it — the exact failure the free-round wall fixed on the AI lane the same morning. So it is metered server-side against the anonymous Firebase uid `js/notifications.js` already mints on every page, in `guest_rounds/{uid}`: admin-SDK writes only, owner read so /spar can say "last free round" honestly instead of discovering the wall at the pair. Verified in production that a client can read its own counter, cannot read anyone else's, and cannot write its own. **Charged when the room opens, not when a proposal is accepted** — a guest who accepts and is stood up by a no-show has not had their round, and both free rounds would burn without them meeting anybody. The peer side is charged from the server's own record of who is a guest, never from the queue doc's `anonymous` field, which is client-written and could be set false to buy extra rounds; the mandatory consent handshake guarantees every guest makes an authenticated call before any room opens, so the record is always there to read. **Conversion rides on linking, not registering.** `auth-modal.js` already links the anonymous uid on every provider, so signing in keeps the uid, the rounds just played, and the record. The gate says so, because "keep what you did" is a different offer from "register to begin". Both gate states are tagged separately in GA4 (`guest_rounds_spent` vs `no_session`) so one blended rate cannot hide whichever is working. **Watch the same number as before, from the other side:** guest matches against conversions at the spent wall. If guests play twice and never sign in, the hook is not hooking and the answer is a better wall, not a wider allowance. Follow-up caught in production: `markGuest` shipped unawaited and Lambda freezes the context on return, so the mark was being abandoned rather than deferred; awaited now, deduped per warm instance. **CLOSED 2026-08-22 morning (named accounts again, ahead of a paid push), REOPENED 2026-08-22 evening on Aidan's second call the same day — see the guest-trial-reopens entry near the top of this log. This entry's design is the live state again.**

- **Paid-upstream endpoints metered by caller, and `body.premium` stopped being taken at its word** (2026-08-19, second pass the same day, after the anonymous free round shipped). Five endpoints that spend real provider money (`tts`, `transcribe`, `translate`, `flow`, `extract-claims`) had each grown their own idea of a caller, and all five had landed on per-IP-and-nothing-else. That is wrong in both directions at once: an IP is not a person, so a school on one NAT shares a single cap, and it is not an account either, so a paying user was metered against the guest on the next desk. New `lib/caller.mjs` resolves a caller once, most specific identity first (named account > anonymous Firebase uid > IP, namespaced so a uid and an IP can never collide), and named accounts now get a larger budget. That is what "sign in for higher limits" in the old TTS error message had been promising and never delivering. **The real find was `/api/tts`,** where entitlement to the expensive providers was one line: `const premium = !!body.premium`. It arrived in the request JSON. Any visitor could open devtools and POST `{premium:true, provider:'cartesia'}` and be routed to ElevenLabs or Cartesia on our bill; App Check meant they had to be in a real browser, which is not much of an obstacle for the person doing it. Entitlement now resolves from the verified account, cached 10 minutes per uid because a round is dozens of speech chunks and a Firestore read on each would be its own cost problem. A free user **downgrades silently to the OpenAI voice rather than erroring**, so the round still finishes; verified live from a guest session forging both flags, which came back `X-TTS-Provider: openai` with audio intact. `PAID_PLANS` was checked against the canonical `PLANS` map in `lib/firestore.mjs` so lifetime and BYOK keep HD voice; only `trial` is excluded. **Four more defects surfaced on the way, all the same family.** `tts`, `transcribe`, `flow`, and `translate` each kept their counter in a module-scope `Map`, and Netlify runs every concurrent invocation in its own isolate, so "100/day" meant 100 per warm instance and a cold start wiped the count. This is precisely the bug `lib/rate-limit.mjs` was written to fix for `realtime-session.mjs`; these four were never moved over. `transcribe` and `flow` read `x-forwarded-for` BEFORE the Netlify-set address, and that header is client-settable, so rotating one header walked straight past the cap. `translate` had a 60-second window and nothing above it, so a caller pinned just under 90/min ran unbounded all day; it now has hour and day layers. And `/api/tts` advertised only `Content-Type` in its CORS allow-headers, so an Authorization header would have failed a cross-origin preflight. **Observability:** TTS responses now carry `X-TTS-Provider`. Without it there was no way to tell a premium response from a free one, which made "is the paid gate actually working" unanswerable from outside the function. **The standing rule this leaves:** any endpoint that spends provider money identifies its caller through `lib/caller.mjs` and meters through `lib/rate-limit.mjs`. Do not write a sixth per-IP `Map`.

- **The free round is now real: server-enforced sign-in wall on the anonymous lane** (2026-08-19, Aidan: "to reduce bots and ppl draining tokens / api costs etc, require sign ins etc to rlly get the ball rolling on retention"). The free tier had never been enforced anywhere a visitor could not reach. `ANON_LIMIT = 1` in practice.html and index.html is a localStorage counter; clear it, open a private window, or POST the endpoint directly, and you are a new visitor with a fresh round, forever. The only server-side ceiling on `/api/claude` was a per-IP layer at 800 calls/day, roughly 160 rounds, sized deliberately wide so a school behind one NAT would not lock itself out. So the actual anonymous allowance was "as many rounds as you care to ask for," and every one of them was a Sonnet call on our bill. **Three findings made the fix cheap.** (1) `js/notifications.js` already calls `signInAnonymously()` on nearly every page, so every visitor is holding a real, signature-checkable Firebase uid before they ever start a round. That is an identity clearing localStorage does not reset. (2) `js/app-check.js` already wraps `window.fetch` to attach the App Check token to `/api/*`, so there was one place to also attach the ID token, no call-site edits. (3) `realtime-session.mjs` had solved the hard half on 2026-07-28: `isNamedAccount()` reads `firebase.sign_in_provider` off the token and tells a Google/password account from the `signInAnonymously()` shadow user. That helper moved to `lib/auth.mjs`. **The gate.** Three lanes on `/api/claude`, tightest first: a named account gets per-uid metering and its team plan; an anonymous uid gets `ANON_FREE_CALLS` (6, env-overridable, sized at one round plus slack since a round is roughly 5 calls) and then a hard 401 `SIGN_IN_REQUIRED`; a caller with no token at all still gets only the per-IP layers. 401 not 429, because waiting does not clear it and the client should open the auth modal rather than render "try again later" — both practice.html and index.html now intercept the code and call `openAuthModal('signup')`, tagged `anon_round_gate` in GA4 so the wall's conversion rate is measurable rather than assumed. **Two holes closed on the way.** An anonymous token used to satisfy `verifyIdToken()` and drop the caller into the SIGNED-IN beta lane at 20/day, skipping App Check and the IP layers entirely, and minting anonymous uids is free and unlimited. And the prep-time warm ping fired `{warm:true}` at the brain endpoint, but the body-level warm short-circuit runs AFTER metering, so the ping would have spent one of the six free calls without generating anything; it now sends `X-Warm: 1`, answered above the lanes (forging it buys nothing, it returns without generating). **Deliberately NOT done:** no wall at the door. The 2026-04-20 reversal is still right about full-screen gates, and the failure it documented (Safari ITP, in-app browsers, blocked Firebase) is real. This wall arrives after a complete round with a ballot already on screen, which is a different object. A token that fails verification falls DOWN to the anonymous lane instead of 401-ing, since app-check.js now attaches tokens automatically and a stale one must not kill a round that used to work. The per-IP minute and hour caps are untouched, because those are what absorb a class starting together; only the day cap moved, 800 to 300, and only because that lane is now the exception path rather than the main one. **Side effect worth knowing:** paid brains were silently broken on /practice. `callAI` never sent an Authorization header, so `requirePaidPlan` 401'd every Grok/Gemini/GPT call and the "brain is broken" fallback quietly rerouted it to Claude. A paid user picking Grok has been getting Claude. Attaching the token fixes that too.

- **Recording template rebuilt camera-first, and the replay stops double-printing the cameras** (2026-08-19, Aidan off a /watch replay screenshot: "make a cooler template that makes the zoom larger and overlay more simple"). The round board a recording shows was mostly empty black chrome (eyebrow, three-line motion, a long speech sentence, an 84px clock, names at the bottom) while the debaters lived in Daily's postage-stamp corner tile. Two coupled changes. (1) **The board now draws both camera feeds INTO the canvas** as two large head-to-head panels: the driver (already in call-object mode, so tracks are readable) wraps each seat's video track in a hidden `<video>` element, cover-crops it into its panel, and refreshes track bindings on a 600ms throttle instead of per frame. Active speaker carries a red edge, each panel gets a name+side chip, camera-off renders the debater's initial. Overlay cut to a one-or-two-line motion up top and one compact timer block below; the eyebrow and the "Speech 1 of 4 · Pro Constructive · Rin A." sentence are gone. Board video refs release on both teardown paths WITHOUT stopping the tracks, which belong to the room. (2) **The recording layout moved off `preset:'default'`** — with the cameras now inside the board, Daily's composite was pasting the same faces a second time as small corner tiles. `round-recording.mjs` starts recordings with `preset:'custom'`, `composition_params:{mode:'single', 'videoSettings.preferScreenshare':true}`, so the live share fills the frame alone; with no share up it degrades to a single camera. Per the standing max_cam_streams rule the body was **verified against the live API on a real throwaway room before shipping** (the bad-key control still 400s with the historical message; the candidate passed validation and failed only on "no call live"), and a 400 at start time retries once on the default preset so a layout rejection can never cost the recording itself. Old recordings are baked video and keep the old look. Honest edge: the opening seconds before the board share comes up record one camera full-frame, then switch.

- **The Open gets a funnel: sitewide countdown strip, redirect-surviving registration, entrant share ask** (2026-08-19, Aidan: ship all four funnel fixes). Live Firestore showed the page converts and nobody reaches it: since Aug 11, /tournaments had ~12 unique visitors against 297 on the homepage, and 7 of the 12 registered. The Aug 12 cut of the tournament band and the first screen's "win money" link left the homepage with no above-the-fold path to the Open, so the fix is a THIN strip, not the 1285px band that cut retired: (1) topbar.js renders a dismissible one-line `.ui-beta-strip` ("The Debatable Open · Sat Aug 29 · $850 in prizes · Free entry ends Friday →") on every topbar page except /tournaments and /tournament-rules; sessionStorage dismiss, tail clause self-swaps after the Friday cutoff and on event day, whole strip self-retires after Aug 29, native app already hides the class. (2) tournaments.html registration now survives a reload: the entry click stashes `{mode, age, t}` in sessionStorage (15-min TTL), a resume block finishes the registration when the signed-in user lands back, and the bare Google-popup fallback falls through to signInWithRedirect on popup-blocked/webview errors. This is the "flow survives a reload" change the 2026-08-12 sign-in sweep deliberately deferred for this page. (3) After "You are in," a share card offers native share / copy-link ("Rounds are better with people you know") with GA4 `tournament_entrant_share`. The Friday deadline email needed no build: admin-open-reminder.mjs already exists on the /admin 'or' card; it fires Friday morning. Known label wart, not fixed here: FOUNDING_CUTOFF_LABEL says "Friday, August 22" but Aug 22 is a Saturday; enforcement (Aug 22 23:59 ET) is more generous than the public "ends Friday" claim, so nobody is wrongly refused.

- **Display face: Inter, on the fifth pick of the day; the live-round board stops drawing in Georgia; the stage rail is retired** (2026-08-19, same session as the type phase 2 entry below). The display-voice search ran three rounds in a few hours, all Aidan on sight of live pages: Archivo-Expanded shipped and reverted ("too spread out and robotic"), Bricolage Grotesque shipped universal off an options board plus an approved homepage trial and was rejected the same hour on /live ("horrible - make it readable professional find another one"), and Schibsted lasted one look ("nah i want a better font"); off a fourth-round board Aidan delegated the choice ("i trust ur pick") to Hanken Grotesk, which also lasted one look; a 23-font interactive tester followed, he delegated again with a sharper brief ("be professional easy to read not confusing or unique"), and **INTER** (400-900 variable) is the standing pick, live sitewide at SW v2560. The brief explicitly rules out character — do not pitch distinctive display faces on this site again without Aidan reopening the question. Mechanics unchanged from the Bricolage rollout it replaced: ui.css `--font-display` carries it to every h1-h6 through the existing !important force; body, interface, buttons, and the Archivo-900 wordmark are untouched; Source Serif 4 stays the judge's voice; every per-page font URL and the ui.css @import swap families in one sed. **Standing lesson, now proven twice: neither a mock card nor even one approved live page predicts the verdict on the surface Aidan actually opens next — expect display-face calls to take iterations, keep each swap a one-token change so iterating stays cheap.** Two riders from his live-round screenshots, same push: (1) the recording layout board + verdict reel canvas still drew in `Georgia, serif` on every line, a Crimson-era leftover the 2026-08-19 Arena sweep missed because ctx.font strings don't grep like CSS — labels and clocks now Archivo, display lines 36px+ Schibsted; (2) the nameplate + AI-judge **stage rail under the live-round video is hidden** ("get rid of the thing at the bottom its weird") — display:none on .stage-rail, ids and the JS that writes into them intact, restore = delete one line. This retires the visible half of the 2026-07-22 "stage rail" decision; the AI-judge presence now reads only through the ballot flow.

- **Betting/points system de-surfaced sitewide** (2026-08-19, Aidan: "remove the betting / point system from website for now - like the 1000 points at top of page"). The whole points economy came off the site chrome in one pass: the topbar wallet chip (the 1,000 pts pill shipped 2026-08-12, which read as "1000 points at the top of the page" because 37 of 38 wallets sat untouched at the opening grant), the Predict flagship tile and Money link in the Explore menu and mobile sheet, the landing footer's /predict link, the prediction-board sentence in the can-I-win-money FAQ (visible + JSON-LD), the masterclass prediction-board pitch, and future.html's "Watch and predict" CTA (now "Watch a round" → /spectate). The Floor band on the landing was already parked since 2026-07-31, so nothing there moved. **Pages stay live** at /predict, /floor, /get-paid-to-debate for direct links, matching every prior nav demotion; the server ledger and settle plumbing are untouched. "For now" is the operative phrase: restoring is re-adding the nav entries (restore notes inline in topbar.js) plus the chip implementation from git history. Cash-prize tournaments are a separate thing and keep their surfaces. Commit `bcfed033`, SW v2550, verified live: no chip, no Predict/Money in the nav, zero console errors.

- **Apple sign-in reaches the web, and Facebook is wired to the last Meta-controlled step** (2026-08-18, Aidan: "ok setup those sign in methods yeah", follow-through on the sign-in gate above). **Apple is LIVE end to end.** Aidan signed into the developer portal (team 35Z3KB54MV); from his browser the rest was driven: Services ID `com.debateai.debateit.web` registered against the primary App ID (which already carried the Sign In with Apple capability), domain + return URL pointed at the Firebase auth handler, signing key `M96PG9S5LA` created (the .p8 was a one-time download; it lives in the Firebase provider config now and the Downloads copy can be deleted), and the `apple.com` provider PATCHed: clientId = the Services ID, `appleSignInConfig.codeFlowConfig` = team + key + private key. **Native iOS sign-in is unaffected**: native token audiences validate against the iOS apps registered in the Firebase project (com.debateai.debateit is one of three), not against the provider clientId; verified before the swap. The web modal offers "Continue with Apple" everywhere now (the `__DB_NATIVE` gate only ever controlled visibility) and the web popup path gained the same anonymous-link dance as Google. **Proven with a discriminating probe:** Apple's authorize endpoint serves the real sign-in page for our client_id + return URL and returns `invalid_client` for a bogus id. Still untested: one real human Apple sign-in. **Facebook is configured but NOT public yet, and the remaining gate is Meta's, not ours.** App "Debatable" (id 2205759213330810) created under Aidan's Meta developer account (he re-entered his password for creation and the secret reveal; terms acceptance was his explicit yes); Facebook Login use case with `email` + `public_profile`, redirect URI = the Firebase handler, strict mode on, app domain + site URL + privacy/terms/data-deletion URLs + Entertainment category all saved; `facebook.com` provider enabled in Firebase with the app credentials. **New Meta apps launch in development mode: login works only for app-role users until the app passes identity verification (business or individual) and is published.** So the "Continue with Facebook" button is deliberately NOT in the modal yet — it would error for the public. AIDAN ACTIONS: (1) Meta dashboard -> Publish -> Start Verification, verify as a person with ID (or as the C-corp once it exists); (2) upload the app icon (ready at ~/Downloads/debatable-meta-app-icon-1024.png); (3) after Meta approves and the app is Published, say the word and the modal button ships the same hour. **Microsoft: skipped on purpose** — the only Microsoft session on this machine is the UChicago tenant, and a personal Azure account is his to create if ever wanted; user overlap does not justify it today.

- **The live queue requires a named account, and the guest door was three doors wide** (2026-08-18, Aidan: "require ppl to sign in with email or google for this tbh i want to increase amount of ppl who actually sign up"). Guest queueing is retired on every live-pairing surface. The gate on /spar now routes everyone into the shared chooser (Google, emailed link, email/password) instead of offering "Start as guest". **The button was the smallest part of it.** `js/notifications.js` signs nearly every visitor in anonymously for the bell, so `currentUser` was almost always truthy, and three separate places read that as signed in: (1) /spar's boot handler dropped the anonymous session straight into `joinQueue()`, (2) the background "Spar live" availability pill accepted any user (`isQueueUser` was `!!u`) and `ensureQueueUser` MINTED an anonymous account on demand when there wasn't one, so the queue could be joined from any page on the site, and (3) `/debate-chat` pairs strangers into a casual room off the same `matchmaking_queue` collection and hid its own sign-in gate on the same truthy check. So a "signed-out" visitor was already queueing, already being matched with real people, and never had a reason to sign up. All three now gate on `isNamedUser()`, and `joinQueue()` on both pages is the single choke point every entry path funnels through (find button, autoqueue from casual-room's "Next opponent", the 400ms requeue, the deep links). **This is the same trap as the 2026-07-28 rate-limit correction and the memory note behind it: an anonymous Firebase user is not a signed-in user, and `!!currentUser` on this site means "has a browser", not "has an account".** Verified on production with a live anonymous session: the gate renders, no queue doc is written, the pill is hidden, and clicking it opens the chooser rather than enlisting the guest. **CLOSED SAME DAY on Aidan's go-ahead ("trust you fixing the problems"), and the audit that followed found the gate was one of five doors, not three.** A read-only sweep of every other pairing surface turned up: (4) **`/live` MINTED an anonymous account to seat someone in a live debate** ("Creating your guest identity" on clicking an empty debater seat), which is precisely the empty-chair failure the 2026-08-11 ready-check work exists to prevent, since nobody can ever sign back into that account; the same page let a guest take the judge seat, post a challenge to the public board, and accept someone else's. (5) **`/partners` read `!!state.user` for the whole page**, so a guest got the full 2v2 pool and duo queue. **And the layer that mattered most: nothing was enforced anywhere.** `spar-pair.mjs`, `partner-match.mjs` and `duo-pair.mjs` verified a Firebase token and never checked the provider, so a hand-rolled POST with an anonymous token still paired two real people regardless of what any client did; `tournament-dropin.mjs` already had the correct guard and was the pattern the other three copied. There are now three layers: the client gate, the server provider check, and `firestore.rules` (`matchmaking_queue` create and `live_challenges` create/accept moved from `isSignedIn()` to the pre-existing `isNamedAccount()`). **Proven rather than assumed:** the deployed ruleset was diffed byte-identical to `origin/main` before editing, and after deploy an anonymous production session running the exact write the old client made returned `permission-denied`. `isNamedAccount()` has gated `waitlist_posts` since 2026-05-20, so it is already known to pass real named accounts. Left permissive on purpose: queue `update`/`delete` and the un-accept branch, all of which can only touch a doc the caller already owns, so a stale anonymous doc can be released rather than stranded. **`live_rounds` create is deliberately untouched** (reached by flows not traced end to end, and the seat and queue gates upstream are what decide who pairs). **Trade-off accepted, and it is the point:** this is a deliberate funnel narrowing on the surface §4 says should stay advise-not-require. The advise-not-require principle still governs the AI surfaces (/practice, voice), where a stranger can still try the product without an account. Live human pairing is the exception, and the reason it is defensible is that the other person in the round is a real person who deserves an accountable counterpart. Watch signups against spar queue-joins; if joins collapse without signups rising, the gate is costing more than it earns and the fallback is a one-round guest trial before the wall, not a return to unlimited anonymous queueing. **PARTIALLY REVERSED 2026-08-19 (see the guest-rounds entry at the top of this log): the guest trial is now live at two rounds, metered server-side. The enforcement layers described above still stand everywhere except /spar's queue; what changed is that /spar meters guests instead of refusing them.**

  - **The gate made sign-in mandatory and then put it below the fold**, measured the same day: the gate reused the searching screen's 460px "fireplace" globe, which is right where the globe IS the content and wrong on a card whose only job is one action, so on 1280x800 both buttons sat at 913px and 969px. A parallel session hit the same problem from the overflow side and replaced the globe with two static preview tiles and a VS disc; that landed first and is better (no canvas to break), so the capped-globe version written here was discarded at rebase rather than merged. Verified after: gate height 1112px → 682px, every element above the fold on desktop and mobile, no horizontal overflow. Same pass, the email button's white-on-`--accent` measured **3.76:1** at its real 15.3px/800 rendering, under AA (the 3:1 allowance needs 18.66px bold), so it moved to `#dc2626` at 4.83:1. **Standing note: the gate is now the only way into the queue, so its contrast and fold position are load-bearing, not polish.**

  - **CLOSED 2026-08-18, Aidan's go-ahead:** `partner_pool`, `duo_queue` and `duo_teams` had **no rules at all** in `firestore.rules`, which ends in an explicit default deny, so `/partners`' client writes to `partner_pool/{uid}` had silently failed on every write since the 2026-07-28 build; the open-pool half of 2v2 matching never held a single doc and the page just rendered an empty pool. The invite-code path (admin SDK) was never affected. Rules now: `partner_pool` mirrors `matchmaking_queue` (signed-in read for the browsable pool, own-doc create/update/delete, NAMED accounts only with no legacy carve-out since no doc ever existed, note capped, and update re-asserts the merged `uid` because the 45s heartbeat and leavePool are merge sets); `duo_queue` is read-only to clients (its writes are the team-keyed pairing transaction in `duo-pair.mjs`, so a client write could forge a match); `duo_teams` stays under the default deny on purpose because no client ever reads it (my-team rides the API). **Verified with the Rules test API against the live project before deploy, 8 cases:** named creates own doc ALLOW, anonymous DENY, cross-user DENY, signed-out read DENY, client duo_queue write DENY, duo_teams read DENY. Deployed from the worktree per the standing rules-deploy discipline (deployed ruleset diffed byte-identical to origin/main first).

- **Audience mode goes live: one competitive-or-new answer forks the copy sitewide** (2026-08-18, Aidan: "make a competitive debater button and the entire site keeps the debate jargon language, but for ppl who aren't competitive, simplify the site"). The plumbing predated this (audience-mode.js, `debateos-experience` in localStorage synced by prefs-sync.js, the onboarding.js question, the /settings control) but it was loaded on 3 pages, nothing visible set it for signed-out visitors, and no copy anywhere read it. Four moves: (1) topbar.js now injects audience-mode.js on every topbar page, same lazy pattern as auth-modal.js, so `html[data-debate-experience]` and `window.DebatableAudience` exist sitewide. (2) audience-mode.js gained a generic reversible copy swap: any element carrying `data-plain="simpler wording"` shows that wording when the answer is new/unsure and its authored debate-register text for competitive and unanswered visitors; MutationObserver covers late-rendered nodes; the authored string is stashed on the element so switching back restores it byte-identical. Annotating a page = adding data-plain attributes, no JS. 13 landing strings are annotated (ballot, flow, speaks, POIs, prelims). (3) The landing got the visible button: a quiet one-line chooser ("Do you compete in debate?" / I compete / I'm new to this), hidden forever once answered, identical in both first_screen_copy_v1 arms so it cannot tilt that test, GA4 `audience_choice`. It shipped overlaying the foot of #first-screen; later the same day Aidan asked for a clean first screen, so it moved down into the #landing-more row and sits to the left of the Full tour button (GA4 surface `landing_tour_row`; the gap rule rides the #landing-more id because the footer-area .lm-wl-row block resets gap to 0 later in the cascade). (4) **The big one: practice.html's `da-is-debater` audience fork was dead code** — its writer (the animated landing intro) was deleted long ago, so ~25 `audienceCopy()` call sites, the plain-vs-circuit `ballotAudienceRegister` LLM block, and the opponent's audience-register prompt block never fired for anyone. They now fall back to `debateos-experience` (competitive→competing, new→learning, unsure→curious), so a competitive answer gets circuit vocabulary from the AI and the UI ("Tough Judge", "Competitive Debater") while everyone else gets plain English ("Strict Judge", "judge feedback"); the explicit per-round `da-opp-register` toggle still outranks both. Verified in a browser in both directions on landing and practice. Design rule going forward: the authored default stays debate-register (SEO + the sharpest-use-case audience), plain is opt-in via the answer; keep plain variants literal, not dumbed down. Rider found while verifying, NOT fixed here: the /practice Saved tab crashes with React error #300 (hooks-order violation) for signed-out visitors on production today; it predates this change. FIXED same day (`6ac57e53`): the saved-view early return sat above the format-quiz keyboard useEffect, so rendering the Saved tab skipped a hook; the view now builds in place and returns after the hook. It hit every visitor, signed in or out, not just signed-out. Standing lesson for practice.html: in that one giant component, any hook added below the saved-view return point would reintroduce this, so new hooks go above all early returns.

- **The Open can be open: entries and drop-in rounds run at the same time** (2026-08-18, found by walking the announcement email's own CTA as a stranger hours after mailing it to 205 people). `register` required status `registration` and `tournament-dropin.mjs` required `running`, so **the two states were mutually exclusive** and the event could only ever be half-open: either people could enter and no round could ever start, or rounds ran and every latecomer was refused. Both the published rules and the email promise the opposite ("doors open at 10:00 AM Eastern and stay open through the day", "turning up late does not shut you out"). The status machine was written for a synchronous tournament, where register → close → pair → run is the correct sequence; a drop-in day needs register and run held at once, and nothing had reconciled the two since the drop-in copy shipped. **Measured rather than reasoned about**, against a local stub serving the real Aug 29 state: the unmodified `/tournaments` hid the entry card entirely and rendered "No bracket is open for entry right now", with the hero still reading "Registration open" — so a person arriving from the email at 2pm on the Saturday would have found no way in at all, on the one day it matters. Three coupled fixes: `register` accepts `registration` or `running` (entries close when the director moves to `break`/`elims`, which is what the rules describe); the public lobby finds a `running` bracket, preferring one still in `registration` when both exist, with status copy to match; and the control room's button, which said **"Close registration"** while its actual effect was starting the day, now says "Start the day (doors stay open)" with "Close entries" as its own evening step. That label was the sharpest trap in the set: a director who wanted latecomers would have avoided pressing the one control that opens the queue. **Standing lesson, and it is the third of this shape in two days: two correct-looking states can be individually right and jointly impossible, and nothing in either file says so.** The engine and the copy were each self-consistent; only walking the promise end to end on the target date surfaced it.

- **Drop-in rounds verify, rate, and score: the two half-built engines become one** (2026-08-18). Two parallel sessions built the drop-in queue twice with different data models, and the halves never met. The 2026-08-11 session wrote `tournament-live.mjs` + `lib/tournament-ledger.mjs` (fields `available`/`waitingSince`/`pairedRoom`, rooms keyed `r{n}`) and wired `live-judge.mjs` to auto-post judged results through it; the 2026-08-12 session wired the queue the PAGE actually calls, `tournament-dropin.mjs` (fields `availableAt`/`inPairing`, rooms keyed `d{seq}`). Three consequences, all of which would have surfaced on Aug 29: (1) **every drop-in room failed `parseTournamentRoom`** because `ROOM_RE` only accepted `r`/`e` keys, so drop-in rounds fell back to casual consent and never auto-recorded a result — the exact silent failure the verifier's own header warns about; (2) `/api/tournament-live` was a zero-consumer endpoint that could still double-book a person (its `claimPairing` checks `pairedRoom` while the wired queue checks `inPairing`, so each was blind to the other's seatings); (3) the old ledger and the director's report action BOTH incremented wins with no mutual guard, so a judge-applied result followed by a director entry double-counted. Fixes: `ROOM_RE` accepts `d` keys (pinned in test-tournament-round); `applyTournamentResult` rewritten onto the wired model with **`resultPatch` as the single field authority**, marking the round-doc pairing `complete` inside the transaction so the judge and the director guard each other (`already_reported` in both directions, the director's amend flow stays the only way to correct); entries release to IDLE on completion (the 2026-08-11 empty-chair posture: rejoining the pool is a deliberate act); an auto-applied elim sets the loser `eliminated` because the director path's post-transaction update can no longer run; the orphan endpoint is deleted. **Standing note on the 2026-07-28 "results are entered by the director" decision: live-judge has auto-posted tournament results since 2026-08-11** (it just wrote them in a shape nothing read for drop-ins and double-countable for prelims) — the human-confirmation posture survives as the amend flow, not as manual entry. `lib/tournament-live.mjs` (the published points rules + its pre-commit guard) is untouched.

- **Type phase 2: dead font payloads out, tabular figures and balanced headlines in sitewide** (2026-08-19, from Aidan's "look into other universal font changes", follow-through approved same session). The Arena swap was a family swap, not yet a type system; this pass ships the objective half. (1) ~32 pages still carried font links nothing renders (17 Fraunces, 14 Inter including index.html's early full-weight Inter link, report.html's Inter+Newsreader) — all shadowed by ui.css forcing Archivo with !important. Removed; Caveat on /exhibition and JetBrains Mono stay because they genuinely render. tournament-rules (no ui.css) moves its Fraunces-first headings to Source Serif 4. (2) ui.css universal rules: `h1-h3 {text-wrap:balance}` + `p {text-wrap:pretty}` (headlines stop stranding single words), and `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums` on time/table/[class*= timer|score|count|stat|odds|pct|clock] so live values (vote %, clocks, streaks) stop shifting layout as digits flip — substring matches over-apply harmlessly since tabular-nums only affects digits. SW v2549, verified live on /pricing (no Fraunces) and ui.css. **RESOLVED same day, both directions in one hour: Aidan approved the Expanded grammar off a rendered comparison ("word"), it shipped universal (h1 Expanded 125%/800, h2 112%, wordmark 118%, SW v2551), and he rejected it on sight of the live /spar ("too spread out and robotic idk i dont like it") — reverted at SW v2552, verified live (font-stretch back to 100%, authored weights restored). Two things survive: the variable Archivo URL (`wdth,wght@62..125,100..900`, one file replacing six static weights) stays loaded everywhere since an unused axis costs nothing, and a guard comment in ui.css says not to re-add font-stretch display rules without a new call. Lesson: a mock approved in a comparison card is not approval of the look at page scale on real surfaces — ship display-grammar changes to ONE page and screenshot it before going universal. Still genuinely open: the display face itself. A six-option board went to Aidan (Archivo 900 as-is / Bricolage Grotesque / Schibsted Grotesk / Instrument Serif / Anton / Source Serif 4), recommendation Bricolage; the weight drift (homepage h1 900 vs /pricing 600) also remains unfixed, deliberately parked until the face is picked so pages only reflow once.**

- **CORRECTION, same day: the Arena type system went UNIVERSAL** (2026-08-19, Aidan mid-session with a screenshot of /spar still in Crimson: "universal font changes should be universal"). The entry below records the landing-only pass; hours later the whole site followed. ui.css tokens re-point to Archivo with a sitewide `--font-judge`, the ui.css @import and 122 per-page Crimson Pro font links swap to Archivo + Source Serif 4, and 734 hardcoded Crimson stacks across 152 files become Archivo at their existing weights. The ui.css "Crimson is the house face" comment is rewritten (it contradicted the call), the /future colophon and three stale typeface comments follow, and Crimson Pro no longer loads anywhere. Commit `103144cb`, SW v2546, verified live on /spar, /practice, /watch, /tournaments and /judge (zero Crimson references served). The "crimson" THEME (red-on-black palette) is a color name, not the font, and is untouched.

- **Landing moves to the Arena type system: Archivo speaks for the arena, Source Serif 4 for the judge, monospace for the score** (2026-08-19, from Aidan's landing-redesign brief at `Codex/2026-08-19/revie/outputs/`, "ok get to it"). The page ran everything in Crimson Pro (136 stack declarations: wordmark, headlines, nav, buttons, ballots, timers), which read as one editorial voice with no functional hierarchy. Now four voices, one page: ARENA statements in Archivo 900, INTERFACE in Archivo 400-800, the JUDGE's written reasoning (first-screen RFD, live-proof ballot, reviews quotes) in Source Serif 4, and every clock, percentage, speaker point and live status in the system monospace stack. **The seam is the token layer:** ui.css's `--font-display`/`--font-body` are re-pointed in a landing-only `:root` block that sits after the ui.css link in source order, so the other ~85 pages keep Crimson Pro untouched; a new `--font-judge` joins them. Brief deviation, recorded: display is Archivo 900 (the family's black cut) rather than the separate Archivo Black face, because ~40 display selectors pin weights 700/800 and a 400-only face would synthesize bold. **Hierarchy moves with it:** the Google sign-in unit moved BELOW the proof board on every width (DOM move, so screen-reader order tracks visual order) and restyled quiet; the judged round is the first thing in the right column on desktop and sits directly under the CTAs on mobile (board top 764→383px at 375w, ballot 1071→689px); the presence globe hides on mobile where it was 233px of ambience ahead of the proof; the five topbar social icons hide on this page only (footer keeps them). The live-proof ballot carries a hard red offset rule in both themes. All CTAs, `data-cta`/`data-ab-test` attributes, the `[data-fs-signin]` handler and the first_screen_copy_v1 arms are untouched. **Truth sweep riding along, and it was live-wrong:** every speaker-point figure on the landing was still on the retired 30 scale while `leaderboard_entries` migrated to 1-100 on 2026-08-18, so the ranked band printed "out of 30" beside 100-scale server rows with every bar clamped to 100%. The renderer is now scale-aware (fmtScore, bandLabel through the migration's comm anchors, bar scaled to the 50-100 band, label computed from rows) and all demo data (24 example rounds, 8 sample-board rows, the ballot mock's 28/26 → 88/62, the how-it-works chip, the "a 29 in APDA" line) converted through the same anchors. NOT done in this pass, deliberately: the story-compression phase of the brief (merging tour chapters) — the one-pager gate already keeps the collapsed page to one screen, and section consolidation touches the TOC/cv/analytics lattice, so it is its own pass; and no dated or numeric claim was added anywhere. Commits `38ea6c76` + `d3a0e70f`, SW v2544, verified on production in both themes at 1280/375/320.

- **Speech 1 starts itself: the kickoff strip becomes a 20s countdown** (2026-08-18, from Aidan's open-ended "improve debatable somehow"; target picked from the funnel, not intuition). In the sign-in gate's first ~6 live hours, `live_kickoff_shown` fired 47 times across 3 rounds against exactly ONE started speech: two people sit in a working video call and nobody presses Start, which is the same do-nothing-at-the-threshold failure as the 411-rounds ready-check finding, one screen later. The opener's strip now counts down 20 seconds ("Your speech starts in 20s", Start now, Wait) and at zero starts speech 1 through `startSpeechTimer()`, the SAME code path as the transport button, so the judge-lock guard, clock sync, and every downstream rule ride along. **The countdown pauses rather than fires blind:** hidden tab, an open resolution-change proposal (`#rmbPropose`), or unsettled judge picks (`judgeLockKey()` falsy) all freeze it, because auto-starting a round whose motion or judge is mid-negotiation would be the machine overruling a consent surface. "Wait, I need a minute" cancels for the whole round and leaves the informational strip; auto-start fires at most once per page load so stopping a speech never re-arms a timer against the person who stopped it. Instrumentation fixed in the same pass: `live_kickoff_shown` was firing on every timer-transition re-render (that is where 47 came from) and is now deduped once per mode per load; new `live_kickoff_autostart` and `live_kickoff_wait` events, and the strip's manual button logs `live_kickoff_start` with `via:'strip'`. Read the three against each other: autostart-heavy means the countdown is doing the work, wait-heavy means 20s is too pushy for real prep habits and the number is the dial to turn, not the feature to remove.

- **A machine flag is evidence, not a verdict: the opponent confirms before anyone is removed** (2026-08-18, Aidan: "ppl are getting removed by the AI thinks reportable behavior is happening feature... dont just auto ban ppl"). The on-device NSFW watchdog (nsfw-guard.js → video-moderate.mjs `self_flag`) was auto-striking, auto-banning (24h→7d→30d ladder) and ejecting on two flagged frames, and it false-positives on normal webcams. New flow: a trip still cuts the flagged side's OWN outgoing camera in the same frame (harm mitigation, reversible) but they stay in the room; a Daily app-message asks the OPPONENT "is your opponent behaving inappropriately?" — Yes routes into the existing peer-report path (ejects; strikes with corroboration), No tells the flagged side it was a false alarm and re-arms the guard so the camera can come back. Server-side, `self_flag` now only logs evidence (`action:'pending_review'`, no strike, no ban, no eject), and a machine flag counts as ONE corroborator in the report path, so flag + one human report = strike where it used to take two humans. A machine alone can never remove anyone; a human alone still can (nsfw category ejects, no ban). Audience cams keep the simpler shape (camera just switches off, no strike) since no round is theirs to disrupt. Applied to casual-room and live-round (seated call-object path carries the app-message listener).

- **Speaker points move to one 1-100 scale, derived from scored axes** (2026-08-18, Aidan: "work on speaker point system. 1-100, make it a much better eval system"). The 2-side live-round ballot scored on the APDA 22-30 scale (screenshot showed 26 / 27.3) and voice rounds on 25-30, while certs already published a 0-100 Communication Score translated through fixed anchors — three scales for one number. Now every new ballot scores speakers 1-100, and the number is DERIVED: the judge scores six axes 1-10 (clarity / reasoning / responsiveness / weighing / strategy / persuasion — the last two are new scorecard rows, optional in the renderer so old 4-axis ballots keep their card), blends them at declared weights (reasoning 2.5, responsiveness 2.0, weighing 2.0, clarity 1.5, strategy 1.0, persuasion 1.0 → 10-100), and may adjust at most ±5 with the moment named. Anchored bands with a 50-65 default and an explicit low-end mandate; 8-15 point separation when one side is outclassed; low-point wins named in the RFD. Voice RFD gets the same treatment (five-axis blend, DIMENSIONS block restored — create-cert had been parsing a block nothing emitted since the 08-18 ballot rewrite) and the BP builder stays on WUDC 50-100, which is format-native and already inside the 1-100 story. Scale plumbing: new entries carry `scoreScale:100`; all 60 existing `leaderboard_entries` (48 seeds + real rounds) were migrated in place via the comm anchors (26.4→67.2, 27.2→77.8, original kept at `scoreLegacy`) so /leaderboard and leaderboard-top never rank across mixed scales; cert-tiers/create-cert/verify/communication-profile are scale-aware (100-scale cert bar = 75, matching the old 27.0 floor exactly; commScore is identity above 30, so aggregates spanning the flip stay comparable). Old stored ballots re-render with their original numbers and a "/ 30"-era label; only values above 30 render "of 100".

- **The ballot read-aloud gets a real voice, and two invisible-text bugs die** (2026-08-18, Aidan: "dont have read aloud feature have shitty audio... eleven labs or realtime gpt api"). The live-round verdict read-aloud now posts `premium:true` to /api/tts, routing everyone through ElevenLabs for this one bounded read per ballot (server still falls back to OpenAI on error); long RFDs truncate at a sentence boundary under the 5000-char server cap. Same pass, same screenshots: the floating "Judge reading" pill styled its text with var(--text) on an always-dark surface — invisible on the light ballot theme (the same trap as #audFab, 2026-08-10) — now pinned light-on-dark; the audience drawer sat at z-index 58 under the sticky round-guide's 90, so LISTEN + PREP pills and the prep card painted over the open drawer — drawer and tab lifted to 120; and the landing's "Full tour" quiet tab restyled everything except `.lm-sub`, which kept the old red-slab's white text on the new light surface — now keyed to the theme text token.
- **Spectators stop taking space in the room, comments pop over the video, and recordings capture the debate layout instead of a bare call** (2026-08-18, Aidan: recordings should "capture the layout UI of the debate not just like a zoom call recording", and spectators' names should not take space — "just say X number of viewers somewhere and have comments pop up"). Three coupled changes on /live-round. (1) **Hidden-presence viewer tokens.** Rooms dropped owner_only_broadcast long ago, so every tokenless spectator rendered as a cam-off name tile in Daily Prebuilt's grid: ten watchers meant ten dead tiles crowding two debaters. `create-daily-room` now reads a client-sent `role` and mints viewers a meeting token with `permissions: { hasPresence: false, canSend: false }` — no grid tile, no people-list row, no send path even if the iframe's AV gate is stripped, verified live by decoding the JWT (`p: { hp: false, cs: false }`). The role is client-claimed and safe to trust: claiming "debater" only gets what every joiner already had, claiming "viewer" only restricts. A rejected mint retries WITHOUT permissions so identity attribution survives on a plan that lacks the API; the room create body is untouched per the 2026-08-15 one-bad-key lesson. The page's own eye-count pill stays the audience number. Caveat for the audience-cam path: it mints its own send token, unaffected. (2) **Comment pop-ups.** The audience deck's comment feed only rendered when someone opened the rail; new comments now also pop over the video pane as fading bubbles (bounded 12-doc listener, backlog skipped on join, 4 bubbles max, hidden while the rail is open and in mini/pip modes, textContent-only so nothing injects). Composing stays in the rail. (3) **The round board: cloud recordings show the debate UI.** Daily records the ROOM, so replays looked like a bare video call; the verdict reel (2026-08-18 above) already proved a canvas screen share reaches the recording. While a consented recording runs, the same lowest-uid driver now publishes a live layout board for the WHOLE round — motion, current speech + running clock + progress bar, both speakers with the active one lit, the watching count — and at judging the SAME canvas hands off to the judging/ballot reel, so the share never restarts (startVerdictReel detects the board and switches phase instead of re-sharing). If the recording stops mid-round the board tears down rather than leaving the room staring at it. The legacy local Record path also now hints `preferCurrentTab` + `displaySurface:'browser'` so a downloaded capture defaults to this tab (the full layout) with a plain-picker fallback. Do not "simplify" viewer tokens back to the bare attributed mint — the hidden presence IS the feature.

- **The judge writes a full ballot now: fast verdict, then a long-form second beat** (2026-08-18, Aidan: "we need the judge to provide a crazy long RFD not a basic summary", plus a user reporting verdicts felt arbitrary). Every judging surface capped the RFD at summary length (live jurors 8-14 sentences, voice a one-line decision on Haiku at 1200 tokens, practice 3-5 sentences in the JSON), and a verdict whose reasoning never shows its work reads as a coin flip even when it is right. The constraint that shaped the fix is the ~26s Netlify execution cap at ~89 tok/s: three concurrent panel jurors cannot each write 1,000 words, so the long ballot CANNOT ride the verdict call. Architecture: the verdict stays exactly as it was (panel-stamped, provenance intact, charter untouched — RFD length is per-surface output shape, which adjudication.mjs explicitly leaves to the surfaces), and a dedicated Sonnet call fires AFTER the verdict renders to write THE FULL BALLOT: 700-1300 words on live-round (synced to all screens through `rfdDeep` on the round doc), 450-800 on voice (new THE BALLOT section, /voice-rfd parses + renders it; read-aloud deliberately speaks only the concise sections because /api/tts hard-slices at 5000 chars), 600-1200 on practice (new server-side `fullWrittenBallot` prompt, always routed to Claude regardless of judge brain so a slow brain cannot 504 it). The ballot walks EVERY argument with the test that settled it, quotes the transcript, does the weighing out loud, lists drops, and closes with how the loser flips it. The deep call is told the verdict is FINAL and explains it, never re-litigates — two verdicts on one screen is the failure mode this design exists to prevent. Voice judging also moved Haiku → Sonnet (viva stays Haiku): the cheap model's flow-tracking was itself part of why voice verdicts felt arbitrary. **Extended same day to the last two judged surfaces**, deliberately left out of the first pass because their ballot length lives near the charter-pinned panel runner. Both use the same second-beat shape, so the charter, rubric, and lib/judge-run.mjs are untouched. **/judge**: after the JSON ballot renders, one dedicated Claude call (always Claude, whatever brain judged) writes 700-1300 words under the verdict; the style/paradigm lens is factored into a shared `judgeLensLines` so the two beats cannot drift onto different judges, four-team rounds get the half-calls structure, and a failed second beat hides its own block because the verdict is complete on its own. **Async rounds**: the deep write cannot ride the judging invocation (the panel already spends 16-24s of the ~26s wall), so a decided round leaves `sweepAt` set and the NEXT sweep pass writes 450-900 words with a whole invocation to itself, stored at `ballot.rfdDeep` (flows through `publicRound` untouched), rendered on /rounds and /r/{id}. Split panels never schedule it (no verdict to explain), pre-existing completed rounds are never backfilled, and a 3-try cap means a round that cannot get its full ballot is left as it was, not stranded. Rider found at ship time: origin/main's two `sw.js` files were live with UNRESOLVED CONFLICT MARKERS around `CACHE_NAME` (a parallel session's rebase resolution gone wrong), a syntax error that made browsers keep the old service worker; this push repaired them at v2485.

- **Content guard stopped flagging hand-raises on jobs motions** (2026-08-18, from the same user report: "flagged me for sensitive info though I did nothing but raise my hand"). Root cause found by executing the real regexes: `/\bhand\s*job/i` matches unpunctuated speech-to-text — "I raise my hand jobs are being lost" transcribes with "hand" directly before "jobs", and `\s*` also spans newlines — and the first-person pattern matched the courage idiom "the balls to say". Jobs/automation is one of the most common motion families, so this was a standing trap for any dictated motion. Compact forms (handjob/blowjob/rimjob) stay hard-blocked; the spaced form now requires a determiner/verb in front; "balls to/of" and "nuts and bolts" carved out. 15-case regression run in both directions before ship. Standing lesson: moderation regexes must be tested against UNPUNCTUATED ASR text, not just typed prose — transcripts have no commas to save you.

- **Identity: social app, not ed-tech, baked into the machine-readable layer** (2026-08-16, Aidan's call, verbatim steer: it's a social app, not ed-tech anymore). The visible marketing already led social (hero "Debate a stranger right now," WebSite JSON-LD "matches people for live timed debate rounds") but the structured identity still said ed-tech: 23 pages carried applicationCategory EducationApplication/EducationalApplication, manifest.json was categories education/productivity, and the /app shell title read "AI debate prep, built by a debater." All flipped: applicationCategory → SocialNetworkingApplication on every product surface (debate-chat keeps CommunicationApplication, the one GameApplication stays, counter.html keeps EducationalApplication because Counter is its own student sub-brand per the 2026-05-10 decision), manifest → social/entertainment, index.html title/og/twitter → "Argue with real people, live" with an arena description (also removed a "9 formats" count that violated the no-format-count rule). New hard rule in §4. Ed-framed SEO pages keep their content as acquisition doors; their page copy is a separate sweep if Aidan wants one.

- **Avatar mode was publishing the raw camera, and fixing it moved live rounds off Daily Prebuilt** (2026-08-15, Aidan: "avatar isn't working"). The bar said Avatar, the self-check tile drew the mask, and the room received the debater's actual face. **The page promises the opposite in a toast** ("the room sees only the animated mask, never your camera"), so this was a privacy claim inverted, not a cosmetic bug.
  - **Cause, measured against a real Daily room rather than reasoned about.** Both rooms ran Daily PREBUILT (`createFrame`), whose call machine lives in a cross-origin iframe. daily-js publishes a custom track by stashing it on the PARENT window (`window._daily.instances[id].tracks`) and posting the sentinel `daily-custom-track` over postMessage; only a client running in the SAME JS context can resolve that back to a track. On prebuilt: `join({videoSource: track})` throws `DataCloneError`, and `setInputDevicesAsync({videoSource: track})` **RESOLVES** `{camera:{},mic:{},speaker:{}}` and then the iframe calls getUserMedia and publishes the DEVICE camera. Daily's docs badge `setInputDevicesAsync` as Prebuilt-supported, which is true only of the deviceId form.
  - **The resolve is the whole reason this survived.** The page already had a fail-closed branch written for exactly this case, and it never fired, because the promise looked healthy: `markCamMode('avatar')` ran and the UI reported success. Prebuilt also withholds the local track object from the parent, so there was no post-hoc check either. **A resolved promise is not evidence the thing happened**, which is the same shape as the 2026-08-11 "a record written at intent time is not evidence of the thing happening".
  - **Shipped in two steps, deliberately.** First the leak was closed on its own: Avatar and Design stopped being offered and every route into the mode landed on camera OFF, never the raw feed, gated behind a `CUSTOM_TRACK_OK` flag. Then the real fix. Turning a broken privacy feature off is not a decision that needs a design; leaving a face on camera under an Avatar label for the length of a rebuild is.
  - **Seated debaters now run `DailyIframe.createCallObject` with our own room UI.** Verified end to end: `publishedTrackId === canvasTrackId`, state playable, and a second client renders the animated mask. Tiles, screen-share tile, audience strip, remote audio elements and a mic/flip/share/leave tray are ours now; Camera / Avatar / Off stays on the existing bar. **`CUSTOM_TRACK_OK` is set at RUNTIME, only when the call object actually mounts**, because a failure there falls back to the prebuilt iframe and that fallback must not inherit a true it cannot honor.
  - **One camera capture for the page now, which is a real fix and not tidiness.** The canvas goes in at `join()` instead of being published afterwards, so Daily never acquires a camera of its own. Prebuilt always held two captures of the same device, the iframe's and the parent's, which phones do not reliably allow, and it left a window where the NSFW watchdog was not yet reading what the room received. `unifyCameraPipeline()` existed only to close that window and is gone.
  - **Spectators and the audience-camera path deliberately STAY on the prebuilt iframe.** Neither publishes a custom track, so neither was broken, and migrating them would have widened a privacy fix into a rewrite of surfaces that work. **(Spectators moved to the call object on 2026-08-24, see the entry at the top of this log; the audience-camera path still rides prebuilt.)** Audience cameras do call `setInputDevicesAsync` with a real camera track, which under prebuilt silently falls back to that same physical camera, so they work by accident at the wrong resolution: the 320x240 constraint is lost. Worth fixing, not urgent, and it is not a leak.
  - **A bug reading could not have caught, and the harness did:** `token: undefined` fails daily-js validation with "token should be a string" and takes the whole join with it, because it validates on the KEY's presence. Tokenless joins are real (create-daily-room returns none when Firebase is unreachable), so the key is omitted rather than set.
  - **Untested and stated rather than implied:** camera mode against a real device, the flip button, and screen share. The verification browser has no camera, so every measurement above came through the canvas path.

- **60 of 114 public pages recorded nothing, and anonymous visitors recorded nothing anywhere** (2026-08-14, Aidan: "make sure we're capturing the data of everytime someone visits the site"). Two independent holes, and between them most arrivals on this site left no record of having happened.
  - **The coverage hole was the bigger one and the easier to miss.** `/js/track.js` is the single telemetry entry point: it drives both the `/api/log-event` pipeline and the `/api/presence-live` beat. 60 of 114 public pages never loaded it, including **`/index`, the app itself**, plus `/privacy`, `/terms`, `/report`, `/floor`, `/predict`, `/ladder`, `/counter` and the whole SEO cluster. Anyone landing there was invisible to /admin AND to `presence_daily`. **The reason nobody noticed: `topbar.js` rides ~85 pages and pulls in `auth-modal.js` and other shared modules, but it does NOT pull `track.js`.** So "the page has the topbar" read as coverage and was not. Same shape as the 2026-08-12 sign-in sweep, where seven topbar-less pages silently no-op'd a site-wide guard: **a shared script that looks global and isn't is worth grepping per file, never assuming.**
  - **The anon hole was a rule that outlived its reason.** track.js carried a hard `if (!currentUser) return;` on the lifecycle, so a signed-out visitor produced no `session_start` and no `page_view`. That was the 2026-05-18 credit-burn call, and its own comment shows the arithmetic: ~17K extra invocations a month against the Netlify 125K ceiling, **sized against a ~7K MAU estimate that §8 has since recorded as an ad-spike artifact.** Real engaged usage is roughly two orders of magnitude below the number the envelope was built on. Anon now gets exactly three events per session. **The heartbeat stays signed-in only**, deliberately: it is the one event that fires per 3 minutes rather than per visit, so it is the only part whose volume scales with dwell instead of with people, and it is what the audit was actually about.
  - **Nothing is filtered on the way in, and that is the whole posture after this morning.** The presence entry above gates its beat on a human because it feeds a public "people active today" caption. This pipeline is the opposite job: it is the RECORD, so a visit is written and the reader decides what it was. `page_view` carries `automated` (navigator.webdriver); `session_end` carries `engaged` (did the presence human-gate ever open) and `views`. **A count of people filters on those flags; a count of visits does not.** Dropping events at the client is how a visit stops existing anywhere, which is the failure being fixed, not a tool for fixing it.
  - **Recording no longer waits on Firebase.** With no persisted session in localStorage nobody can be signed in, so the visit writes immediately under the anon path; with one, it waits for the real uid behind a 6s backstop and an init-failure fallback. Browsers that cannot load Firebase at all (Safari ITP, in-app browsers, strict blockers) are the exact population the 2026-04-20 gate reversal was written for, and they were recording nothing at all.
  - **Rider: `index.html` mapped in-app TAB SWITCHES to `page_view`.** With a real page view now firing on the same page those were indistinguishable, so the tab rows carry `inAppNav` and the nav key. Both stay on the record rather than one being dropped.
  - **DISCONTINUITY, and it points the opposite way to the two before it.** Anon subject and event counts step UP from 2026-08-14, where 08-10 (device keying) and this morning (the presence gate) both stepped DOWN. All three are corrections. Do not read the rise as growth and do not compare a window that spans it. `/admin`'s day/week/month event counts read rows in `events`, so they absorb ~3 more per anon session from today.
  - **Two numbers now describe traffic and they are not interchangeable.** `presence_daily` (the Visitors card) is human-gated and undercounts on purpose; `events` `page_view` is ungated and complete. The gap between them is the gate, not a bug. The header comment on `admin-visitors.mjs` says so, because reconciling them would delete the distinction that makes either one honest.
  - **Verified in a browser on a clean anonymous origin before shipping**, per the standing habit that a passing read of the diff proves nothing: exactly session_start, page_view, session_end per session; no duplicate when the auth callback re-fires with a user; session_end idempotent across two pagehides; `anon_id` present so the server keys by device and not by tab; `engaged` false on an untouched load and true after one pointerdown; index.html still mounting at 1131 nodes. Then live on production, where `/privacy` returned two 200s from `/api/log-event` on load having sent nothing an hour earlier.

- **Presence floor doubled to 200 and the ambient city pool doubled with it** (2026-08-18, Aidan's call, same day as the sign-in gate above). `PRESENCE_BASELINE` 100 -> 200 in `presence-live.mjs`. The 08-14 entry below is the parent decision and everything it says still holds: this is a declared cosmetic floor on a marketing surface, the real measured count is always `online24 - PRESENCE_BASELINE`, `online30` / `online5` stay unpadded, `presence_daily` and every /admin read come off the raw collection and never see it, the padded figure never goes in investor or press material, and `PRESENCE_BASELINE=0` still kills it with no redeploy. What is new is the size and the spread. `AMBIENT_CITIES` went from 52 to 138 (second tier: US and Canadian college towns, UK and EU circuit cities, Latin America, more of Africa and the Gulf, the Indian and Pakistani circuits, Southeast and East Asia, the rest of ANZ), so the extra 100 sessions light NEW dots instead of stacking on the same 52. Measured at 200 against the day-seeded shuffle: ~75-80 distinct cities a day across ~48 countries, max 4 per pin, zero duplicate cities, geometry stable within a UTC day and reshuffled the next. Which cities light up now rotates daily, which a 52-city pool could not do because 200 always exhausted it. The measured Firestore count is still the only part of `online24` that moves between refreshes; the floor is flat by construction, which is what makes the two separable.

- **Four SEO surfaces still promised guest human pairing after the gate landed** (2026-08-18, shipped with the presence floor above). The sign-in entry above gated `/spar`, the availability pill, and `/debate-chat`, but `/debate-strangers`, `/omegle-alternative`, `/prep`, and `/how-it-works` all still answered "do I need an account" with "no, start as a guest" in both the visible FAQ and the JSON-LD `FAQPage` mirror, which is the copy Google shows in the rich result. So the pages that exist specifically to rank for "debate strangers" and "omegle alternative" were sending people to a wall they had just been told was not there. All eight strings now say a live round with a real person takes a free account and that AI practice does not. `/live` and `/tournaments` watching genuinely need no account and were left alone. **The lesson is the same shape as the track.js coverage hole: a product gate is not shipped until the pages that advertise the ungated behaviour are swept too, and the JSON-LD mirror is a second copy that greps differently from the visible one.**

- **Ambient baseline on the presence globe: +100, Aidan's explicit call** (2026-08-14, hours after the cell-cap correction below). Aidan looked at the corrected caption ("102 visits in the last 24 hours"), and asked for a floor: the public number should sit ~100 higher, with the map dots filled in to match. Shipped server-side in `presence-live.mjs`: `PRESENCE_BASELINE` (env-overridable, default 100) pads `online24` and injects the same number of sessions as seeded ambient pins over a fixed 52-city global pool (day-seeded PRNG, so the layer holds still across the 5-min cache and reshuffles daily; jittered to the same 0.1° grid as real pins). The REAL measured count is always `online24 - PRESENCE_BASELINE`; `online30` / `online5` are untouched, and `presence_daily` + every /admin read come from the raw collection and never see the pad. This knowingly coexists with the entry below: the 08-14 correction fixed a number that was *mislabeled* (crawler sessions read as people); this is a *declared cosmetic floor* on a marketing surface, recorded here so nobody treats it as a measurement or quotes it externally. Claims discipline unchanged: never put the padded figure in investor or press material. Kill switch: set `PRESENCE_BASELINE=0` in Netlify env, no redeploy.

- **The landing said "725 people active today" and it was one datacenter crawling our own sitemap** (2026-08-14, found because Aidan looked at the globe caption and asked whether the number was real). It was not, twice over, and neither half was a fabrication: `/api/presence-live` invents nothing, and every pin behind that number came from a real HTTP request.
