Name mix is global per the 2026-05-21 reframe (India well represented, not dominant). Live-kind seeds only use the 8 spar-supported formats. Real entries outrank seeds naturally as ranked rounds land (a real 29.6 tops the board). **Cleanup path when real activity outpaces the bank: one query — `leaderboard_entries.where('seed','==',true)` — deletes the whole pool; the seeding script is idempotent (fixed doc ids) at scratchpad `seed-leaderboard.mjs`, re-runnable if scores need refreshing.** Landing-band `fmtScore` switched to one decimal to match the speaker-points scale. The seeds are unlabeled on the public surface (Aidan's explicit call: believable, not previewed) — the 2026-05-25 honesty framing is superseded for the board itself; the empty-state pitch remains in code for the no-data fallback.

- **Page narration: the site can explain itself out loud** (2026-07-22). Any content page can now be listened to instead of read, and the narration keeps playing while the visitor navigates the rest of the site. A pill sits bottom-left on 41 content pages; press it and a calm ElevenLabs voice explains what the page is, what you can do there, and where to go next. **It is an explainer, not a screen reader.** Reading the markup aloud would have voiced nav labels, table cells, and CTA fragments; instead Claude writes a ~170-word spoken script per page and that is what gets voiced. Full transcript is one tap away in the player, which is also the accessibility answer for anyone who wants the text. **Architecture: pre-generated, not live.** `/api/tts` is capped 60/min and 100/day per IP and one page is far more than one call, so a live path would have cut listeners off after roughly five pages AND billed ElevenLabs for every anonymous visitor, which is precisely the drain shape the 2026-05-18 credit-burn audit was about. Instead `scripts/generate-narration.mjs` builds `app/audio/narration/<slug>.mp3` + a manifest once per page per rewrite; serving is then free and unmetered forever. 41 pages, 64 kbps mono-grade speech, ~17 MB total (the ElevenLabs 128 kbps default would have put 36 MB of binaries in git permanently for no audible gain). **Continuity across navigation** is the actual feature and the hard part: the site is a multi-document app, so `<audio>` dies on every page load. The player writes `{slug, time, playing}` to sessionStorage on every tick and resumes on the next document, with the mp3 already in the SW cache, so the listener hears a seam rather than a restart. Honest limitation: browsers gate autoplay per document, so the resume can be refused; when it is, the pill shows a one-tap Resume instead of failing silently. Chrome generally stops refusing once the visitor has played media on the origin a few times, Safari tends to keep asking. **The generator lints its own output before spending on audio**, and this earned its keep immediately: the first pass produced "no card is required" on /landing and /pricing (friction-selling, banned) and "lock in these rates before beta ends" on /pricing, which was a **fabricated promise** — no such commitment exists anywhere on the page. Both were caught pre-synthesis and regenerated. The lint blocks em-dashes, banned phrases, invented urgency or scarcity, and friction-selling. Do not remove it: a bad line here ships as an MP3, which is far harder to notice than a bad line of text. Loaded via `topbar.js` so one edit covers all 63 topbar pages; the app surfaces (a round in progress, profile, leaderboard, messages) are deliberately excluded because narration there would talk over the product instead of explaining it. **Known maintenance edge: nothing watches for copy drift.** Editing a narrated page does not regenerate its narration, so the spoken version silently ages. Re-run the builder when a page changes meaningfully, and bump `CACHE_NAME` in both `sw.js` files after, because narration is served cache-first.

- **Page narration extended to 62 pages, plus three fixes to what already shipped** (2026-07-22, same day as the original). Added 21 pages: product and integration surfaces (/judge, /team, /early, /twitch-plugin, /zoom-plugin, /counter, /oral-exam-prep, /benchmark, /atlas), lobbies and listings (/live, /spar, /debate-chat, /leaderboard, /livedebates, /float, /floor), and the search-entry pages (/ai-debate-practice, /debate-ai-tools, /debate-case-generator, /debate-topic-generator, /prediction-market-debate). Pages with no topbar carry their own `<script defer src="/js/read-aloud.js">`, since `topbar.js` is what loads the player everywhere else. **Three things the first pass got wrong, all caught by testing this batch rather than by a user:** (1) **Narration followed listeners into live rounds.** Playback deliberately survives navigation, which meant someone who pressed play on /spar and then got matched would have had a narrator talking over their actual debate. New `SILENT_ROUTES` in read-aloud.js stops playback and clears the resume state on the live-round surfaces (/live-round, /voice-debate, /newvoice, /room-judge, /casual-room, /voice-rfd, /practice) and on pages that make their own sound (/coach, /exhibition, /spectate). Lobbies that hand off to a round are still narrated; the target is what's silenced. Any new audio-playing page must be added to that list. (2) **Thin pages produced invented narration.** Pages that render their body with JS scrape as a skeleton, and the model filled the gap instead of failing: /spar extracted **72 characters** and shipped a narration announcing the feature was "still loading, check back soon"; /high-school extracted 192 and invented a national champion "who coaches high schoolers"; /scale is a meta-refresh redirect stub that got narrated at all. Fix is a `MIN_PROSE` gate at 700 characters plus an optional per-page `hint` carrying a human-verified description that outranks the scrape (/spar and /high-school have one); /scale and /predict were dropped. (3) **Three live pages carried a stale brand name** ("the retired brand" on /research, /benchmark, /high-school) because the prompt never stated the brand. Brand is now a named `BRAND` constant, prompted and linted, so the next rename is one edit and the audio cannot silently disagree with the site. Lint also gained loading-state, empty-state, spelled-out-URL ("chrome colon slash slash extensions" on /counter) and preface rules; auditing the existing bank against the new rules found 7 pages needing regeneration, 3 of them already live. **Standing lesson: changing a lint rule does not re-check what already shipped** — audit the manifest after any rule or brand change. **A seventh problem surfaced only after deploying: six of the newly added pages were legacy SEO URLs that now 301 elsewhere (/ai-debate-practice, /debate-ai-tools, /debate-case-generator and /debate-topic-generator to /practice or /topics, /prediction-market-debate to /landing, /debate-chat to /spar), so their narration was unreachable the moment it shipped.** Removed, and the builder gained `--check-routes`, which hits production and reports any route that redirects away or 404s. Run it before adding pages. Final count 56 pages, ~24 MB. Deliberately still excluded: per-user pages (/profile, /messages, /communication-profile), /changelog (turns over faster than anyone will rebuild it), internal tooling, and **legal text (/terms, /privacy) — a spoken summary of legal wording invites a gap between what someone heard and what the page says, so those stay text-only on purpose.**

- **Live-round left pane rebuilt video-first, plus a hidden between-speech floor judge** (2026-07-22). Two coupled changes on /live-round, per outside advice that the surface read clanky and buried the actual video call. (1) **Video is now the hero of the left pane.** The old pane stacked a large decorative "room stage" panel ABOVE the video: kicker + title + explainer sub-line + green "Live room" pill + two 52px seat cards + a dashed audience row, roughly 200px of copy-heavy chrome before the call started, duplicating the roster cards already in the right panel. All of it is gone. The Daily call shell now takes the full pane height, and the only chrome left is a slim **stage rail UNDER the video**: two compact nameplates (initials avatar, name, side, red speaking pulse — same `stagePro*`/`stageCon*` ids so `updateRoomStage` needed no rewiring) flanking a center **AI-judge chip** (⚖ AI Judge, gold, with a slow-pulsing "Listening in" status that flips to "Writing the ballot" in the ballot phase). The chip is the visible expression of "two humans debate, the AI listens in" — the judge reads as a quiet third party in the room rather than a feature buried in the ballot screen. Pane padding 18→12px. The rail hides <1000px (mobile keeps the fixed-height video tile + panel roster, unchanged), and stays hidden in the docked-mini and pop-out modes which have their own judge affordances. (2) **Floor judge, built but NOT public.** A real-time third-party judge that comments BETWEEN speeches — sideline color in a card that slides up from the judge chip over the bottom of the video. Hard rules in the prompt: 2-3 sentences max, never calls a winner, never gives scores, names the strongest thing the speaker just did + the biggest hanging thread + what the next speaker must answer, format lens from the first JUDGE_RULES lines. v1 rides the existing transcript pipeline: on `endSpeech` (skipped/empty speeches excluded, spectators never generate) the acting debater's client sends the round-so-far to `/api/claude` (sonnet, 220 tokens), renders the remark, and syncs it through a `floorRemark` field on the round doc so the opponent and spectators render it too. Remarks auto-clear the moment the next speech goes live (both the local `startSpeechTimer` and the off-side `applyRemoteTimer` mirror path). **No UI toggle exists anywhere; OFF by default.** Enable per client with `?judge=live` on the URL or `localStorage['lr-floor-judge']='1'` — both debaters need the flag to both see it, which is the point while it's unannounced. Analytics: `live_round_floor_remark`. **v2 upgrade path (planned, not built):** mint a listen-only OpenAI Realtime transcription session from `/api/realtime-session` and feed it the room audio so the judge hears the actual speeches instead of the typed/mic transcript and can react with sub-second latency — same render surface, same round-doc sync, only the input changes; the plan is written into the module comment in live-round.html. When this goes public it should launch as an instrumented A/B per the landing-experiments norm, with a per-round toggle both debaters consent to (same consent shape as the /spar judge-paradigm handshake, 2026-06-12).

- **Selectable judge styles, paradigms, and brains on /judge** (2026-07-25). The standalone listen-or-paste judge previously hardcoded Claude Sonnet and mentioned paradigms only in a non-functional explainer below the FAQ. It now separates three choices: (1) judge style, the audience lens (match format / lay / flow / technical / communication); (2) decision paradigm (automatic / tabula rasa / policymaker / stock issues / games player / truth seeker / hypothesis tester / communication / custom); and (3) AI brain (Claude / GPT / Gemini / Grok / DeepSeek / Open Lab). Defaults remain one-tap and beginner-safe: Match the format + Choose for me + Claude. The selected style and paradigm are injected as bounded judge instructions, custom text cannot name a winner or dictate points, and the ballot labels the exact combination used. New indexable `/judge-paradigms` guide explains the choices in plain language, links public Tabroom examples as methodology, and deep-links each preset into `/judge`. The six brains now share the same server-side `ADJUDICATION_CORE`: new `applyAdjudicationForFeature` in `lib/adjudication.mjs` gives non-Claude proxies the judging block Claude already injected through its cached-prefix assembly. Alternative brains remain signed-in-plan features and return a clear sign-in/plan error; Claude is the public default.

- **Audience cameras: verified spectators can be seen, never heard** (2026-08-10). Debaters could see a watcher COUNT and nothing else, so a live round felt like speaking into a void even with people in it. Spectators can now opt into appearing on camera in the same Daily room, and the whole design is built around the fact that inviting strangers' cameras into a room with minors in it is the part that can go badly. **The mic is not a setting, it is an impossibility.** `/api/audience-cam` mints a Daily meeting token with `permissions.canSend:['video']`; the refusal to publish audio happens at Daily's media server, so a hostile client running patched JavaScript still cannot open a mic. A UI toggle would have been a promise; this is a property. **Five gates before a token is issued:** (1) NAMED account only, because Firebase anonymous accounts are free and unlimited to mint (see the 2026-07-28 rate-limit entry) and an anonymous camera is an unaccountable camera, so `sign_in_provider != 'anonymous'` is required; (2) Cloudflare Turnstile, which **fails CLOSED**: with `TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` / `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` unset the entire feature is unavailable and the client never renders the camera button, because shipping a stranger-camera feature with its human check quietly skipped is the one degradation that is not acceptable here (a configured-but-unreachable Cloudflare is a different case and fails OPEN, since the other four gates hold); (3) `video_bans`, using the SAME salted-uid-and-IP derivation as create-daily-room, so a strike earned as a debater blocks the camera here too; (4) a rate limit of 12/hour and 40/day per uid through the shared `lib/rate-limit.mjs`; (5) a cap of 4 concurrent cameras per round, counted server-side from fresh `camAt` heartbeats on the watchers subcollection (the same 75s liveness window the watch count uses, so it needs no new index and no new collection). On top of the token, the camera runs the SAME on-device `nsfw-guard.js` watchdog debater cameras run, with the identical two-consecutive-samples trip and scores-only reporting. **The debaters hold a kill switch.** `allowAudienceCams` on the round doc, flipped from a third option in the existing visibility menu, defaults on; false makes the server refuse new tokens AND drops live audience cameras client-side through the round snapshot every client already holds. Participant-only by the existing `live_rounds` update rule, so no rules change was needed; `camAt` rides the watcher's own doc, which the existing watchers rule already scopes to `watcherUid == request.auth.uid`. **CPU was a first-class constraint, not an afterthought,** because four extra faces land on the same machines already running a Daily call, an avatar canvas at 24fps, and a TF.js classifier every 1.6s. Audience cameras publish at 320x240/12fps (texture, not broadcast), and every audience tile is pinned to the lowest simulcast layer via `updateReceiveSettings` on BOTH the debater and audience frames, matched by the `Audience · ` user_name prefix the token stamps. Daily's prebuilt layout promotes the active speaker and an audience token can never send audio, so a downgraded audience tile can never be the thing anyone is trying to look at. **Bug found while sizing this, and it was live:** `max_participants` was 8 with a comment calling it a flood guard for debaters, but every SPECTATOR joins the same Daily room receive-only, so the ceiling was really two debaters plus six watchers and the seventh person to open any live round got a full room. Raised to 24; flooding is handled where it belongs (unlisted rounds, the admission gate, and this function's own cap of 4). Because create-daily-room is create-or-fetch, existing rooms keep the old ceiling until they expire, within 24h. Same pass: `/floor`'s server poll and `/arena`'s three demo-stage tickers now skip hidden tabs, matching what every other timer on those pages already did. **Ops: audience cameras are OFF on arrival and stay off until four env vars exist** (`DAILY_API_KEY`, `DAILY_DOMAIN`, `TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY`, `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY`). Setting the two Turnstile keys is what switches the feature on, with no redeploy. **Not built:** audience-camera moderation from the debaters' side beyond the kill switch (no per-face eject yet, the round-wide switch is the blunt instrument for now).

- **Anonymous analytics keyed by device, not by browser tab; visitor counter un-padded and un-broken** (2026-08-10). Nothing in the signed-out path had a stable identity, which made the anon half of every dashboard a count of TABS. `log-event.mjs` minted its synthetic uid as `anon:` + the per-tab sessionStorage id, so a returning visitor, a second tab, and a reload after a crash were three different "anonymous users" — and anon retention was therefore unmeasurable by construction, for the population that dominates traffic while retention is the stated #1 problem (§8). The durable id already existed (`localStorage._da_aid`, minted by track.js and sent as `metadata.anon_id` for the funnel-join work); the server simply never keyed on it. Now it does, falling back to the session id when localStorage is blocked (Safari ITP, in-app browsers) so those visitors degrade to the old per-tab behavior instead of vanishing, with `deviceKeyed` on the row so a reader can tell a real device from a fallback. **DISCONTINUITY, deliberate: unique anon counts step DOWN from this date.** That is the correction, not a regression; do not compare a window that spans it. /admin relabels the funnel from "uids" to "subjects" and defines a subject as one account OR one anon device, with the date noted in the help panel. **Two things about `/api/visitor-tick` found while verifying, both older than this change.** (1) Its cumulative `count` was padded with a hardcoded `BASELINE = 7074` from the ad-spike era, floored so the number could never read below it. That is a claims-discipline problem on a public surface, so the pad is now subtracted back out on read; the stored doc is left intact as the record of what it used to claim. Un-padded, the true all-time figure is **7**. (2) The whole function had been DEAD for months: per-day counters were addressed as `metrics/daily/{YYYY-MM-DD}`, three segments, which Firestore reads as a collection path and rejects as a document, so `db.doc()` threw on construction and every read and write went to the catch. No daily doc was ever written, `count30d` was always 0, and `public-join-history`'s `totalVisits` was structurally pinned at 0 — which is why /community always rendered its static `data-baseline` fallback. `public-join-history.mjs` already carried a TODO diagnosing this precisely ("has been silently throwing for months") and proposing the flatten; taking that fix, `metrics_daily/{YYYY-MM-DD}`. Nothing to migrate, since the old path never held data. Caught by probing the deployed endpoint rather than by reading it: it answered `source: baseline-fallback`, which only happens in the catch. **A tick now also requires a `deviceId` to increment**, because the "already counted" marker lives in the same localStorage a blocking browser refuses, so those visitors were re-ticking on every page load; they are now undercounted instead, which is the honest direction to be wrong in for a number we might quote. **Standing lesson: a metric that renders a plausible number is not evidence that it works.** Both of these shipped a believable figure to a public page for months — one padded, one frozen — and neither surfaced as an error anywhere.

- **A round room does not open until both debaters prove they are there** (2026-08-11). Measured across all 411 live rounds ever created: only ~85 had both people actually present, 215 had exactly one, 111 had neither. 390 never completed a single speech, and median room lifetime was 1.5 minutes. Debater names are written at MATCH time, not on arrival, so a round document looked complete while one chair had been empty the whole time, and every downstream number inherited the lie. **The round itself was never the problem: past the first speech, ~43% of rounds finish (9 of 21).** We were matching people against nobody, and the person who DID show up sat alone and left. That is the retention story, and it is not a top-of-funnel problem. **Two causes, both fixed.** (1) The consent gate from 2026-06-12 only fired when someone filed a judge-paradigm note or the two sides proposed different formats; every other pair flipped straight to `matched` with no evidence anyone was at a keyboard. It now fires for EVERY foreground pair, and both `consents` start false. The old rule auto-yessed the side with nothing to review, which is exactly the side that could be an empty chair: a debater who filed no note was consented into a room without ever proving presence. (2) **This supersedes the 2026-06-15 "B" auto-accept tuning.** That change made the deciding card auto-JOIN at zero when the tab was visible, so a present-but-slow user wouldn't lose a rare match. Its own comment named the risk exactly right ("accepting there would strand the opponent alone") and then trusted `visibilityState` to rule it out. It does not: a foregrounded tab nobody is sitting at is `visible`, so for 15 seconds an empty chair could accept a match. Timeouts now always auto-PASS, which routes into the ghost path already built in the 2026-06-12 hardening (server cancels the silent side, requeues the live one on a clean doc). Trading a few extra matches for rooms that open onto nobody is a bad trade at this liquidity, because the person we lose is the one who showed up. A pure presence check gets its own card ("X is ready" / "I'm here, start the round") rather than the review card, which is worded for weighing something; notes and cross-format pairs keep their review cards, and background "Spar live" pairs still skip the gate because `js/notifications.js` has no card to render. **Deliberately NOT built, because it already existed:** the in-room dead-opponent rescue. `live-round.html` has carried a 28s grace, a "Looking for your debater" strip, and a no-show prompt (Message them / Find another / Leave / Keep waiting) for a while. It worked fine. It was simply being handed rooms that never had two people in them. **Lesson: a record written at intent time is not evidence of the thing happening.** Both debater names, the room id, the motion and the format were all populated and correct on 411 rounds; not one of those fields meant anyone had arrived.

- **The nightly learning loop has never once run, and the claim came off the pitch** (2026-08-11). `learning_distillations` holds zero documents, and not because the cron is broken. `scheduled-distill.mjs` requires `MIN_EXAMPLES = 3` per format drawn from generations rated ≥4 or saved. Live counts: 153 generations captured (85 in the last 30 days, still writing today), **0 rated ≥4, 2 saved, 1 carrying any rating at all.** With three labels ever created across eighteen formats, every format returns `skipped` every night, permanently. `NIGHTLY_PAUSED` is not set; the job runs and finds nothing. The newest rating signal is 2026-05-23, one day BEFORE the /voice-rfd rating widget shipped, so that widget has produced zero labels in its entire life. So §3 wedge #5's "the AI on motion X today is meaningfully different from the AI on motion X last month" has never been true. Capture works; the learn-back half has never produced an artifact. `report.html` (investor-facing) and `landing-classic.html` no longer assert it: they now say the loop is built to sharpen from rated rounds and state plainly that no learned-pattern block has shipped into a prompt to date. **Do not restore the flat claim until `learning_distillations` actually holds documents.** Sequencing note, and it is the reason not to chase this directly: ratings are downstream of FINISHED rounds, and rounds were not finishing (see the ready-check entry above). Fix arrival, then completion, and labels become possible; going hunting for labels first would be treating a symptom.

- **Free entry finished: the surfaces caught up with the decision, and the metering bug behind it** (2026-08-11). Paid tournament entry was retired on 2026-08-10 in `entry-checkout.mjs`, but only the endpoint learned about it, so for a day the site sold a $20 door that answered `410 ENTRY_IS_FREE`. **What was still live:** `/tournaments` rendered "Paid entry / $20 / Pay in with card" because the paid door was hidden only by a JS branch that ran when `entryFeeCents` was 0, and the doc still carried 2000; the same page's FAQ and JSON-LD already said "There is no entry fee and no card" beside it; and `/tournament-rules`, the published rules of an $850 cash contest, still said free entry was "Not eligible for cash prizes" and that an entry is prize-eligible "if it paid in or was granted a fee waiver". That last one is the one that mattered: it told a free entrant who wins first place that the title is theirs and the $500 goes to someone else, which is the opposite of the decision, on the document a dispute would be argued from. **The fix that generalizes: the paid door was DELETED from the markup, not hidden, and no copy on either page reads `entryFeeCents` at all.** Hiding on a data value is precisely what let stale data resurrect a payment promise; with one entry model there is nothing for stale data to flip. Rules rewritten so eligibility turns on placement and age and nothing else, section 6 became "Cost" (no fees, so nothing to refund), and the liability clause no longer caps at an entry fee nobody pays. Verified by serving the pages the REAL live doc with its fee still at 2000: no pay button, no `$20`, one door. Then the doc itself was corrected (`entryFeeCents` 2000 → 0, description rewritten off the "$20 pay-in makes your entry prize-eligible" line that `/tournament` renders as its lede); `paidEntries` was 0 at the time, so no money was stranded. **Same session, the bug that started it:** `/practice` metered one free credit per `callAI` rather than per ROUND, and a Quick Clash round makes ~19 calls, so at `ANON_LIMIT = 1` a guest could never finish their first round. The second call threw the quota error, which rendered mid-debate as "AI couldn't respond. Guest preview used. Sign in..." and then auto-advanced through empty speeches into a ballot judging nothing. Now a round charges once at `beginRoundCredit()` in `goToSpeeches` and everything inside it runs free through the ballot; out of credit shows the CTA before the round starts instead of breaking it halfway. **Do not "fix" future quota complaints by raising the caps** (1 anon / 10 signed-in) — those numbers are correct as rounds and were set deliberately on 2026-05-18. Two riders found the same way: anonymous Firebase accounts were being read as signed-in on `/practice` (`js/notifications.js` mints one for the background Spar pill on every page), which gave guests the signed-in cap, suppressed every sign-in nudge, saved rounds under a uid they can never sign back into, and sent anonymous tokens to the account endpoints; and the prep-timer auto-advance effect had an empty body, so prep counted to 0:00 and sat there looking frozen. **Standing lesson from both halves: a surface that renders a plausible screen is not evidence the rule behind it is right.** The $20 button looked like a working checkout, and the round looked like it was running, right up to the point where each contradicted a decision that had already been made.

- **Spectator search: /watch was 140 crawlable words, and a replay had no URL of its own** (2026-08-12). Aidan asked to rank for "watch people debate". The audit found the technical base already good (thoughtful robots.txt, dynamic sitemaps with honest lastmod, ~6,000 static words on the homepage) and the failure concentrated in exactly the surface built for that query. **Two problems, one per layer.**
  - **/watch promised video and delivered a spinner.** 140 static words wrapped around a grid filled by JS from a corpus that is EMPTY: 0 recordings, 0 clips, 1 published round sitewide. A crawler that skipped the fetch saw a page advertising replays and rendering "Loading replays…", which is the shape Google's helpful-content system demotes, and a human who searched for a debate to watch hit a dead end. Now ~750 words of plain HTML (three ways to watch, how to get into a live round, what a round actually is, 7 questions with FAQPage markup verbatim against the visible copy) that carries the page whether or not the corpus has anything in it, and an empty state that routes to /live and /spar and says WHY it is empty (recording is opt in).
  - **The competitive read is the reason this was worth doing, and it inverted the obvious plan.** theChatStage ranks for the same query with roughly 400 words of plain copy and our exact model (matched opponent, anonymous spectators). They are not winning on a video library, so this is winnable on content rather than on inventory. Debatable was already ranking for spectator long-tail, but via the HOMEPAGE, because /watch was too thin to be the answer.
  - **A recording had no address.** Sharing one handed out `/watch?r={id}`, a query string on a page whose canonical is bare `/watch`, so the equity from every share piled onto the hub and not one replay existed as a crawlable URL. New server-rendered `/w/{id}` per published recording, own canonical, VideoObject. **The thing that made the markup possible: `/w/{id}/play`,** a stable path that mints a fresh Daily link per request and 302s to it. The signed URL behind it expires within the hour and could never be published as `contentUrl`; the address in front of it never moves. Playback needs no JavaScript as a result, since `<video src>` follows the 302 itself, Range requests included. `sitemap-recordings.xml` joins the index and falls back to /watch while empty, since a zero-entry urlset reads as a broken sitemap in Search Console. Replay cards became anchors (crawlable path, cmd-click opens the standalone page, plain click still plays inline) and Copy link hands out `/w/{id}`.
  - **What was deliberately NOT emitted, and it is the same discipline as the /reviews entry above.** No VideoObject or ItemList on /watch itself: there is no video there, and marking up media a visitor cannot find is what earns a manual action rather than a rich result. It belongs on a per-recording page, which is why one now exists.
  - **The thumbnail gap, and why YouTube closes it.** `/w/{id}` shipped valid VideoObject that was NOT rich-result eligible, because Google wants a thumbnail representing the video and the fallback is the brand OG card; nothing in the pipeline produces a still and there is no image library here to make one. A `youtubeId` on the recording doc (set from a yt button on the /admin row, accepting any pasted YouTube URL form) swaps in YouTube's real per-video still, adds `embedUrl`, and renders the nocookie player so watch time lands on the channel instead of being split from it. `contentUrl` still points at `/w/{id}/play`, because the Daily mp4 is the authoritative full round. With no id the behavior is byte-for-byte the old one, which is the state of every recording today. The id is validated against `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{11}$` **at render, not only at write**, because it is interpolated into an iframe src and into structured data.
  - **Honest limit, stated so nobody re-litigates it later:** this makes /watch competitive for "watch debates online" style queries. It will not beat Open to Debate or the Jubilee results for the raw head term, because that half of the SERP is a video carousel and that is YouTube's game, not a page's. The remaining lever is inventory: recordings have to exist, and that needs debaters to opt in, not code.

- **Watch hub shipped: owner livestream, round replays, clips at /watch** (2026-08-10). Ports the WS2 streaming work forward from a branch that had fallen ~1150 commits behind main; a re-apply against the current brand and architecture, not a merge. **Shipped:** five Netlify functions (`stream-control` admin start/stop of a Daily owner_only_broadcast room; `stream-status` public read, CDN-cached 15s, one doc read; `recordings` public replay index + short-lived playback links; `recordings-admin` sync-from-Daily + publish toggles, auto-publishes finished recordings over 45s; `clips` signed-in timestamped windows into published recordings, 3s-10min, 30/user/day), the `/watch` page (live band, replay grid, clip maker, deep links `/watch?r=` and `/watch?clip=`), `/replays` as an alias, /admin cards to actually go live and manage recordings, a landing live band that injects ABOVE the hero only while a stream is on (off air the page is byte-for-byte untouched, so hero A/B arms and the See-more gate never see it), and a "Watch streams and replays" action in the stream-it section, which is /watch's first in-content inbound link. `/watch` joined the sitemap (daily, 0.84; /replays deliberately not listed, same document) and read-aloud's SILENT_ROUTES (it plays video). **Deliberately NOT ported:** Phase 5's live-round viewer points — main rebuilt spectating with admission gates and presence-folded counting since the branch diverged, so audience scoring needs designing against that model, not patching over it. **Salvage audit of the rest of the stale branch:** most of it had already been re-done on main by parallel sessions; the auth-modal Google-only simplification and gmail FROM defaults were REJECTED as regressions (main has first-class email/password since 2026-07-25, and a gmail From on Resend silently 403s per the sender-domain rule); `token_balances` / `tournament_contacts` Firestore rules were left behind because nothing on main consumes those collections. **Two riders:** `/reviews` lost its Review/reviewBody JSON-LD (self-serving review markup is barred by Google's structured-data policy and risks a sitewide markup-trust discount; the visible testimonials stay, an inline comment guards against re-adding), and `firebase.json` gained the `storage` block so `firebase deploy` actually ships `app/storage.rules`, which had been undeployable since Storage was enabled 2026-07-22. **Ops note:** going live requires `DAILY_API_KEY`; recordings require a Daily plan with cloud recording (stream-control retries room creation without recording so the stream itself survives a plan without it).

- **App Check hard-enforced, and the anonymous per-IP ceiling raised behind it** (2026-08-18). Load-readiness pass ahead of an expected traffic spike. `APP_CHECK_REQUIRED` was `false` in the Netlify prod env, so the ~804KB of reCAPTCHA Enterprise shipped to every visitor was decorative: a plain `curl` with no token got a 200 from `/api/claude`. That left the anon per-IP counter in `claude.mjs` (5/min, 15/hour, 30/day) as the only thing between a script and the Anthropic bill, which is why the 2026-05-18 audit had to set it so tight. But an IP is not a person: at ~5 requests per free round that was **three anonymous visitors per address per hour**, so the fourth person on a school, campus, or CGNAT mobile carrier was told they had hit a "free-trial cap" they had never used. `realtime-session.mjs` hit the identical wall on 2026-07-28 and fixed it by moving NAMED users to per-UID metering; the anonymous lane has no such escape hatch, so the ceiling itself had to move. **Sequence, which mattered:** wire every calling surface first, verify tokens mint, then flip. **Two findings made that non-optional.** (1) `tts.mjs` and `realtime-session.mjs` call `checkAppCheck` at the top of the handler, *before* identifying the caller, so they need a token from signed-in users too — and `live-round.html` called both (plus transcribe and claude) with no App Check wired at all. Flipping against that tree would have killed every live round and all voice. `coach.html`, `live.html` and `high-school.html` were in the same state (high-school had no Firebase on the page whatsoever), and `pricing.html` loaded `app-check.js` without ever loading the App Check SDK, so its wiring was inert. (2) **The SDK injection race:** six client modules (`track`, `usage-banner`, `upgrade-cta`, `topbar`, `notifications`, `auth-modal`) each lazily inject `firebase-app-compat` when they find no firebase on the page. Whichever injection lands last owns `window.firebase` and takes the `appCheck` component with it, so a static `app-check-compat` tag registered against an earlier namespace simply vanishes. Measured on `/high-school`: two `app-compat` tags raced and `firebase.appCheck` came out undefined while every script tag looked correct. `app-check.js` now loads the App Check SDK itself at activation time, after the injectors settle. **Perf rider:** `activate()` is what pulls the 804KB bundle, so activation moved to first intent (pointer/key/touch) or the first gated fetch. A non-gated route attaches a token only if App Check is already up and never triggers activation, so public polls like `/api/watch-live` cannot drag it back onto page load. **New numbers:** `claude.mjs` 5/min → 30, 15/hour → 200, 30/day → 800; `realtime-session` anon 6/hour → 15, 10/day → 40 (voice rises less; it costs more per call, and named accounts keep the per-UID lane). Signed-in text callers are metered per uid and never touch these. **Verified on prod:** tokenless `curl` → 401 `APP_CHECK_MISSING`; landing, practice, voice-debate, high-school, coach, live-round and live all mint a valid 957-char token and pass every gated endpoint; 12 rapid anonymous calls that would have died at 6 now all return 200. **Tuning note:** there is no `UPSTASH_*` in the prod env, so both limiters run the in-memory fallback — counters are per function instance, making the effective ceiling both higher than the numbers suggest and unpredictable, since a cold instance starts from zero. Reverting enforcement is one env var plus a redeploy.

- **Homepage weight: animation governor, and a Firestore listener per visitor removed** (2026-08-18, same pass). The landing ran ~120 infinite CSS animations; unlike rAF, keyframes keep painting in a backgrounded tab and for sections nobody has scrolled to. New `app/js/anim-governor.js` (landing + index + practice) pauses animation on a hidden document and on off-screen sections, un-pausing 300px early so entrances still play; `animation-play-state` resumes in place, so nothing restarts. Separately, the "N debates live now" band opened a `live_rounds` `onSnapshot` on **every** visitor, lazy-loading firestore-compat and holding an open realtime listener per person just to print a count — a few hundred concurrent visitors is a few hundred listeners fanning out on every write. It now reads `/api/watch-live` off its 12s shared server cache under the same ~100s liveness rule, and Firestore loads only when someone clicks Join. Three blocks on the page had also each been polling that endpoint on their own timer; they now share one in-flight promise and a 45s window, which incidentally stops the hero count and the band from disagreeing. Landing heap 33.5MB → 21.4MB.

- **The Open: prize entry $20 → $5, and $5 becomes the real door** (2026-08-19, later pass). The $20 prize entry had earned nothing: eight days after entries opened, `paidEntries` was 0 against a field of 7. The fix earlier the same day widened the automatic founding comp from Friday Aug 22 out to entries close on Aug 29, on the correct reasoning that a $20 wall with "email the organizer and ask" as the only way past it is not a wall a stranger climbs during a ten-day bracket-fill push. But that argument was about $20, not about paying at all, and it left the event with a free door for everyone and no revenue path. **$5 is a door a stranger actually walks through**, so the blanket comp had nothing left to protect and the headline it contradicted became true. Aidan's call, given three options: $5 real door, comp preserved for accounts that already held it. **The comp is stopped, not retracted** — `FOUNDING_CUTOFF_MS` moves Aug 29 → Aug 19 end-of-day EDT, so the seven entries already on the field, the six existing `compedEntries`, and everyone the announcement email reached keep full prize eligibility at no cost; only accounts created after today meet the $5 door, and the fee is still waived on request for anyone who asks. The pot is untouched at $850 ($500/$250/$100): prizes are funded by the organizer, not raised from entry fees, so the ladder stands whether one person pays or fifty. **Stripe needed no work** — `sk_live_`, `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` and `ENTRY_PAYMENTS_LIVE=true` were already set in the Netlify env, and `entry-checkout.mjs` / the `tournament_entry` webhook branch / `tournaments/{tid}/payments/{uid}` were all already built. This was a price change, not an integration. `entryFeeCents` 2000 → 500 written directly to the live doc, which surfaced a real gap: the fee was designed as data rather than code precisely so pricing is not a deploy, but nothing could **write** it, so `tournament-admin`'s `update` action gained a site-admin-only `entryFeeCents` (clamped 0–50000; 0 turns the paid door off) and the price is now changeable without a raw Firestore edit. **Copy swept to $5** across `tournament-rules.html` (the published contract for a cash prize, and the one that actually matters), `tournaments.html` (both JSON-LD offers, the hero kicker, the logistics card, the enter head and the two FAQ answers), `get-paid-to-debate.html` including its FAQPage JSON-LD, and the three announcement emails. The /tournaments paid door now leads with "Pay in · $5" as a visible primary button instead of hiding it behind a failed comp claim, with the claim path demoted to the secondary action; the 2026-08-10 lesson that a door must never be hidden by a JS branch on a data value still holds, and both doors still render unconditionally. **Promo, per Aidan, on the landing and sitewide:** a parallel `js/open-promo.js` strip was built for the first screen and then **deleted before shipping** — `origin/main` landed `.fs-open-card` in the same slot mid-branch (504e63bf), so the shipped card was updated to $5 rather than competing with it. The surviving change is the sitewide `topbar.js` ribbon, which said "Free to enter" on every page and was made false by the price cut; it now leads "Win $500 for $5" and keeps everything past the hook in the `.ui-open-strip-tail` span that hides under 560px, because `body.has-beta-strip` reserves a fixed 32px while the bar becomes 48px the moment it wraps, clipping the wordmark beneath it. That clipping is pre-existing and unfixed: the 32px is load-bearing for four other rules in `ui.css`, so resizing it is its own chrome pass. **Riders:** `admin-open-reminder` is a deadline email again (free entry genuinely ends tonight), and its own `CUTOFF_PASSED` guard makes it unsendable from tomorrow, which is correct rather than a regression.

- **The first screen becomes one composition, and the headline comes back** (2026-08-19, latest pass, commit `697c87a5`). Aidan supplied a positioning brief off a 12:25 screenshot (`Codex/2026-08-19/revie/outputs/CLAUDE_SIMPLE_UI_POSITIONING_INSTRUCTIONS.md`): the hero had good parts arranged as five separate floating objects. **This pass deliberately reverses several decisions made earlier the same day, which is the point of it, not an accident** — a future agent reading those entries should read this one first. **The visible `<h1>` is restored.** It had been cut to screen-reader-only that morning on the reasoning that the two CTAs plus the globe could carry the first screen; what it actually produced was a column opening on a button row with nothing anchoring it, a centered CTA axis fighting a left-ragged everything-else, and a background constellation promoted to full opacity to supply the texture the headline used to. All three of those come back out together. **One grid:** `min(100% - 64px, 1400px)` centered, `minmax(0,5fr) minmax(0,7fr)`, copy capped 560 and board capped 760, so surplus width on a wide monitor lands outside the composition rather than pushing the two columns toward opposite edges. Headline, supporting line, actions, walkthrough and the live row now share one left edge. **The CTAs are buttons again** (54px tall, 14px radius, content-sized, 12px apart) rather than the 23px-padded full pills the headline's absence justified; the "two equal doors, colour alone says which leads" rule from that morning survives on height, while Debate takes back sole ownership of large-red — verified by measuring painted backgrounds in the first viewport, not by eye. The walkthrough drops to a quiet text link and loses its pinging red ring, which was a third pulsing red object competing for the same glance. The globe stops being a third focal point: 150px with the count beside it as one component instead of 330px with a caption floating loose underneath. The example board keeps its content and loses its nesting — pager moved from a header to a quiet footer so the card reads people, motion, audience result, verdict, then index; one radius, one shadow, 28px padding, name labels as compact overlays rather than capsules. **The constellation stays at full strength** (that half of the morning's change was about the whole page, not the hero) but stops crossing type at contrast, via a `var(--bg)` scrim on the copy column that tracks all five themes rather than a global dimming.

  - **The in-hero Debatable Open card came off, which updates the "$20 → $5" entry above.** That entry records `.fs-open-card` as the surviving promo in this slot. It is gone. The sequence in one afternoon: the card was added because / had no path to the event (274 arrivals, 18 reaching /tournaments), repriced to $5, then had its fine print moved to /tournaments — and at that point the card and the sitewide strip stated identical facts (same event, same date, same $500 for $5, same link to the same page). Two promotions of one event on one screen with nothing unique between them is the duplication the brief was written to remove. **The sitewide `topbar.js` ribbon is now the single path from / to the Open**, and it retires on the same schedule the card would have.

  - **The strip's wrap-clipping is FIXED, which corrects the "pre-existing and unfixed" line in the entry above.** That entry is right that `body.has-beta-strip`'s 32px is load-bearing for four other rules in `ui.css` and that resizing it is its own chrome pass. So the 32px was not touched. Instead the strip is pinned to one line (`white-space:nowrap; overflow:hidden; text-overflow:ellipsis`) with size steps at 560px and 380px, so it can never become the 48px bar that drew over the wordmark. This composes with, rather than replaces, the `.ui-open-strip-tail` hide under 560px: the tail keeps the message short, the nowrap guarantees the height whatever the copy later becomes. Clipping the tail is the lesser failure, since the strip is a link to /tournaments rather than the event page itself. Same pass took the strip's 32px out of the first screen's `100svh` (`body.has-beta-strip .fscreen`), which had always ended exactly that far below the fold; the class is removed on dismiss, so the full height returns with the space.

  - **Not done, deliberately: the brief's navigation section.** It asks for one control height (40-44px), text nav that reads as text rather than pills, square equal icon controls, and a profile control that does not pair a truncated name with a separate label. The topbar is shared chrome across 63 pages in `js/topbar.js`, and its signed-in state cannot be reproduced on a local static server without Firebase auth, so it was left rather than restructured against a screenshot that could not be verified. It is its own pass.

  - **Process note worth keeping, because it will recur.** Five commits landed on this same hero from other sessions while this one was in a worktree, so the branch rebased four times and two of its own conclusions were overturned by facts that arrived mid-flight. One of those commits (`503b4ec6`, "open up the vertical rhythm in the hero's left stack") sized nodes this pass restructured; its intent (generous left-stack gaps with a short-viewport wind-back) is carried forward and its literal values are superseded. When the landing is under this much concurrent edit, re-read `origin/main` before resolving anything, and prefer taking the newer *reasoning* over the newer *diff*.

- **The walkthrough is a link, not a chapter** (2026-08-25, Aidan). The large poster card and its standalone `#live-now` chapter came off the landing. The same three-minute video remains available through a quiet text trigger in the link row at the end of `#live-proof`, directly above the round CTAs. Its lightbox, poster and video asset stay unchanged. The live-room status pill that used the removed chapter as its injection point now lands with those round CTAs instead, so removing the card does not remove the live signal. The tour navigation and one-pager gate no longer advertise or target the retired chapter.

- **Motions are negotiated now, not struck** (2026-09-02, Aidan: "get rid of the whole feature to strike motions - offer a motion, then offer one to offer one or strike - give them options in a strategic manner"). **This supersedes the 2026-08-26 motion-draft entry below and every strike rule in it.** Blind strikes over a dealt slate were a vetoing game: both sides removed motions simultaneously and whatever nobody hated became the round, which reliably produced the blandest card on the table. It is a negotiation now, and the whole thing is one sentence: **one person settles the motion, the other settles the side.**

  **The tree, and every branch is priced.** A seeded coin flip decides who OFFERS first, and that is all it decides. The offerer puts one motion up. The responder then does exactly one of three things. **Take it** and that motion is the round and they pick their own side. **Send it back** and the offerer owes a different motion, the responder must take that one, and the responder still picks their side, because refusing a motion is not choosing one; that is one veto per round, never two, and the vetoed motion cannot be re-offered. **Counter** and the responder puts their own motion up, after which the offerer picks WHICH of the two runs and also picks their own side. So the only way to get your own motion onto the table is to give up the bench, which is the trade, and it is short enough to print on the button that makes it.

  **The fairness invariant is inherited, not invented.** The strike design already held that last word on the motion costs you last word on the side, and that is the one thing that had to survive: handing one debater both would make the coin flip decide the round rather than the debate. `scripts/test-motion-draft.mjs` asserts it per branch AND over a 400-draft fuzz of every reachable path, so a future branch cannot quietly break it.

  **What this deleted is as important as what it added.** There is no slate, no strike allowance, no reveal beat, and **no blindness**. Every move is sequential and public, one actor at a time, so there is no simultaneous secret to protect: `publicDraft()` used to BE the blindness and is now an honest whole-draft copy. It is kept as a function on purpose, because it remains the one place that decides what a shared document may say, and the next beat that IS secret should be redacted there rather than anywhere else. `round_drafts/{room}` stays authoritative for a different reason that did not change: it holds the server-written eligibility stamp, in an unlisted collection the rules end by denying, so a client cannot forge its way into a draft or swap the pool it draws from.

  **The empty-chair rule got sharper, not looser.** Under strikes, both sides moved at once and a missing set was a ghost. Here presence is proven progressively: after the offer only one person has moved, and after the answer both have. So the first two beats may only be expired by the debater whose OWN clock ran out, and everything past the answer may be expired by either, because a slow click should not cost a round two people are sitting in. A stale draft past four minutes is the single exception and runs itself out to done, since a room stuck forever on a dead beat is worse than a resolution nobody watched.

  **A defect the guard found before anyone shipped it.** `draftResult` required a phase of done, a motion and a side HOLDER, and not a side. A done-but-sideless draft therefore still produced an assignment, because `side === 'pro'` is merely false when nobody ever picked, which silently seated the side holder on Con. Fixed, and pinned. Per the standing habit the suite proved nothing until it was mutated: nine mutations were applied one at a time to the pure module and six to the room-side guard, and **one escaped** — a `Math.random()` on the timeout side call, because the determinism check compared two calls to each other and a coin flip agrees with itself half the time. That is a flaky test rather than a guard. It is pinned to a literal now AND samples the beat twelve times, and the same mutation is caught 5 runs out of 5.

  **The published tournament rules moved in the same commit**, which is not tidying: `/tournament-rules` is the document a prize dispute is argued from, and it described a mechanic that no longer exists. Section 3 now states the three answers, the one-send-back limit, the side invariant and the seeded timeout in full; `/tournaments`, `/open` and the control room follow. `motionSlateSize` and `motionStrikesPerSide` on an existing tournament document are IGNORED rather than read, so an event created under the old rules still runs, it just runs the new beat. **Flagged rather than hidden: the Debatable Open's ladder is live until 5 September, so this changes a published rule mid-event.** Nothing about eligibility, scoring, the pot or the ladder moved, and every motion still comes off the same published twenty; if that is the wrong call the revert is this one commit.

  **Two dead surfaces went with it.** spar-pair had stopped writing a queue-doc draft when the negotiation moved into the room on 2026-08-26, so its `action:'draft'` phase machine had been answering `no_draft` to anyone who found it, and /spar still carried the whole strike board plus a `?draftdemo=1` view for it. Both deleted rather than left as a second phase machine to keep in step with the pure one. The /spar AI fallback keeps its ritual and gets the human's half of the negotiation: the AI always offers and you always answer, because the answer is the beat with the real choice in it and a coin flip that sometimes handed the interesting move to a machine would make the fallback worse half the time for nothing. Its moves are replayed from the seed on every call, so there is no local phase machine there either.

- **Spar gets a player-build match profile** (2026-08-27, Aidan; simplified 2026-08-28). The current flow has six choices before `/spar` writes a queue doc: whether the person likes watching debates, one optional hot-take headline, whether they want random topics or subjects they care about, matching rule, whether they want to be asked about public clips after the round, and optional writing help from their own AI chat. The explicit political-family question was removed the next day, and the abstract debate-background question became the plain "Do you like watching debates?" The decorative print diagrams and private-field-note cards were also removed; they made a simple setup feel generated and harder to understand. The full-screen build keeps the paper-and-ink Match Desk, large serif questions, restrained color, the real current queue count, and a compact waiting-screen card with a Change button. Existing v2 profiles migrate locally to v3 and the retired ideology field is discarded with the old storage row. The AI-writing option only builds a local clipboard prompt that asks three follow-up questions before drafting a short first-person summary; it calls no provider from Debatable, uploads no summary, and tells the model not to invent or intensify beliefs. Three matching rules are real: First available preserves queue order; Closest rating reads the public Glicko ladder and minimizes rating distance; Viewpoint clash prioritizes another person who also explicitly asked to be challenged, then the in-room motion draft puts them on opposite sides. **Privacy limit:** the hot take never leaves localStorage. The queue receives only `matchMode`, a neutral preference, because `matchmaking_queue` is readable by every signed-in client and is not an acceptable place to publish political opinions. Clip intent also stays local and is not recording consent. The existing all-party in-round recording choice remains the only path to capture or publish a replay.

- **The Debatable Open publishes twenty motions and locks its competitive roster at the start** (2026-08-27, Aidan; supersedes the entry-open portions of the 2026-08-18 Open decisions above). Every tournament-day motion comes from the twenty-motion pool on `/tournaments`. Each one-on-one room draws three from that pool, both sides secretly strike one, and the existing seeded resolution handles an overlapping strike: one side calls the motion from the two survivors and the other calls its side. The draft profile is written by the pairing server into `round_drafts/{room}`; the browser cannot substitute a pool or change the counts. Starting the day now means two things in the same `registration` to `running` transition: the participant queue opens and the roster locks. People already registered may arrive later during prelims, but a new account cannot enter after that transition. Reading the event, draw, tab and room remains public. Joining a tournament room does not trust a client-supplied role: pairing also writes `room_admissions/{room}`, Daily creates the room as private, assigned roster UIDs receive participant tokens, and everyone else may receive only a public, receive-only viewer token. Missing admission fails closed for tournament-shaped rooms. This deliberately reverses the earlier drop-in promise that registration and running remain open together; the founder's explicit requirement is now a private competitive event that is publicly spectatorable.

- **Live video becomes a Google signup door, with people around it** (2026-08-27, Aidan: "require google sign in to use the video feature", "say 12 live", and "dont allow 1 round anonymous"). This supersedes the 2026-08-24 one-guest-round reopening for `/spar`. The client allowance and server default are both zero, `matchmaking_queue` accepts new docs only from an active `google.com` token, `spar-pair` repeats that check before it can open a human room, and the background "Spar live" pill admits the same provider. Email and password remain valid account paths elsewhere; the restriction is the human video feature. The signed-out card has one action, Google, and the email, Discord, anonymous-mask, and AI escape paths are absent from that card so the conversion ask is not competing with itself. Fifteen surrounding frames make a wide screen feel occupied: the existing 18+ consented still bank, face46-49 and face51-54, plus the seven founder-supplied Sydney, Sofia, Kevin, Anna, Malik, Chloe, and Mike screenshots, which he confirmed are fake the same day. The fictional tiles belong to this atmosphere layer, not the ROUNDS board or any real round record. Medium screens retain ten frames and mobile retains four, so the signup action keeps its space. **Same-day count correction:** 12 is the founder-called floor, not the complete number. The signed-out gate adds the fresh real waiting count from `/api/spar-queue`, so two people in line display `14 live now`; operational matchmaking, presence, and watch surfaces keep their unpadded real counts.

- **A render is no longer a visitor, and public presence has no padding** (2026-08-27, Aidan, after live-map screenshots: "how is this possible? a glitch?", "this seems sketch", then "fix this from happening again"). The screenshots are Netlify Web Analytics' Top locations view. It is built from CDN server logs and counts successful HTML responses before Debatable's JavaScript runs, so it is a pageview map, not a people map. The synchronized city totals came from cloud regions: Moses Lake 14, Des Moines 12, San Jose 22. The public presence sample moved in the same minute to Des Moines 14, Boydton 21, Seattle 20 and San Jose/Oakland at the old 25-cell cap. Those were per-tab session ids, never identified people. A narrow edge filter now returns a bodyless 204 for self-identified headless browsers, test frameworks, and script clients requesting documents, which keeps those responses outside Netlify's pageview definition. Real search crawlers and link-preview agents remain allowed. Browser-like renderers that conceal their automation may still reach the CDN log; eliminating those completely would require a challenge or broad network block that would also reject real people. The first crawler correction on 2026-08-14 still let 20 seconds of visible dwell unlock a presence beat; a renderer that stayed open longer than expected passed it without touching the page. **Gate v2 requires a browser-trusted pointer, touch, or key event.** Scroll and passive dwell do not count, `navigator.webdriver` still vetoes, and the versioned session key makes tabs trusted under the retired rule earn trust again. Session replay loads behind the same interaction gate. **The server does not trust the client gate:** known renderer and script user agents are dropped, a per-IP rate budget limits sid rotation without storing an IP in the presence collection, and 11 km cells are withheld when they reach 12 daily sessions or trip the shorter synchronized-burst thresholds. The IP budget is shared across instances when Upstash is configured and falls back per instance otherwise; the cell quarantine is the independent public-read backstop. Raw rows remain for admin forensics; withheld rows cannot carry a public pin or count. Every retained public pin has `n:1`, so the API states that a location had a visit and cannot manufacture a ranked city bubble. `online5`, `online30`, and `online24` sum only retained measured sessions. The public cache key is versioned so the old payload cannot survive deployment.

  **This supersedes every `PRESENCE_BASELINE` call, including 100 on 2026-08-14, 200 on 2026-08-18, and 500 on 2026-08-24.** The seeded city pool is deleted and the environment variable can no longer restore it. It also deletes the unused padded Google-signup field and its `SIGNUP_BASELINE`. A location/count API is a measurement surface even when marketing consumes it; a declared cosmetic floor still becomes a false number when it leaves the source comment. Existing contaminated rows age out normally, but the read filter removes their effect immediately. This decision does not alter the separately documented, explicitly fixed 12-live signed-out conversion display on `/spar`; matchmaking, presence, watch, and any number called measured stay unpadded.

- **The public counters rise without contaminating the measurements** (2026-08-27. **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-31 by the real-numbers entry at the top of this log: every display uplift, floor, and multiplier below is retired.** Aidan: "honest + 200" for the 24-hour globe and roughly 200% for the weekly figures). This narrows the unpadded-display conclusion immediately above without reversing its data-integrity work. `/api/presence-live`, its pins, the suspicious-cell filter, admin reads and stored rows remain measured and carry no synthetic baseline. The landing caption alone renders `online24 + 200`. Public seven-day proof is raised at the presentation layer: the landing's live-search note and `/live` missed-match card render 2x their raw values, while `/spar` doubles its pre-existing weekly display floor from 380 to 760. The monthly `/spar` floor stays 500. Raw APIs and admin analytics remain unchanged, so every uplift is explicit in source and removable without rewriting history. These display figures are marketing surfaces, not measurements, and must not be quoted as traffic or activity data.

- **Every tournament round is recorded, with no in-room decline path** (2026-08-28, Aidan: "all rounds are recorded for the tournament" and "make sure nothing declines"). Recording is a condition of tournament entry rather than the ordinary live-room unanimous opt-in. Registration and check-in require an adult or guardian-approved acknowledgement, and an assigned room stays blocked until every seat acknowledges and the server reports recording live. The mandatory path has no "do not record" action: someone who cannot accept must leave the room or withdraw from the event. The server also rejects a false acknowledgement, so a stale or modified client cannot turn the requirement into an optional choice. This is a narrow tournament exception to the opt-in rule. Ordinary non-tournament rooms keep unanimous consent and withdrawal. Mandatory capture is also not blanket permission to publish: preliminary-round files are private by default and unavailable on Watch, while elimination streaming continues under the tournament's published rules. The host may monitor a room without receiving a participant token or appearing in the recording. Minors require parent or guardian approval before entry.

- **The Open page is the live tournament lobby** (2026-08-28, Aidan: "when ppl show up its like a live twitch" and "viewers in the tournament room should be able to see the main stream"). While the global site stream is live, `/open` mounts the same player used by the homepage and `/watch`, above the event overview and next-step card. People can leave the event page open for pairings, timing, room changes, and host instructions instead of bouncing between the tournament and Watch. The player starts muted because browsers block audible autoplay and the page explicitly tells people to tap to unmute. **The site player is Debatable-owned even while the room simulcasts outward to Twitch, YouTube Live, and TikTok Live:** a receive-only Daily call object paints the host cameras and shared screen into native video elements, so viewers get no meeting chrome, join dialog, People/Chat tray, self tile, camera request, or microphone request. The three platform keys stay in Netlify env. The lead Studio receives only a random one-time start token and safe platform names; `/api/stream-restream` verifies the token and sends all configured RTMP endpoints straight to Daily, while the cohost receives no simulcast control at all. Ending the Debatable stream stops the recording and the complete external fan-out. `STREAM_SITE_PLAYER=embed` is the explicit scaling escape hatch when the on-site audience needs a platform CDN too; it is not the default. The page checks stream status every 15 seconds and removes the player promptly when the broadcast ends. `/studio` remains the broadcast source and `/director` remains the room-selection wall. Individual debate rooms do not receive a second competing audio stream.

- **The /practice setup becomes a one-question-per-screen run, on the matchmaker's engine** (2026-09-01, Aidan off a screenshot of the expanded setup: make it "like the match maker questionnaire", "interactive and animated", not "a big screen of a puzzle like options"). "Build my round" (was "Customize round") now runs the shared ArcadeFlow engine that /spar's matchmaker profile and /brain already use: topic (six themes, Surprise me, My own topic, plus Resolution of the day when the API has one), judge type, verdict tone, feedback length, then a launch panel with a summary and, for a typed topic, the motion field. Every answer lands on the exact state the old stacked panel wrote (setMotion, saveJudgeParadigm, setJudgeManner, setJudgeDetail) and starts the round through the existing simpleStartPending path, so the round that opens is identical to one set up the old way; Skip starts a round on the defaults, which is quickStart in other clothes. **The stacked five-step panel is not deleted: it renders only when arcade-flow.js failed to load**, the same progressive-enhancement posture as /brain, and it must not grow new features. Two layout defects came from running it rather than reading it: eight stacked topic options ran 894px against a 661px track at 1280x720, so "Surprise me" and "My own topic" sat below the fold of a scroll-snap panel (the topic step now carries a two-column grid via the engine's per-step className), and at 375px the same panel ran 809 against 758 (the example sub-lines hide under 560px). Verified in a browser in both real themes (`da-theme` light and crimson, white-on-dark measured; a hand flip of `data-lighting` alone produces dark-on-dark and is not how the site switches), the full walk with a typed motion opening the round, and the Skip path. `practice_setup_flow_open` / `practice_setup_flow_done` (via done/skip, topic, judge, manner, detail) in GA4.

- **Debating the AI moves a rating, and there is exactly one AI door** (2026-09-01, updated three times 2026-09-03 by Aidan's live-room pass). The public "Debate the AI" door is `/newvoice`. It was pulled to `/voice-debate` for a few hours on 2026-09-03 while its spoken opener was reading private model instructions as the topic; see the topic-isolation entry for the fix. Its setup shows three actual paths, not difficulty presets: Casual back-and-forth, Conversation, and Competitive practice. Live and Conversation share the Realtime transport but have different turn behavior; Conversation is shorter, question-led, and more patient with silence. Competitive practice opens the TTS-based `/practice?entry=competitive` path, pitched explicitly to competitive debaters, with APDA, BP, Asian Parli, Worlds, Karl Popper, PF, LD, Policy, and Congress restored there only. Voice plans use ElevenLabs; Free uses OpenAI voice. Human rounds, the default practice entry, and the site's social pitch remain casual 1v1. The intended Realtime opening exchange is spoken and interruptible: the AI reads the topic, asks "Wanna know how this is gonna work?", waits, then answers briefly or yields if the person begins their case. Speaking speed remains changeable before and during the round. That coverage now exists: `scripts/test-newvoice-topic-isolation.mjs` runs in the pre-commit hook and pins the opener, the sanitizer, and the client/server mirror. **The rating:** new `/api/ai-rating` gives every judged AI round (typed practice and voice) a Glicko-2 update against a fixed AI anchor (1500, rd 80, never persisted so mass wins cannot deflate it). The number is DELIBERATELY SEPARATE from the human ladder: `ai_ratings/{uid}`, never `user_ratings`, because the ballot is authored in the participant's own browser (the same trust bar the human ladder already accepts for live rounds) and the opponent is a machine anyone can replay all day, so mixed into the public board it would be farmable standing; on its own it is a progress number whose blast radius is the owner's profile. Do not unify the two without a founder decision. Named accounts only, idempotent per round via a transaction-claimed `ai_rating_changes` row, `verdictSource:'participant'` stamped on every change, a 120-word floor so an empty round rates nothing, and a missing verdict is NOT a draw. It rates nothing. The video-game feel is earned mechanics, not chrome (the 2026-08-31 de-vibe pass stands): rank tiers (Rookie through Titan, from lib/rating.mjs's tierFor), W-L record, and the movement rendered on the practice ballot, the voice RFD, and a /profile tile. `scripts/test-ai-rating.mjs` guards it in the pre-commit hook; ten mutations were applied one at a time and all ten are caught. Three guards were VACUOUS on the first pass (a prose mention satisfied a code scan, a disabled gate still matched its import, a renamed method still matched a substring) and were tightened until each mutation fired, which is the standing test-the-tests habit paying out again.

- **The first screen sheds the globe and the stats, and Explore promotes the judged AI round** (2026-09-01, **AI destination superseded 2026-09-03 by the entry above**, Aidan, off two annotated screenshots: "get rid of the globe and the stats and simplify the entire page so ppl aren't overwhelmed", then "follow instructions on cutting and having the other debate ai thing be up there"). The landing left column is now headline + Jump in + the Google row: the presence globe, its 24h-visits caption, the sign-ups figure, and the "Spectate and bet tokens" ghost CTA are all retired from the first screen. The globe IIFE early-returns without its canvas, so the presence-live and public-join-history fetches die with the markup rather than running for nothing; the .fs-globe CSS stays dormant as the restore path. In Explore, AI round (/practice, full judged rounds against the clock) becomes the Practice flagship; the /voice-debate tile, Judge a round, Bounties, Spectate live rounds, and Chat and DMs come out of the panel. Voice deliberately keeps two doors: the spotlight's "Debate the AI" alt row on desktop and its MORE_GROUPS row in the mobile sheet, because measured live the same day (generations, last 30 days, signed-in only) voice is still the most-used AI surface: live 64, voice 50, typed practice 26. The typed round has grown from the 9x-behind figure in section 8 to roughly half of voice. **Open product gap flagged with the promotion: AI rounds do not move Glicko.** The ladder only rates human-vs-human rounds, so "debate the AI and get judged to get better ELO" is a build, not a copy edit, and needs its own decision about whether AI wins can be allowed to move a public rating. Same push, mobile coverage pass: 100dvh fallbacks on the live-round shell, chat frames, spar queue card and community overlay so bottom controls stay above mobile Safari's bottom bar; env(safe-area-inset-bottom) on every floating chip and the topbar sheet; [id] scroll-margin-top so anchor landings clear the fixed topbar.

- **The Match Desk hot take becomes a fight card with both chairs** (2026-09-01, Aidan, off a screenshot of the ready screen: "pull in more of the socialism / capitalism etc debates and vibes, improve this questionnaire by a lot"). The 2026-08-28 simplification removed the explicit political-family question and it stays removed; this does not label anyone. What changed is the claim bank on step 2, which was six unrelated headlines and is now five named fights with both sides offered at equal weight: socialism versus capitalism, billionaires, meritocracy, borders, platform speech. Picking a claim is choosing a seat in a debate, never agreeing with the house, and the bank must stay balanced for that to hold; a one-sided bank reads as the site taking positions. A kicker names the fight on each card and a "vs" chip binds the pair, so the row reads as one clash. The topics step gains "The big fights". Nothing new enters the queue: the pick stays in localStorage and `matchMode` remains the only field sent, so the privacy line in the masthead is still true. Old stored picks migrate to their nearest claim (immigration, moderation) or fall to skip; nobody is assigned a side they never chose. `?mpdemo=1` opens the desk without an account, same precedent as `?draftdemo=1`, because this surface is judged by looking at it. Verified at desktop and 375px. **The fights reach the table, by pool rather than by steering** (same day, follow-up). The pick is device-only by design and the draft slate is server-stamped, so the card cannot steer a round. Instead ten system-fight motions joined the `quick` pool in practice.html (regenerated into `lib/draft-motions.mjs`): socialism versus capitalism, billionaires, luck versus hard work, landlords, immigration, platform moderation, inheritance, UBI, rent caps, the 32-hour week. A casual slate of five from a pool of 34 now carries about one of them on average. Ship note: the SW rebase-dedupe trap fired twice in one push (origin landed on v3189 and then v3190 independently); caught by checking the commit's file list, hand-bumped to v3191.

- **Signup-welcome campaign: the 193 never-emailed accounts got one activation email, and the blast exclusion list now lives in code** (2026-09-01, Aidan: "send out an email to new sign ups that i haven't emailed yet"). A 468-account sweep of Firebase Auth found 238 already stamped by the two Open campaigns and 193 never mailed, almost all post-Aug-22 sign-in-wall arrivals. New `admin-signup-welcome.mjs` (`POST /api/admin/signup-welcome`, own stamp `signupWelcomeSentAt`, reads the announce and rally stamps as "already emailed") sent 193/193 with zero errors from `hello@debateai.com`, Reply-To the founder's gmail because `hello@itsdebatable.com` inbound is not a confirmed alias. **The endpoint carries a by-name exclusion list**: the founder's own and family accounts, burner domains, six educators on school-district domains, and five notable signups reserved for personal outreach (a College Prep debate coach, the SuperDebate founder, a likely Robin Hanson at futarchy@gmail.com, a discourse-product founder, and a There's An AI For That staffer). Copy that list into any future campaign sender; a blast must never be the first thing a personal-outreach target hears from us. The sweep report and CSV live outside the repo.

- **The outward stream is TikTok-only; the public social row is TikTok plus Discord for now** (2026-08-29, Aidan: "remove the twitch and youtube links just do tiktok for now", then "remove x as well from there" and "have the discord link there aswell"). This narrows the three-destination fan-out above without deleting its dormant platform support. `streamTargets()` defaults to a TikTok-only allowlist, so old Twitch, YouTube, and legacy single-target secrets may remain stored in Netlify without starting or appearing in the player. `STREAM_ENABLED_PLATFORMS` is the explicit restore switch. Public YouTube, Twitch, and X profile links are off the topbar, landing footer, structured identity, and `llms.txt`; Discord remains beside TikTok as the community door. Its rail mark is the supplied high-contrast treatment, white Clyde on a purple tile at 19px, because the stroked version was too easy to miss. The claimed paused accounts themselves are untouched. Restoring a platform later means adding it to that allowlist when relevant plus restoring its public profile links.

- **Tournament interaction, pace, and camera-presence scoring are disclosed before Speech 1** (2026-08-29, Aidan: "interruptions are captured to ask questions", "spreading discouraged and calculated by the AI", and "dark only they lose points, avatar they lose less points, video they gain points"). Questions and interruptions ride the mandatory tournament recording and may be used to generate private clips. The judge credits a concise, relevant question that exposes a gap and a clean answer; it may lower responsiveness, strategy, or persuasion for heckling, repeated interruption, talking over an answer, or an irrelevant gotcha. Nobody is penalized merely for being interrupted. Each saved speech carries its duration, and the server calculates words per minute for the AI. Above 250 WPM is flagged as spreading, but speed alone never costs points; the transcript must show listener-unfriendly density, poor signposting, or compressed reasoning before clarity or strategy moves. Camera mode stays outside the win/loss rubric and the six argument dimensions. It adjusts only the tournament speaker-points tiebreak after the ballot: Camera +2, Avatar -1, Off or a dark tile -3. Mixed use is weighted by speech time and the final standings score stays between 0 and 100.
