The room shows the exact rule before recording acceptance and the ballot shows the base argument score, the camera adjustment, calculated pace, and final standings points.

- **The full live leaderboard is back on the landing page, before Full tour** (2026-08-29, Aidan: "bring the leaderboard fully to landing page bc ppl want to be high up on it"). **This supersedes the 2026-08-24 removal of `#ranked-band`.** The middle leaderboard card now scrolls to a visible top-eight ladder instead of leaving the page. The board uses the real `/api/leaderboard-top` payload, puts the numbered race in one compact stack, shows ratings and records, and gives every row a Challenge action. It never flashes sample names: the only non-live states are an explicit loading skeleton, an honest empty board, or a link to the full standings when the feed is unavailable. The privacy boundary stays narrower than the first-screen rail: a named row may show the person's own saved avatar or account photo, otherwise it uses an abstract emblem; the founder-supplied stand-in photos remain exclusive to the unlabeled rail. Measured at 1280px and 390px with all eight real production row shapes: no horizontal overflow, header contrast holds on the dark slab in the light theme, and the mobile board keeps rank, name, rating, and Challenge visible.

- **The supplied profile pictures now cover the full landing leaderboard too** (2026-08-29, Aidan, after seeing the abstract letter tiles beside the live names: "give profile pictures that are cool and what i gave as images like use these!"). **This supersedes the last two sentences of the leaderboard entry immediately above and expands the 2026-08-25 stand-in exception beyond the first-screen rail.** The top-eight board now uses the same ten-photo pool as the rail for anybody without an identity of their own, seeded stably and de-duplicated within the visible board. A person's chosen Debatable avatar or own account photo still outranks the stand-in. The child image excluded from the supplied batch remains excluded, and the photo tier remains unavailable in the account avatar picker, so nobody can select a stranger's picture as their identity. The board waits briefly for the deferred picture module before painting; if that module is blocked, it falls back to the existing abstract emblem rather than withholding the standings.

- **The live tournament is a continuous checked-in queue with an open door** (2026-08-29, Aidan: "just have the people who are active and checked in then be able to do tournament" and "allow new ppl to check in too and join in"). Registration stays open while the event is running. A new person registers, explicitly checks in, and can then press Ready to enter live pairing. Merely being registered never makes someone pairable. Finishing a ballot clears the active seat, and pressing Next round deliberately returns that present person to the queue. The matcher waits for an unseen checked-in opponent whenever one remains anywhere in the live field. A repeat becomes eligible only in a later cycle after that field has been exhausted, and the immediately previous opponent stays last choice. Tournament seating remains exclusive with general Spar, so one person cannot owe two opponents a room at once. If a live event switches from fixed draws to this queue, the host releases only unresolved fixed seats; completed results remain on the standings, while the old room cards stop acting like active assignments.

- **Every live queue room is a visible tournament round** (2026-08-29, Aidan: "live rounds are going on but they dont seem to be built into the tournament"). A drop-in matcher pass writes its own round document, so multiple round documents can be live simultaneously. Participant prompts, the tournament draw, the Open room wall, and host result controls must index every pairing that still owns an active entry seat, never only the last released document. The Open wall is scoped to those tournament rooms and enriches them with physical presence and viewer counts from the public watch feed; unrelated public debates do not appear as event rooms. An assigned room remains visible while its two people enter and settle the motion draft. The AI ballot posts the result into the same tournament ledger, updates wins, losses, speaker points and standings, then clears the seat. The host can report any simultaneously live room manually if the judge needs recovery.

- **The director spotlight is editorial promotion, not broadcast permission** (2026-08-29, Aidan: "like the director watch panel i want to be able to spotlight certain debates"). The director wall defaults to one live view across every simultaneous drop-in round document. An admin may spotlight exactly one active tournament room or remove the spotlight. The chosen room moves first and renders larger on the Open tournament floor, and receives a Spotlight marker on the generic tournament draw. The choice is reversible and disappears from the active-room view when the pairing completes. Spotlighting never changes the room, its participants, the judge, recording or publication permission, or the separate host main broadcast. A room that says Do not stream remains barred from that broadcast even while the public spectator floor spotlights its already-public watch door.
- **The Open reopens as an all-day event rating ladder** (2026-08-29, Aidan: "allow more tournament rounds today so its an ELO competition"). This supersedes the fixed-round limit and elimination cut for the live event. Completed ballots stay intact. Everyone starts the event ladder at 1500, each completed tournament result moves both event ratings with Elo K=32, and an amended ballot rebuilds the table from the stored results. The global Debate Rating remains Glicko-2 and is still updated by the same judged human round; the event rating is deliberately separate so a prior record cannot buy position today. Registration, check-in and the checked-in Ready queue remain open while the ladder runs. Only checked-in entries appear on the live rating board. Checking out hides the row without erasing its results or event Elo, so checking back in restores the same record. Fresh opponents come first; repeats only open in a later cycle after the checked-in field has been exhausted. `TOURNAMENT_PUBLIC_PAIRING_ENABLED=0` is an emergency stop, not a launch dependency, so a missing environment setting can no longer silently hide the queue.
- **Rating mode cannot break to eliminations, and tournament replays need a second yes** (2026-08-29, Aidan: "dont break to tournament elims" and "collect videos from all these rounds and put them on watch site"). The server refuses fixed draws, break creation, and elimination status while `ratingCompetition` is on, so an old cached host control cannot cut the field. Registration, check-in, and the Ready queue remain the only round-spawn path. Every tournament video is still captured and indexed into the private recording archive. Public Watch publication is separate: each recorded person gets an optional Watch checkbox before the round and an approval action after the ballot. The replay publishes only when every recorded seat agrees, while elimination broadcast permission remains the existing event exception. Mandatory recording consent never silently becomes blanket public-use permission.

- **The opening beat becomes a card, and it gets a 15-second tempo** (2026-09-02, Aidan: "do 2", off a live read that morning). The 09-01 beat below rewrote the grey hint line under Start and gave the waiter a nudge. Measured the day after it shipped: 9 live rooms, 7 with both seats beating, 2 that ever started a speech, and last night's 8 PM Clash Hour slot was 3 seated rooms with zero speeches. A hint line under a button is not a stage moment. The beat now renders as `#openBeatCard`, the first thing in the speech header on BOTH screens, spanning the grid: a green "Both of you are here" eyebrow, a large "You speak first." or "[Name] speaks first.", speech 1 of N with its name and minutes, the motion, and one action line. The opener's line is an arrow to the single Start button directly under the card; the waiter's line carries the existing nudge. **Nothing auto-starts and there is still exactly one Start button**, per the 08-24 and 08-26 rules; the card wraps the control, it does not duplicate it. At 15 seconds with both seated and nothing started the card turns red and the Start pulses fast (`OPEN_BEAT_HOT_MS`); at 90 seconds the copy says the room has waited. The "Now speaking · Name" line hides while the card is up, since nobody is speaking. The hint under Start now says only what the press does. `?openbeatdemo=opener|waiter` renders the card against synthetic state, the `?draftdemo` precedent, because a timed beat has to be watched to be judged. GA4 `live_open_beat_shown` carries `variant:'card'` so the two versions can be read against each other. **The number is still two-seat rounds reaching speech 1**; read it against the 09-01 figure, not against zero.

- **The round opening becomes the final beat of the guided sequence** (2026-09-01, from the founder's "what should we work on" and a data autopsy; design approved before build). Measured over 7 days: 55 of the last 60 live rounds seated both people, only 23 ever started a speech, and round_abandoned beacons show people leaving speech-0 rooms after a MEDIAN of 3 minutes with the opponent visibly present (34 cases past 5 minutes). Every pre-round beat completes in the dead rooms — draft done, judge agreed, recording consent answered, several with a cloud recording that started and stopped around a round with zero speeches — and then the guided sequence simply stops and waits for one human to volunteer. The three prior fixes (countdown, kickoff strip twice, the full-width button) all attacked "can they find the button"; the data says the failure is "nobody's move is forced." New ROUND OPENING beat on /live-round, live only when the ONLY remaining act is pressing Start (speech 1, clock ready, both seats beating under the kickoff-strip trust rule, draft settled, judge locked, no prep clock): the opener's ready hint names the move ("You open: Pro Opening, 5 minutes. [Name] is in the room, waiting on you"), and the waiting side gets the one thing it never had, an action — a "Ready when you are" nudge that writes an additive `openerNudge` field to the round doc (the contextPicks pattern, no rules change), which lands on the opener as a named line, a faster pulse on the existing start-cue classes, and one soft chime per nudge. At 90 seconds both sides get louder. **Nothing here starts a speech**: the no-auto-start rule stands, and the opener's control remains the single #playPauseBtn per the 2026-08-26 one-action rule — the beat rewrites the hint around it and never grows a second Start button. The waiter card also hides the stale "Starts the timer and mic" hint, which had been describing a button that side cannot press. Gating logic unit-tested with stubs (12 cases) and mutation-checked (two deleted guards both caught). GA4: live_open_beat_shown (per role), live_open_beat_nudge (sent/received). **Read the funnel again in a week**: two-seat rounds reaching speech 1 is the number this exists to move, 23/55 at ship time.

- **Tournament rounds ask for content, and featuring becomes a standing opt-in** (2026-08-31, Aidan: "i want ppl in the tournament to help me generate content", then "yeah build one and 2" on the two proposed levers). Every tournament round is already recorded by rule, so the raw material always exists; what was missing was anyone asking. Two additions. **(1) The ballot on a tournament round now carries a content ask**: a card above the actions row telling the debater the round was recorded and offering one tap into the existing 9:16 share-card generator (same `openShareClipModal`, its own GA4 `via:'tournament_ask'` tag so the ask's pull reads against the quiet button's). Debaters only, tournament rounds only, and the Watch-replay approval keeps its single home in `replayNoteHtml` so there is exactly one approve control per screen. **(2) Standing feature consent at registration.** After someone is confirmed in the bracket, /tournaments offers an OPT-IN "Feature my rounds" card, deliberately separate from the mandatory recording confirm, because bundling optional publicity consent into a required gate would make the consent worthless. Saying yes re-calls the idempotent register action with `featureOk:true`; the server stores `featureConsents[uid]` (versioned `tournament-feature-v1-2026-08-31`) on the entry, only ever on an explicit true, so a stale client that omits the field can never revoke a grant. It never affects pairing, standing, or eligibility, it stays out of the public `publicEntry` projection, and the pool is read through a new host/site-admin `feature-pool` action on `/api/tournament-admin` (entrant names, consenting uids, consent times). **Not built, on purpose:** auto-applying the standing consent to the per-round `recordingPublishConsents` in round-recording.mjs. That compose is the natural next step (a standing grant counting as the per-round Watch approval, with a receipt pointing at the registration grant), but it writes consent records the person did not click in the room, so it gets its own pass with its own entry rather than riding this one.

- **A second consented still batch from real rounds, and the landing product shot becomes a real spectated round** (2026-08-31, Aidan: "put these images in places where screenshots of users are put on the site", consent given in the same session: "all consented to"). He supplied screenshots of real live rounds; three webcam stills were cropped out as face63-65 (Ray, Pascal, Yael) and join `FACE_M_REAL`, the rotator `REAL` list, and the /spar sign-in gate frames, on the same standing as the face46-54 batch. One Avatar-mode mask tile (`mask-ano.jpg`, a silver ANO mask mid-speech from the Pascal round) joins the /spar gate only: a mask is not a face, needs no consent, and must never enter the face pools where the deal would caption it with a character's name. The gate grows from 15 to 19 frames via nth-child slots 16-19 tucked into the edge columns; medium screens still hide n+11 and mobile still shows the first four, so the additions render only on the widest layout. `app/landing-shot-live.jpg` was recut at the same URL and size (585x571) from the full spectator view of a real round, Lars Sawyer vs Ace on free public-university tuition, with Lars mid-speech, both name pills, and the AI Judge chip in frame; the CSS mockup fallback and the aspect-locked panel are untouched. Numbering note: face55 stays deleted-never-reuse and face56-62 are the generated female batch, which is why this batch starts at 63. Still on offer, not yet supplied: frames of Jonas, Steven Aitch (his self tile there is Aidan and must stay cropped out per the anonymity sweep), and the Lars close-up with the judge chip.

- **The fixed topbar was eating the top of four pages, and the trap is a specificity clobber in ui.css** (2026-08-31, Aidan off a /friends screenshot: "fix positioning across the board for diff pages on this site. i suspect this issue happens a lot"). He was right about the class of bug and the sweep found its real size: all 97 topbar pages were measured in a browser (first visible TEXT position against the fixed bar's bottom edge, because a wrapper's box top lies when the wrapper carries internal padding), and exactly four overlapped: /chat by 38px, /livedebates by 32px, /friends by 18px, /settings by 14px. The other 93 self-compensate. **The finding that generalizes: `body.has-beta-strip{padding-top:32px}` in ui.css out-specifies any page's own plain `body{padding-top:...}` compensation**, so /chat and /livedebates had BOTH written correct-looking 52px topbar offsets that the strip class silently replaced with 32 the moment the sitewide ribbon mounted — correct code, defeated by a cascade rule in another file, rendering a plausible page. All four now state both arms (`body{padding-top:52px}` plus `body.has-beta-strip{padding-top:84px}`), /chat's viewport-height math subtracts the strip in the strip arm (measured exact at 84 to 900 and 52 to 900 in the two states), the trap is documented next to the rule in both ui.css mirrors, and AGENTS.md's pitfalls carry the standing rule: a new topbar page owns its own 52px bar offset, in both strip arms, verified by measuring first-text against the bar in a browser rather than reading the diff.

- **A mid-round reload could rewrite the resolution, and now it cannot; the turn gets a 20-second breather and Start moves to the top** (2026-08-31, Aidan from a live round: "there is a glitch where its changing the resolution once we already started talking about it - it changed it from student debt to this random shit", plus "when ppl are done with their speech give 20 secodns buffer time or allower userto just start speech. put that button higher up").
  - **The glitch was the spar auto-kickoff firing into a live round.** The kickoff treats any client with an empty motion and phase `setup` as a fresh match, and a MID-ROUND reload arrives exactly like one, because the spar URL carries `motion=` empty. When the round-doc snapshot lost the race against the kickoff timer (600ms lex-first, 3s peer), the reloading client seeded `pickSparMotion(room)` — deterministic per room, which is why the replacement read as one specific "random" motion ("This House would bring back extinct species", straight from SPAR_MOTIONS) — called startRound, and `publishRoundInit` merged motion + `speechIdx:0` + judgePicks over a round at speech 4; the snapshot handler's unconditional `d.motion` adoption then swapped the resolution on every screen. **Two layers closed it.** The kickoff now READS the round doc before seeding and never seeds over a doc carrying a motion, a speech, or round status; an unreadable doc seeds nothing, because a snapshot deciding late beats a wrong motion deciding instantly. And `publishRoundInit` is a transaction that refuses to regress a live round outright: once speeches have run, only the additive identity fields (avatars, seat uids where absent) land, whatever a stale client believes. `live_kickoff_seed_blocked` / `live_init_regress_blocked` in GA4 make both guards visible. The ballot of the round it happened in judged the stomped motion and is unsalvageable; no future round can lose its motion this way.
  - **The breather narrows the same-day turn cue above.** Chiming the moment the turn arrived nagged someone who had just finished listening, so the turn now opens a visible 20-second breather counted down in the hint under Start my speech, which stays pressable the whole time; the chime and title flash land only when the breather runs out, and the on-screen reminder ten seconds after that. Nothing auto-starts, ever.
  - **The speech header (clock + Start + End) moved ABOVE the resolution bar** in the round view, so the primary action is the first thing in the panel instead of living below a screen of motion text. Ids and handlers unchanged; markup order only.

- **The turn announces itself, the judge cards get quiet, and a blocked camera is retryable** (2026-08-31, Aidan in two messages: "it asks users to connect the audio but not camera so some ppl are having issues - fix this", then "remind users to press 'start speech' when its there turn - a lot of ppl forget - lets simplify the UI of the right side"). Three /live-round changes, one push.
  - **The camera failure was a poisoned cache, not a missing prompt.** The join's combined getUserMedia degrades to mic-only whenever the camera half fails (blocked for the site, busy, or denied on an earlier visit), and that degrade cached an audio-only pipeline in `camConv.camP` FOREVER: clicking Camera got the cache back, threw, fell into a cycleCamera that cannot fix a denial, and ended at a toast about an in-call picker the custom UI does not have. The Camera click now re-asks with a fresh video-only getUserMedia in the click's own gesture context and rebuilds the pipeline around the SAME audio track, because `cam.stop()` stops every track on its stream and the transcriber borrows that exact mic (`srvBorrowTrack`); the join says so out loud when it lands mic-only; the failure toast is permissions-aware. The degraded case also stops falling into the raw cycleCamera branch, which published the device camera past the safety watchdog, and the watchdog's videoEl closure reads the CURRENT pipeline so a rebuilt camera stays watched.
  - **The reminder gap was the SILENT debater on speeches 2+.** The opening beat covers speech 1 and the we-can-hear-you reminder covers talking into a stopped clock; someone who just watched their opponent finish and is reading notes when the turn lands got nothing. Once per speech now: a soft chime and a title flash the moment the turn arrives, one on-screen "It is your turn" bar if the clock is still off ten seconds later. **Nothing auto-starts; the no-auto-start rule stands**, and one reminder per speech total, whichever fires first. GA4 `live_turn_cue` beside the existing cue events.
  - **The judge-type cards were the right panel's bulk**: three bullet lists (~12 lines of judge philosophy) expanded on every fresh round, before anyone could start. Compact now (name, one-line tag, the You/Opponent marks) with the bullets behind one "Compare the three" toggle. Presentation only; the pick-the-same-type consent machinery is untouched. If the panel still reads busy, the next candidates are the context card and the recording row, against a fresh screenshot rather than a guess.

- **The friend graph ships: /friends, the post-ballot ask, challenges, typing dots, replies, and every cap sells the plan that fixes it** (2026-08-31, Aidan in one burst: "have a friends page - i want you to build it", "to challenge a friend etc and have friends and chats work - also make group chats have ... generating when ppl are typing. and allow users to reply to others", and "make sure limits on voice usage and usage after a certain time gets ppl to the payment page"). Three coupled builds, three commits, shipped before the open-week email.
  - **Friendships are one doc per pair** (`friendships/{a_b}`, the dm_threads sorted-uid convention), created client-direct by the requester carrying ONLY their own half of the consent; the own-key MapDiff guard in `firestore.rules` is the entire security model, same shape as `motionProposalAccepts`, so a friendship cannot be forged from one keyboard. uids and requestedBy freeze at create, the doc id must equal the joined pair, declining and unfriending are deleting the doc. Named accounts only. Verified with the Rules test API against the live project, 22 cases in both directions, then three guard mutations each shown to fail; deployed and the released ruleset confirmed. `friendships` joins the account-deletion sweep, and the list query is array-contains with no orderBy so no composite index exists to deploy.
  - **Friendships come from rounds, not browsing.** The ballot on /live-round grows an "Add [name] as a friend" slot (fill-after-render like roomShiftSlot; 1v1 only, named only, spectators never see it), and /friends carries the rest: incoming and outgoing requests, the list, a personal invite link (`/friends?add=uid`) for pulling real-life people in, and Message into the existing DM engine. **The availability dot is the honest signal and nothing more:** it reads the friend's own `matchmaking_queue` doc, so green means "in the live queue right now", never "online". Real presence would need a sitewide heartbeat write and that is a drain decision for another day; the challenge deliberately survives an offline friend instead.
  - **Challenge-a-friend reuses everything.** It mints a direct-link room whose URL carries both uids (so the seats lock to the pair the moment both arrive, and the motion draft correctly stays off, since only spar-pair stamps eligibility), then pings the friend as a DM plus `push-send` web push. Riding dm_threads is the whole trick: the bell badge, toast, OS notification, email notify and push all come free, and no new server code or notifications collection exists.
  - **Typing dots and replies live in the ENGINE, so all three DM surfaces got them at once.** dm-core carries `typing: { uid: serverTimestamp }` on the thread doc (throttled to one write per 2.5s of composing, cleared in the same write as the send, expiring client-side so the dots come down without another write) and exposes computed names via `view().typing`; a new own-key rule guards the map because the thread update rule was otherwise unfielded and either side could have faked the other's state. Replies are DENORMALIZED onto the sending message (`replyToId/Name/Text`, snippet bounded in rules), because messages are append-only and the read window may not hold the quoted message. /spar's modal, /messages, /chat and `community-channels.js` each render the quote block, a per-bubble Reply button, a composer chip, and the typing row; channels put typing on the channel PARENT doc, which a new rules block allows to carry nothing but that map. Verified by driving dm-core against a stubbed Firestore in a browser: fresh names appear, stale ones expire, the reply fields land on the message doc, the send clears the sender's stamp, rapid typing() calls collapse to one dotted write.
  - **The paywall audit found the money leaks and closed the routable ones.** `/app?upgrade=voice` was a silent dead end (the resume regex never learned the plan /pricing's own Get Voice hands off, so signed-out Voice intent vanished); the voice cap card promised 8 free rounds against a server cap of 2 and linked bare /pricing, whose hero sells the one plan that does NOT lift the voice cap; /newvoice's anonymous 401 dead-ended with no sign-in door; the /practice signed-in cap's only button was the Team tab with pricing as unlinked prose; upgrade-cta.js announced "you've hit the free cap" and sent people to a workspace; and the /app brain-swap notice gated its See plans link on an async flag that starts true, so early clicks got no CTA at all. Every one now lands on the plan that fixes the thing that just stopped, with the real numbers on the button. Left deliberately: create-checkout's NEEDS_TEAM shape (documented design, the resume flow carries the intent through team creation) and the thrown plan-limit strings in /app.
  - **Honest limits, stated:** no real two-account friendship, challenge, or typing exchange has run end to end against production yet (the engine and rules were exercised against stubs and the live Rules API; the first real pair is the thing to watch), and friend requests carry the same block-exposure DMs already accept, since `user_blocks` is not client-readable and pairing remains where blocks are enforced server-side.

- **The searching screen becomes board, globe, actions** (2026-08-31, Aidan, three messages on one screenshot session: "have it be on the left the leaderboard too with the ELO showing - globe in middle", "delete" on the rail's "Use the wait." head and the two hero reassurance lines, and "simplify how this is messaged - bigfer text. 3 main clash hours to show up and they are specified as those hours" on the Spar Night card). At >=1281px /spar's searching state is now three columns: a sticky leaderboard rail on the left (top eight real `/api/leaderboard-top` rating rows, rank, picture, W/L record, the rating number; pictures are the person's own photo, then the shared pfp stand-in pool, then initials), the globe in the middle, the wait rail on the right. **The left rail only exists while it has at least three real rated rows** — `spar-elo-on` on the wrap is what opens the third grid column, so a failed fetch or an empty ladder falls back to the two-column composition instead of a dead track squeezing the globe, and no synthetic row ever dresses it (the same honest-empty rule as the landing board). Deleted on his word: the rail's "MATCH SEARCH ACTIVE / Use the wait." head with its chime line (the rail now opens on Challenge a friend), and the searching column's AI-judge and avatar lede lines. The Spar Night rail card now leads "Three clash hours every Wednesday." over the hours themselves big ("7:00 AM · 3:00 PM · 8:00 PM ET", derived from SESSIONS so copy cannot drift from the countdown), and the stale "one fixed hour a week" quiet line reads "Everyone shows up at the three clash hours." **New QA arm `?searchdemo=1`** paints the searching screen with no queue join and no doc write, same precedent as `?draftdemo=1`, because this screen normally needs a signed-in Google session in a live human queue and kept shipping unverified; it seeds synthetic board rows CLIENT-side for the local preview only, and they never touch the live fetch path. The rebase SW-dedupe trap fired again on this push (origin landed v3133 independently, both sw.js files dropped from the commit) and was caught by the standing file-list check, hand-bumped to v3134.

- **/leaderboard wears the stand-in photos, and the /spar map becomes readable** (2026-08-31, Aidan: "update the leaderboard to match and give the profile pictures to them at the top - we need the leaderboard to attract ppl", and "make the map have a stronger contrast it hard to tell its a world map"). The pfp stand-in exception now covers its third surface: /leaderboard loads `/js/pfp-set.js` and any row with no built avatar and no account photo draws `pickPhotoSvg` from the same ten-photo pool the first-screen rail and the landing's full board use, deduped per render so the podium and the top of the table stay distinct; with 10 photos against up to 100 rows, repeats further down are expected. The 2026-08-25 limits hold unchanged: stand-in tier only, a built identity or the account's own photo outranks it everywhere, the photo ids stay unpickable as an identity (`DBPfp.canWear`), and the drawn emblem remains the fallback for a blocked script (a late-loading module triggers one repaint rather than leaving emblems for the visit). Seed rows keep their seed portraits. The map half: /spar's `#presenceMapBg` shipped at host opacity .28 over `landAlpha` 0.5 with sub-pixel dots, ~0.14 effective ink, which is the "hard to tell it's a world map" in his screenshot. Host opacity .5, `landAlpha` 0.75, dot radius multiplier 0.75 to 0.95 in `presence-map.js`; measured ~0.37 effective, verified in a browser that continents read while the sign-up card stays legible. Presence data itself is untouched, measured-only per 2026-08-27.

- **Responsive surfaces size themselves by the container they actually occupy, and spectators get a broadcast rather than spare seats** (2026-08-31, Aidan, from the live screenshot pass). Three defects shared one pattern: desktop viewport rules were being applied to narrow embedded surfaces. `/live`'s 420px drawer inherited the full board's four-column seat grid, so Pro, Take, Con, and the occupant name painted over each other; the drawer now owns a one-seat-per-row intrinsic grid and phone actions become a two-track stack. `/spar` had closed its `min-width:1281px` block before the search-shell and wait-rail rules, so the desktop sticky rail rendered at full viewport height on every iPhone and pushed its three links to the bottom of a blank card; those rules are desktop-only again. The landing live-room band now clears the fixed topbar at both desktop and phone heights instead of placing the room row behind it. In `/live-round`, the resolution scales against the panel container and caps at 2.65rem instead of using the whole viewport to reach 4.25rem. Audience mode is now visibly a broadcast: a blue audience marker, a dominant camera with the second assigned seat as a reaction window, a cooler console surface, and a wider stage. Its Daily stage is filtered back to the round's assigned roster before rendering, so a stale visible participant record cannot become a third black seat; a normal casual room never shows more than its two assigned people. Spectator media attempts playback immediately and never interrupts the picture with a separate sound-gate button. The ambiguous dangling "AI tools" arrow is now "Neutral context" for debaters and is truly hidden whenever its context button is absent; the folded section names its actual contents, "Notes and fact checks." Debater controls, recording, timing, transcript sync, and judging are unchanged.

- **The live-video door takes Google or a verified phone number, and phone leads inside in-app browsers** (2026-09-01 evening, Aidan: "fix the sign in door"). This narrows the Google-only rule from 2026-08-27 and earlier the same day without reopening anything anonymous. The reason is the paid-social measurement of 2026-08-24: 339 of 340 TikTok sessions arrived inside TikTok's in-app browser, where Google refuses OAuth outright and the redirect fallback dies on partitioned storage, so the only door on /spar was one that could not open for the population an ad buys. Making /spar Google-only in the afternoon made that worse, not better, and an ad was about to be bought against it. A verified phone number is as accountable as a Google account (one real number per person, code round-trip, and Firebase Phone Auth completes inside a webview), so it is the second key on the same door rather than a softer one. **The provider set now lives in five places that must agree:** `isLiveVideoAccount()` in `firestore.rules` (create and update on `matchmaking_queue`; the `authProvider` marker must equal the caller's OWN verified `sign_in_provider`, so a client cannot stamp a provider it does not hold), `LIVE_VIDEO_PROVIDERS` in `spar-pair.mjs` (token check and both seat markers) and `create-daily-room.mjs` (debater tokens), and the client copies in `spar.html` and `js/notifications.js`; `live-popup.js` and `live-round.html` accept the same pair. `scripts/test-spar-google-gate.mjs` pins all of it. **The rendering follows the browser.** On the open web the /spar gate leads with Google and offers phone underneath; inside an in-app browser (the shared `__ditIsInAppBrowser` detector, `?inapp=1` to force it for QA) phone takes the primary slot, every secondary "sign in" click on the page routes to phone instead of a Google popup that will fail, and the shared chooser gains a `liveVideo` mode (Google plus phone, no email door the queue would refuse) and a `startWith:'phone'` option so the prompt opens on the phone form. Email and password remain full accounts everywhere else and still cannot sit in a video room. The guest allowance stays at zero. **Watch `spar_gate_phone_click` against `spar_gate_view` with `inapp` set** before any social ad is bought; if in-app visitors are not converting through phone, the door is still the problem and the ad waits.

- **Sign in with Apple opens the live video door** (2026-09-01, Aidan, after a screen recording for App Review showed the iOS app looping between the /spar gate and the sign-in chooser). The App Store requires the Apple button in the iOS app, and an Apple account then landed on a live queue that accepted only Google or phone, so the feature the app is named for was a dead end for the sign-in method Apple mandates. The reviewer's email demo account hit the same wall. `apple.com` now joins the live-video provider set in all seven places it is defined (spar.html, notifications.js, live-popup.js, live-round.html, spar-pair.mjs, create-daily-room.mjs, and `isLiveVideoAccount` in firestore.rules, deployed). Email-only accounts are still refused at that door. Known cost, accepted by Aidan: Apple relay addresses cannot receive our mail, so those users are hard to reach later. Same push: the native shell pins text controls to 16px and the viewport to maximum-scale=1, because iOS zoomed the page on the .95rem name prompt; and `isInAppBrowser()` returns false under `__DB_NATIVE`, because the shell's own WebView was being warned that Google sign-in does not work in it while the native Firebase plugin handles it fine.

- **Phone sign-in is retired two days after it shipped; the web chooser is Google, an emailed link, and email/password, and Apple stays in the iOS app** (2026-09-03, Aidan: "we're just going to do google and then whatever other tools u can setup quickly as sign in but not text and not phone. apple for IOS sure but then i can't email them"). **This supersedes the phone half of the 2026-09-01 entry directly below.** What went: the Continue with phone button, the number and code screens, the reCAPTCHA verifier and the link-collision recovery in `auth-modal.js`; `startWith:'phone'` on every live-video prompt; the /spar gate's phone button and the in-app rendering that led with it. The live-video provider set is `['google.com', 'apple.com']` again in all seven places (`isLiveVideoAccount()` in `firestore.rules`, `spar-pair.mjs`, `create-daily-room.mjs`, `spar.html`, `notifications.js`, `live-popup.js`, `live-round.html`), and `scripts/test-spar-google-gate.mjs` now greps every one of them for a stray phone entry, verified by reintroducing one and watching the guard fail. The Firebase phone provider itself is left enabled in the project config, because disabling it buys nothing and re-enabling is a console setting; nothing on the site can reach it. **Cost, measured before the change: four accounts, all phone-only, all without an email, created 2026-08-13, 09-02 and 09-03 (two).** They can no longer sign in anywhere, and they could never have been emailed, which is the founder's reason for the call. **What an in-app browser visitor gets now.** Phone existed because TikTok and Instagram webviews refuse Google OAuth (339 of 340 TikTok sessions on 2026-08-24). With it gone there is no key on the live-video door that turns inside a webview, so /spar and the shared chooser say the true thing instead of failing twice: open itsdebatable.com in Safari or Chrome, with a Copy link button because a webview has no address bar. The email doors still work in a webview on every surface that offers them. This is a known conversion cost on paid social traffic and it is the founder's call to carry it; the lever if it bites is the emailed link on those surfaces, never phone.

- **Web sign-in is Google-only; Apple remains in the iOS app** (2026-09-03, Aidan: "NO WE ONLY Allow google"). This supersedes the email doors in the phone-retirement entry above and the 2026-09-01 generic-chooser entry below. Every public web account ask now renders Google and nothing else. The iOS shell still renders Apple beside Google because App Store login-choice rules require it. The old email link and password implementation remains dormant only so a link already in flight can finish without corrupting an account; no public chooser advertises or opens those forms. Known cost: an existing email-only account has no public way back in, and a TikTok or Instagram in-app browser still has no provider that can complete there. Those browsers get the honest Safari-or-Chrome instruction and a Copy link button.

- **The waiting-person sign-in prompt moves from the corner into the shared centered dialog** (2026-09-03, Aidan, on the landing screenshot: "have a pop up not bottom right but middle ... for signing in with google"). When `/api/live-now` finds someone waiting and the visitor is signed out, `live-popup.js` opens the shared Google dialog directly instead of rendering the bottom-right invitation card first. A signed-in person still gets the compact open-seat card because it is an actionable live alert, not an account ask. The persistent topbar Sign in control and every account-gated action open that same centered dialog. The copy says explicitly that one Google button signs in returning accounts and creates new ones, with no Debatable password to remember. Dismissing the automatic dialog snoozes it for four hours and does not redirect to `/spar`. Web stays Google-only. Twitch and Discord are not authentication providers: adding either creates account-linking and identity-recovery work without improving email reachability, while Discord remains the community door. Google supplies the account email used by the one-time welcome note and later opted-in lifecycle messages.

- **Live spectators use the same Google door as the people arguing** (2026-09-03, Aidan: "require watching to have google sign in too tbh 'sign in to spectate on live debates'"). This supersedes every earlier claim that live spectating is anonymous or needs no account. A human opening a Watch live row, the sitewide live-round card, or a direct `?spectate=1` link gets the exact prompt **"Sign in to spectate live debates"**; completion returns them to that exact room. A persisted anonymous uid and a retired email-only account are both refused, because truthy Firebase state is not the same thing as the live-video provider set. `/api/create-daily-room` repeats the check for `role:'viewer'`, so stripping the client overlay does not mint a human viewer token. Replays remain public and this changes no recording access. The sole exception is `?stage=1`, which is the non-person chromeless renderer captured by `/air` and legacy OBS browser sources; it sends a distinct `role:'stage'` and remains hidden and receive-only, so the conversion door cannot black out the production feed.

- **The competitive-or-new question changes explanations only** (2026-09-03, Aidan: "this is just so we explain ideas well"). The entry card asks, "Are you a competitive debater or new?" and immediately says either is fine. Competitive leaves authored debate terminology visible; new swaps supported interface terms for plain-English explanations. It does not change the opponent, match, topic, difficulty, judge, score, entitlement, or price. New public rounds already use accessible language for everyone, so this preference does not change how the AI argues either.

- **The competitive-or-new question waits for one visible minute, and casual language is the default** (2026-09-03, Aidan: "ask the question ... after user has used app for like 1 min" and "default to casual user writing"). The sitewide entry card no longer appears 2.2 seconds after arrival. It counts cumulative visible time across full-page navigation in the current browser session and asks at 60 seconds; a backgrounded tab earns no time, and round pages may earn the minute without being interrupted. Until someone explicitly chooses competitive, `audience-mode.js` applies the same accessible plain-language swaps used for new and unsure visitors. Dismissing the question also leaves that casual default in place. The stored answer still changes explanations only.

- **Uploaded profile photos render as normal images in mounted account faces** (2026-09-03, Aidan, from the signed-in topbar: "i added a profile image but its not showing up here"). The upload and public endpoint were both healthy: the production blob existed and returned a valid 512px JPEG. The failure was the renderer. `mountIdentity()` nested that JPEG in an SVG `<image>` over a hardcoded dark rectangle; Chrome painted only the rectangle in the topbar, producing an unexplained black dot. Mounted identities, including the topbar and `/profile`, now place a normal absolutely positioned `<img>` over a deterministic avatar fallback and remove it on an error. The topbar's fast-path probe also includes the uploaded-photo version key, so a stale Firebase `photoURL` cannot outrank the explicit upload. String-only public surfaces keep the SVG renderer they require.

- **Every new account gets one welcome email, sent to land in Primary** (2026-09-03, Aidan: "send an email congratulating and introducing debatable when people sign up so it lands in their main. explain the story, the vision, need for contributions"). Before this, a new account heard nothing until a campaign happened to run; the catch-up welcome (`admin-signup-welcome.mjs`) is a button, not a trigger. Now `lib/welcome-email.mjs` owns one template and one eligibility rule, and two entry points share it: `POST /api/welcome-email`, which `auth-modal.js` fires with a keepalive fetch the instant a sign-up completes so the note arrives while the tab is still open, and `scheduled-welcome-sweep.mjs` every 30 minutes as the backstop for every sign-in that never reaches the client path (a native Apple or Google sign-in, a tournament page's own popup, a closed tab). **One send per account, ever.** The stamp is `user_profiles.signupWelcomeSentAt`, the same field the catch-up campaign reads as "already emailed", so the two can never double-mail; a Firestore transaction claims the send before Resend is called, which is what stops the client path and the sweep racing onto one inbox. **Eligibility is pure and guarded** (`scripts/test-welcome-email.mjs`, in the hook): created on or after 2026-09-03 (the catch-up campaign owns the older cohort), a real provider, an email on the account, not an Apple relay or a test domain, no stamp from any campaign, not opted out. The endpoint can only mail its own caller, decided from Auth's own creation time rather than the token, so a hostile caller can at most send themselves the note they were getting anyway; no App Check needed. **Primary placement is the design constraint, and every choice serves it:** one recipient per send, From on the domain the person just signed up on (`hello@itsdebatable.com`, verified, measured landing in INBOX on the sign-in link), a real text/plain part, no image, no button, no pixel, four links, and Reply-To on the founder's real inbox because the email asks for a reply and a reply is the strongest inbox signal there is. The `welcome` stream deliberately carries no List-Unsubscribe headers (a one-to-one note, not a list); the footer still links a working unsubscribe on the onboarding stream. **The copy** covers the three asked-for beats inside the standing rules: the story (judging is the bottleneck, built from inside competitive debate, no founder name or credential), the vision (a public ladder, replays, an audience, eventually rounds against people you already watch, written as a thing to earn and never as a thing that has happened), and the contributions (argue one round, reply with what broke, bring one person, and one no-pressure line naming the canonical Individual price). The guard blocks em-dashes, banned phrases, the founder's name, "debaters" for the crowd, any off-canonical price, images and buttons, and more than four links. Subject is lowercase per the outreach memory. **Watch:** replies to the founder's inbox, and `signupWelcomeSource` on profiles (client vs sweep); if the sweep is doing most of the sending the client trigger is being missed somewhere and that is the bug to find.

- **The example board says "choose what you argue about" again, and carries partisan clash cards under short titles** (2026-09-03, Aidan: "bring back in red 'choose what you argue about' - also add options for 'democrats versus republicans' basic prompts and titles to some"). The red line at the top of the first-screen board now reads "Choose what you argue about. Press this card to debate.", keeping the 2026-08-31 press instruction behind the invitation it had lost. A ROUNDS entry may now carry `title`, rendered as a small red uppercase eyebrow above the motion and hidden otherwise; seven new judged cards ride it (four under "Democrats vs Republicans", three under "Left vs Right": party and economy, working-class realignment, too-far-left, third party, taxes on the rich, immigration, guaranteed healthcare), three existing cards gained a "Left vs Right" title, and one matched open card invites the partisan fight as its topic. Every new seat reuses an existing cast name so castFaces deals it a portrait, and every ballot names only the two people in the round. Deck is 81 cards.

- **The site gets a post-deploy browser smoke, and Supabase is turned down** (2026-09-03, Aidan: "how can we integrate playwright, supabase, and chromedevtools", then "set that all up now whatever helps"). The recurring failure in this log is a feature fully built, correct in every line, and never executed until a person happened to look. `e2e/` is a Playwright suite that runs in a real Chromium against production after every push to main (`.github/workflows/e2e-smoke.yml` waits for the served `sw.js` to carry the commit's `CACHE_NAME`, then runs) and checks the promises a stranger meets: the first screen and its Debate door, the signed-out `/spar` Match Desk then the Google-only gate and chooser, `/watch`, `/practice` mounting, the `/partners` redirect, `sw.js` parsing with a `CACHE_NAME`, `/api/claude` refusing a tokenless call, the public read endpoints, and the judge season calendar not having expired. **Two things it found on its first runs, both worth writing down.** The edge traffic filter from 2026-08-27 reads `sec-ch-ua` as well as the UA, and Playwright's default headless shell still brands itself HeadlessChrome in that hint, so every page test died with `net::ERR_ABORTED` (a 204 on a navigation); the suite runs `channel: 'chromium'` for that reason and the config says so. And `/` throws an uncaught "No Firebase App '[DEFAULT]' has been created" on a timing-dependent minority of loads, the six-module SDK injection race the 2026-08-18 App Check entry describes; it is allow-listed as a KNOWN OPEN bug that annotates the run rather than failing it, and the allow-list entry is the reminder to fix it. A two-person spar test is scaffolded, self-skipping until two throwaway Google sessions exist and refusing to run while anyone real is waiting in the queue. **`.mcp.json` gives every Claude Code session the Chrome DevTools MCP** for performance traces, long tasks, throttling, and a headed Chrome where `document.hidden` is real, which is the harness artifact that faked two negative results in this log. **Supabase was evaluated and not adopted:** auth, rules, storage and App Check are Firebase, and a second source of truth on this deploy topology costs more than the Firestore query gaps it would close; the native answer to those is the Firestore-to-BigQuery export, and pgvector over the argument corpus is the only thesis-aligned use, worth revisiting once `contributable` is in the hundreds. Rider: the pre-commit SW bump matched `e2e/*.mjs` as client files and bumped the cache for tests nobody is served; `e2e/` is excluded now.

- **The Match Desk questionnaire is readable on a phone, and the reason it was not is sitewide** (2026-09-03, Aidan off a live screenshot: "research the most easiest to read and stand out text ... this questionare, esp on mobile, is a mess"). Measured at 375px before touching anything: the option labels a person actually reads in order to answer were **16.3px of Source Serif 4 inside two 171px columns**, so "Yes, and I compete" wrapped to two lines in a cell narrower than a business card; the hint was 14.1px; the step label 11.2px under .18em of tracking; and a five-word question spent **39px and three lines** at -1.77px letter-spacing while two thirds of the screen below it sat empty. Every mobile-typography guideline puts the floor at 16px for text you act on, 44px for a touch target, 1.5 line-height, and 35-45 characters a line on a phone. This surface met the touch target and missed the rest.
  - **The fix that matters is one column.** `js/arcade-flow.js`'s own component ships options single-column and says why in its comment ("big, thumb-sized"); the desk skin overrode it to two. Every panel is single-column under 620px now, and the `!important` is deliberate rather than lazy: topics, mode and hot each set their own column count at higher specificity, so a panel added later would otherwise reintroduce exactly this bug.
  - **The option label goes SANS, and the question keeps the serif.** The editorial identity lives in the masthead and the question, which are read once. The options are scanned and acted on, which is the job a humanist sans does better at 17px on a small screen. Question 26px on one line, hint 16px, step 12px, options 17px in 60px full-width rows.
  - **The finding underneath, and it is not the Match Desk's:** `app/css/ui.css` sets `html{font-size:13.5px !important}` under 560px, and that beats the 2026-08-18 "Readability floor" block that sets 15.5px for phones **even though the floor block is later in the file and its own comment says "Keep this block LAST so it wins"**. `!important` beats source order. So every rem on every page resolves ~21% smaller than authored on a phone, and the two decisions have been quietly contradicting each other since. The Match Desk's critical type is px for that reason, which is stated where the next person edits it. **Reconciling the six competing root declarations is a sitewide pass and it has not been done here**; doing it blind would resize ~116 pages at once.
  - Same push: the paper texture behind the type dropped from .38 to .2 opacity (contrast before atmosphere), and the rail's red SKIP pill went quiet because the fixed SKIP bar Aidan asked for on 2026-09-02 already carries that job. Two red skips either side of the question were competing with the question.

- **Session replay is PostHog, injected by track.js, and it records movement rather than content** (2026-09-03, Aidan: "setup posthog session replays"). The Clarity loader had sat on ten pages with a placeholder id since May and never recorded a session; it is deleted. `js/posthog.js` is injected by `track.js`, so replay coverage equals tracking coverage (every public page, none of admin / og-image / offline / _more-preview) with no per-page wiring. Four boundaries are the design, not settings: recording starts only on a browser-trusted pointer, key or touch (the same gate as the presence beat, so renderers and untouched bounces never record); every input is masked and DM / chat bodies are masked by selector, with `/messages`, `/chat` and `/admin` skipped outright; `<video>`, `<canvas>` and `<iframe>` are blocked, so a camera feed, screen share, Daily call or avatar mask is an empty box and camera frames never leave the room; named accounts identify by Firebase uid only, anonymous uids never identify. `?replay=off` is the per-browser kill, `REPLAY_SAMPLE` is the cost dial (free tier is 5,000 replays a month). Privacy §2 and the sub-processor table disclose it. **The key is the one thing code cannot supply:** `POSTHOG_KEY` in `js/posthog.js` no-ops until a `phc_` project key is pasted in, which is a one-line commit.

- **/newvoice asks which subject first, then hands over resolutions from the archive** (2026-09-03, Aidan, off a phone screenshot of the topic screen: the lineup was "absolutely abhorrent"). The morning's dinner-table bank ("Gym class should be optional", "Cats are better than dogs") and its four subject cards ("School life", "Phones and AI") are gone. The bank is grouped into eleven subjects (AI, Money, Work, Politics, Internet, The world, Climate, Cities, School, Culture, Health), about twelve resolutions each, drawn from the Aug 31 bank and the canonical quick pool in practice.html, in plain words on the questions people are actually arguing about now. The picker asks "What do you want to argue about?" as a row of subject chips; a chip reveals five resolutions on that subject, "Show me five more" redraws from that subject's deck, and New topic shuffles inside the chosen subject. The topic box stays the single source of truth, so the side echo, summary and spoken opener are untouched; a subject pick overwrites the box only while it holds a claim nobody chose, never something typed. Talk-it-through stays on top. The guard-pinned `randomClaim()` lines are byte-identical, and `scripts/test-sensitive-motions.mjs` still scans the bank. **Same day, the twelfth lane: Storytime** (Aidan: "hyper specifc scenario - storytime mode"). Each entry is a two or three sentence scene with a name, a sum and a date, ending in one call to defend or attack ("Your landlord raises the rent by $300 but offers to freeze it if you sign for three years. You should sign"). Same for-or-against shape, so nothing downstream changes; 220 characters is the ceiling because the topic box and sanitizeTopic() cap there.

- **The first-screen resolution is set in a serif** (2026-09-03, Aidan off a six-way type board: "this lowk gas for the resolution - apply it"). Supersedes the 2026-09-01 Archivo-bold call for that one element. The motion is the one line a stranger reads and it wore the same face as the Friends button, so it now runs Instrument Serif at regular weight, upright, about 20% larger, black on light and warm white on dark, on the desktop board and the phone home. Every label, button and the wordmark stay on the sans. Instrument Serif loads regular only; no italic, per the site rule. The brand-entity guard pins the new face. This reopens the display-face question the 2026-08-19 Inter entry closed, for this element only; the rest of the site is unchanged.

- **First session-replay audit: seven fixes, one finding that outranks them** (2026-09-05, Aidan: "watch the entirety of these user experiences" then "fix everything"). PostHog replay went live 09-03; the first 36 hours held ~150 sessions with events and ~60 replays, Aidan's own uid excluded. The finding: **in fourteen days of recorded live rooms, nobody pressed Start my speech.** Rooms open, people go to Avatar, Design, Chat, Options, Switch sides, and leave in one to four minutes. Spar funnel over the window: 56 saw /spar, 24 entered the queue, 22 signed up with Google, 13 pressed "I'm here", 4 got "Join them", about 8 reached a room, 0 speeches. 17 sessions left the queue through "Debate the AI". Fixed in one push, each traced to a replay: `live-round`'s Hide me tray button called `paintParticipants()`, which never existed (`paintRoom()`); the server-transcription recorder's `start()` threw NotSupportedError uncaught on Leave; `/api/user/style-summary` 500'd for everyone because `generations` had no `uid` + `createdAt` composite index, so every /profile said "Could not load your style profile"; `/newvoice`'s anon wall offered a "Sign in free" link that only worked if `auth-modal.js` happened to be loaded already (one guest pressed it twice, got nothing, and took eight minutes to reach a round), so the page now loads the chooser on demand through one `openSignInDoor`; `app-check.js` minted one token per concurrent gated call, which on a bad reCAPTCHA produced twenty-error storms and a 24-hour SDK throttle, so it shares one in-flight mint and backs off 30s after a refusal; the homepage example round now carries an "Example round · press to debate live" chip, because a visitor read "3 live right now" over a clock and two faces as a stream and pressed the card and its arrows eighteen times; /spar's ready button said "Starting…" while it was really waiting on the other person, and now says so; /newvoice's New topic is a no-repeat shuffle bag after one person pressed it thirty times; and saving an avatar in a room with an unstarted speech now points at Start my speech. **Not fixed, stated:** Google One Tap fails with a FedCM NetworkError on about a third of the pages it prompts on, which is Google-side; and the in-room avatar designer is still one tap from the first screen a debater sees, which the replays say is where the round goes to die. Method notes live in Claude memory (`reference_posthog_data_pull`).

- **The desk interrupts the questions when someone is waiting, and the site starts shouting in a few places** (2026-09-04, Aidan: "if 2 ppl are actually in the middle of questionnaire, just skip straight to meeting them. disrupt questions by offering thru pop up: meet or question. someone is literally waiting!", then "have a bigger character of using all caps in some areas across the website to humanize the feeling"). While the Match Desk is open the live queue count is polled every 10 seconds (`/api/spar-queue`, shared-cached 15s server-side, so the poll adds no reads). The first reading with a person waiting interrupts the step with one card: SOMEONE IS WAITING, MEET THEM NOW runs the existing skip path straight into the queue with the neutral profile, Keep answering dismisses. It re-asks only if the queue empties and a NEW person arrives; a card that returns on every poll is a nag and dismissing is an answer. Never while editing a saved profile, never on the launch panel (its own button is one tap from the same place), never in a hidden tab. **Limit, stated:** two people both inside the questionnaire are invisible to each other, because neither has a queue doc yet. The interrupt fires for a person already IN the queue. Making questionnaire-fillers visible to each other means a presence write before consent, which is a separate decision. **The caps pass is deliberately small.** Caps went where a line is a person telling you one thing: the interrupt headline, KEEP THIS TAB OPEN on the waiting rail, YOU ARE THE ONLY ONE HERE and LOOKING FOR YOUR DEBATER on /live-round. Page headlines and the landing hero were left in sentence case: the hero copy is inside a running A/B and under concurrent edit, and a site that shouts everywhere is not humanised, it is loud. Next spots, if Aidan wants more: the /spar paired moment, the live-round kickoff strip, the ballot reveal.

- **The Match Desk reserves space instead of floating over it, and asks whether you argue what you believe** (2026-09-04, Aidan, off a screenshot of the SKIP bar sitting on top of the "None of them" row: "really be smart about positioning stuff, now do a sweep across the board"; then "ask a question of 'do you care to argue what you believe in, or do you also want to argue opinions you dont believe for the fun of it', make that like the 2 or 3 question"). Measured before touching anything: at 1440x800 the fixed SKIP bar covered the last option on the portrait step and the launch panel; at 1000x567 the step label was clipped off the top on eight of nine panels; on a landscape phone every step was covered. One cause: the bar was `position:fixed` over the track, and the 132px panel padding meant to keep options clear lived inside a vertically centred panel, so any panel taller than the track pushed its own heading off the top and its last option under the bar at once. **The bar is now a footer row of the flow.** `.afl` is a flex column with a `flex:1` track, so a footer appended after the track takes its height out of the track and nothing can sit underneath it at any size. Panels anchor to the top, so the step label lands at the same y on every step (20px on desktop, 12px in short landscape) instead of hunting between 33 and 101px. The rail's own skip pill is hidden in this flow because the footer says the same thing bigger, which also removes the two-line pill on phones. Laptop heights drop the masthead's second line so the portrait step fits without its own scroll; landscape phones get a one-line masthead, a 42px rail and a one-line footer. On phones the six portraits go two-up with the picture above the name, three rows instead of six, and the launch button sits above the nine-row review. The footer stands down on the launch panel, whose own button is renamed "Find my opponent" because a person who just answered everything should not be pressing something labelled Skip. The masthead count derives from the steps, so it stopped saying "seven" for a flow that had eight. **The new question is step 2:** "Do you only argue what you believe?" with "Only what I believe" and "Any side, for the fun of it". It is stored as `conviction` and shown on the profile card; nothing reads it into the queue or the private profile yet, and a profile saved before it existed reads as the wider answer, which is what the room's motion draft assumes of everyone today. Wiring it into side assignment is the next real use of it. Verification note for whoever touches this next: an IntersectionObserver never fires while the document is hidden, and the Browser pane runs hidden, so the launch toggle is scroll-driven; and a stale service worker on a reused localhost port intercepts `/js/arcade-flow.js` with ERR_FAILED before the static server ever sees it, which looks like a broken page and is not.

- **Every generic account ask leads to one chooser, and phone is a real sign-in method** (2026-09-01, Aidan: "i want user experience to lead to sign ins always. email or phone"). The guest trial infrastructure remains because it owns the first-round meter, but every conversion surface points at a durable account rather than presenting anonymous auth as an identity. The shared chooser offers Google, phone OTP, emailed link, and email/password; generic nudges and the landing sign-in row name the account outcome rather than forcing Google. Phone login uses Firebase Phone Auth with invisible reCAPTCHA, links the current anonymous uid when possible so guest work survives, and falls back to the existing phone account when the number already belongs to one. The separate `/api/phone` flow is still notification consent after sign-in, not an authentication system. `/spar` remains Google-only under its live-video identity rule.

- **The mobile home loses three pieces of persistent chrome** (2026-09-03, Aidan, from a phone screenshot: "get rid of this", "get rid of feedback button", and "get rid of that [sign out] button entirely"). The example board no longer carries the red "Choose what you argue about. Press this card to debate." instruction; its native hit target and pager remain, so the card still opens `/spar` without explaining itself twice. The landing's floating Feedback button is gone at every width, while the non-floating footer and tour feedback links still open the same form. The signed-in header is now one Profile link: name on wider screens, avatar alone on phones. The inline Out action is deleted, and sign out remains inside Profile and the hamburger account sheet. This supersedes the same-day entry above that restored the red board line.

- **Political discovery becomes a Democratic-versus-Republican challenge funnel** (2026-09-03, Aidan: "make them more polarized politics like D vs R issues" and "have the choose a political question automatically go into challenges or something"). The politics hub and topic guide lead with eight recognizable party fault lines, each showing a common Democratic case and a common Republican case grounded in the parties' published national platforms. "Common case" is load-bearing: the cards summarize a recurring party argument without assigning it to every voter. Pressing either side opens `/challenges` with the question, neutral clash, both party labels, and the chosen side prefilled; the person may edit and review it, and nothing posts until they sign in and confirm. The random topic picker goes straight to the same prefilled composer, and every evergreen political question has a challenge handoff beside its issue brief. There is no new politics record type: posted prompts are ordinary public asynchronous challenges, so matching, notifications, the board, acceptance, rounds, and the existing content guard all stay one system. The judge receives the question and arguments, never party affiliation as a scoring input.

- **Watch is a dark editorial surface, not a decorated hero card** (2026-09-03, Aidan, from the live page: "fix the design its cut off at the top weirdly" and "make page feel less vibe coded"). `/watch` defaults to dark independently of the sitewide light default, while an explicit saved Watch choice still wins and the shared toggle stays available. The masthead clears the fixed navigation with real page spacing, sits directly on the background, and uses a single bottom rule instead of a clipped rounded panel, gradient, orbit, glow, or shadow. The page no longer loads the neural constellation or `social-depth.css`. Source Serif 4 carries the one editorial headline, DM Sans carries the interface, and the two debate shelves use restrained 14px panels. The large red live-room action remains the only loud element above the fold.

- **The politics funnel is for people who talk politics, and Independent challenges the system rather than impersonating a third platform** (2026-09-03, Aidan: "or independent - the case for independent parties etc", then "make it for poliical talker ppl"). The acquisition promise is now the social habit: bring the argument that would otherwise live in a group chat and find somebody willing to answer it. Democratic and Republican case cards stay, while a dark Independent lane adds four structural fights: voting for a long-shot independent, ranked-choice voting, open top-four primaries, and independent access to major televised debates. Independent voters do not share one national policy platform, so the page never invents a single Independent position on taxes, immigration, health care, or culture; each card instead pairs an `Independent case` with a `Two-party case`, grounded in NCSL election-system guides and the FEC's debate rules. The challenge handoff now carries a presentation-only `lane` (`partisan`, `independent`, or `general`) so the composer names the right frame. That also fixes the generic topic path, which previously opened every political question under a misleading "Democrats vs Republicans" eyebrow and said "You chose the For" even when no side had been picked. Storage, moderation, the two-seat round, and judging are unchanged.

- **Political pages are an SEO doorway into the live product, not a separate product loop** (2026-09-03, Aidan, on the issue-brief CTA: "start a round should go to live debates 'debate someone now'" and "this should simply be for SEO but direct back to main page better"). The primary action on `/political-debate`, `/political-debate-topics`, `/contested`, `/debate`, and every question brief is `Debate someone now`, linked directly to the live Match Desk at `/spar`. The back path to `/` is explicit in the political page masthead and topic hero. Party-side and question-specific Challenge handoffs remain secondary because they preserve the earlier request to carry a selected question and side into the public challenge composer. They must never outrank the live action or make the SEO cluster feel like a separate politics product. Issue-brief Pro and Con buttons may still open the AI round because they promise that exact selected question and side; a generic `Start a round` action may not silently send political search traffic into AI practice.
